# Taranga 5. The Parikrama of Vraja

*Raghava Gosvami leads Srinivasa and Narottama through Vraja: the glory of Mathura and its tirthas; the forests of Vraja; Radha-kunda and Shyama-kunda; Govardhana; Kamyavana, Varshana, Sanketa, Nandishvara, Javata; the ages of Krishna; Gokula, Raval, Mahavana; stories of Sanatana, Rupa and Jiva; the birth and childhood of Advaita; the travels and initiation of Nityananda; Chir-ghat, Nidhuvana, Vamshivata and the rasa; the theory of music and dance; instruction on offences against Vaishnavas*

**1.** Glory, glory to Sri Gaura-Govinda, the Lord of all!
Glory, glory to Nityananda-deva, Haladhara!

**2.** Glory to Sri Advaita, the crown jewel of those who give bhakti!
Glory to Sri Pandita Gadadhara, the mine of prema!

**3.** Glory, glory to Srivasa Pandita, the friend of the helpless!
Glory to Sanatana and Rupa, oceans of compassion!

**4.** Glory to the merciful devotees of the Lord!
Be merciful, all of you — I have taken shelter.

**5.** Glory, glory to the listeners, abode of virtues!
Listen kindly to what I shall tell now.

**6.** What Sri Jiva's affection for Srinivasa Acharya
and Narottama Mahashaya was like cannot be told.

**7.** One day Sri Jiva Gosvami thought:
"I shall quickly send the two everywhere to see everything."

**8.** As he was considering in his mind who would go with them,
Raghava Gosani came from Govardhana.[^1]

**9.** Sri Jiva Gosvami, delighted to see him,
seated him and asked about his welfare.

**10.** He said, "I shall wander through Vraja;
that is why I have come so quickly."

**11.** Sri Jiva says, "This is good in every way:
Srinivasa and Narottama will go with you."

**12.** Hearing this, Sri Raghava was very glad;
just then Srinivasa and Narottama came.

**13.** When the two bowed, he embraced them both;
the hearts of both became greatly joyful.

**14.** Sri Jiva Gosvami joyfully told Narottama and Srinivasa
about the journey through the holy forests.

**15.** Hearing this, Srinivasa and Narottama, glad at heart,
took leave of everyone at that very moment.

**16.** Sri Jiva Gosvami, with great satisfaction of heart,
gave Narottama and Srinivasa leave to go.

**17.** Sri Raghava, taking Srinivasa and Narottama with him,
went to Mathura in great joy.[^2]

**18.** Near the temple of Sri Keshava-deva
they stayed, where Sri Subuddhi had lived.[^3]

**19.** Telling of the virtues of Sri Subuddhi Raya,
at evening time they performed the kirtan of the holy name.

**20.** Raghava Gosani is always intoxicated with the bliss of prema;
there is no end to telling of Raghava's life.

**21.** He is a brahmana of the South, renowned for his most noble lineage;
his deeds as a great Vaishnava — who can describe them?

**22.** To the lowly and wretched he showed the utmost mercy;
it is he who wrote the *Bhakti-ratna-prakasha* and other books.

**23.** His all-in-all is Sri Govardhana Hill;
he lives at Govardhana and is learned in all the shastras.

**24.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika (162):*
*"She who in Vraja was Sri Champakalata, the very life of Sri Radha,*
*is now Raghava Gosvami, who has made his dwelling at Govardhana,*
*by whom the book called Bhakti-ratna-prakasha has been revealed."*[^4]

**25.** From time to time he joyfully wanders through Vraja,
from time to time he stays with Dasa Gosvami.[^5]

**26.** Sometimes, coming together with him to Vrindavana,
he finds great bliss in seeing the Lords.[^6]

**27.** He always sings of the deeds of Radha-Krishna and Chaitanya;
unable to keep his composure, he floats away in tears.

**28.** He is grey with dust and has no desire to eat;
his intense renunciation — who can understand it?

**29.** Srinivasa and Narottama are full of prema-bhakti;
regarding the two as one, he loves them exceedingly.

**30.** In the evening, in private, he tells the two
of Krishna's endless pastimes in Mathura-mandala.

**31.** In Mathura-mandala there was King Vajranabha;[^7]
he founded many villages named after Krishna's pastimes.

**32.** He established the service of Deities, manifested kundas and so on;
his desire was fulfilled in many ways.

**33.** Some time later everything became almost hidden;
no one anywhere speaks of the holy places.

**34.** Sri Krishna-Chaitanyachandra, the son of the King of Vraja,
came to Mathura — boundless was the wonder.

**35.** Wandering, He gave some indication of the way,
and through Sanatana and Rupa He revealed everything.[^8]

**36.** Although all those places were known to the two,
still they accepted the way laid down by the shastras.

**37.** Gathering the evidence of many shastras,
the two wandered through Vraja.

**38.** With great care they recovered the hidden holy places
and made manifest the sweetness of the rasa of Radha-Krishna.

**39.** By the mercy of Rupa and Sanatana, dear to the Lord,
all people now sing the glory of Mathura.

**40.** This Mathura-mandala [extends] for twenty yojanas;[^9]
bathing anywhere in it dispels all sins.

**41.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"My Mathura-mandala is twenty yojanas;*
*a man who has bathed anywhere in it is freed from all sins."*

**42.** As the rising of the sun drives away darkness,
as mountains tremble in fear of the thunderbolt,

**43.** as a snake is afraid on seeing Garuda,
as a mass of clouds is dispersed by the touch of the wind,

**44.** as through knowledge of the truth not a trace of sorrow remains,
as a deer trembles on seeing a lion,

**45.** as happens to a heap of grass on contact with fire —
so at the sight of Mathura all sins perish.

**46.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"As darkness perishes at sunrise, as mountains in fear of the thunderbolt,*
*as snakes on seeing Tarkshya [Garuda], as clouds scattered by the wind,*

**47.** *as sorrow through knowledge of the truth, as deer on seeing a lion —*
*so sins perish in a moment at the sight of Mathura."*

**48.** *And further, as [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Patala-khanda, in the dialogue of Hara and Gauri:*[^10]
*"As sparks set a heap of grass ablaze,*
*so the city of Mathura burns up great sins."*

**49.** In this Mathura-mandala of twenty yojanas
at every step the merit of an ashvamedha sacrifice is gained.

**50.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"My Mathura-mandala is twenty yojanas;*
*at every step [there is] the merit of an ashvamedha — of this there is no doubt."*

**51.** The sin one has acquired knowingly or in ignorance,
the sin committed elsewhere — Mathura destroys it.

**52.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Sin committed elsewhere perishes in Mathura —*
*the sin acquired knowingly or even in ignorance."*

**53.** Mathura destroys the sins accumulated over many births;
the glory of Mathura is proclaimed in all the Puranas.

**54.** *[Thus it is said] in the Padma Purana, Patala-khanda:*
*"Sins accumulated over many births depart;*
*sin born in Mathura perishes in a single moment."*

**55.** If one sins in Mathura, Mathura destroys [that sin];
one who dwells [there] attains dharma, artha, kama and moksha.

**56.** *Thus [it is said] in the Vayu Purana:*
*"Sin committed in Mathura perishes in Mathura;*
*dwelling there, a man attains what is called dharma, artha, kama and moksha."*

**57.** The prarabdha sin that elsewhere is worked off in ten years
in Mathura is worked off in ten days.

**58.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Patala-khanda:*
*"The sin of prarabdha that elsewhere is worked off in ten years*
*is [worked off] in Mathura, O Mahadevi, in ten days."*

**59.** Sri Mathura is surely above all holy places;
a place so dear to Krishna exists nowhere else.

**60.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Neither in the nether worlds, nor in the heavens, nor in the world of men*
*is there [a place] equal to Mathura, so dear to Me, O Vasundhara."*[^11]

**61.** The fruit that in Bharata-varsha is gained over many days —
that fruit is gained by remembering this Mathura.

**62.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, Mathura-khanda, in the words of Narada:*
*"The fruit [gained] in Bharata-varsha in thirty thousand years and thirty hundred years —*
*that fruit [is gained by] one who remembers Mathura."*

**63.** One who has not seen Mathura and sets out to see it,
wherever he dies, is born in Mathura-mandala.[^12]

**64.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Patala-khanda:*
*"One who has not seen Mathura, but in whom the desire to see it has arisen —*
*wherever he dies, he is born in Mathura-mandala."*

**65.** Sri Mathura is the best of all, the refuge of many holy places;
the holy places in Mathura are beyond number.

**66.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Sixty thousand crores and sixty hundred crores —*
*such, O Vasudha, is the number of holy places in Mathura told by Me."*

**67.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, Mathura-khanda:*
*"The specks of dust of the earth, O king, might in time be counted,*
*but of the holy places that are in Mathura-mandala there is no count."*

**68.** All the shastras teach to dwell in Mathura;
by dwelling in Mathura every perfection is attained.

**69.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Patala-khanda:*
*"Make your home, O make your home in the city of Mathura,*
*where the gopis dwell and Govinda, the illuminator of the three worlds."*

**70.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place:*
*"O you, O you sunk in samsara! Hear this one instruction:*
*if you desire complete happiness, make your home in the city of Madhu."*[^13]

**71.** One who leaves Mathura and longs for other places
is a wretched fool, bewildered by the maya of the Lord.

**72.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"One who leaves Mathura and becomes attached to another place —*
*that fool, bewildered by My maya, wanders in the round of births."*

**73.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, Mathura-khanda:*
*"One who, even after attaining Mathura, longs for another place —*
*what knowledge has that witless man? He is bewildered by ignorance."*

**74.** One who has no refuge of any kind —
Mathura is his refuge; this is known from the scriptures.

**75.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Those abandoned by mother and father, forsaken by their own kin, who have no refuge at all — for them Madhupuri is the refuge."*[^14]

**76.** *"The place that is the essence beyond all essence, the highest secret of secrets: for those who seek a refuge, Mathura is the supreme refuge."*

**77.** In Mathura Krishna Himself abides constantly;
it is the best of all holy lands, vast and enchanting.

**78.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"There is no holy land in the three worlds higher than Mathura — that Mathura in which, O Devi, I always dwell."*[^15]

**79.** *Thus [it is said] in the Srimad-Bhagavatam, in the Fourth Canto (4.8.42):*
*"Therefore, child, go — may good fortune be yours — to the pure bank of the Yamuna, to sacred Madhuvana, where Hari is always present."*[^16]

**80.** *Thus [it is said] in the Vishnu Purana:*
*"…where Shatrughna, having slain the mighty rakshasa Lavana, son of Madhu, built the city named Mathura —*[^17]

**81.** *there too is the presence of the God of gods, Harimedhas; at that tirtha, which takes away all sins, he performed austerities."*[^18]

**82.** *Thus [it is said] in the Vayu Purana:*
*"Mathura lies over forty yojanas; there the Lord Hari Himself visibly abides at all times."*[^19]

**83.** By Sri Krishna's mercy attachment to Mathura arises;
by piety, charity, austerity and the like it is certainly not to be had.

**84.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi Purana:*
*"This is not attained by pious deeds, nor by charity, nor by austerities, nor by japa, nor by various sacrifices; it is attained by My grace."*

**85.** *"By the mercy of Sri Vishnu, surely, residence there will come about; without Krishna's grace one does not stay [there] even a moment."*

**86.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Uttara-khanda:*
*"Only in those blessed ones whose devotion to Hari is steady and on whom His mercy is abundant does attachment to Mathura arise."*

**87.** Mathura is attained through meditation on the Lord and the like;
otherwise Madhupuri is certainly unattainable.

**88.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Nirvana-khanda:*
*"When people are purified by austerity and the like, have taken refuge in the auspicious, and unceasing meditation is their wealth — only then do they see My supreme city, and not otherwise, even in hundreds of kalpas, O best of the twice-born."*

**89.** Sri Mathura grants liberation in every respect;
the Puranas and other [scriptures] say so clearly; it is known to the world.

**90.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Whatever destination belongs to the wise knower of Brahman absorbed in yoga, that same destination belongs to a man who gives up his life in Mathura."*

**91.** *"In a tirtha or in a house, in a courtyard or on a road — wherever they die, O Devi, they attain liberation; it is not otherwise."*

**92.** *"If Kashi and other cities exist in the world, among them Mathura alone is blessed, which, through birth, the maunji rite, dying and cremation, grants people liberation of four kinds."*[^20]

**93.** *"Worms, insects, moths and the rest that die in Mathura, and the trees that fall from the bank — even they attain the supreme destination."*

**94.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Patala-khanda:*
*"For chandalas, pukkasas, women, and one given to killing living beings, through the offering of pinda in Mathura there is no rebirth."*[^21]

**95.** *"In a drain, on bricks, at a cremation ground, in the open air, on a platform, or in a lofty building — those who die in Mathura, O Devi, attain liberation."*

**96.** *Thus [it is said] in the Saura Purana:*
*"Here there is [a city] named Mathura, famed in the three worlds, whose streets are purified by sand mixed with the dust of Krishna's feet."*

**97.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*"By the touch of her dust one is freed from the bondage of birth."*[^22]

**98.** *Thus [it is said] in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"'I shall live in Mathura, I shall go to Mathura' — one who has such a thought is also freed from bondage."*

**99.** This Mathura-mandala grants Vishnu-loka;
by every teaching, it destroys the misfortunes of living beings.

**100.** *Thus [it is said] in the Brahmanda Purana:*
*"Those who see the Lord Achyuta, the son of Devaki, in Mathura, having reached Vishnu-loka, never fall."*

**101.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*"One who with faith and a collected mind makes a pilgrimage to Krishna, freed from all sins, goes to Vishnu-loka."*

**102.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Patala-khanda:*
*"Women, mlecchas and shudras, cattle, birds and beasts — those who die in Mathura attain the supreme destination."*

**103.** *"Those bitten by snakes, killed by beasts, perished by fire or water, and those who met an untimely end — in Mathura they go to the abode of Hari."*

**104.** The scriptures say: Sri Mathura fulfils all desires;
whatever anyone desires, that he obtains.

**105.** *Thus [it is said] in the Brahmanda Purana:*
*"Truly, truly, O best of sages, I say under oath: nowhere is there anything that fulfils all desires equal to Mathura."*

**106.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, Mathura-khanda:*
*"Where Mahadeva, the guardian of the holy land, always abides, where there is the Vishranti-tirtha — what fruit is hard to obtain there?*[^23]

**107.** *She grants the three [aims of life] to those who have desires, liberation to those who seek liberation, devotion to one who desires devotion; let the wise take refuge in Mathura."*

**108.** Sri Mathura-mandala is beyond the material world;
who can describe the virtues of Mathura?

**109.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"She is some wholly other creation, distinct from [the creation of] the Creator, for even the Lord is not able to describe the virtues of this holy land."*[^24]

**110.** *Thus [it is said] in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"This Mathura-mandala rests upon the disc of Vishnu; it is lotus-shaped and abides there eternally, O king."*

**111.** Sri Mathura, delight of the heart, is the form of the three gods;
the meaning of the word "Mathura" is known from the Puranas.

**112.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Patala-khanda:*
*"From the syllable 'ma', the syllable 'thu' and the syllable 'ra' standing at the end the word 'Mathura' is formed; therefore it is equal to [the syllable] Om.*

**113.** *'Ma' is Maharudra, 'thu' is called Vishnu, 'ra' standing at the end is Brahma; thus 'Mathura' is a threefold word.*

**114.** *Therefore it is truly the most excellent holy land: Mathura always abides as a form consisting of the three gods."*

**115.** In Mathura devotion to Sri Vishnu is attained —
many Puranas tell of this in various ways.

**116.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Patala-khanda:*
*"In other holy places the great fruit is liberation alone; but devotion to Hari, which even the liberated pray for, is attained in Mathura.*

**117.** *O sage, to people who live there even three nights Hari grants a happiness rare even for the liberated."*

**118.** *Thus [it is said] in the Brahmanda Purana:*
*"The perfection full of supreme bliss, which is hard to obtain by serving the tirthas of the three worlds, [is granted] by merely touching Mathura."*

**119.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, Mathura-khanda:*
*"Those who remember Mathura and the Lord of Mathura, O lord of the people, obtain the fruit of all tirthas and gain devotion to the Supreme Hari."*

**120.** Mathura, when remembered, bestows the highest fruit;
who would not take refuge in such a Mathura?

**121.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Patala-khanda:*
*"Oh, blessed is Madhupuri, greater even than Vaikuntha: from a single day's residence [there] devotion to Hari is born."*

**122.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"If one desires the highest perfection and release from samsara, let him always glorify Mathura in deed and in mind."*

**123.** Sri Krishna's Mathura-mandala is the highest of all;
its boundary of twenty yojanas is supremely beautiful.

**124.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"My Mathura-mandala is twenty yojanas."*[^25]

**125.** The boundary of Mathura-mandala, according to the scriptures,
runs from Yayavara to Shaukari and Vateshvara.[^26]

**126.** The place Yayavara [is named] after the brahmana Yayavara;
Shaukari is named after Adi-Shukara.[^27]

**127.** Vateshvara is Shiva, worshipped by all;
the kingdom of Sri Shurasena is known everywhere.[^28]

**128.** Now people call [them] "Varahadashana-hrada";
Yayavara and Shaukari are renowned in the Puranas.[^29]

**129.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, Yamuna-mahatmya:*
*"The lovely place of the apsaras, where once the brahmana Yayavara, an ascetic who had conquered his senses, fell into agitation;*

**130.** *having touched with a drop of water this [Yayavara], who had long practised severe austerities, tormented by the fire of Indra's curse, and then freed him from sin…" and so on.*[^30]

**131.** *Thus [it is said] in the same [work]:*
*"Then he, turning to the east, reached the city of Shaukari, where Adi-Shukara appeared in order to lift up the Earth."*

**132.** Just as Yayavara and Shaukari are proclaimed as boundaries,
so in every direction [the mandala] extends twenty yojanas.

**133.** Within these twenty yojanas there are many tirthas;
among them the Puranas speak of a special [region].

**134.** Mathura-mandala extends over twelve yojanas;
there are many tirthas there — places of the play of Rama and Krishna.

**135.** *Thus [it is said] in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"This Mathura-mandala is twelve yojanas; there are thousands of tirthas there and [places of] the deeds of Krishna and Rama."*

**136.** And within it there is a distinction: the most excellent Mathura,
enchanting, extending over twenty-four kroshas,

**137.** joined with the twelve forests beginning with Kumudavana;
she is known everywhere as the bestower of all perfections.[^31]

**138.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Extending over twelve gavyutis, joined with twelve forests — there too [abides] the goddess Mathura, bestower of all perfections."*[^32]

**139.** And within it a distinction: Sri Mathura is lotus-shaped;
in its whorl abides Keshava-deva, the destroyer of afflictions.[^33]

**140.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"This lotus, O fortunate one, grants liberation to all; in its whorl, O Devi, abides Keshava, the destroyer of afflictions.*

**141.** *Those people who die in the whorl partake of liberation; and those who die among the petals — for them too there is liberation, O Vasundhara."*

**142.** On the western petal is enchanting Harideva,
who dwells at Govardhana, the giver of supreme bliss.[^34]

**143.** *Thus [it is said] in the same [work]:*
*"And in the west — having seen the Lord Hari who dwells at Govardhana, the Lord of the god of gods, why, O mind, do you still grieve?"*

**144.** On the northern is Sri Govinda, full of supreme bliss,
by the sight of Whom one is freed from all sins.[^35]

**145.** *Thus [it is said] in the same [work]:*
*"Having seen in the north Govinda, the supreme and auspicious Lord, a person does not fall into samsara until the universal deluge."*

**146.** On the eastern petal abides the Lord called Vishranti;
by the sight of Him a man attains liberation.[^36]

**147.** *Thus [it is said] in the same [work]:*
*"The Lord called Vishranti, abiding on the eastern petal — having seen Him, a man attains liberation; of this there is no doubt."*

**148.** Sri Varaha-deva shines on the southern petal;[^37]
by His mercy people attain every perfection.

**149.** *Thus [it is said] in the same work:*
*"In the south know Me to be the Deity of divine form,*
*of mighty body, equal to Keshava.*
*Seeing Me, O goddess, a person is honored in the world of Brahma."*[^38]

**150.** What fruit living and the like in Mathura bring at particular times —
this is revealed in the Puranas.

**151.** One who in the month of Jyeshtha, on the twelfth day of the bright fortnight, bathes at Mathura
and sees Sri Hari attains the supreme goal.

**152.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"One who on the twelfth day of the bright fortnight of Jyeshtha, with senses controlled, bathes*
*and sees Hari in Mathura attains the supreme goal."*

**153.** In Chaturmasya the fruit [of staying] in Mathura is immense:[^39]
all the tirthas of the earth reside in Mathura.

**154.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"All the tirthas that are on the earth, and all the waters up to the ocean,*
*go to Mathura while I sleep, O Vasundhara."*

**155.** Likewise what times such as Janmashtami in the month of Bhadra [bring] —
what is there to say? It is clearly set out in the Puranas and other shastras.

**156.** The city of Mathura lies within Madhuvana;[^40]
in describing its glory no one can reach the end.

**157.** Here Bhagavan killed the demon Madhu;
therefore the names "Madhuvana" and "Mathura."

**158.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"First of all is the forest of Madhu, where the city of Mathura stands*
*and where Hari, whose form is the universe, killed the demon Madhu."*

**159.** The tirthas that are here are beyond counting;
who has the power to name all those tirthas?

**160.** *Thus [it is said] in the same work:*
*"What is unattainable, O king, in that Madhuvana dear to Hari?*
*To name [all] the tirthas I am not able now."*

**161.** Thus speaking of the great glory of Mathura,
Raghava Pandita cannot stay composed for joy.

**162.** When night gave way to dawn, he took the two with him
and, having performed the morning duties, sets out to tour Mathura.

**163.** First he went to where the Sanodiya brahmana had lived,[^41]
in whose house Sri Krishna Chaitanya had taken His meal.

**164.** He was a disciple of Madhavendra Puri Gosvami
and had seen the supreme secret of Gauranga.

**165.** Sri Raghava Pandita says to Srinivasa:
"Here Gaurachandra danced, overwhelmed by prema.

**166.** Countless people came to see the Lord;
all became wildly ecstatic in the kirtana of the holy name.

**167.** From everyone's eyes tears flowed ceaselessly;
everyone realized that He was the son of the king of Vraja.

**168.** No one could leave Him for even half a moment;
all swim in the boundless ocean of prema.

**169.** Here are the wonderful pastimes of Gaurachandra."
Having said this, Sri Raghava heaves a long sigh.

**170.** Hearing of the lila of Gaurangachand,
Srinivasa and Narottama weep.

**171.** Lamenting, they utterly lost their composure
and in the brahmana's courtyard became gray with dust.

**172.** Moment after moment, how many waves rise in their hearts!
After some time, becoming calm, they look all around.

**173.** Raghava Pandita says to Srinivasa:
"I heard from the mouths of the elders this well-known story.

**174.** During His pilgrimage to the holy places, Advaita Gosani,
seeing the beauty of Mathura, stayed in this very place.

**175.** In Mathura there was a brahmana from another land, the lowest of brahmanas;
he constantly reviled the Vaishnavas — this was his habit.

**176.** Proud of his learning, wicked in every way —
the good people of Mathura trembled in fear of him.

**177.** One day, in the presence of Lord Advaita,
he reviles the Vaishnavas — unbearable to hear.

**178.** Hearing this, Advaita was seized by the utmost anger:
His lips tremble, both His eyes turned blood-red.

**179.** With great fierceness He says again and again:
'You there, unbeliever! There is no deliverance for you!

**180.** Look — here I am, with the chakra in My hand!
I will cut off your head and smash it to pieces!'

**181.** No sooner had He said this than the Lord became four-armed;
seeing this, the lowly brahmana began to tremble with fear.

**182.** Joining his palms, he says again and again:
'Lord, give me whatever punishment is fitting.

**183.** Through bad association my intelligence was ruined;
not knowing the truth about the Vaishnavas, I committed offense.

**184.** The offenses I committed are beyond number.
Lord, deliver such an unbeliever as me!'

**185.** Having said this, the lowly brahmana weeps;
the Lord withdrew His four-armed form.

**186.** Seeing the brahmana's condition, He was moved to compassion
and graciously speaks sweet words:

**187.** 'You have committed an offense for which you would suffer a great hell.
Now carefully do what I tell you.

**188.** Always regarding yourself as an offender,
give up everything and perform the sankirtana of the name.

**189.** Even at the cost of your life, satisfy the Vaishnavas;
always be careful in your dealings with the Vaishnavas.

**190.** Engage yourself in the practices of bhakti;
and the form you have seen, keep it secret.'

**191.** Having said many such things, the Lord went on with His wanderings;
the brahmana became wildly ecstatic in the kirtana of the holy name.

**192.** He goes from house to house of the Vaishnavas in Mathura
and weeps, showing great humility.

**193.** Seeing the brahmana's conduct, all the Vaishnavas,
pleased, wish the brahmana well.

**194.** Some say, 'We are seeing a sudden wonder.'
Others say, 'There is a reason — we will tell it.

**195.** A brahmana pilgrim came to Mathura
and stayed here in secret; His radiance is like the sun.

**196.** We have concluded that He is the Lord in human form;
by His grace the brahmana has become like this.'

**197.** Seeing the brahmana's bhakti, they said many such things.
By seeing this place one obtains the jewel of bhakti."

**198.** O Srinivasa, see how beautiful [this place] is!
The glory of this place, Ardhachandra, is well known.[^42]

**199.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"There, in the middle, is a place shaped like a half-moon;*
*the people who live there attain liberation — of this there is no doubt.*

**200.** *One who, restrained in eating, bathes at Ardhachandra*
*attains the imperishable worlds — of this there is no doubt.*

**201.** *O goddess, those who die at Ardhachandra go to My abode.*
*And those who died elsewhere but performed the rites at Ardhachandra, O goddess,*
*they too will attain liberation, even without cremation and the other [rites].*

**202.** *For as long as the bones of an embodied being remain at Ardhachandra,*
*for so long is he, even if a sinner, honored in the world of Brahma."*

**203.** Having said this, he took Srinivasa Acharya by the hand
and, with joy in his heart, speaks again unhurriedly:

**204.** Mathura within Madhuvana is full of splendor;
at particular times the fruit of pilgrimage [here] is immense.

**205.** By touring Mathura all sins go far away,
and even others are purified by seeing such a person.

**206.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The killer of a brahmana, the drinker of liquor, the killer of a cow, and the breaker of a vow,*
*by circling Mathura, are purified of sin.*

**207.** *When a person who has come from afar, from another land, performs the parikrama [of Mathura],*
*by merely seeing him [people] are freed from impurity and become pure."*

**208.** Look — this is the house of Vasudeva and Devaki:[^43]
here Krishna, the Lord of the universe, was born.

**209.** The Puranas clearly speak of the glory of the birthplace:
at particular times the fruit [of visiting it] has no limit.

**210.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana:*
*"One who in Mathura, devoted to japa and fasting, O Six-faced One,*
*reaches the birthplace is freed from all sins."*[^44]

**211.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana:*
*"People who in the month of Kartika enter the house of Keshava's birth even once*
*attain Krishna, the Supreme, the Imperishable."*

**212.** O Srinivasa, take darshan of Keshava!
Here Sri Chaitanya performed a wonderful dance.[^45]

**213.** All the people floated in a flood of prema;
everyone said, "He is this very Sri Keshava-raya!"

**214.** Who is able to describe the glory of Keshava?
Circling Him equals circling the seven continents.

**215.** By glorifying Keshava all sins are destroyed;
and the fruit [of this] at particular times has no end.

**216.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"He has circled the whole earth with its seven continents*
*who has circled Keshava in Mathura.*

**217.** *The sin committed in this life and that committed in other lives —*
*all of it is swiftly destroyed by glorifying Keshava."*

**218.** Look, look, what a wonder in the city of Mathura:
here the Deities of Sri Bhagavan always shine,

**219.** named Dirgha-vishnu, Padmanabha and Svayambhuva;[^46]
whoever sees Them even once has all his desires fulfilled.

**220.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Having beheld in Mathura even once Dirgha-vishnu, Padmanabha and Svayambhuva,*
*O goddess, a person will obtain all he desires."*

**221.** Look, Srinivasa — the family of Sri Krishna:
the goddess Ekanamsha, Yashoda, Devaki, and also[^47]

**222.** Mahavidyeshvari; by seeing them all,[^48]
one is freed from [the sin of] killing a brahmana — so the Puranas clearly state.

**223.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Having beheld the goddess Ekanamsha, then Yashoda, and also Devaki*
*and Mahavidyeshvari, a person is freed from [the sin of] killing a brahmana."*

**224.** Here is Mahadeva Bhuteshvara, the guardian of [this] holy land:[^49]
merely by being seen he takes away sins; he is supremely merciful.

**225.** Whoever worships him attains devotion to Krishna;
but whoever turns away from him is put to shame.

**226.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"And in Mathura you, O god, will be the kshetrapala;*
*whoever sees you, O Mahadeva, will obtain the fruit of My kshetra."*[^50]

**227.** *"Having seen the god Bhutapati, the giver of boons, the destroyer of sins —*
*by that sight, O Vasudha, one obtains the fruit of Mathura."*[^51]

**228.** *Thus [it is said] in the Nirvana-khanda:*[^52]
*"Where [dwells] the god Bhuteshvara, who grants liberation even to sinners —*
*the supreme god Bhuteshvara is ever most dear to Me.*

**229.** *How could that sinful man attain devotion to Me*
*who does not properly worship My supreme devotee, Shiva?*

**230.** *The lowest of men, whose minds are bewildered by My maya, are for the most part those*
*who do not remember Bhuteshvara, do not bow to him or praise him."*

**231.** Look: here is the great tirtha named Vishranti —
here Krishna, having slain Kamsa, took His rest.[^53]

**232.** O Srinivasa, here the Crest-jewel of sannyasis
performed a wondrous deed — I do not know how to tell it.[^54]

**233.** Women and men, children, the old, the young — as many as there were,
all ran in all directions, maddened.

**234.** Hundreds of thousands of people cry out loudly:
"The Crest-jewel of sannyasis has come to Mathura!"

**235.** Saying this and much more, all float in tears;
raising their arms, everywhere they call out, "Hari!"

**236.** Seeing the beauty of Gaurachandra, which enchants the worlds,
no one can turn away his unblinking eyes.

**237.** The Lord fulfilled the desires of all the people —
such was the wondrous pastime at Vishrama-tirtha.

**238.** The glory of Vishranti-tirtha is known throughout the world;
at Vishranti one attains an abode most difficult to reach.

**239.** It takes away all sins and all the afflictions of samsara;
who could tell how great is the fruit of bathing at Vishranti?

**240.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"There, O great king, is the tirtha Vishranti, renowned in the world;*
*having wandered through all the tirthas, people come to eternal rest (vishranti)."*[^55]

**241.** *Thus [it is said] in the Saura Purana:*
*"Next is the tirtha called Vishranti-tirtha, the destroyer of sins,*
*which gives people rest from the hardships of wandering in the desert of samsara.*

**242.** *The man who, having bathed in that tirtha, worships Achyuta*
*is freed from the burning of worldly existence and becomes fit for immortality."*

**243.** *In the Padma Purana, in the Yamuna-mahatmya:*
*"Where [the Yamuna] breaks forth from Mount Kalinda, likewise in the city of Mathura,*
*at the westward-facing Shaukari, and at the confluence with the Bhagirathi —*[^56]

**244.** *the fruit declared for the northern bank is a hundred times greater in the Kalindi,*
*and at Vishranti, the wise say, it is multiplied ten million times."*[^57]

**245.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The tirtha named Vishranti is renowned in the three worlds;*
*the man who bathes in it, O goddess, is honored in My world."*

**246.** Look: here in a lovely place is the Lord Gatashrama;[^58]
the sight of Him brings the fruit of all the tirthas.

**247.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Bathing in all the tirthas and the fruit of all the tirthas —*
*that fruit, O goddess, he obtains who sees the Lord Gatashrama."*

**248.** O Srinivasa, here, in Ardhachandra, lie
the twenty-four renowned tirthas of Sri Yamuna.[^59]

**249.** This is the tirtha Avimukta: bathing in it brings liberation,
and on giving up one's life, the attainment of Vishnu-loka is certain.

**250.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The man who bathes at Avimukta attains liberation without doubt;*
*and he who gives up his life there goes to My world."*

**251.** Look: here is Guhya-tirtha; whoever bathes here[^60]
is freed from samsara and attains Vishnu-loka.

**252.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"There is also another, secret [tirtha], which frees from all samsara;*
*the man who bathes in it, O goddess, is honored in My world."*

**253.** This [tirtha], hard to reach even for the gods, is called Prayaga-tirtha;
whoever bathes here receives the fruit of the agnishtoma.[^61]

**254.** *Thus [it is said] in the Saura Purana:*
*"The tirtha named Prayaga is hard to reach even for the gods;*
*the man who bathes in it, O goddess, will obtain the fruit of the agnishtoma."*

**255.** This is Kanakhala-tirtha: whoever bathes here
obtains supreme opulence — the Puranas are the proof.

**256.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Likewise Kanakhala-tirtha, My supreme secret tirtha;*
*merely by bathing there too, one rejoices on the vault of heaven."*

**257.** Look: here is the great tirtha named Tinduka;
whoever bathes here attains Vishnu-loka.

**258.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"There is a supreme secret kshetra named Tinduka;*
*the man who bathes there, O goddess, is honored in My world."*

**259.** This Surya-tirtha destroys all sins;
here Bali, the son of Virochana, performed austerities.[^62]

**260.** Whoever bathes at an eclipse of the moon or the sun, at a sankranti, or on a Sunday
obtains the fruit of the rajasuya.[^63]

**261.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Next is Surya-tirtha, which releases from all sins;*
*here, long ago, Bali, the son of Virochana, worshiped Surya.*

**262.** *The man who bathes in it on a Sunday, at a sankranti,*
*or at an eclipse of the moon and the sun, O goddess, will obtain the fruit of the rajasuya."*

**263.** Look: here is Vatasvami-tirtha, the best of tirthas;
here is Surya Vatasvami, renowned in the world.[^64]

**264.** Serving this tirtha with devotion destroys disease;
opulence is gained, and at the end the highest destination.

**265.** *Thus [it is said] in the Saura Purana:*
*"Next is the best of tirthas, called Vatasvami-tirtha,*
*where Divakara, the Sun god, is renowned as Vatasvami.*

**266.** *Whoever serves that tirtha with devotion on a Sunday*
*obtains health and opulence, and at the end the highest destination."*

**267.** This is Dhruva-tirtha, the place of Dhruva's austerities;
whoever bathes here will certainly attain Dhruva-loka.[^65]

**268.** This is the chief of tirthas: a shraddha here delivers the ancestors;[^66]
whoever performs japa and the like here receives the fruit of all the tirthas.

**269.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Where Dhruva, moved by desire, performed supreme austerity —*
*there, merely by bathing, [a man] is honored in Dhruva-loka.*

**270.** *And whoever performs a shraddha at Dhruva-tirtha, O Vasudha,*
*delivers all his ancestors, especially [if he performs it] in the pitri-paksha."*[^67]

**271.** *Thus [it is said] in the Saura Purana:*
*"Next is the chief of tirthas, known as Dhruva-tirtha,*
*where for one devoted to bathing liberation is certain; of this there is no doubt."*

**272.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"The fruit that people obtain by offering pinda at Gaya —*
*a hundred times greater than that is [the fruit] of offering pinda at the tirtha of Dhruva.*[^68]

**273.** *Japa, fire sacrifice, austerity, charity and worship at Dhruva-tirtha*
*bring people a fruit a hundred times greater than at all [other] tirthas."*

**274.** Look: here is Rishi-tirtha, south of Dhruva-tirtha;
bathing in this tirtha leads to Vishnu-loka.

**275.** The Puranas say that Rishi-tirtha is dear to Krishna;
whoever bathes here attains devotion to Krishna.

**276.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"South of Dhruva-tirtha is the renowned Rishi-tirtha;*
*the man who bathes there, O goddess, is honored in My world."*

**277.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"There, in Madhuvana, is the holy Rishi-tirtha, dear to Hari;*[^69]
*merely by bathing in it, O king, one attains supreme devotion to Hari."*

**278.** This is Moksha-tirtha, south of Rishi-tirtha;
here merely immersing oneself brings liberation.

**279.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"South of Rishi-tirtha, O Vasundhara, is Moksha-tirtha;*
*merely by bathing there too, a man attains liberation."*

**280.** This is Koti-tirtha, hard for the gods to reach; whoever here
bathes and gives charity attains Vishnu-loka.

**281.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"In the same place is Koti-tirtha, hard to reach even for the gods;*
*by bathing and giving charity there [a man] is honored in My world."*

**282.** This is Bodhi-tirtha: by offering pinda here
one attains Pitri-loka — so the Puranas say.

**283.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"In the same place is the tirtha called Bodhi-tirtha, hard to reach even for the gods;*
*having offered pinda [there], O Vasudha, a man goes to Pitri-loka."*

**284.** These twelve auspicious tirthas lie south of Vishrama;
by remembering them all, one is freed from all sins.

**285.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"These twelve tirthas are hard for the gods to reach;*
*merely by remembering them [a man] is freed from all sins."*

**286.** Look: here is Nava-tirtha, north of Asikunda;
there is no such tirtha on earth, nor will there be.[^70]

**287.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"And north of Asikunda is the tirtha named Nava;*
*a tirtha higher than Nava-tirtha has never been, nor will be."*

**288.** This tirtha, known in the three worlds, is Samyamana;
the fruit of bathing here is going to Vishnu-loka.

**289.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Next is the tirtha named Samyamana, renowned in the three worlds;*
*the man who bathes there, O goddess, goes to My world."*

**290.** Bathing at this Dharapatana-tirtha takes away grief;
[the bather] gains great opulence, and on giving up his life, Vishnu-loka.

**291.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Having bathed at Dharapatanaka, a man rejoices on the vault of heaven;*
*and whoever gives up his life here goes to My world."*

**292.** This Naga-tirtha the scriptures call the best of tirthas;
bathing [in it] brings heaven, and one who dies [here] is not born again.

**293.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Next is Naga-tirtha, highest of the highest among tirthas;*
*having bathed in it, people go to heaven, and those who die [here] are not born again."*

**294.** The foremost [tirtha], Ghantabharana, destroys all sins;
whoever bathes here is honored in Surya-loka.

**295.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The tirtha Ghantabharanaka releases from all sins;*
*the man who bathes in it, O goddess, is honored in Surya-loka."*

**296.** This Brahma-tirtha is known as the best of tirthas;
bathing and the rest [here] surely lead to Vishnu-loka.

**297.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi Purana:*
*"The best of tirthas is Brahma[-tirtha], greatly renowned in the world;*[^71]
*whoever, having bathed there and drunk [its water], self-controlled and steady in his seat,*
*receives the permission of Brahma, goes to Vishnu-loka."*

**298.** O Srinivasa, here is the site of Soma-tirtha;
look — the waters of the Yamuna flow pure.

**299.** Whoever bathes here attains all perfections
and is happy in Soma-loka — of this there is no doubt.[^72]

**300.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"At Soma-tirtha, O Vasudha, in the pure waters of the Yamuna,*
*let [a man] established in his own duties perform his bath;*
*he rejoices in Soma-loka — so it is, of this there is no doubt."*

**301.** Whoever bathes at the tirtha Sarasvati-patana,
though outside the varnas, becomes a yati, and his sins go far away.[^73]

**302.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"And Sarasvati-patana, auspicious, removing all sins;*[^74]
*having bathed there, O goddess, a man, even if outside the varnas, becomes a yati."*

**303.** Look, Srinivasa: here is the renowned Chakra-tirtha;[^75]
here people bathe, fasting for three nights.

**304.** Merely by bathing [here] a man's [sin of] killing a brahmana goes away;
what more to say — he obtains the supreme fruit, hard to attain.

**305.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Chakra-tirtha is renowned in My Mathura-mandala;*
*the man who, having fasted for three nights, bathes there,*
*merely by bathing is freed from [the sin of] killing a brahmana."*

**306.** Look at Dashashvamedha-tirtha: in ancient times the rishis[^76]
constantly worshiped the Lord here, floating in happiness.

**307.** Those who regularly bathe at such a tirtha —
for them the heavenly abode is not hard to attain.

**308.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Dashashvamedha was in ancient times constantly worshiped by the rishis;*
*for those who, self-controlled, bathe there, heaven is not hard to attain."*

**309.** This Vighnaraja-tirtha destroys the stain of sin;[^77]
whoever bathes here, Vighnaraja does not trouble.

**310.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The tirtha of Vighnaraja is holy, removes sin, and is auspicious;*
*a man who has bathed right there Vighnaraja will not trouble."*

**311.** Here, look: Koti-tirtha, the supreme good;[^78]
merely by bathing here one obtains the fruit of ten million Gangas.

**312.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Next is Koti-tirtha, pure, supreme, auspicious;*
*merely by bathing right there one obtains the fruit of ten million Gangas."*

**313.** Not counting Vishranti, to the north and south of it —
twelve and twelve: twenty-four are known.[^79]

**314.** *Thus [it is said] in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"Twenty-four tirthas — to the south and north of that tirtha,*
*up to Dashashvamedha and up to Moksha, O Yudhishthira."*

**315.** O Srinivasa, at the twenty-four ghats
Mahaprabhu bathed with a heart full of great bliss.[^80]

**316.** How at each ghat He was overcome by the rapture of prema —
even one [of these] only Shesha knows how to describe.[^81]

**317.** Hundreds of thousands of people bathed together with the Lord;
all those people floated on the waves of prema.

**318.** All the gods came and mingled with men;
all cried: "Glory, glory to Sri Krishna Chaitanya!"

**319.** Such was the wondrous pastime in Mathura.
Look, Srinivasa, at the other tirthas in Mathura too.

**320.** This is the tirtha of Vishvanatha, named Gokarna;[^82]
it is dear to Vishnu, known in the world, incomparable.

**321.** *Thus [it is said] in the Saura Purana:*
*"Next there is the tirtha of Vishvanatha called Gokarna-tirtha,*
*renowned in the world, exceedingly dear to Vishnu."*

**322.** Whoever bathes every day in this Krishna-ganga[^83]
obtains a fruit ten times greater than from five tirthas.

**323.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Whatever fruit a man obtains by bathing in five tirthas —*
*Krishna-ganga is seen [to give] ten times as much, day after day."*

**324.** By bathing at Vaikuntha-tirtha one obtains a great fruit:
freed from all sins, [a man] goes to Vishnu-loka.

**325.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Whoever bathes at Vaikuntha-tirtha is freed from all sins;*
*completely freed from all sins, he goes to Vishnu-loka."*

**326.** Look, Srinivasa: here is Asikunda-tirtha;
the great fruit of bathing here is declared in the Puranas.

**327.** Sri Varaha, Narayani, Langali, Vamana —
whoever, having bathed in the kunda, sees these four,[^84]

**328.** of all the tirthas there are up to the ocean [and] in Mathura —
the fruit of circumambulating them all comes to him.

**329.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"One is called Varaha, then another is Narayani,*
*the third is Vamana, the fourth the auspicious Langali.*

**330.** *Whoever sees these four, having bathed in the kunda called Asi,*
*by him the earth up to the four oceans has certainly been traversed,*
*and he enjoys the fruit of all the tirthas of Mathura."*

**331.** This is the well named Chatuh-samudrika;[^85]
whoever bathes here enjoys himself in the world of the gods.

**332.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The well named Chatuh-samudrika is renowned in the worlds;*
*the man who has bathed there, O auspicious one, rejoices together with the gods."*

**333.** O Srinivasa, such is the glory of the Yamuna —
who can tell how much of it? There is no limit to the telling.

**334.** In Mathura-mandala she is a hundred times [greater] than the Ganga;
whoever bathes in the Yamuna is honored in Vishnu-loka.

**335.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"In My Mathura-mandala the renowned Yamuna, O goddess,*
*is declared a hundred times [greater] than the Ganga; here there is nothing to deliberate.*

**336.** *There will be My secret tirthas, O sinless one;*
*the man who bathes in them, O goddess, is honored in My world."*

**337.** By bathing in the waters of the Yamuna, by drinking [them], by glorifying her,
one gains merit, and by seeing her — the supreme good.

**338.** By bathing and drinking, seven generations of the family are purified;
for one who gives up his life [here], the highest destination: this is certain.

**339.** *Thus [it is said] in the Matsya Purana, in the dialogue of Yudhishthira and Narada:*
*"Having bathed there, in the Yamuna, and drunk [her water], O Yudhishthira,*
*[a man] gains merit by glorifying her, and, having seen [her], sees what is auspicious.*

**340.** *Having immersed himself [in her] and drunk [her water], he purifies [his] family to the seventh generation;*
*whoever gives up his life there goes to the highest destination."*

**341.** Whoever performs a shraddha here, his fruit is imperishable;
it is proclaimed that the Yamuna is herself sat-chit-ananda and the rest.

**342.** *Thus [it is said] in the Vishnudharmottara:*
*"Having performed a shraddha there, in the flowing Kalindi, O ruler of men,*
*[a man] obtains an imperishable fruit and rejoices on the vault of heaven."*

**343.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, in the Patala-khanda, in the Marichi-sarga:*
*"That rasa which is the supreme foundation, marked by eternity, knowledge and bliss,*
*sung in the Upanishads as Brahman — that is the Yamuna herself."*

**344.** The fruits of bathing and so on in the Yamuna at particular times
all the Puranas describe in every detail.

**345.** O Srinivasa, by the mercy of this Kalindi
one obtains the desired fruit — this is known to the world.

**346.** As iron becomes gold by the touch of the philosopher's stone,
so sin becomes merit by the touch of the water of the Krishna.[^86]

**347.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana:*
*"As by the touch of the philosopher's stone iron becomes gold,*
*so by the touch of the water of the Krishna sin becomes merit."*

**348.** Such is the boundless glory of the brahmanas of Mathura:
the Lord with His own mouth speaks of it in various ways.[^87]

**349.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"[There is] a Mathura who knows no hymns, and another who knows the four Vedas;*[^88]
*passing over the knower of the four Vedas, one should feed the twice-born Mathura.*

**350.** *A ploughman, of bad conduct, turned away from the path of dharma —*
*even such a Mathura is worthy of worship: he bears My form.*

**351.** *The form of the Mathuras is My form, O Vasundhara;*
*when one [such] brahmana is fed, ten million are fed.*

**352.** *The Mathuras are indeed worshipable to Me, the Mathuras are dear to Me;*
*when a Mathura is pleased, I too am pleased; of this there is no doubt.*

**353.** *Where the Mathuras dwell, [those places] become holy tirthas,*
*the dwellings become holy, and everything auspicious."*

**354.** O Srinivasa, all the residents of Sri Mathura —
their glory is highly esteemed in the Vedas and Puranas.

**355.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Other people who live in Mathura, O greatly fortunate one,*
*they too attain the supreme perfection by My grace; of this there is no doubt.*

**356.** *The residents of Mathura — all of them are worthy of liberation,*
*even if they are worms and insects or born from the wombs of animals.*

**357.** *Those attached to others' wives, men whose senses are unconquered —*
*all the residents of Mathura are gods in human form."*

**358.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, in the Nirvana-khanda:*
*"But those wretched people who see faults in the residents of Mathura*
*do not see their own fault, which brings thousands of births and deaths."*

**359.** O Srinivasa, look at the city of Mathura —
the charming places of Krishna's countless pastimes.

**360.** Here is the house of Sudama the garland-maker, dear to Krishna;[^89]
what more to say — his story is known everywhere.

**361.** Here Krishna, having slain Kamsa's washerman,
playfully put on wonderful garments together with His companions.

**362.** By this road Krishna went to Kamsa;
seeing [His] beauty, the city of Mathura was enchanted.

**363.** Here Krishna, having broken the bow, in great delight
walks with a wondrous gait together with His friends.

**364.** Here Kuvalayapida blocked the road;
Krishna, having slain him, playfully took [his] tusks.

**365.** This is the arena — here He fought the wrestlers;
this is the dais — here Kamsa sat.

**366.** Here Nanda and the other cowherds sat in joy;
Krishna fought the wrestlers — they watched with delight.

**367.** Krishna, in play, takes Kamsa's life.
This is Kamsa-khali — here is Kamsa's end.[^90]

**368.** Here stood the house of Sri Kubja;
here, look, is Kubja's well — everyone knows it.[^91]

**369.** Krishna's wondrous pastime with Kubja
became known in the three worlds.

**370.** This is Baladeva-kunda, this is Krishna-kupa;
here Rama and Krishna sport with Their companions.

**371.** O Srinivasa, Narottama! What bliss there was here —
who could tell of it?

**372.** Sri Krishna Chaitanyachandra, having wandered through Mathura,
sat down, surrounded by countless people.

**373.** Whatever rapture of feeling Mahaprabhu was in —
seeing it, the people were amazed.

**374.** The brahmanas of Mathura said to one another:
"This sannyasi in disguise is Krishna, for certain."

**375.** How utterly unearthly — who can understand such a play?
He concealed Himself, taking the form of Gaura.

**376.** Someone said, "Our fortune is boundless:
we have seen the Lord's arrival in Mathura!"

**377.** So spoke many people with joy in their hearts;
seeing Gaura's sweetness, they floated in supreme bliss.

**378.** As he told of all this, Sri Raghava Pandita
lost his composure, thinking of Chaitanya's deeds.

**379.** Srinivasa and Narottama could not restrain themselves;
crying, "Ha, ha, Prabhu!" they fell to the ground and wept.

**380.** Clasping the feet of Sri Raghava Pandita,
the two said so much — hearing it, the heart breaks.

**381.** Sri Pandita, calming himself, calmed the two
and showed them the other tirthas of Mathura.

**382.** He speaks to Srinivasa in sweet words:
here Gopala stayed for a whole month.[^92]

**383.** Sri Rupa Gosvami, taking his dear companions with him,
was overwhelmed on beholding Sri Gopala.

**384.** The residents of Mathura, having obtained the Gosvamis,
were immersed in bliss and did not know day from night.

**385.** Look, Srinivasa: here is an ancient tree;
here, long ago, the son of Rohini was absorbed in play.

**386.** That same Lord Nityananda, wandering among the holy places,
came to Mathura and stayed here.[^93]

**387.** Seeing the land of His former birth, His heart rejoicing,
He, unrecognized, wandered everywhere in that rapture.

**388.** Seeing Avadhutachandra, the people of Mathura
attained great bliss and forgot sorrow and grief.

**389.** By the sight of this place all afflictions go far away,
and devotion to the feet of Nityananda grows abundantly.

**390.** Whoever with faith hears of the wandering through Mathura
easily has his desires fulfilled.

**391.** Raghava Pandita, with great joy in his heart,
gently says something to Srinivasa.

**392.** Sri Mathura-puri, endowed with twelve forests,
is holy, removes sins, is auspicious and of unprecedented sweetness.

**393.** *"And he saw that lovely [city], like the city of Vasava,*
*endowed with twelve forests, holy, removing sins, auspicious."*[^94]

**394.** The twelve forests are attested in all the Puranas;
on hearing their names the heart is soothed.

**395.** Madhu, Tala, Kumuda, Bahula, Kamya, and also
Khadira and Sri Vrindavana — on this side of the Yamuna.

**396.** Sri Bhadra, Bhandira, Bilva, Loha and Mahavana —
these charming forests are on the far bank of the Yamuna.

**397.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana:*
*"Bhadra, Shri, Loha, Bhandira, Maha, Tala, Khadira,*
*Bahula, Kumuda, Kamya, Madhu, and also Vrindavana —*[^95]

**398.** *these are the twelve forests: seven west of the Kalindi;*
*in the east five forests are named; there is the secret, most excellent [forest]."*

**399.** *In the Skanda Purana:*
*"Mahavana, called Gokula, Madhu, and also Vrindavana;*[^96]
*in the east are five, beginning with Bhadra, and seven, beginning with Tala, in the west;*
*and the other [places], where Krishna relishes the rasa of play, are called upavanas."*[^97]

**400.** O Srinivasa, look: here is Madhuvana;
merely by seeing it all desires are fulfilled.

**401.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Lovely is [the forest] named Madhuvana, the unsurpassed place of Vishnu;*
*having seen it, O goddess, a man obtains all he desires.*

**402.** *There is a pond of clear water, adorned with blue lotuses;*
*by bathing there and giving charity one obtains the desired fruit."*

**403.** In Talavana the Lord slew the asura who guarded the palms,
playfully — to the joy of every heart.[^98]

**404.** *In the Skanda Purana, in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"O holy Talavana, where the asura was slain by means of the palms —*
*for the welfare of the Yadavas and for His own sport!"*

**405.** Look: here is Kumudavana, the supreme wonder;
merely by coming here one becomes honored in Vishnu-loka.

**406.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"And the forest Kumudvana is the third, most excellent forest;*
*having gone there, O goddess, a man is honored in My world."*

**407.** O Srinivasa, look: west of Mathura,
in this grove, Krishna slew Dantavakra.[^99]

**408.** Vajranabha named it Datiha;
as the "Dati upavana" it is celebrated in the Padma Purana.[^100]

**409.** In connection with Dantavakra I will tell one story,
by hearing which the pain of the heart departs.

**410.** From Vraja, with their people, Nanda and all the others
went to Kurukshetra for Krishna's sake, on the pretext of a pilgrimage.[^101]

**411.** Everyone met with Krishna;
the son of the king of Vraja did what was fitting for each.

**412.** Having comforted everyone in many ways, Krishna
said, "Soon I will come to Vraja and meet [you]."

**413.** Drinking the nectar of Krishna's words, their hearts glad,
all took their leave and went away from there.

**414.** For Krishna's sake they stayed on the bank of the Yamuna;
all their thoughts were [that] they would take Krishna home.

**415.** Having bidden farewell to everyone at Kurukshetra, Krishna
grew distressed and could not hold His heart.

**416.** Going to Dvaraka and quickly slaying Shishupala,
He came to Mathura on the pretext of slaying Dantavakra.

**417.** Having slain Dantavakra, He crossed the Yamuna
and hurried to where Nanda and the others were.[^102]

**418.** Seeing Krishna, the cowherds run, beside themselves with joy;
crying, "Ayore, ayore!" they raise a clamor.[^103]

**419.** Krishna met with everyone and, taking them all with Him,
crossed the Yamuna and came to His own home.

**420.** Supreme bliss arose in Vraja, in every house;
as before, Sri Krishna sports with everyone.

**421.** Where the cowherds met [Him], crying, "Ayore!" —
there arose a village named Ayore.

**422.** The place where Nanda and all the others settled —
who does not know that the village is called Gauravai?

**423.** Hear the story of how that name came about:
there is a large village there named Dhana.

**424.** The distinguished zamindar of that village of Dhana
was on very friendly terms with Sri Nanda Raya.

**425.** Hearing that Nanda was coming from Kurukshetra,
in great delight he went out to meet [them] and brought [them].

**426.** He settled [them] — and there was no limit to the honor;
for this reason the village came to be named Gauravai.[^104]

**427.** Now that village is called Gaurai;
it lies near Dhana, Ayore and other villages.

**428.** The story of this village is told elsewhere as well;
and [there are] other villages whose names I have not mentioned.

**429.** *Thus [it is said] in the verses of the Sri Gopala-champu:*
*"Having somehow set out from the land of the Kurus toward [the country of] Mathura, so as to return again to their own home,*
*the cowherds led by the king of Vraja, their hearts then turned away [from home], crossed the daughter of the Sun*
*and settled [their] encampment at a distance, in the place known as 'Gaurai.'*[^105]

**430.** *By the name 'Gokulapati,' [by the name] 'Gaurava,' and also 'Gaurai' —*
*[names] born of Sanskrit, of Prakrit and of [the speech of] the village — this place is called.*

**431.** *The place of the Lord of Gokula is known by the name 'Gokulapati,'*
*just as the abode of Purushottama is renowned as 'Purushottama.'"*[^106]

**432.** All those villages are places of Krishna's pastimes;
the fortunate one beholds them with joy in his heart.

**433.** Having told of so much, the Pandita, joyful at heart,
goes along the parikrama path to wander through the holy forests.

**434.** As it is laid down in the Adi-varaha,
so it will not be [at once]; [but] gradually it will become so.[^107]

**435.** As Raghava Pandita went along the road,
he wished to show the forest of Shashthikara.[^108]

**436.** Leaving the parikrama path and taking another road,
he enters Shashthikara and says to Srinivasa:

**437.** Formerly its name was the forest of Shashthikara;
now among the people it is known as Shashthighara.

**438.** Look, Srinivasa: here is Shakatarohana;
this place, dear to Krishna, is most beautiful.[^109]

**439.** Bees always hum in the flowering grove;
bathing in this pond brings supreme bliss.

**440.** Whoever spends one night here fasting
enjoys happily in the world of the Vidyadharas.[^110]

**441.** At particular times the fruits are manifold;
now people call this [place] the village of Shakata.

**442.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The place named Shakatarohana, supreme for Me in that holy land,*
*is in the western part of Mathura, not far off, at half a yojana.*

**443.** *Many thousands of bees dwell there;*
*let a man who has fasted for one night bathe there;*
*he, going to the world of the Vidyadharas, enjoys happiness [there]."*

**444.** Look, Srinivasa: here is Garuda-Govinda;
here Krishna performed a wondrous pastime.[^111]

**445.** Shridama, becoming Garuda, plays joyfully,
and four-armed Govinda mounts his shoulders.

**446.** Garuda and Govinda — both are surpassingly beautiful;
for this reason [the place] is called Garuda-Govinda.

**447.** *Thus [it is said] in the Laghu-bhagavatamrita:*
*"As when Shridama had attained the nature of Tarkshya, He too [became] four-armed," and so on.*[^112]

**448.** Having thus shown the two of them many places,
he joyfully came back to the former parikrama path.

**449.** From afar he says: "Look: here is the place of Gandheshvara;
Krishna put on fragrant substances [here], hence this name."[^113]

**450.** "Look: here is the village of Satoha and a kunda, perfectly pure —
the place of austerities of the sage Shantanu."[^114]

**451.** Having said this, he, taking Srinivasa and Narottama with him,
went ahead, showing many charming places.

**452.** Raghava Pandita, filled with joy, says:
"This is Sri Bahulavana — look, Srinivasa!"[^115]

**453.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya, at the time of His wandering through the forests,
maddened in the rapture of prema, came by this path.

**454.** Hundreds of thousands of cows ran with their tails raised
and, surrounding Gaurachandra on all sides, gazed at Him.

**455.** Sri Gaurasundara, touching the cows with His hand,
revealed how He had once herded [them].

**456.** Deer and other animals, peacocks, cuckoos and other birds,
in great rapture, roamed on all sides by hundreds of thousands.

**457.** The trees showered flowers on Gaurachandra;
countless people looked on in supreme bliss.

**458.** Someone said: "O brother, this is what I think:
this is the son of the king of Vraja — a pretended sannyasi.

**459.** Covering His glossy dark form,
taking on a golden form, He goes about deceiving people."

**460.** Thus people said many things, their hearts having lost all composure;
Gaura-raya fulfilled all their desires.

**461.** O Srinivasa, in this Bahulavana
look at the wondrous kunda in which there is a lotus grove.

**462.** And here is the incomparable Sankarshana-kunda,
and here is Mana-sarasi, most charming.[^116]

**463.** Darshana of them and bathing in them bring a great fruit:
[the bather] sees Krishna with Lakshmi — so the Puranas say.

**464.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The fifth [forest], named Bahula, is the best of forests;*
*going there, O goddess, a man goes to the abode of Agni."*

**465.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"Bahula, the wife of Sri Hari, always resides there;*
*in that lotus grove, O king, are the fruits of great merit.*

**466.** *There Vishnu always takes His pleasure together with Lakshmi;*
*there is Sankarshana-kunda, there Manasa-saras, O king.*

**467.** *Whoever bathes there in the month of Madhu, O best of kings,*[^117]
*sees Hari there together with Lakshmi, O lord of the people."*

**468.** There is the village of Mayura-grama: there Krishna
watches the dance of the peacocks together with His beloveds.

**469.** What a wonder — hundreds of thousands of peacocks in a circle:
raising their tails, they gaze at Rai and Kanu.

**470.** Amid the peacocks Rai and Kanu take Their pleasure:
They dance and make [them] dance — what wondrous joy arises!

**471.** On all sides, clapping their hands, the sakhis
behold the wondrous beauty that enchants the world.

**472.** Look: there, a little way off, are Dakshina-grama and other [villages];
in those places Krishna roams in bliss.[^118]

**473.** In Dakshina-grama Krishna joyfully takes His pleasure;
the mood of the dakshina heroine is manifest here in the highest degree.

**474.** Ahead is the village of Vasati — look, Srinivasa:
here King Vrishabhanu lived.

**475.** As far as Shashthikara and Raval, Nanda resided;
the village of Raval is now called Ral.[^119]

**476.** Near Vasati, at Tosha-sthana of Rama and Krishna,
[They] play in great satisfaction with all Their friends.[^120]

**477.** Here, ahead, look: the village named Arita;
here are the incomparable pastimes of Krishnachandra.[^121]

**478.** The asura Arishta came, assuming the form of a bull;
in great sport Sri Hari slew him.[^122]

**479.** Playfully, Krishna wished to touch Sri Radha's body;
laughing, Radhika says: "This is not proper.

**480.** Though he was an asura, he bore the form of a bull;
You killed him and have become very impure.

**481.** If You can bathe in all the tirthas,
only then will this fault be removed — I tell You."

**482.** Laughing, Krishna speaks the sweetest words:
"Right here I shall bathe, bringing all the tirthas."

**483.** Saying this, He struck the ground with His foot —
and the kunda was filled with the waters of all the tirthas.

**484.** The tirthas, each announcing who it was,
appeared in person and offered praises to Krishna.

**485.** Showing [them] to Sri Radhika and the sakhis,
Krishna bathed, addressing the tirthas.

**486.** This was accomplished exactly at midnight;
even today people bathe in the kunda in the same way.[^123]

**487.** Sri Radhika, hearing Krishna's bold words,
quickly dug a kunda together with the sakhis.

**488.** Radhika's wondrous lake came into being;
seeing it, Krishna rejoiced in His heart.

**489.** With the waters of Sri Manasi-ganga, which contains all the tirthas,
[She] was about to fill the kunda with great eagerness.

**490.** Knowing this wish, Krishna gave orders to the tirthas —
and they entered Radhika-kunda from Shyama-kunda.

**491.** The tirthas, offering Radhika many praises,
deem [themselves] fortunate, in unceasing great joy.

**492.** Both kundas were filled with the waters of the tirthas;
the Two with the sakhis joyfully behold [their] beauty.

**493.** The two kundas are surrounded by various trees and creepers;
this is the wondrous place of the Two's play.

**494.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa:*[^124]
*"Surrounded by kadambas, rows of champakas, fresh fine ashokas, multitudes of mango trees,*
*punnagas, bakulas, lavanga and vasantika creepers,*
*charming and supreme — the middle region where the banks of Their two beloved kundas meet,*
*this place, dear to Radha and Madhava for Their play — in that alone I take shelter."*

**495.** Incomparable is Sri Radhika-kunda; on all its sides
are the charming kunjas of the eight sakhis headed by Lalita.[^125]

**496.** On all sides of Shyama-kunda are the kunjas of Subala and others;
the Two take Their pleasure [there] in endless variety.

**497.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (raga Saranga):*
"The best of nagaras, most composed, standing on the bank of Radha-kunda,
gazes at the most auspicious, sweet beauty of the lake.
It is filled to the brim with pure water, in it are lotuses of so many kinds;
incomparable bees, circling about, hum melodiously, enticing the heart.
With the light, light fresh breeze the gentlest waves arise,
many joyful water creatures dart about in all kinds of play.
The rows of ghats inlaid with gems gleam — a wondrous painted spectacle;
the adorned cottage-pavilions shatter the pride of Madana's abode.
The fine mango trees blossom, and also kadamba, bakula and champaka trees;
high [on them] are hung splendid swings made of jewels.
The parrots joyfully sing without pause; hearing them, the birds grow rapturous;
the peacocks dance; "kuhu, kuhu, kuhu" — the cuckoos sweetly call.
Golden platforms shine in the forest, ceaselessly served by the six seasons;
so many flowers of rare beauty bloom, their fragrance incomparable.
[The lake] is surrounded by the kunjas of Lalita and others, fashioned [as] clusters born of rasa;
[this] abode of bliss shatters the weight of composure — says Narahari."

**498.** *Raga Saranga:*
"Radha, doe-eyed and fair, linking arms with the Nagara,
with a delighted heart gazes at the beauty of Ghanashyama's lake.
Its pure water, filled to the brim, robs a wealth of nectar of its pride;
the gentle breeze touches it, its soft waves entice the world.
Hosts of new kunjas blossom, bees drunk with honey hum;
wagtails dance charmingly, delighting people beyond compare.
Cranes and swans sport by hundreds of thousands, and chakravakas roam there;[^126]
delightful is the clamor of krauncha herons, parrots, cuckoos and peacocks.
The incomparable shore of the lake gleams, trees, creepers and bakulas are in flower;
their shade, ringed by the shimmer of the water, spreads a great beauty.
The new cottage-pavilions are adorned with many jeweled platforms;
the well-built ghats, studded with gems, are a delight to the eyes.
On all sides rasa flows in abundance; [the lake] is surrounded by the kunjas of Subala and others;
there, beautifully made, shine dwellings of many kinds.
The six seasons serve [it] abundantly; its wondrous glory the gods
[and] Narahari ceaselessly sing, meditating [on it] in the heart."

**499.** Everyone calls Shyama-kunda by the name Arishta-kunda;
the glory of these two kundas is immense.

**500.** Whoever bathes in these two kundas
obtains the fruit of the Rajasuya and the Ashvamedha.

**501.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"By bathing in Arishta-kunda and Radha-kunda one obtains the fruit*
*of the Rajasuya and the Ashvamedha; about this there should be no doubt."*

**502.** O Srinivasa, the glory of Radha-kunda
is known from the Puranas; there is no limit to telling it.

**503.** *Thus [it is said] in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"At the festival of lamps in Kartika at Radha-kunda, O Yudhishthira,*
*the whole universe is beheld by beings devoted to Vishnu."*[^127]

**504.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, in the Kartika-mahatmya:*
*"On beautiful Govardhana Hill is Radha-kunda, dear to Hari;*
*bathing there in Kartika, on the Bahula-ashtami, a man becomes a devotee dear to Hari,*
*O brahmana — [for this] gives delight to Him who dwells there.*

**505.** *As Radha is dear to Vishnu, so is Her kunda dear [to Him];*
*among all the gopis She alone is supremely beloved of Vishnu.*

**506.** *Having bathed in Her kunda in Kartika, on the eighth [lunar day], one should worship Janardana;*
*as He is pleased on Prabodhini, so is He pleased [also] by this."*[^128]

**507.** Look, Srinivasa: the two kundas, Radha[-kunda] and Shyama[-kunda];
the beauty of the forests around enchants even the best of sages.

**508.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya, wandering through the forests,
came and sat down beneath this tamala.[^129]

**509.** He asked the people of the village of Arishta,
but no one could tell [Him] about the two kundas.

**510.** The brahmana who had come with Him from Mathura —
He asked him as well, but he too could not tell.

**511.** The Lord is omniscient — He beholds the hidden tirthas:
the two kundas had become two rice fields.

**512.** There, in a little water, He bathed with a joyful heart
and praised Sri Kunda in many ways.

**513.** Carefully taking clay, He applied tilaka;
seeing this, the villagers were struck with great wonder.

**514.** Someone says: "This honorable sannyasi —
where has He suddenly come from?"

**515.** Someone says: "O brother, when I look at Him,
I do not know what happens to my heart — I cannot understand."

**516.** Someone says: "This is never a human sannyasi;
I cannot put into words what is in my mind."

**517.** Someone says: "Who would call Him a sannyasi?
In this form, in this dress, this is Krishna.

**518.** Look: the witnesses of this are the various birds;
all of them, drawing near, gaze [at Him].

**519.** The parrots and cuckoos happily call Him 'Krishna';
the peacocks dance with great jubilation in their hearts.

**520.** The birds make various sounds, an elixir for the ears;
look how wondrously the trees are blossoming!

**521.** O brother, upon this pretended sannyasi —
look — the trees together with the creepers shower flowers.

**522.** The deer and does, coming near,
gaze fixedly at His face.

**523.** Raising their tails, the cows come running
and, surrounding Him on all sides, gaze at His face.

**524.** Look: tears of joy are flowing from everyone's eyes;
this shows that they have met after many days."

**525.** "O brother, again and again I praise [our] good fortune:
in such a form, in such a dress, I have seen Krishna."

**526.** "O brother, obeisance to the feet of this Lord!
I think this incarnation [has come] to give knowledge to the people."

**527.** "These rice fields we cultivated under the names 'Kali' and 'Gauri';
by His mercy we have come to know that they are the two kundas."[^130]

**528.** Thus all spoke of many things among themselves,
exceedingly intoxicated by drinking the nectar of the holy sight [of the Lord].

**529.** The absorption in bhava that came over the Lord on seeing the kunda —
even Brahma and the others cannot describe a trace of it.

**530.** O Srinivasa, these two kundas, [once] rice fields,
are now filled to the brim with water.

**531.** How this came about, when the rice fields were gone —
hear that account; I will tell it briefly.

**532.** Suddenly this thought came into Raghunatha's mind:
"It would be good if the two kundas were filled with water."

**533.** "But this betrays some desire for wealth."
Thinking thus, he became almost stunned.

**534.** Again and again he reproached himself:
"Why did such a desire arise in my mind?"

**535.** Admonishing his mind in various ways,
he remained in solitude, exceedingly vigilant.

**536.** What is in a devotee's mind cannot turn out otherwise:
Krishna fulfills the wishes of the devotee's heart.

**537.** A certain rich man went to Badarikashrama
and had darshan of the Lord, offering many coins.[^131]

**538.** Narayana commanded him in a dream:
"Take the coins and go to Arit-grama in Vraja.

**539.** There [lives] Raghunatha Dasa, the foremost of Vaishnavas;
give him the coins in My name.

**540.** If he does not accept these coins,
then remind him of this:

**541.** 'You have thought of [filling] the two kundas with water for bathing and drinking;
do this, then, taking the coins.'"

**542.** Saying this, He dismissed him at once,
and he came to Arit-grama with a joyful heart.

**543.** Coming before Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami,
he fell to the ground, bowed down and presented the coins as an offering.

**544.** He told everything just as the Lord had commanded;
hearing it, Raghunatha remained stunned.

**545.** After a while, praising again and again, he said:
"Quickly clear the mud out of the two kundas."

**546.** Hearing this, the merchant was overjoyed
and at once engaged many people.

**547.** The two kundas were quickly and carefully dug out.
Why Shyama-kunda is crooked — hear attentively.

**548.** On the bank of Shyama-kunda [stood] this old tree;
all decided: "Tomorrow we shall cut it down."

**549.** In a dream King Yudhishthira said to Raghunatha:
"In the form of trees we five dwell here.[^132]

**550.** Tomorrow morning you will go to the Manasa-pavana-ghat
and, seeing the five trees, protect [them]."[^133]

**551.** Having seen the dream, Raghunatha at daybreak
saw on one tree five trees, one after another.

**552.** He forbade everyone to cut the tree;
for this reason Shyama-kunda did not become even (chaurasa).[^134]

**553.** Seeing the two kundas filled with clear water,
Raghunatha was exceedingly delighted.

**554.** Day and night Raghunatha stayed under a tree;
he never had any wish to build a hut.

**555.** One day Sanatana came from Vrindavana
here, to the residence of Sri Gopala Bhatta.

**556.** He went to bathe at the Manasa-pavana-ghat
and saw a tiger drinking water at that very spot.

**557.** Raghunatha was sitting absorbed in meditation;
the tiger went off into the forest, passing close by him.

**558.** After some time Raghunatha looked around in all four directions
and saw Sri Sanatana coming to bathe.

**559.** Falling to the ground, he bowed down to Sanatana;
Sanatana, overcome with affection, embraced him.

**560.** Lovingly he said softly to Raghunatha:
"From now on you will live not under the tree but in a hut."

**561.** Having made this especially clear, the Gosani went to bathe,
and that very day the building of the hut was begun.

**562.** For the good of others, from then on Raghunatha
lived in the hut by the Gosani's order.

**563.** O Srinivasa, all of Raghunatha's deeds —
how many of them can I tell with one mouth?

**564.** A Vrajavasi named Dasa lives here;
Dasa Gosvami had very great affection for him.

**565.** One day he went to the village of Sakhisthali
and, seeing large palasha leaves, picked them and took them [along].[^135]

**566.** To himself he thought of Dasa Gosvami:
"In terrible separation he has given up rice and other food.

**567.** His rule is to drink one leaf-cup (dona) of buttermilk;
in this [larger] one there will be a little more food."

**568.** Thinking thus, he came home and made a dona,
and with buttermilk in it came before Raghunatha.

**569.** Seeing the dona of fresh leaf, the Gosani asked:
"Where did you find such a large leaf today?"

**570.** Dasa said, "I went to Sakhisthali herding the cows;
finding excellent leaves, I brought [them] here."

**571.** Hearing the name "Sakhisthali," he was filled with anger
and flung the dona with the buttermilk far away.

**572.** After some time, becoming calm, he said to Dasa:
