"That is Chandravali's village — do not go there."

**573.** Hearing this, Dasa the Vrajavasi grew calm
and understood: [these are] the acts of a perfected one (siddha) in the body of a practitioner (sadhaka).[^136]

**574.** The bodies of all these [devotees] are eternally perfect (nitya-siddha);
whoever doubts this is a wretch.

**575.** O Srinivasa, one day Raghunatha
ate in his mind prasada of rice cooked in milk.[^137]

**576.** Indigestion arose, and his body became exceedingly heavy;
how his body became heavy, no one could understand.

**577.** Hearing [of it], Sri Vitthalanatha, the son of Sri Vallabha,
came in person, bringing two physicians.[^138]

**578.** Feeling the pulse, the physician said again and again:
"He has eaten rice with milk — from this comes the heaviness of his body."

**579.** Sri Vitthalanatha, astonished, said:
"Rice with milk is never possible for him."

**580.** Raghunatha said, "This is the very truth:
in my mind I ate rice with milk."

**581.** Hearing this, everyone was amazed.
What more can I say of such deeds of Raghunatha?

**582.** O Srinivasa, know this for certain in your heart:
dwelling at Radha-kunda [is attained] by Raghunatha's mercy.

**583.** Sri Kunda, the Sri Govardhana-shila, the gunja necklace —
this service of Sri Raghunatha is widely known.[^139]

**584.** The trees and creepers at the kunda are supremely radiant;
see the meeting place of the two kundas, Radha-kunda and Shyama-kunda.

**585.** This is the "Malyahari" kunda, O Srinivasa;
here [took place] a wondrous pastime under the pretext of a pearl necklace.[^140]

**586.** In the book *Mukta-charita* Sri Raghunatha Dasa,
bestowing his mercy, described all this in detail.

**587.** These are the two kundas "Shivakhora" and "Bhanukhora."
Having said this, Raghava rejoiced at heart.[^141]

**588.** Thus, showing other kundas and various places,
he went with the two of them to Sri Dasa Gosvami.

**589.** Sri Raghava Pandita reported everything;
hearing it, Dasa Gosvami rejoiced at heart.

**590.** Srinivasa and Narottama, with great care,
fell to the ground and bowed at the Gosvami's feet.

**591.** The Gosvami's withered body was exceedingly weak;
even so, rising, he stretched out both arms.[^142]

**592.** Embracing Srinivasa and Narottama,
what [words] he softly spoke to Srinivasa!

**593.** Krishnadasa Kaviraja came there;
what was fitting in bowing to him, that he did.[^143]

**594.** He regards Srinivasa as equal to his own life.
What can be said? Most wondrous is his conduct.

**595.** Dasa Gosvami's dear Dasa the Vrajavasi
also came there quickly and met [them].

**596.** And all the Vaishnavas who were on the bank of the kunda —
Srinivasa and Narottama met with them all.

**597.** All, delighted, gave [them] leave to bathe
and very quickly had food prepared.

**598.** The two quickly went to bathe
and gazed to their eyes' fill at the beauty of the kunda.

**599.** Subala's kunja lies north of Shyama-kunda;
there the Manasa-pavana-ghat shines in beauty.

**600.** Manasa-pavana is very dear to Radhika;
there the five Pandavas abide in the form of trees.[^144]

**601.** At that ghat the two bathed, absorbed in prema;
the happiness of both grew without end or measure.

**602.** Where the hut of Sri Gopala Bhatta Gosvami stands,
there they honored Sri maha-prasada.

**603.** Having spent that day in the highest bliss,
the Pandita set out in the morning, taking the two with him.

**604.** South of Sri Kunda is the village of Mukharai;
going there, the Pandita says to Srinivasa:[^145]

**605.** Mukhara, Radhika's aged maternal grandmother —
this is her dwelling place, as everyone knows.

**606.** Here, to her great amusement, Mukhara, unnoticed,
brings Radha and Krishna together, filled with delight.

**607.** Saying this, he went ahead and says to Srinivasa:
all around Govardhana are many places of pastimes.

**608.** Look: here in this forest is Kusuma-sarovara —
the wonderful play of the two in gathering flowers.[^146]

**609.** And this is Narada-kunda: here Narada,
performing austerities, fulfilled what was in his heart.

**610.** The sage's desire is told at length in the Puranas;
for the fulfillment of his desire [there was] Vrinda's instruction.[^147]

**611.** This is Ratna-simhasana — there are many stories about it:
here Sri Radhika sat on a jeweled throne.

**612.** The cause of the slaying of Shankhachuda arose from here;
how Krishna slew him is known from the *Bhagavata*.[^148]

**613.** Look: here is Paligrama, a wondrous garden —
the dwelling of the leader of a band of gopis named Palika.[^149]

**614.** Look over there: far away, in the village of Yamuna-ata,
there Krishna sports with His friends.[^150]

**615.** This is Indradhvaja-vedi: here Nanda-raya
used to worship Indra — all people sing of it.[^151]

**616.** Look: here Krishna herds the cows;
with the sound of His flute He calls the cows to Him.

**617.** These [kundas] are called Rina-mochana and Papa-mochana:
bathing in these two kundas removes debts and sins.[^152]

**618.** Look: here is the radiant Sankarshana-kunda;
whoever bathes here has his desire fulfilled.[^153]

**619.** This is the village of Parasauli — look, Srinivasa:
in springtime [Krishna] performed the rasa here.[^154]

**620.** Look: here is the incomparable Chandra-sarovara;
here, absorbed in the rasa, Krishnachandra rested.

**621.** Look: Gandharva-kunda is a most charming place;
here the gandharvas, singing Krishna's qualities, were beside themselves.

**622.** All the play there was at Govardhana in the spring rasa —
it is the highest sweetness; who could describe it, and how much?

**623.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Stavavali, in the Govardhanashraya-dashaka:*[^155]
*"That supreme Rasa-sthali where in spring, in the rasa dance, Radha, [beloved] of Madhava,*
*surrounded by beautiful friends worthy of worship by hundreds of Lakshmis,*
*her neck adorned by Krishna's fragrant, radiant, nectar-sweet arm, dances charmingly —*
*it is on him; ah, what pious soul would not take shelter of that lofty Govardhana?"*

**624.** Look: the village named Paitha, most beautiful;
I shall tell a little of how it came by the name Paitha.

**625.** During the rasa Krishna vanished in this forest;
the gopis wander about, searching for Krishna.

**626.** Krishna, becoming four-armed, appeared [before them];
under Rai's gaze two arms entered His body.[^156]

**627.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Ujjvala-nilamani, in the section on heroines, the fifth and sixth verses:*
*"Though Hari in jest sometimes displays four arms,*
*the love of the Queen of Vrindavana makes Him two-armed.*

**628.** *When the rasa was beginning, [Krishna], hiding, stayed in a kunja;*
*to conceal Himself from the gaze of the doe-eyed ones, with a bold design He skillfully displayed*
*four-armedness; — ah, such is the glory of Radha's love! — by its power*
*even the all-mighty Hari could not maintain that four-armedness."*

**629.** [The arms] enter the body — [He is] two-armed: this is a boundless wonder;
therefore the name Paitha spread among the people.[^157]

**630.** Having shown the village of Paitha and other charming places,
the Pandita turned back and came to Gauri-tirtha.[^158]

**631.** The Pandita joyfully says: look, Srinivasa,
here at Gauri-tirtha wonderful pastimes take place.

**632.** At Gauri-tirtha is a captivating king of trees, a nipa;
look: here is the most beautiful Nipa-kunda.[^159]

**633.** This is the village of Aniyor near the hill;
who could tell of the play that took place here?

**634.** Nanda and the other cowherds, giving up the worship of Indra,
at Krishna's word worship Govardhana Hill.

**635.** They offered Govardhana many kinds of foodstuffs;
Krishna, in one form, partook of everything there.[^160]

**636.** In a voice deeper than [the rumble of] a cloud He proclaims,
"Aniyor, aniyor!" — He says it again and again.[^161]

**637.** The cowherd men and gopis feed Him with boundless delight;
therefore this place bears the name Aniyor.

**638.** This is the place of the Annakuta — look, Srinivasa;
by seeing this place one's desires are fulfilled.[^162]

**639.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the seventy-fifth verse:*
*"Where the Enemy of Agha, eager, assuming a lofty, enormous body,*
*eats the foods offered by the best of the lords of Vraja and tricks Radha with a boon —*
*in that Annakuta I take shelter."*[^163]

**640.** This is Sri Govinda-kunda — great is its glory;
here Indra performed the abhisheka of Govinda.[^164]

**641.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the seventy-fourth verse:*
*"Here the lord of the gods himself, bowing low out of great fear and clasping [His] feet,*
*with the waters of the heavenly Ganga, through Surabhi, performed a new festival of abhisheka —*
*openly, with delight — [installing] Govinda on the throne of sovereignty over the cows;*
*may that Govinda-kunda which arose from those [waters] always shine before my eyes."*

**642.** How many are the fruits of bathing in this Sri Govinda-kunda —
the Puranas proclaim them; who could describe them, and how much?

**643.** *Thus [it is said] in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"Where the Bhagavan was anointed by Maghavan, the enemy of the Yadus —*
*there Govinda-kunda arose; merely bathing in it grants liberation."*

**644.** Here Shakra praised Krishna in many ways;
at Shakra-tirtha bathing and tarpana bring abundant fruit.[^165]

**645.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Near the Annakuta is a tirtha made by Shakra;*
*by bathing and tarpana there one obtains the fruit of a hundred sacrifices."*

**646.** Look: near the kunda is a dense forest;
it was right here that Gopala stayed in hiding.

**647.** Look: here is Dana-nivartana-kunda;
this is a very secret place — others do not know of it.[^166]

**648.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the seventy-eighth verse:*
*"Because here, in secret, the [play of the] toll came to its end,*
*this [pond] received that name in their assembly;*
*by that pond, hidden from those averse to rasa and known only to those who know it —*
*by Dana-nivartana may my dwelling be."*

**649.** Here Madhavendra Puri stayed beneath a tree;
Gopala appeared to him on the pretext of bringing him milk.[^167]

**650.** Look: over there on the hill is Gopala's place;
from time to time Gopala stays at Ganthuli.[^168]

**651.** Look: at the edge of Govardhana is Apsara-kunda;
here the most fortunate bathe.

**652.** Look: here is an old palasha tree;
people call it Shyamadhak; this place is very secluded.[^169]

**653.** Saying this, he goes on with joy in his heart;
coming to his own dwelling place, he says to Srinivasa:

**654.** This is my cave — I live right here;
seeing the beauty of Govardhana, I find great happiness.[^170]

**655.** This cave of Govardhana is most captivating;
here Radha and Krishna constantly enjoy their pastimes.

**656.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the sixty-fifth verse:*
*"Where somewhere on them Madhava, with His loving friends, lavishly painted with streams of their minerals,*
*and Himself painted by them, sports with games*
*and with herding the cows, and somewhere in the caves enjoys festivals of amorous jests*
*together with Sri Radha — those [peaks], the best of mountains, I worship."*

**657.** Look: the footprints of Airavata; here Indra
spoke of Krishna's wonderful mercy.[^171]

**658.** Look: Surabhi-kunda — boundless is its glory;
who is able to tell of the many wonders here?[^172]

**659.** Look at the beauty of Rudra-kunda in the secluded forest;
here Mahadeva became absorbed in meditation on Krishna.

**660.** This is Kadamkhandi: here Krishna
stands gazing at the path by which Radhika will come.[^173]

**661.** O Srinivasa, this place is Danaghati;
here Krishna, the king of connoisseurs of rasa, levies a toll on dairy goods.[^174]

**662.** Here Sri Chaitanya slowly
questions the brahmana who was with Him about the [play of the] toll and so on.[^175]

**663.** The brahmana told in detail of the [play of the] toll and so on;
hearing it, Gaurahari, delighted, softly smiles.

**664.** Absorbed in prema, having the darshan of Harideva,
He performs a wonderful dance; everyone watches.

**665.** The people are maddened with prema; streams of tears flow from their eyes;
all say, "This is an incarnation of Harideva."

**666.** How the Lord manifested Himself at Govardhana —
O Srinivasa, who knows how to describe it?

**667.** Danaghat is a most secluded place;
some call Danaghat by the name Krishna-vedi.

**668.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the seventy-seventh verse:*
*"Where the young king of lovers, His heart eager for the play of the toll station,*
*becoming a toll collector on the pretext of the dairy toll [due to] King Madana,*
*in the morning, surrounded on all sides by His friends, stopped*
*Sri Gandharva, surrounded by her own [companions] — that Krishna-vedi I praise."*[^176]

**669.** The play of the toll here is beyond compare;
Sri Rupa described it in the *Dana-keli-kaumudi*.[^177]

**670.** Look: here is Brahma-kunda — boundless is its glory;
the Puranas proclaim that on its four sides are beautiful tirthas.

**671.** *Thus [it is said] in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"Here Brahma-kunda arose, [where] Hari was pleased by Brahma;*
*and the ponds of Indra and the other guardians of the worlds arose too."*[^178]

**672.** *In the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"There, O fortunate one, is a lake with trees, shrubs and creepers;*
*there are four tirthas, holy and auspicious.*

**673.** *The tirtha of Indra is on the eastern side, the tirtha of Yama on the south,*
*the tirtha of Varuna on the west, and the tirtha of Kuvera on the north;*
*and I, staying in the middle, shall play as I please."*

**674.** Look at Manasi-ganga: here Sri Krishna,
with a joyful heart, goes boating and enjoys other pastimes.

**675.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the sixty-fourth verse:*
*"At the foot of the serpent, filled with the rasa of delight in the boating play of Gandharvika and the Crusher of Mura,*
*Manasa-jahnavi with the surge of her waves raises up heaps of the shining stones of Govardhana —*
*may she protect me."*[^179]

**676.** The water of Sri Manasi-ganga is supremely pure;
who could tell what the fruit of bathing here is?

**677.** Having said this and having had darshan of Harideva,
he joyfully speaks of the glory of Govardhana:

**678.** O Srinivasa, Govardhana is full of bliss;
the road to it from Mathura is eight kroshas.

**679.** In the western part of Mathura lies Govardhana-kshetra;
at its mere sight the grievous miseries of samsara depart.

**680.** Whoever bathes in Manasi-ganga,
has darshan of Harideva at Govardhana

**681.** and circumambulates Annakuta and Govardhana —
he never again comes and goes in samsara.

**682.** This Govardhana Krishna held up with His left hand
and protected Vraja, crushing Indra's pride.

**683.** At Govardhana Krishna's happiness has no limit;
the Puranas sing its glory in many ways.

**684.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"There is a holy land named Govardhana, supremely hard to attain,*
*in the western part of Mathura, not far — two yojanas away.*

**685.** *Reaching Annakuta from there, one should circumambulate it;*
*for him there is no return [to this world], O goddess — I tell you the truth.*

**686.** *Having bathed in Manasi-ganga, having seen Hari at Govardhana,*
*having circumambulated Annakuta — why should a person still grieve?*

**687.** *When Indra was pouring down water beyond measure, tormenting the cows,*
*to protect them I held up the best of hills."*

**688.** *In the Skanda Purana, in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"[There is] the Bhagavan Govardhana, where Govardhana was held up*
*and all the Yadavas were saved, since Indra's rain was warded off.*[^180]

**689.** *Ah, Govardhana, where Vishnu always dwells!*
*There Brahma, Shiva and Lakshmi certainly live."*

**690.** *In the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Having circumambulated Govardhana and seen the god Hari, the Lord,*
*[a person] without doubt obtains the fruit of the Rajasuya and the Ashvamedha."*

**691.** O Srinivasa, near Govardhana
there lived a brahmana — everyone knew he was wealthy.

**692.** He was always in rapture, loved Balaichand,
and constantly meditated on the deeds of Balarama.[^181]

**693.** Fixing it firmly in his mind — "[He] will surely appear to me" —
he wandered through these forests of Govardhana.

**694.** The good fortune of that brahmana cannot be described:
suddenly there came [to him] a command: "I shall meet you."

**695.** For the sake of His dear devotee, Nityananda-Rama,
joyfully wandering among the holy places, came to Govardhana.

**696.** Having come, He stayed right here, seeing that the place was secluded;
His form, which enchants Kandarpa, captivates every heart.[^182]

**697.** Seeing [Him] from afar, that brahmana thought to himself:
"From where has this avadhuta come here?

**698.** With the radiance of His body He has lit up the forest;
this is not a mere human being — He [only] resembles a human.

**699.** By His mercy my desire will be fulfilled."
Reflecting thus, the brahmana could not keep still.

**700.** Taking yogurt, milk, curd, fresh butter and other things,
he came before Prabhu and, bowing down, said:

**701.** "O avadhuta, this is my prayer:
be merciful, so that I may see the son of Rohini.

**702.** Accept the little that I have brought."
Hearing this, Prabhu smiled and with great delight partook [of it].

**703.** Taking the remnants, the brahmana went to his own place;
as he ate them, he was overwhelmed with prema.

**704.** He could not go to Prabhu again:
at about the time of dusk, sleep overcame him.

**705.** In the guise of a dream Prabhu Nityananda appeared to him;
seeing Avadhutachandra, the brahmana rejoiced.

**706.** At that very moment Prabhu took the form of Baladeva;
the brahmana fell down and rolled at Prabhu's feet.

**707.** How [beautiful] was Baladeva's form, enchanting the worlds!
On His body many ornaments glittered.

**708.** When, having shown mercy to the brahmana, He vanished from sight,
his sleep broke — the brahmana looks around in all directions.

**709.** To the place where he had seen Prabhu, the avadhuta,
he hurries — his mind finds no rest.

**710.** A voice from heaven sounded: "Have patience now:
from here you will go there when the night is over."

**711.** Hearing this, the brahmana reflects within himself:
"The hope I had has been fulfilled.

**712.** I have found Prabhu — now I will not let [Him] go;
falling at [His] feet, I will take this attire off Him.

**713.** At daybreak I will send for a goldsmith
and put many kinds of ornaments on Prabhu."

**714.** Just as he said this, sleep overcame him;
in the guise of a dream Nityananda gave him darshan.

**715.** [His] body was adorned with many kinds of ornaments;
seeing [this], the best of brahmanas offers abundant praises.

**716.** When Prabhu vanished, his sleep broke;
in the morning, going before Prabhu, [the brahmana] told Him everything.

**717.** Smiling gently, Prabhu took the brahmana by the hand
and in His mercy revealed to him the whole truth.

**717a.** And again with sweet words He said to the brahmana:
"You have set your mind on putting ornaments on Me."[^183]

**718.** The brahmana said: "The ornaments I saw on Prabhu —
who is there who could make their equal?"

**719.** Prabhu, who is subject to His devotees, said: "After some time
I shall certainly be adorned with many kinds of ornaments.[^184]

**720.** For now, set this wonderful stone of Govardhana
in gold and give it to Me — I shall wear it at My neck."

**721.** The brahmana set the stone in gold and brought it [to Him];
the crest-jewel of avadhutas put it on His neck.

**722.** This lila of Nityananda is rare even for Brahma and others;
He forbade the brahmana to reveal it to others.

**723.** Out of love for His devotee He stayed here for some days;
by seeing this place one attains rare love.

**724.** Look at this Chakra-tirtha, O Srinivasa:
by its mercy desires are fulfilled.[^185]

**725.** Chakra-tirtha is most renowned at Govardhana;
here is the swing pastime of Sri Radha and Krishna.

**726.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the seventy-ninth and eightieth verses:*
*"The finest ponds, bearing the names of the Plough-bearer, Brahma, Kadamba-khanda, Sumano, Rudra, the Apsaras, Gauri,*
*Jyotsna, Mokshana, Malyahara, the Enemy of the gods, Indradhvaja and others,*
*which shine all around Mount Govardhana — them I praise,*
*and also Chakra-tirtha, the divine hill and the beautiful Ratna-pitha.*[^186]

**727.** *Ah, where every spring the host of friends, showing their joy,*
*again and again swings those two — Sri Gandharva and the Holder of the best of hills —*
*whose faces have blossomed with the abundance of the highest rasa of the swing play —*
*that renowned, bountiful place of Govinda I, ah, worship."*

**728.** O Srinivasa, Chakra-tirtha commanded
Sri Sanatana Gosvami to live here.[^187]

**729.** He lived here with a jubilant heart;
look: here is his hut in the midst of the forest.

**730.** Every day he circumambulated Govardhana —
walking twelve kroshas: who else has such strength?

**731.** Seeing his great toil in old age, Gopinatha
appeared before him in the guise of a cowherd boy.

**732.** Carefully wiping the sweat from Sanatana's body,
with tears He spoke sweet words:

**733.** "In old age you will not be able to toil so much.
O svami, what I tell you, you must surely accept."

**734.** Sanatana said: "Speak — once I know it, I will accept."
Hearing this, the cowherd boy went and climbed Govardhana.

**735.** Bringing a stone of Govardhana bearing the print of His own foot,
He again spoke most sweet words to Sanatana:

**736.** "O svami, take this footprint of Krishna;
from today you shall circumambulate it.[^188]

**737.** By this the whole circumambulation will be accomplished."
Saying this, He brought the stone and gave [it to him] in the hut.

**738.** Having handed over the stone, Krishna vanished from sight;
not seeing the boy, Sanatana became anxious.

**739.** Seeing Sanatana distraught, [Krishna], remaining invisible,
overcome with tenderness, revealed who He was.

**740.** Sanatana was drenched with his own tears;
after much lamenting he [at last] took firmness of heart.

**741.** The son of the king of Vraja, subject to Sanatana's prema,
enjoys many pastimes in this flower forest.

**742.** Sri Radhika comes with her friends;
going out to meet them, He brings them here.

**743.** At this ghat of Manasi-ganga, taking a boat,
He becomes the boatman and ferries them all across.

**744.** Here are wondrous pastimes with Sri Radhika;
Lalita and the other friends fulfilled the desire.

**745.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Stavavali, in the Sri Govardhanashraya-dashaka, the sixth verse:*
*"She on whom Madhava the boatman, seating Radha, full of rasa, in the boat,*
*in the midst of the restless waters, rocking [the boat] as if it were about to capsize,*
*took the toll He desired from her as she praised Him in fear — that Manasa-jahnavi flows upon him;*
*who would not take shelter of that Govardhana, the surety of that young couple?"*[^189]

**746.** In this village of Saunkarai a playful scene unfolded:
the friends made Krishna swear an oath.[^190]

**747.** Having sworn, Krishna says again and again:
"Besides Sri Radhika, I never know anyone else."

**748.** O Srinivasa, this is the village of Sakhisthali,
where Chandravali lived; now it is called Sakhithara.

**749.** Look: sitting here, at this place, Uddhava
speaks about Krishna to [Krishna's] beloveds from Dvaraka.[^191]

**750.** Here, beside Govardhana, Krishna in great joy
plays various games together with the cowherd boys.

**751.** Look: here the two brothers, Rama and Krishna,
sat, surrounded by their friends.

**752.** Saying this, the Pandita took Srinivasa with him
and, with joy in his heart, went to the bank of Radha-kunda.

**753.** Sri Govinda-ghat is very dear to Govinda;
having bathed there, he says to Srinivasa:[^192]

**754.** O Srinivasa, beneath this tree
a pastime took place — I shall tell you of it.

**755.** One day Sanatana came here from Govardhana
to see Rupa and Raghunatha.[^193]

**756.** Sri Rupa Gosvami was composing verses;
he compared a braid to the hood of a female serpent.[^194]

**757.** Sanatana Gosvami, seeing this, says something:
"You have given such a comparison — is it right or not?"

**758.** Saying this, he came and stepped into the water of the kunda;
he sees girls playing beneath the tree.

**759.** On a girl's head the braid hangs down over her back;
Sanatana sees it — and he took it for a snake.

**760.** He says to the girl in great agitation:
"A snake is climbing onto your head by way of your back!

**761.** Heedless girls, be careful!"
Saying this, he rushed forward to drive it off.

**762.** Seeing Sanatana extremely agitated,
they all smiled slightly and vanished.[^195]

**763.** Sanatana was overwhelmed right here;
regaining his composure, he went to Rupa Gosvami.

**764.** He says to Rupa: "What you wrote is true."
Sri Rupa understood Sanatana's heart.

**765.** With joy in his heart Sri Gosvami Sanatana,
having stayed [there] for a while, went to Govardhana.

**766.** Sri Rupa Gosvami too went to Vrindavana.
I shall tell something of the reason for which he had come [here].

**767.** The *Lalita-madhava*, in which is the utmost limit of vipralambha,
he had earlier given to Raghunatha to relish.[^196]

**768.** Reading the book, Raghunatha weeps day and night;
from grief he fell into divine madness — he cannot keep his composure.

**769.** Sometimes, abandoning the book, he goes and stays far away;
sometimes he lies fallen on the ground, holding the book to his chest.

**770.** Moment by moment various states arise;
all were anxious whenever he fell unconscious.

**771.** Sri Rupa Gosvami, considering a remedy in his mind,
quickly composed the *Dana-keli-kaumudi*.

**772.** He says to Raghunatha: "Relish this;
give me the former book — I shall revise it."

**773.** Raghunatha cannot part with that jewel of a book;
hearing "I shall revise it," he gave [it] to Sri Rupa.

**774.** Reading the *Dana-keli*, Raghunatha, the best of the wise,
was constantly immersed in an ocean of happiness.

**775.** Of all the ways of Sanatana, Rupa and Raghunatha —
O Srinivasa, how much can I tell?

**776.** Saying this, the Pandita took Srinivasa with him
and set out for their lodging with great joy in his heart.

**777.** All the places that lie near Radha-kunda —
to see them all, he quickly set out.

**778.** Raghava Pandita says to Srinivasa:
this is called Nimgrama — it is known [so] for this reason:

**779.** all of them, coming out from Govardhana,
gazing at Krishna's face, performed nirmanchhana with what is dearer than life.[^197]

**780.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the forty-third verse:*
*"Those gopis who out of love, even with their sons, dearer to them than life, perform nirmanchhana*
*over a tiny drop of the sweat trickling from Mukunda's pair of lotus feet,*
*and for a long time kiss His head, beautiful with a large peacock feather —*
*the dust of their feet I constantly and openly honor with nirmanchhana."*

**781.** Look at the village of Patala: here with Her friends
Rai joyfully gathers patala flowers.[^198]

**782.** This is the village of Deravali: going from Shashthighara
to Nandishvara, Nanda made camp here.[^199]

**783.** Look: ahead, by this kunja, is the village of Nava;
from here begins the boundary of the kunjas of Sri Kunda.[^200]

**784.** Now people call this village Kunjera;
here are the incomparable pastimes of Radha and Krishna.

**785.** This is the village of Surya-kunda, called Morana;
look at the Deity of Surya, at Surya's temple in the forest.[^201]

**786.** With Her friends Rai worships Surya in great happiness;
Krishna, becoming the priest, playfully performs the puja [with Her].

**787.** This merciful Surya grants love for Krishna;
what to say of his glory — who does not worship him?

**788.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*"To the father of the Yamuna, Surya, who removes all diseases,*
*whose form is the abode of auspiciousness — to him, the giver of love for Krishna, I bow."*[^202]

**789.** Look: here ahead is the village named Keunai;
here Ghanashyama is distraught without Rai.

**790.** "Keu na ai?" Sri Krishna asks the messenger;
hence Keunai — and now it is called Konai.[^203]

**791.** Look there: this is the village named Bhadayar;
it is here that Bhadra, the leader of a group of friends, enjoys her pastimes.[^204]

**792.** Look: over there is the village of Magahera;
there everyone gazes at the road by which Krishna goes away.[^205]

**793.** How distraught they all were cannot be told;
now people call this [place] Maghera.

**794.** Thus showing other places of pastimes as well,
they came, filled with joy, to Radha-kunda.

**795.** Whoever loves to see and hear about all these —
his terrible misfortune is removed without effort.

**796.** That day they stayed right on the bank of Radha-kunda;
in talks about Krishna they spent that night until dawn.

**797.** Thus, making the circuit along Govardhana,
they came, filled with joy, to the village of Ganthuli.

**798.** Raghava Pandita says to Srinivasa:
I shall tell how the village came by the name Ganthuli.

**799.** Here, having played Holi, the Two sit on a throne;
a friend secretly tied the garments of Them both in a knot.

**800.** When the Two rose from the throne,
They see the knot in Their garments — the friends laugh.

**801.** There was great merriment; They both were abashed;
someone, receiving the phagua, untied the knot.[^206]

**802.** Hence Ganthuli; in the water of this Gulal-kunda
people now see the phagu in springtime.

**803.** Saying this, he went for darshan of Gopala;
seeing Gopala's beauty, he lost his composure.

**804.** The Deity of Krishna Chaitanya whom Vitthala served —
supreme eagerness arose for His darshan.[^207]

**805.** Sri Vitthalanatha Bhatta, the son of Vallabha,
showed such affection that it cannot be told.

**806.** From time to time Gopala stays at Ganthuli;
in every way He fulfills the desires of the devotees.

**807.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya, the crest jewel of sannyasis —
by His pilgrimage to the holy places this earth is blessed.

**808.** In Mathura, Sri Vrindavana, at the Kunda, at Govardhana,
the pastimes He reveals are seen by the fortunate.

**809.** In the mood of a devotee the Lord does not set foot on Govardhana;
He wished to see Gopala.

**810.** Gopala came to the village of Ganthuli on a pretext;
seeing Him, Gaurahari was immersed in dancing and singing.[^208]

**811.** Sri Mahaprabhu's supernatural rapture of prema —
on seeing it, no one kept even a trace of composure.

**812.** At that time the service of Gopala was entrusted
to those two brahmanas whom Puri had made his disciples.

**813.** By the mercy of Madhavendra the two brahmanas from Gauda
were strong in renunciation and full of the rasa of prema-bhakti.[^209]

**814.** What to say — after the departure of those two brahmanas,
for some time a certain fortunate person served.

**815.** Sri Dasa Gosvami and others, after consulting together,
entrusted the service to Sri Vitthaleshvara.

**816.** After the departure of his father, Sri Vallabha Bhatta,
he lived for some time in Mathura in seclusion.

**817.** Utterly overwhelmed by the pastimes of Gaurachandra,
he is now always attentive in the service of Gopala.

**818.** Having told of Gopala's qualities, Raghava Pandita,
filled with joy, goes on from Ganthuli.

**819.** Going some distance, he says to Srinivasa:
look: this is the village named Rehej.

**820.** Here Indra, deeming himself utterly low,
comes before Krishna, taking Surabhi as his helper.[^210]

**821.** And this place of pastimes is full of great splendor;
look: Devashirsha-sthana — the kunda shines beautifully.[^211]

**822.** Seeing Krishna herding the cows with His friends,
here the gods in great joy offered praises.

**823.** Look: Munishirsha-sthana, the kunda full of sweetness;
here the sages, by performing austerities, attained Krishna.

**824.** Look: in all these places Rama and Krishna,
herding the cows with Their friends, played various games.

**825.** In this village of Pramodana, Krishna, playfully,
gave delight to all the beauties of Vraja.[^212]

**826.** For this reason the village bears the name Pramodana;
now all people call it Paramadana.

**827.** This is Setu-kandara — a most charming place;[^213]
look: [here is] the merciful Adi-Badri-narayana.

**828.** Most wondrous is the service [of Him] deep in the forest;
on the charming hill Gandha-shila is set.[^214]

**829.** Here Krishna, bringing Nanda and the other cowherds,
dispelled their sorrow by showing them Narayana.[^215]

**830.** Here ahead, look, is a grove of kadambas alone;
here Radha-Krishna and the sakhis are immersed in happiness.

**831.** Here they engage in many kinds of play;
in the month of Shravana, having made a swing, they swing joyfully.

**832.** At this Indroli, Indra was absorbed in meditation on Krishna;
now everyone calls the village Idaroli.

**833.** O Srinivasa, look: here nearby
is the village of Kanoyaro, the place of the sage Kanva's austerities.[^216]

**834.** Look: this is Kamyavana, the best of all forests;
whoever comes here is honored in Vishnu-loka.[^217]

**835.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The fourth is Kamyaka-vana, the best forest among forests;*
*having gone there, O goddess, a man is glorified in My world."*

**836.** Kamyavana grants the fruit of all desires;
wherever one bathes here, all sorrows are destroyed.

**837.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"Next is Kamyavana, O king, where you stayed in childhood;*
*by mere bathing it grants everyone the fruit of all desires."*

**838.** In this Kamyavana Krishna's pastimes are enchanting;
you will see a great many places and kundas.

**839.** O Srinivasa, look: Vishnu-simhasana;
[this is] Sri Charana-kunda: here [He] washed [His] feet.

**840.** What can I say, oh, of the glory of this place!
Brahma and the others, describing it, find no end to it.

**841.** Look: Shiva Kameshvara, full of great splendor;[^218]
the place of Garuda's seat is most charming.

**842.** This is Dharma-kunda: Narayana in the form of Dharma
sports here; [its] beauty cannot be described.

**843.** This is the altar named Vishoka — everyone knows it;
look: here is the kunda of the five Pandavas.[^219]

**844.** This is Manikarnika, of which all people sing;
here are the power of Vishvanatha and much else.[^220]

**845.** Bathing in this Vimala-kunda destroys all sins;
one who gives up his life here attains Vishnu-loka.

**846.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"In the kunda of Vimala one is freed from all sin;*
*whoever gives up his life there goes to My world."*

**847.** The account of Vimala-kunda cannot be told;
here Sri Vimala-devi always resides.

**848.** Look: Yashoda-kunda, supremely pure;
here Krishna herds the cows, rapt beyond Himself.

**849.** Look: Narada-kunda; here Narada
lost all composure singing Krishna's pastimes.

**850.** This is Kamana-kunda, which everyone knows;
here all the desires of the heart are fulfilled.

**851.** This is Setubandha-kunda — much is told about it:
here Krishna performed the pastime of bridging the ocean.[^221]

**852.** This is the place of Lukalukani and Michali;
here the play of Radha and Krishna is boundless.

**853.** "Michali" means here "with the eyes closed";
in "Lukalukani" happiness grows by hiding.[^222]

**854.** Lukalukani-Michali-kunda is beautiful;
this very dense forest is full of darkness.

**855.** Look: Kashi-kunda, Gaya, Prayaga, Pushkara,
Gomati, Dvaraka-kunda — secluded and beautiful.[^223]

**856.** This is Tapa-kunda, the place of the sages' austerities;
this is Dhyana-kunda: [here] Krishna meditated on Radha.

**857.** Look: the marks of the holy feet on the hill;
in this Krida-kunda Krishna plays in the water.

**858.** Charming are the kundas of the five cowherd boys — Shridama and the others;
this is Ghosharani-kunda, supremely beautiful.

**859.** Ghosharani is the daughter of the cowherd Yashodhara;
the king of the cowherds gave [this] girl in marriage here.[^224]

**860.** Look: Vihvala-kunda; here Rai
was overwhelmed by the song of Krishna's flute.

**861.** This is Shyama-kunda: here Shyama, full of rasa,
stays gazing at the path [by which] Radhika [will come].[^225]

**862.** This is Sri Lalita-kunda, and this one is named Vishakha-kunda;
here the two fulfilled the desire of Krishna's heart.

**863.** Look: Mana-kunda — here Radha was sulking (mana);
Krishna broke her sulking with playful words.

**864.** At this Mohini-kunda Krishna became Mohini —
that form of Mohini in which He distributed the nectar.[^226]

**865.** Look: this Dohani-kunda is the place of milking the cows;[^227]
this is Balabhadra-kunda, made by Brahma.

**866.** This is Surya-kunda, near Krishna-kunda;
Surya, staying here, offered praises to Krishna.

**867.** On Chandrasena hill is this Pichhalini-shila;
here Krishna with His friends plays this game:[^228]

**868.** sitting gracefully on top of the low hill,
He slides down — so He does again and again.

**869.** Look: Kama-sarovara — Gopika-ramana;
who could describe the enchanting play [that took place] here?

**870.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"There, O king, is Kamasaras, the lake Gopika-ramana;*
*there are thousands of tirthas and lakes, each one apart."*

**871.** This Kama-sarovara is full of great happiness;
Kama-sarovara is called Kama-sagara.

**872.** Look: Surabhi-kunda — its beauty is boundless;
Krishna with the cows and cowherds sports here.

**873.** This is Chaturbhuja-kunda, supremely secluded;
the wonderful sports [that took place] here cannot be described.

**874.** Look: Bhojana-sthali — here Krishna
made merry over a meal with His friends.

**875.** Look: Bajana-shila, O Srinivasa;
here everyone rejoices to the sound of many instruments.[^229]

**876.** Behold the place where Parashurama stayed;
here Narayana sat on the throne.

**877.** This is Santana-kunda, Veda-kunda, Damodara;
this is Gandharva-kunda, Prithudaka — an excellent kunda.[^230]

**878.** Look: Ayodhya-kunda, supremely secluded;
I cannot set out at length the account of this kunda.

**879.** Look: Sri Nrisimha-kunda, and also Arghya-kunda;
this is Madhusudana-kunda — its glory is proclaimed.

**880.** Rohini-kunda, Gopala-kunda, Godavari;
look at Devaki-kunda, full of wondrous sweetness.

**881.** On this hill is the place of the stealing game; Vyomasura
Krishna playfully slew at the mouth of this cave.[^231]

**882.** Look: Prahlada-kunda, and also Lakshmi-kunda;
the tirthas in Kamyavana are beyond counting.

**883.** This is the place of Krishna's play on top of the hill;
look from here — in all four directions [all is] enchanting.

**884.** Look, Srinivasa, there is the village of Dhulauda;
there the dust from the cows' hooves covered the sky.[^232]

**885.** That village all people call by the name Udha;
staying there, Uddhava went to Nanda's home.[^233]

**886.** This is the village of Ator, charming and secluded; here
Krishna is absorbed in play through all eight praharas.[^234]

**887.** Look: Kadamkhandi, the village of Svarnahara,
Ratna-kunda, the incomparable place of Chaturmukha.[^235]

**888.** At the place of Svarnahara the play is boundless;
now it is called Sona-ar and Sonahera.[^236]

**889.** Look at the hill: here Krishna, herding the cows —
who could tell the joy He finds?

**890.** This is Vrishabhanupura, called Varshana;
near the hill is Vrishabhanu's home.[^237]

**891.** A wondrous hill: here the son of the king of Vraja
performed the dana-lila, hidden from others.

**892.** Here [He] broke Radhika's sulking (mana);
here Krishna was intoxicated with many kinds of play.

**893.** Between the two hills, on this narrow path —
the sport [that took place] there, no one can tell.

**894.** Now everyone calls this place Sankari-khor;
the hills of Dana, Mana and Vilasa — three garhs.[^238]

**895.** O Srinivasa, Sri Radhika with her sakhis
played many games here in a childlike mood.

**896.** In the wondrous time when Radhika was at the junction of ages,
here, in great delight, she sports together with the sakhis.

**897.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Ujjvala-nilamani, in [the chapter] on uddipana, [in the section] on vayah-sandhi, the sixth verse:*[^239]
*"The junction of childhood and youth is called vayah-sandhi."*

**898.** The junction of childhood and youth is wondrous in this way:
as when another [king] takes possession of a kingdom.

**899.** *For example, [it is said] in the same work, the eleventh verse:*
*"The hips, girded with a cord, learning of their rise, bring music — the kinkini bells;*
*the waist, dwindling and seeing its own ruin, seeks alliance with the folds;*
*the bosom diligently gathers a pair of fruits fit to be a gift for the king —*
*when into the kingdom of Radha's body there enters the new lord of the earth, youth."*[^240]

**900.** In this kunja the sakhis, having dressed Radhika,[^241]
gaze their fill at the beauty of her fresh youth.

**901.** *Thus [it is said] in the same [work], in [the section] on uddipana, on the characteristics of fresh youth, the twelfth verse:*
*"The breasts have barely budded, the eyes are slightly restless, the smile is charming,*
*and feeling shows itself only a little — this is called fresh youth."*

**902.** In this kadamba grove Radha joyfully sports;
the sakhis behold the beauty of Her manifest youth.

**903.** *Thus [it is said] in the same [work], in [the section] on uddipana, on the characteristics of manifest youth:*[^242]
*"The breasts have fully developed, the waist bears three beautiful folds,*
*and the limbs are radiant — when manifest youth unfolds."*

**904.** All is possible: She is always in manifest youth;
this Rai increases rasa with the art of Ananga.

**905.** In this Madana-kunja, joyfully, together with the sakhis —
how wondrous is that beauty in full youth!

**906.** *Thus [it is said] in the same [work], in [the section] on uddipana, on the characteristics of full youth, the forty-first verse:*
*"The hips are broad, the waist slender, the body of splendid luster,*
*the breasts full, the thighs like plantain trunks — in full youth."*

**907.** What shall I say! In this kunja of tamalas the sakhis,
on a pretext, arranged the meeting of Radha and Krishna.

**908.** The village of Chiksauli [was called] Chitrashali before;
here the sakhis, skilled in wondrous attire, [dressed] Rai.[^243]

**909.** Look: in the mountain gorge is a dense forest;
now people call it Gahvara-vana.[^244]

**910.** This is Shitala-kunda, surrounded on all sides by trees;
look at Dohani-kunda — here the cows were milked.

**911.** This is the village of Dabhararo; here Krishna's
eyes filled with tears at the sight of Radhika.

**912.** Dabhararo means "with eyes full of tears," so it is said;
now among the people it is known by the name Dabharo.

**913.** Look: Mukta-kunda; here lovely Radhika,
contending with Krishna, made a field of pearls.[^245]

**914.** Look: east of Vrishabhanupura is Bhanukhora;
its water is very clear, its beauty has no limit.[^246]

**915.** Look: north of the village is Piyala-sarovara;
here the Beloved and the Lover — both — engage in many kinds of play.

**916.** Here is a forest of jiyala trees, very extensive;
seeing [its] beauty, both rejoice together with the sakhis.[^247]

**917.** This is Pilukhora; here, on the pretext of [gathering] pilu fruits,
Rai with the sakhis merrily plays with Kanu.[^248]

**918.** Now people say "Bhanukhora," "Pilukhora";
formerly they were named Bhanu-sarovara and Pilu-sarovara.

**919.** Near Varshana is this river, the Triveni;
how Krishna's pastimes [took place] here, I do not know how to tell.

**920.** Look at Krishna's places of pastimes — they are beyond compare;
how many of the villages founded by Vajra have vanished!

**921.** O Srinivasa, look: this is Prema-sarovara;
here the feeling of prema-vaichittya was manifested.[^249]

**922.** Look: Vihvala-kunda; here, on hearing
the name "Sri Krishna," Rai was overwhelmed.

**923.** In this kunja of Sanketa the sakhis, giving a signal,
bring both Rai and Kanu with great care.[^250]

**924.** Unnoticed, at an auspicious moment, the first going [to the tryst] took place;
here, in the time of purva-raga, there was a brief meeting.[^251]

**925.** How wondrous the delights of purva-raga are cannot be told;
the characteristics of purva-raga are defined in the shastras.

**926.** *Thus [it is said] in the Ujjvala-nilamani, in the section on separation, the fifth verse:*
*"The attraction (rati) that arises before meeting from seeing [each other], hearing [of each other] and so on,*
*and blossoms in the two of them, is called purva-raga by the wise."*

**927.** Look at Krishna-kunda and other enchanting places;
in Sanketa are endless pastimes, beyond the sight of others.

**928.** The people of the villages of Nandishvara and Varshana —
all of them come and go along this road.[^252]

**929.** Along this road Sri Radhika, from Her father's house,
goes to the village of Javata, to the house of Her father-in-law.

**930.** This wondrous forest is always in cool shade;
birds sing in many voices, the bees hum.

**931.** O Srinivasa, look: Nandishvara, the abode of Nanda;
here Rama and Krishna, hidden [in human form], enjoy Their pastimes.

**932.** *Thus [it is said] in the Tenth [Canto], in the forty-fourth chapter, the twelfth verse:*[^253]
*"How pious are the lands of Vraja, where this Primeval Person, hidden in human form,*
*adorned with a colorful forest garland, tends the cows together with Bala, plays the flute,*
*and roams about in various play — He whose feet are worshipped by Giritra and Rama!"*

**933.** Look: this is the boundary of Nanda's settlement;
east of Nanda's house is a wondrous garden.

**934.** From Javata Sri Radhika with the sakhis
comes along this road to Nanda's house.

**935.** O Srinivasa, whoever, having bathed in this Pavana-sarovara,
sees Krishna on Nandishvara

**936.** and has darshan of Sri Nanda and Sri Yashoda —
all his desires are fulfilled at that very moment.[^254]

**937.** *Thus [it is said] in the Mathura-mahatmya:*[^255]
*"Having bathed in the Pavana lake, [beholding] Krishna on Nandishvara hill,*
*and having seen Nanda and Yashoda, one obtains all that one desires."*

**938.** This Pavana-sarovara is very dear to Krishna;
who, seeing this wondrous beauty, can keep his composure?

**939.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the fifty-ninth verse:*
*"Surrounded by multitudes of kadambas, charming with the humming of swarms of bees —*
*where the lotus-eyed [gopis], on the pretext of sweet play in the water and of fetching water,*
*again and again hasten to the son of the king of the cowherds*
*with merriment and love — may this Pavana lake protect [us]."*

**940.** Look: all around Nandishvara are kundas and forests,
places of Krishna's pastimes that purify the worlds.

**941.** Look: on the hill, together with their son,
Sri Nanda and Yashoda shine in a wondrous cave.[^256]

**942.** O Srinivasa, here Sri Chaitanya-raya,
having come to receive darshan, enters the cave.

**943.** Sri Nanda and Yashoda — two persons, one on each side,
and in the middle Krishnachandra; seeing [this], His eyes blossomed.

**944.** Bowing at the feet of Sri Nanda and Sri Yashoda,
He touches all of Krishna's body, overflowing with joy.

**945.** In the rapture of prema He began to dance and sing;
seeing this, all the people were amazed.

**946.** Some say, "He is certainly not a human being;
is such beauty possible in a human?"

**947.** Others say, "He is Narayana of Vaikuntha,
wandering in Vraja in human form."

**948.** Someone says, "Oh, this is what comes to my mind:
has the son of Nanda appeared again?

**949.** "Otherwise why would He act like this?
Again and again He falls at the feet of Nanda and Yashoda.

**950.** "From His beautiful lotus eyes tears flow incessantly;
with joined palms He says something softly — I do not know what.

**951.** "What shall I say, O brother! Seeing Him,
I have become certain in my heart: this is Krishna."

**952.** Saying many such things, they float on the waves of prema;
crying "Hari bol!" they dance together with the Lord.

**953.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya, the crest-jewel of sannyasis —
what prema He manifested here, I do not know how to tell.

**954.** This is Tadaga-tirtha, renowned everywhere;
how beautifully trees and creepers adorn it on all sides!

**955.** O Srinivasa, I will briefly tell one more account:
here was the dwelling of Parjanya, the son of Devamidha.[^257]

**956.** Out of mercy Narada came to Nandishvara
and gave Parjanya the Lakshmi-Narayana mantra.

**957.** Parjanya performed austerities at Tadaga-tirtha;
his desire was fulfilled — five sons were born:

**958.** Upananda, Abhinanda, the one named Nanda,
Sananda and Nandana — thus the five brothers are known.

**959.** Look: this is that very pond, and it is dear to Krishna;
the devotees always pray for service to this pond.

**960.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the sixtieth verse:*
*"Where grandfather Parjanya, giving up food, intently worshipped Narayana*
*when his own son, the lord of the pastures, was without a son,*
*and where a grandson was obtained — the slayer of the enemies of the gods, Giridhara, the sole mine of virtues —*
*that pond, renowned on earth as Kshunahara, is my refuge."*[^258]

**961.** O Srinivasa, look at Kshunahara-sarovara;
what shall I say of what Krishna's pastimes are like here!

**962.** This is Dhoyani-kunda, to the northeast of Nandishvara;
here remains the water with which the curd pots were washed.

**963.** Look: by this Krishna-kunda is a forest of kadambas;
here the son of the lord of Vraja joyfully sports.

**964.** Look: Lalita-kunda; here Lalita,
bringing Radhika on a pretext, unites [Her] with Krishna.

**965.** Here is the most wondrous Surya-kunda;
Surya lost his composure on seeing Krishna.

**966.** Look: this is Vishakha-kunda;
here, in great delight, Rai and Kanu met.

**967.** Look: Paurnamasi-kunda in deep seclusion;
Paurnamasi lives here in a hut of leaves.[^259]

**968.** In the pastimes of Radha and Krishna her joy is unceasing;
who has the power to understand what her deeds are like?

**969.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the twenty-fifth verse:*
*"She who in secret, through friends honored for their skill in this, arranges for Them —*
*with love, expertly, every day — the festival of mana and abhisara of the two connoisseurs of rasa,*
*and who herself again and again tastes the nectarean rasa of the happiness of Radha and Madhava —*
*to that Bhagavati Paurnamasi, who brings about good in Vraja, I offer worship."*[^260]

**970.** Here is the charming abode of Nandimukhi,
who is always happy in the happiness of Radha and Krishna.[^261]

**971.** The beautiful character of Sri Nandimukhi
the great bhagavatas described with care in the past.

**972.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the thirty-fourth verse:*
*"She who, her heart enchanted by abundant hearing of the glory [of Radha and Madhava], [left] Avanti*
*and in deep longing chose the land of Vraja [as her home],*
*and joyfully increases the happiness of Radha and Krishna's ujjvala-rasa — that*
*Nandimukhi I always greet with love."*

**973.** Look: everywhere are most delightful kunjas;
in these places there is no end to Krishna's pastimes.

**974.** This is Sri Yashoda-kunda; here [dwells] Yashoda;
look: Rama and Krishna play with Their friends.

**975.** O Srinivasa, Krishna, full of the bliss of prema,
here in His various ages enjoys pastimes beyond measure.

**976.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu, in the Dakshina-vibhaga, in the first lahari, the hundred and fifty-eighth verse:*
*"Age — kaumara, pauganda and kaishora: thus it is threefold.*
*Kaumara lasts until the end of the fifth year, pauganda up to the tenth,*
*kaishora up to the sixteenth; after that comes youth."*[^262]

**977.** Here, in the age of kaumara, Yashoda towards Krishna
shows such parental love — who could tell of it?

**978.** Krishna, ever absorbed in the mood of the kaumara age,
increases His mother's happiness, which is beyond the reach of others.

**979.** *Thus [it is said] there, the hundred and fifty-ninth verse:*
*"As is fitting, in the rasa of parental love kaumara is to be described."*

**980.** Krishna in the pauganda age in this kadamba grove —
whatever merriment arises, His beloved ones see it.

**981.** The pauganda age is threefold: the beginning, the middle and the last;
its games and the rest are full of supreme bliss.

**982.** *Thus [it is said] there, the hundred and fifty-ninth verse:*
*"Likewise in the rasa of friendship, pauganda, in connection with the various games and the rest."*

**983.** *There, in the Pashchima-vibhaga, in the third lahari, the twenty-third verse:*
*"Pauganda is of three kinds: the beginning, the middle and the last."*[^263]

**984.** In early pauganda Krishna's body is exceedingly beautiful;
here are the herding of calves and other charming activities.

**985.** *Thus [it is said] there:*[^264]
*"A deep redness of the lips and so on, slenderness of the waist,*
*the appearance of a conch-like neck and so on — when the first pauganda comes.*

**986.** *Varied ornaments of flowers, designs drawn with mountain minerals,*
*yellow silk garments and so on — this is here called adornment.*

**987.** *Herding the herds of fine cows while roaming through all the forests,*
*the beginning of training in wrestling play, dancing and so on — such are the activities here."*

**988.** In middle pauganda, almost touching kaishora,
[He] sports here — His activities cannot be described.

**989.** *Thus [it is said] there, the twenty-fifth verse:*
*"The nose finely tipped and high; the cheeks round;*
*the sides and other limbs beautifully rounded — when middle pauganda comes.*

**990.** *Here the turban, with a cord of silk threads gleaming like lightning;*
*the staff, dark, three cubits tall, tipped with gold — such is the adornment.*

**991.** *Games at Bhandira, the lifting of the hill and so on — such are the activities."*

**992.** *There, the twenty-seventh verse:*
*"Already in middle pauganda this Hari, playing, shines*
*as if partaking of the beginning of kaishora, for His form is wondrous with sweetness."*

**993.** In the last pauganda [His] body is supremely beautiful;
[His] activities are wondrous: here He sports with His friends.

**994.** *Thus [it is said] there, the twenty-eighth verse:*
*"The braid whose tip hangs down to the hips, the luster of dark curling locks,*
*the height of the shoulders and so on — when the last pauganda comes.*[^265]

**995.** *The tilt of the turban, a lotus for play in the hand,*
*a vertical tilaka of Kashmir saffron and so on — this is here called adornment."*

**996.** *There, the twenty-ninth verse:*
*"Here: graceful turns of speech, the delight of whispering in the ear with the narma-sakhas,*
*praising to them the beauty of the girls of Gokula and so on — such are the activities."*[^266]

**997.** Kaishora is threefold: the beginning, the middle and the last;
in these forest pastimes [He] attracts the hearts of all.

**998.** *Thus [it is said] there, in the Dakshina-vibhaga, in the first lahari, the hundred and fifty-ninth and hundred and sixtieth verses:*
*"Although the pre-eminence of this kaishora is only in the ujjvala [rasa],*
*nevertheless, since it is generally fitting for all the rasas, it is illustrated here in order.*
*Kaishora is of three kinds: the beginning, the middle and the last."*[^267]

**999.** In the first kaishora — a radiant complexion, lovely beauty;
[He] sports in this kunja, [showing] many activities that captivate the mind.

**1000.** *Thus [it is said] there:*
*"A certain [indescribable] brilliance of complexion, a reddish glow at the corners of the eyes,*
*the appearance of the line of hair — when the first kaishora comes."*

**1001.** *There, the hundred and sixty-first verse:*
*"The vaijayanti garland, the peacock feather and so on, the dress of the best of dancers,*
*the sweetness of the flute, the beauty of the garments — such is the attire here."*

**1002.** *There, the hundred and sixty-second verse:*
*"Here the sharpness of the tips of the nails, the bow-like play of the brows,*
*the coloring of the teeth with coloring powders and so on — such are the activities."*

**1003.** In middle kaishora [He] sports in these kunjas;
His activities, which enchant Kandarpa, cannot be described.[^268]

**1004.** *Thus [it is said] there, the hundred and sixty-third verse:*
*"A certain [special] beauty of the thighs, the arms and also the chest,*
*the sweetness of the form and so on — when middle kaishora comes.*

**1005.** *The face rich with the play of smiles, the eyes trembling with playfulness,*
*song that enchants the three worlds and so on — such is the sweetness here.*

**1006.** *The unfolding of the very essence of artfulness, the great festival of play in the kunjas,*
*the beginning of the rasa-lila and so on — such is the beauty of activities and so on here."*

**1007.** The last kaishora is new youth;
[He] is absorbed in play in these kunjas, His activities are delightful.[^269]

**1008.** *Thus [it is said] there, the hundred and sixty-fourth verse:*
*"The limbs bear an excellence far greater even than before;*
*the appearance of the three folds and so on — when the last kaishora comes."*

**1009.** *There, the hundred and sixty-fifth and hundred and sixty-sixth verses:*
*"This very [age] the wise call Hari's new youth.*

**1010.** *Here: possession of the whole treasure of the feelings of the goddesses of Gokula,*
*unprecedented festivals of play according to the science of Kandarpa, and so on."*

**1011.** In these lovely places Krishna, full of rasa,
sports in the four kinds of the kaishora age.

**1012.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Ujjvala-nilamani, in the section on uddipanas, the fifth verse:*
*"There, in madhura-rasa, age is said to be of four kinds:*
*the transitional, and also the new, the manifest and the full — in that order."*[^270]

**1013.** Look: [here is] Karela-kunda, the forest of karil;
here Krishna, staying, gazes at the beauty.[^271]

**1014.** On Mount Nandishvara are the footprints of Krishna;
many see [their] power — so the elders say.

**1015.** Here is Madhusudana-kunda amid the flower groves;
here is Krishna's great joy: the bees hum.[^272]

**1016.** Look: Panihari-kunda, supremely pure;
at mealtime Krishna drinks this water.[^273]

**1017.** And here, look, Srinivasa, is the kitchen:
here Radha joyfully cooks together with Rohini.[^274]

**1018.** Here Krishna takes His meal with His friends;
after walking a hundred steps, He comes here and lies down [to rest].[^275]

**1019.** Sri Radhika, having tasted the remnants of Krishna's food,
goes and sits in this pleasant garden within the homestead.

**1020.** A friend, unnoticed by anyone, brings Krishna and unites [Them];
whatever merriment arises — who could find its end?

**1021.** Here Sri Yashoda, having dressed Rama and Krishna,
sends [Them] off to the forest — and her heart breaks.

**1022.** Amid Their friends, Rama and Krishna along this road
go to herd the cows — to what could that beauty be compared?

**1023.** Here Yashoda, taking Radha on her lap
and sending [Her] off to Javata, is bathed in tears.

**1024.** How much affection she has for Lalita and the other friends —
who could tell of it with a single mouth?

**1025.** Yashoda and Rohini, having sent off Radhika with Her friends,
cannot become calm.

**1026.** Look: here is the place where curd is churned;
and here, look — the boundless power of the goddess.[^276]

**1027.** Paurnamasi, having come and said much to Yashoda,
goes joyfully along this road to her own dwelling.

**1028.** Here, a little way off, in this secluded place, Vrinda-devi
ponders in her mind how to unite the two of Them.

**1029.** Having united the two of Them, she and her friends are bathed in happiness;
who would not proclaim such virtues of Vrinda?

**1030.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the thirty-first verse:*
*"She who, filled with a flood of prema, having adorned one after another*
*the ever-new kunjas with abundant fragrant flowers —*
*ah! — arranges there the festival of the pastimes of Radha and Krishna,*
*surrounded by Their dear ones — in that Vrinda I take refuge."*

**1031.** Here is Sahasi-kunda: here a friend, having inspired Krishna
with boldness, unites [Him] with Rai.[^277]

**1032.** Here, having made a wondrous swing on the branch of a tree,
Rai and Kanu swing with Their friends, overwhelmed with happiness.

**1033.** Here is Mukta-kunda: here the son of Nanda
made a field of pearls — and there was boundless merriment.[^278]

**1034.** O Srinivasa, this is Akrura's place;
when one tells of him, the heart breaks.[^279]

**1035.** Sent by Kamsa from Mathura, Akrura
was to take Rama and Krishna away to the city of Madhu.

**1036.** For this reason, coming here, he reflects within himself
and sees here the footprints of Krishna.

**1037.** Here Akrura was overwhelmed with prema;
this place of Akrura people call Krur.[^280]

**1038.** Look: [here is] Yogiya-sthana — here Uddhava
spoke of yoga in many ways.[^281]

**1039.** This is Udho-kriya-sthana: here Uddhava,
seeing the conduct of the gopis, considered himself blessed.[^282]

**1040.** Here Uddhava consoled Nanda and the others;
seeing [their] wondrous bhava, he lost his composure.

**1041.** For some days Uddhava stayed here;
by seeing this place all aims are fulfilled.

**1042.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the ninety-ninth verse:*
*"He who is always full of the rasa of prema, the servant and friend of the Enemy of Mura,*
*leaving His two feet, dearer to him than millions of his own lives,*
*lovingly lived here for ten months and with talk of Him again and again revived the cowherd settlement,*
*[saying,] 'Look, Krishna is coming!' — that Uddhava I carry on my head."*

**1043.** O Srinivasa, here Krishna with His friends
decides where they will go to herd the cows.

**1044.** Look, Srinivasa, all these are the places of the cowsheds;
here are Krishna's pastimes with the cowherds.

**1045.** Krishna with Subala and the others, with a joyful heart —
exceedingly beautiful [is He] going to this forest.

**1046.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (raga Dhanashri):*
"Today Kanu comes to the forest — Kusumabana embodied;
[His] body lovely as a dense rain-cloud, the posture of the best of dancers enchanting.
His moon-face smiles slightly, His eyes are a snare for the eyes of young women;
on His lips, [red as] the bimba, the playing of the flute enchants the minds of the three worlds.[^283]

**1047.** His locks are entwined with flowers — all around the bees and she-bees hum;
a crown made of a mass of peacock feathers, makara earrings swaying.
His restless eyes — a pair of khanjana birds — again and again run toward His ears;
on His neck shine kingly jewels, a pearl necklace swings.

**1048.** At His waist are yellow cloth and shining kinkini bells; His intoxicated gait surpasses the king of elephants;
a kadamba garland hangs to His knees, [over it] enraptured, intoxicated bees.
His lotus feet of reddish hue put to shame the rays of the young sun;
delighting the heart of Govindadasa, the lovely anklets sweetly ring."[^284]

**1049.** Look: the pegs to which the calves are tied —
the women of Vraja worship them with care even today.

**1050.** At Nandishvara there are many places of Krishna's pastimes;
it is impossible to tell which pastime was where.

**1051.** To the right and left of this parikrama path
there are many places of Krishna's pastimes — who can name them?

**1052.** Having seen a few places on all four sides of Nandishvara,
we will again go on further by this path.

**1053.** So saying, they set out from Nandagrama;
their joy grew as they looked on all four sides.

**1054.** [Raghava] says to Srinivasa: "There is no end to this beauty;
to the northwest of Nandishvara, look — Gendukhora.[^285]

**1055.** Here at Gendukhora, joyfully taking a ball,
Rama and Krishna with Their friends are absorbed in the rasa of play.

**1056.** Here, look, is the beautiful kadamba grove;
here Balarama sports in many delightful ways.

**1057.** Here Baladeva lay down,
and Krishna massaged His feet.

**1058.** *Thus [it is said] in the Purva-gopala-champu, in the twelfth purana, the forty-eighth verse, a song:*[^286]
*"Krishna plays around Rama, thirsting for the qualities of His friends' songs;*
*He sings after the song of the cuckoos and bees, chatters like the parrots and swans.*

**1059.** *Likewise, with ringing laughter, He imitates the chakravakas, chakoras, cranes and other [birds];*
*to frighten the little ones, He produces as it were the cry of beasts terrified by a leopard's jaws.*

**1060.** *Birds, beasts and others that make indistinct sounds — all of them He calls by names [He] has invented;*
*and if some friend is wandering there, by laughter He draws out his affection too.*

**1061.** *The animals that have strayed far He calls by name with a chant that delights the hearts of the cows and cowherds;*
*when He called the cows, the cry of the peacocks arose, for that [call] was like the rumbling of a cloud.*

**1062.** *Joining His hand with His brother's, He praises His friends' many good deeds and laughs;*
*together with His friends, giving rest to the Elder, He performs the service of caressing His feet.*

**1063.** *Having made a bed of tender shoots, resting His head firmly on a friend's thigh,*
*after the fatigue of play wishing to lie down — He whose body the most pious ones fan,*

**1064.** *whose feet here some [of His friends] caress, whose service leaves us only thirst —*[^287]
*He fell asleep to the songs and amusements of His loving [friends], delightful with melody.*

**1065.** *For us who remember this, something in the heart can wait for its time, but cannot bear any other state;*
*who are we here, only fancying ourselves thirsty, when the blessed ones headed by Shuka thirst for Him?"*

**1066.** *Thus [it is said] in the Srimad-Bhagavatam, in the Tenth Canto, in the fifteenth chapter, the fourteenth verse:*[^288]
*"Sometimes, when the Elder, tired by play, laid His head in a cowherd boy's lap,*
*[Krishna] Himself gave Him rest by massaging His feet and serving Him in other ways."*

**1067.** This is Gupta-kunda: here, in secret, are various delights;
Krishna wanders in the forest with Subala and the others.[^289]

**1068.** Here, look, is the village of Meheran — everyone knows it;
here is the cowshed of the cowherd Abhinanda.[^290]

**1069.** O Srinivasa, and here is another lovely place:
look, this is Yaograma, called Javata.[^291]

**1070.** All the places of pastimes in the village of Javata
are most wondrous; who can tell how many there are?

**1071.** Look, here is the dwelling of Abhimanyu;
here Rai enjoys Her pastimes with the sakhis.[^292]

**1072.** Abhimanyu, by the power of Sri Yogamaya,
cannot touch even Radhika's shadow — what to speak [of Her Herself].

**1073.** Abhimanyu stays in his own company of cows and cowherds;
Jatila and Kutila are always busy with household work.

**1074.** A clever sakhi, bringing Krishna here,
with delight in her heart beholds the pastimes of the Two.

**1075.** Having tricked Jatila, Kutila and Abhimanyu,
Krishna, coming right here, playfully enjoys His pastimes.

**1076.** Mukhara, seeing her granddaughter here, joyfully
speaks so many gentle words to Jatila.

**1077.** Here Kutila, greatly pleased,
plots how to disgrace Radhika.

**1078.** By this road Radhika goes to the temple of the Sun;
Krishna, staying in the kadamba grove, watches.

**1079.** Coming out onto the road, He pulls at Radhika's garment;
great is the fun of Rai and Kanu, the Two of Them.

**1080.** *Thus [it is said] in the Gitavali of the Stavamala (raga Dhanashri):*[^293]
*"Do not torment [me] here on the road,*
*seeing that I am a faithful wife, without protection.*

**1081.** *Restless one, let go of the edge of my veil:*
*I must now perform the worship of the Sun. (Refrain.)*

**1082.** *Do not delay, O hero of Gokula;*
*O moon-faced one, again and again I bow to you in entreaty.*

**1083.** *Alone with you I am afraid, seeing you*
*with the corners of your eyes darting about, O Sanatana-deva."*

**1084.** This is Krishna-kunda, surrounded by banyans and other trees;
here Sri Krishna's pastimes are exceedingly charming.

**1085.** This is Mukta-kunda: here in the hot season
a sakhi puts ornaments of pearls on Rai.

**1086.** This is Pivana-kunda [and] the stream in the kadamba grove;
here Radha and Krishna happily sport with the sakhis.[^294]

**1087.** Supremely playful Krishna, receiving a sign from a sakhi,
intoxicated, drinks the nectar of Radhika's lips.

**1088.** And this is Ladili-kunda: here Lalita
secretly arranges the meeting of Rai and Kanu.[^295]

**1089.** Look, O Srinivasa — Narada-kunda;
whoever bathes here has his desire fulfilled.

**1090.** Here the sage gave Radhika a boon —
She became Amrita-hasta, as everyone knows.[^296]

**1091.** Here Sri Radhika, standing with the sakhis,
watches Sri Krishna when He goes to herd the cows.

**1092.** With His friends, merrily playing the flute,
Krishna goes to herd the cows by this road.

**1093.** Krishna, the enchanter of the worlds, amid the cows and cowherds
unnoticed gives His eyes to Rai's eyes.

**1094.** *Thus [it is said] in a song:*
"The brightly shining cows of fierce majesty, honoured by the world — iya;
the restless dust from their hooves rose to the sky, delighting the hosts of gods — iya.[^297]

**1095.** He comes to the forest, delighting those who surrender [to Him]; [His] gait is lovely, putting the elephant to shame;
His softest body is like glossy collyrium; His eyes dance — young khanjana birds;
shattering the composure of beautiful women, He sports among the cowherds — iya.

**1096.** His clear, shining face surpasses a forest of blossoming white lotuses;
His lovely locks, like bees, ripple in a dark wave — iya.

**1097.** Ta ta thira mira kiti jhik jhik, jhankiti ta jhuk,
buddha jhik jhik jhik, tenati ai aiya.
Aiya! Shyamaghana, wondrously praised — iya. (Refrain.)[^298]

**1098.** The instruments sound, the singing is fine, the shrutis pure, the metre sweet in accord with the svaras;
hearing the sound of the flute, Radhika leaves the house together with a host of sakhis.

**1099.** She gazes at the charming attire of the best of dancers, Her unblinking eyes rejoicing;
Manasija is strong, Her body trembles, from trembling Her gait is very slow.[^299]

**1100.** Ta ta takuta takuta nakuta, taku thai ta, thai thai diga ta, thaita,
ta ta kititak, tho di kiti tak,
thunna dramakata jha jha kitijhak, jhankana jhankana krina krina.

**1101.** The corners of the eyes meet, the hearts of both are captured — who knows [this] wondrous attachment!
Great is the merriment in the lanes of Vraja; [all is] immersed in Shyamaghana, the ocean of beauty;
Shyamaghana sports — [all is] immersed [in Him].

**1102.** Diga diga thai thai thai thai thaita, dhidhi kata dhidhi kata ti, ai aiya;
jha kina kina jha, kina kina kina jha, jha kina kina jha,
jhankana jhankana krina krina krina."

**1103.** O Srinivasa, in this village of Javata
Krishna meets Radhika with great merriment.

**1104.** Radha's sister-in-law Kutila and mother-in-law Jatila
cannot perceive Krishna's boundless cunning.

**1105.** What to say — there is no end to all those joys;
the fortunate ones relish them in many ways.

**1106.** *Thus [it is said] in a song. Once (in any raga):*[^300]
The best of the best of nagaras, the stealer of Vraja's peace,
His heart joyful, full of the rasa [of love] for His beloved,
having made ready flowers, taking the guise of a malini,
enters the village of Javata.[^301]

**1107.** Looking at Himself again and again,
covering His face with the end of His cloth, smiling,
in a very sweet voice He calls at every house:
"Who will take a garland? Come, take one!"

**1108.** Hearing a voice that surpasses the cuckoo,
Vinodini says to Her sakhi Vishakha:
"It must be a wondrous garland; lest someone else take it,
quickly bring the malini here."[^302]

**1109.** Vishakha, hearing these words, in the greatest joy
says to the malini: "Hey, come here!"
Turning the malini back, she leads [Him] into the house
and lovingly says: "Sit right here."

**1110.** Looking at the malini, Radhika runs to meet [her]
and, delighted, thinks to Herself:
"Such a malini as this I have seen nowhere —
surely she must be a dweller of the city of the gods."

**1111.** Thinking so in Her heart, sitting beside the malini,
[She] says: "Come, show me your garland."
Hearing this, [the malini] shows the garland, which has no comparison:
by its beauty it enchants everyone's mind.

**1112.** Radhika, full of rasa, greatly
tormented by Madana, again thrills in Her heart.
Looking at the garland, She reflects in Her mind:
"Just so does my beloved string [garlands]."

**1113.** The beautiful one cannot be calm and says to the malini:
"I feel like giving you my life!
What can I say of your skill? Blessed, blessed are you!
Tell me what the price is."

**1114.** The malini says: "Listen, I will not say it again and again;
I never tell anyone an untruth.
I will put this garland on [you], and I will take that gaja-moti;
once [you] are adorned, whatever you give I will take afterwards."[^303]

**1115.** The young beauty speaks kind words to the malini:
"Whatever you wish — take it as you please."
Hearing this, [the malini], floating in rasa, with a slight smile, smiling,
puts the garland on Radhika's neck.

**1116.** With great care arranging [the garland]
over the lovely hills of Her breasts, covers [them] with a hand;
at the malini's touch, with delight in Her heart,
the young beauty at once trembles all over.

**1117.** Understanding [all], — [says] Narahari, — all the companions
stay at a distance, glad at heart.
In the secluded chamber, unable to remain calm,
the Two become intoxicated in the play of Ananga.

**1118.** *And once (raga Pauravi):*
The best of nagaras, the moon of Vraja, taking a fine woman's attire, smiling,
full of rasa, enters Javata.
Surpassing a rain-laden cloud, the radiance of each graceful limb [of His body]
[and] the beauty of the clothes and ornaments He wears steal the life-breath.

**1119.** Radhika, seeing Him from afar, cannot turn Her eyes away even for a moment
and says to Her sakhi, taking [her] by the hand:
"Where has this beautiful woman come from? Look, she has lit up everything with her beauty!
Bring her right here, with every courtesy."

**1120.** Hearing Vinodini's agitated words, the sakhi, knowing [Her] heart,
hurries to where that beautiful woman is coming;
cleverly going near, speaking the sweetest words,
she joyfully brings Him and leads Him with honour into the house.

**1121.** The beautiful woman joyfully comes near; the jewel among women, seating Him,
lovingly says: "I have never seen you.
Today I have found boundless happiness — I speak the truth, without guile.
On what business are you going alone, and where? Tell me."

**1122.** Hearing words like nectar, the beautiful woman, plunged in still greater happiness,
like a poor man who has found the greatest jewel,
looking at the lovely moon-face, with cunning in mind says:
"Listen: since you have asked, I must tell you something.

**1123.** With great eagerness, as my heart desired, I learned all the art of adornment;
I put endless effort into it, young though I am.
To display all this I wander through this town of Vraja,
but I find no expert who could judge such skill.

**1124.** And then a woman said to me: 'In vain do you wander through the town:
here [lives] the wife of Abhimanyu, supremely skilful.
What is Rama [beside her] in beauty and virtues? In the world there is none equal to her;
by the touch of her feet the earth counts itself blessed.[^304]

**1125.** There are many heroines here — how much could I tell of them! —
who cannot keep Him in their power even for a moment.
But that moon Shyama, the best of the accomplished, above whom there is no one,
subdued by her prema, constantly roams [after her].

**1126.** Go there, heed [my] word: there your skill will be honored.'
Hearing this, I was greatly delighted at heart.
Why should I repeat her words to you? I came, heeding what she said;
just as she told [me], so I have found [it]."

**1127.** Hearing these words, beautiful Rai, feeling great joy at heart,
says: "Dress me, dear, regarding [me] as your own."
Having received [this] kindly permission, [the guest] eagerly rises and sits beside [Her];
a maidservant brings all the articles of adornment.

**1128.** Carefully, keeping [her] composure, the lovely one, gazing at the sweet charm of [Rai's] back,
braids the plait — [like] a serpent, at the sight [of which] the sages lose their senses.
With a heart delighted by the rasa of touch, applying lovely kajal to [Her] eyes,
with the end of the sari [she] lovingly wipes [Her] face — [and it] shines all the more.

**1129.** Placing lovely champaka [flowers] on [Her] ears, [the guest] deems herself blessed;
applying sindur in the parting of the hair, [she] paints designs on [Her] forehead.
Putting a beshar pendant in [Her] nose and swinging it, [she] speaks sweet words:
"There is no comparison [for You]: at this Madana will surely swoon."

**1130.** As [she] places a lovely dot of musk on [Her] chin, an ocean of bliss surges up;
seeing this, [she] cannot turn away [her] unblinking eyes.
Touching the most lovely breasts, [she] puts on [Her] bodice — and [her] hands tremble:
the Holder of the mountain cannot keep even a trace of composure.

**1131.** The incomparable body quivers again and again; tenderly [the guest] presses [her] face to that face;
seeing this, a sakhi, putting a finger to her chin, says:
"What an unheard-of wonder! Being a woman, with a woman
you do such a thing — how ever can you [do it]?

**1132.** A wondrous attire you have made! What shall I say to my own friend?
Without discerning, she takes anyone at all as her own.
It is well that no one is here — otherwise this would be most shameful.
To whom shall I tell this sorrow? She never heeds any prohibition."

**1133.** Hearing this, smiling Rai, abashed, looking at Shyama,
says: "Oh, you rogue! Who would not laugh at this?"
The Nagara says: "Do what is proper: quickly bind [me] in the noose of your arms —
then Ghanashyama will float in the ocean of happiness."[^305]

**1134.** *And at another time — raga Gauri:*[^306]
Shyama, the best of handsome nagaras, the giver of joy, having devised a plan with Kundalata,[^307]
takes on the wondrous guise of a woman, joyful in the hope of seeing Rai.
Virtuous Kundalata, sent by Yashoda, goes to Javata with incomparable gait;
with her goes the Beautiful One, carrying in lovely hands a plate with some articles.

**1135.** Entering Javata, seeing Jatila, with a joyful heart [Kundalata] bows at her feet;
taking [her] by the hand, Abhimanyu's mother speaks so many sweet words of every kind.
Kundalata, showing her cleverness there, sitting close by, displays the articles,
