conveys Yashomati's words and, having received permission, goes to where Rai is staying.

**1136.** Rasavati, filled with great joy, smiling and looking at Kundalata,
in so many ways playfully, lovingly taking [her] by the hand, seats [her] beside [Her].
Having carefully asked about the beloved of [Her] life, Rai, glancing at that [guest], speaks again:
"Where did you find this new merry girl? And why has she come with you?"

**1137.** Hearing, Kundalata, floating in bliss, says: "[She] lives in our neighborhood,
this young wife, very dear to me; [who she is], I will tell later.
Hearing from my lips the account of your virtues, every day she longs to come here;
seeing how she pines, I brought [her] today; regard [her] as your own.

**1138.** With many virtues the Creator has fashioned her — to whom in the world could I compare her?
She always keeps very much to herself, is skilled in her own work, of lovely conduct.
What more shall I say of her cleverness: her touch drives away the pains of the body and the rest.
O blissful one, among your sakhis no one has such soft hands."

**1139.** Hearing, Vinodini, her heart delighted, wished [that one] to touch [Her] body;
understanding [this], Kundalata, making a sign to the dark young wife,[^308] says with a slight smile:
"The wish that was in your heart is fulfilled: Radha has accepted you as her own;
so [now] with the lovely lotuses of your hands press [Her] feet and pour forth floods of nectar."

**1140.** Hearing [these] words, feeling great happiness at heart, gazing to the full at the face as if smeared with nectar,
[the guest], sitting beside the couch, smiles softly and, overflowing with rasa, touches [Her] feet.
Startled, Rai's body trembles restlessly — like a golden creeper in the wind, like lightning;
displaying incomparable skill, smiling, the moon Shyama cannot remain still.

**1141.** Wondrous: each of the Two captivates the other's heart; in the play of prema the beauty of both grows manifold;
there is no end to new embraces, frequent kisses; abundant thrills fill [Their] limbs.
Far off, the sakhis, with great happiness at heart, smiling, keep their faces covered with their cloth;
exchanging signs from the corners of their eyes, [they] tease Kundalata.

**1142.** Choosing the moment, Kundalata again, smiling, coming there to Vinodini's side,
looking at Shyama, says to Rai: "What an unseemly thing you have done!
You embraced the young wife as if she were your sweetheart — I do not know what she will think.
Had I known you would act like this, I would not have brought her."

**1143.** Rai playfully, with lowered face, says: "I understand: having bidden farewell to shame for good,
dressing up your own brother-in-law like this, you take him along and enjoy yourself every day.[^309]
For so many days this was a secret; be that as it may, now it has come to light."
Thus the two say so much; hearing it, Ghanashyama's mind is utterly immersed [in bliss].

**1144.** *Again, raga Gauri:*
Shyama, the handsome nagara, the ocean of rasa,
overwhelmed with the hope of seeing Rai,
seeing the moonrise, taking on the guise of a brahmana,
went to Javata, to Jatila.

**1145.** Seeing the excellent brahmana, joining both palms,
bowing to Him, Jatila says:
"Today I deem myself blessed; [I wish to] hear your words —
tell [me], why have you come to a cowherd's house?"

**1146.** Hearing, the Dvijaraja says: "I have a purpose:[^310]
having worshiped the moon, today I have eaten nothing.
Knowing your daughter-in-law to be devoted to her husband,
I have come to receive something from her hand."

**1147.** Hearing this, Jatila, delighted,
speaks sweet words to Vishakha:
"Take [him] to where Radha is;
whatever he asks, let her give it, deeming [it] a meritorious deed.

**1148.** Tell her, joining her palms and clasping [his] feet,
to receive [his] blessing.
Misfortune will go, good fortune will come —
cows and wealth — by the boon of this brahmana."

**1149.** Hearing this, taking the brahmana along,
[Vishakha] came to where the jewel of women [was];
she conveyed the words of the mother-in-law —
hearing them, [Rai] felt supreme joy.

**1150.** Seating the brahmana on a wonderful seat,
bowing, speaking humble words,
curd, milk, ghee and all such things
[She] carefully brought before [Him].

**1151.** The brahmana, looking again and again at Rai,
says to Vishakha: "Listen, sakhi!
The moon whom I worship every day in so many ways —
that moon I see in her face.

**1152.** I have found [it] close at hand — how could I neglect [it]?
First I must drink the nectar."
Saying this, smiling, floating in the rasa of prema,
[He] kissed the moon of Rai's face.

**1153.** Vinodini says: "What is this you are doing, hey!
You are a brahmana — why do you act like this?"
The brahmana says: "My hunger and the sorrow of my heart are gone —
I find happiness in drinking the nectar of [your] lips."

**1154.** Rasavati in anger says to Vishakha:
"I do not understand this game of yours."
Vishakhika replies: "I have understood:
[this is] an unearthly pastime of mother-in-law and daughter-in-law!"

**1155.** Hearing, the Moon-faced One laughs with lowered eyes;
seeing this, Ghanashyama, the Beloved, smiling,
taking Rai on His lap, trembles all over —
how He floats in that play of Ananga!

**1156.** What shall I say, O Srinivasa, O Narottama!
Krishna's charming pastimes are hard to attain even for Brahma.

**1157.** Krishna, the embodiment of rasa, knows all languages;
He is most skilled at imitating the calls of cuckoos and other [birds].

**1158.** By means of agreed signals He meets [Radha] in Abhimanyu's house;
[but] by the will of fate on some days the meeting does not happen.

**1159.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padyavali:*
*"The enemy of Kamsa gave the agreed signal — the call of a cuckoo and other [birds] —*
*and again and again heard the jingling of the conch-shell bangles, trembling as the door was being opened;*
*[but] His heart was tormented by the words of the bold old woman, 'Who is it? Who is it?' —*
*and [so His] night passed in the shelter of the koli tree in the corner of Radha's courtyard."*[^311]

**1160.** Krishna, the great jester, full of supreme bliss,
for the good fortune of the cuckoos meets [Her] by means of that call.

**1161.** To the west of Javata is this charming forest;
hundreds of thousands of cuckoos call [here] without ceasing.

**1162.** One day Krishna, coming to this forest,
joyfully makes a call like [that of] a cuckoo —

**1163.** sweeter than [the voice of] all the cuckoos;
whoever hears it even once, his composure goes far away.

**1164.** Jatila kindly says to Vishakha:
"Never have I heard such a cuckoo's call."

**1165.** Vishakha says: "This is in the minds of all of us:
if you allow, we will go to the forest and see this cuckoo."

**1166.** The old woman says, "Go." Hearing [this], with boundless joy
Rai entered the forest together with the sakhis.

**1167.** There was great merriment, there was no limit to happiness;
all, coming, met in one place.

**1168.** By the cuckoo's call Krishna unites with Radhika —
therefore this [place] is called Kokilavana.[^312]

**1169.** O Srinivasa, look: [here is] the village of Ajanak;
here the pastimes of Radha and Krishna are incomparable.[^313]

**1170.** Sri Radhika was dressing in seclusion;
She was adorned with many jewels and [other] ornaments.

**1171.** Having arranged Her hair and the rest, [She was about] to apply anjana,
[when] suddenly the sound of the flute entered [Her] ears.

**1172.** That very moment Sri Radhika, together with the sakhis,
coming here, met Krishna with great delight.

**1173.** Going forward [and] leading [Her] in, Krishna was overwhelmed
[and] seated [Her] on a flower seat made by Vrinda.

**1174.** He sees the beauty of [Her] limbs, [but] does not see anjana in [Her] eyes;
when He asked, the sakhis told what had happened.

**1175.** Overcome by rasa, Krishna, taking anjana,
with great joy applied [it] to Radhika's eyes.

**1176.** On the pretext of the anjana there was much joking;
therefore the name of this place became Ajanak.

**1177.** This is the village of Vidyudvari; [people] call [it] Bijoari;
who would not melt on hearing the story of this village?[^314]

**1178.** O Srinivasa, when Akrura came to Vraja,
this cry arose: "He has come to take Rama and Krishna!"

**1179.** Having joyfully spent the night in Nanda's house,
at dawn [Akrura] sets out for Mathura together with Nanda and the others.

**1180.** Vraja became empty with the departure of Rama and Krishna;
what is there to say — whoever saw it, he [alone] knows.

**1181.** The women of Vraja run to see Krishna;
tears stream from their eyes like the current of a river.

**1182.** At the sight of that state wood and stone split apart;
no one could describe it even with hundreds of thousands of mouths.

**1183.** On all sides Krishna's beloveds are distraught;
and here Krishna mounted the chariot.

**1184.** The gopis fixed their eyes on the lotus of Krishna's face
and, crying, "Alas, alas, Lord of [our] life!", fell senseless.

**1185.** As a mass of steady lightning falls from the sky,
so the gopis fall to the earth.

**1186.** Everyone took [them] for a mass of lightning —
for this reason [the village] has the name Bijoari.

**1187.** Look: ahead is the village named Parasho;
how it got the name Parasho, I will tell briefly.[^315]

**1188.** Mounting the chariot, Krishna set out for Mathura;
seeing the state of the gopis, He became distraught.

**1189.** Through a messenger He said, swearing an oath:
"Tomorrow or the day after I will come and meet [you]."

**1190.** For this reason its name became Parasho;
I do not know how to tell what the gopis went through.

**1191.** Near Parasho is this village named Shi;
I will tell briefly how the name Shi came about.[^316]

**1192.** Here Krishnachandra cannot keep His composure;
seeing the state of the gopis, He says again and again:

**1193.** "From Mathura I will come [back] soon."
For this reason [from "shighra," "soon"] everyone calls [it] Shi.

**1194.** Mounting the chariot, Krishnachandra goes to Mathura;
without Krishna the gopis became all but lifeless.

**1195.** Together with the collyrium of the eyes of countless gopis,
the tears of their eyes, flowing down their breasts, fall to the earth.

**1196.** Merging together, the water flows like a river;
all say: "This is a stream of the Yamuna."

**1197.** This is the place full of the gopis' tears of prema;
O Srinivasa, the fortunate one sees it.

**1198.** Look: here are two villages, Kamai and Karala;[^317]
in the village of Kamai Vishakha was born.

**1199.** Here in the village of Karala is Lalita's place;
it is known in Vraja that she also lived in the village of Ludhauni.

**1200.** In this village of Karala resides Chandravali,
whose husband is Govardhana, the son of Karala.[^318]

**1201.** Her father is Chandrabhanu, her mother Indumati;
Chandravali is the elder sister of Radhika.[^319]

**1202.** Sri Chandravali's father is [one of] five brothers born of one mother;
the eldest of all is Vrishabhanu, the best of kings.

**1203.** Chandrabhanu, Ratnabhanu, Subhanu, Shribhanu —
in order; the splendour of these five is as if like the sun's.

**1204.** Govardhana Malla together with Chandravali
[lives] in the village of Sakhisthali, and sometimes stays in Karala.

**1205.** Padma and the other group leaders live in this place;[^320]
how they meet Krishna — there is no end to that fun.

**1206.** There, in the village of Piyaso, Krishna became thirsty;[^321]
Baladeva brought water and gave Krishna to drink.

**1207.** In this village of Sahara is the dwelling of Upananda,[^322]
advanced in years, very wise in counsel.

**1208.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the sixteenth verse:*
*"He whose face is beautiful with an abundance of white beard, dark, skilful, versed in counsel,*
*who always has his place in the assembly of the lord of Vraja and is honoured,*
*who would gladden the Slayer of Mura, his brother's son, at the cost of hundreds of millions of his own lives —*
*dwelling in Sahara, he who is called Upananda, may he always protect the cowherd settlement."*

**1209.** Wonderful is the affection that the cowherd Upananda shows;
his son Subhadra is Krishna's elder brother.[^323]

**1210.** He is dear to Sri Nanda; his virtues cannot be told;
he is very learned, boundlessly affectionate to Krishna.

**1211.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place, in the Vraja-vilasa, the seventeenth verse:*
*"Dark, of subtle mind, exceedingly sweet [in speech], foremost among the knowers of the heavenly bodies,*[^324]
*who by his learning has surpassed the Lord of Speech, who has his place at the left hand of the lord of Vraja,*
*who here lovingly protects Krishna completely, even with hundreds of millions of lives,*
*and with counsel — him, the son of Upananda, Subhadra, we here praise with love."*

**1212.** The wife of Subhadra is named Kundalata;
Krishna is the life of her, Radhika's sakhi.[^325]

**1213.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place, in the Vraja-vilasa, the thirty-second verse:*
*"She who, most charming with abundant friendship adorned with jests,*
*by order of the queen of the lord of Vraja brings Radha for the cooking,*
*and, all along the way, constantly and lovingly delighting [Her] with news of Hari,*
*is herself supremely pleased — her, the Lata whose name begins with Kunda, I worship here."*

**1214.** The bliss that is in the village of Sahara day and night —
who has the power to tell of it in detail?

**1215.** Look at this village named Sakhi: here[^326]
Krishna Himself slew the wicked Shankhachuda.

**1216.** The jewel that was on Shankhachuda's head [He] took,
and, coming to Baladeva, joyfully gave [it to him].

**1217.** A little way from here, where Balarama was,
there is Rama-kunda; now its name is Rama-talao.[^327]

**1218.** Baladeva, through Madhumangala, gave the jewel
to Radhika — there was great fun in that.[^328]

**1219.** O Srinivasa, Narottama! Here
Rai with Her sakhis was overwhelmed with fun.

**1220.** In Chhatravana the friends, having made Krishna king,
by the power of the king's command rule everywhere.

**1221.** Madhumangala and all the others, with bold words,
roam from forest to forest, proclaiming Krishna's name:

**1222.** "The great king, the Chhatrapati, is Nanda's son;[^329]
in his kingdom no one else has any authority.

**1223.** If anyone comes here to gather flowers,
we will punish him, taking him to the king."

**1224.** Lalita and the other sakhis say angrily again and again:
"Who claims authority in Radhika's kingdom?"

**1225.** Saying many such things, Lalita and the other sakhis
at that very moment made Radhika umrao.[^330]

**1226.** Sitting on a throne fit for an umrao, Rai,
looking in all four directions, says to the sakhis:

**1227.** "Whoever claims authority in my kingdom —
defeat him and bring him here this very moment."

**1228.** Hearing this, they arm themselves and go to fight,
taking in their hands flower staffs made by Vrinda.

**1229.** Thousands upon thousands of sakhis go in all four directions;
Subala and the other friends see it from afar.

**1230.** Without saying anything to Madhumangala, they ran away;[^331]
some sakhi went and seized Madhumangala.

**1231.** With a flower garland they bound his hands
and quickly brought him to the umrao.

**1232.** Seeing Madhumangala, she says again and again:
"In whose kingdom are you establishing whose authority?

**1233.** Together with all of you I will punish that king,
so that he never again does such a thing."

**1234.** Hearing this, Madhu says, bowing his head:
"Punish me in such a way that my belly is filled."

**1235.** The umrao says: "This brahmana who seeks only his belly —
release him; let him go into the king's presence."

**1236.** The sakhis released Madhumangala;
still bound, Madhu ran off.

**1237.** The king, sitting in great pride on the royal throne,
says to Madhumangala: "Why are you in such a state?"

**1238.** Dejected, Madhu says again and again:
"I made You king — this is the fruit of it.

**1239.** She is the umrao, Her power is boundless;
what authority will You establish in Her kingdom?

**1240.** That Kandarpa who steals the world's treasure of composure —
that Kandarpa trembles at the play of Her eyes.

**1241.** Therefore accept my counsel:
offering Yourself, take refuge in Her."

**1242.** Krishna says: "Madhu, what you have said is above all;
that they bound you — that pain I cannot bear."

**1243.** Madhu says: "I want only Your welfare;
even if I am dishonoured, there is no sorrow."

**1244.** Saying this, pulling Krishna by the hand,
he comes that very moment to Radhika.

**1245.** Seeing the coming of the Lord of Her life, Rai in happiness
lost Her composure — there is no limit to Her shyness.

**1246.** Rai wants to take off the umrao's attire;
a sakhi says: "In this attire you will stay here."

**1247.** Seeing Radhika in such attire from afar, Krishna
became agitated and cannot keep His composure.

**1248.** Seeing Krishna's state, Madhu, with joy in his heart,
quickly brought Krishna to Radhika.

**1249.** He seated Krishna at Radhika's right side;
Rai at Krishna's left — what wonderful beauty appeared!

**1250.** Madhu says to Radhika again and again:
"Now take possession of the kingdom of Krishna's body.[^332]

**1251.** The jewel of an embrace that Krishna will give —
that is the tribute to you; accept it with care."

**1252.** Hearing Madhu's words, Lalita, laughing happily,
put a sweet, a modaka, into Madhumangala's mouth.

**1253.** Madhu says: "You committed an offence — you bound me;
feed me a hundred thousand such laddus, and the offence will go."

**1254.** Saying this, he eats the modaka with comic gestures
and gazes at the beauty of the two of Them, surrounded by the sakhis.

**1255.** Having eaten the modaka, in very sweet words
saying, "I have much to do," he goes off joyfully.

**1256.** The umrao and the king — both entered
the nikunja house with very joyful hearts.

**1257.** In the battle of love both became tired;
with various amusements the sakhis removed [Their] fatigue.

**1258.** O Srinivasa, what more is there to say of this play?
For this reason this village is called Umrao.

**1259.** Exceedingly dear to the daughter of Vrishabhanu
is this Kishori-kunda, always full of beauty.[^333]

**1260.** Seeing this wonderful forest, with great joy in his heart,
Lokanatha Gosvami lived here.

**1261.** His renunciation — there is no end to telling of it;
Sri Radha-vinoda showed [him] mercy at this place.

**1262.** Fruits, roots, greens, food — whatever was obtained at any time
he would carefully offer to Sri Radha-vinoda.

**1263.** In the rains, the cold and so on, he lived under this tree;
with him a worn patched quilt and a very worn outer cloth.

**1264.** He himself would get soaked by the water of heavy rains,
but he kept the Deity in the hollow of this tree.

**1265.** At other times, placing [Him] in a worn bag,
he kept [Him] at his chest with a very joyful heart.

**1266.** Remembering the pastimes of Sri Gaurachandra,
he would become distraught and weep here.

**1267.** Having said many such things, he cannot hold himself back:
Raghava Pandita swims in the tears of his eyes.

**1268.** Srinivasa and Narottama roll in the dust,
heave long sighs, float in streams of tears.

**1269.** After some time Sri Pandita, becoming calm
and calming the two, goes on ahead, taking them both.

**1270.** The Pandita says: this village is Nari-Semari;[^334]
Shyamari-Kinnari is the former name of this village.

**1271.** Seeing no way to break Radhika's sulking (mana),
here Sri Krishna became Shyama-sakhi.[^335]

**1272.** Playing the vina, [He] came here;
Sri Radhika says: "This is a kinnari in every way."[^336]

**1273.** Hearing the vina's playing, Rai was overwhelmed
and put Her own jewel necklace around her neck.

**1274.** The kinnari says: "Give Me the jewel of your sulking;
be gracious and make Me your own."

**1275.** Hearing these words, Rai smiles gently, gently;
Her mana vanished — She was immersed in delight.

**1276.** In this way these two villages received their names;
here the power of this Devi is boundless.[^337]

**1277.** O Srinivasa, ahead, look — Chhatravana;
here the son of the lord of Vraja became king.[^338]

**1278.** When Krishna had become king, after some days Paurnamasi,
floating in happiness, performed Radhika's anointing [to the throne].

**1279.** The anointing of Radha, queen of Vrindavana, at the place Radha-sthali —
who could tell what merriment [there was]?[^339]

**1280.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the sixty-first verse:*
*"Where, together with the hosts of rivers headed by Manasi-ganga, bearing an abundance of colors,*
*and with the hosts of goddesses headed by Savitri, at the heavenly voice of the Creator,*
*Sri Paurnamasi, for the supreme kingdom of Vrindavana, joyfully*
*abundantly sprinkled Radha — may that Radha-sthali sprinkle us with happiness."*[^340]

**1281.** Look: Khadiravana, known to the world;
merely by coming here one attains Vishnu-loka.[^341]

**1282.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The seventh forest on earth is Khadira, renowned in the world;*
*having gone there, O auspicious one, a man goes to My world."*

**1283.** O Srinivasa, look: here Krishna
with His friends, while herding the cows, plays many games.

**1284.** Look: Sangama-kunda, exceedingly charming;
here is the sweet meeting of the gopis with Krishna.[^342]

**1285.** Here it is supremely secluded; here Lokanatha
from time to time lived happily together with Bhugarbha.

**1286.** Here is this Kadamba-khandi — enchanting is its beauty;
here the young son of the lord of Vraja performs wonderful pastimes.[^343]

**1287.** This is the village of Bakathara, near Javata;
here Krishna killed the demon Baka.[^344]

**1288.** This is the place Neochhak — look, Srinivasa;
here Sri Krishna enjoys His meal.

**1289.** In Vraja, eatables are called by the word "chhak";
Krishna will eat — therefore Yashoda sends [them here].

**1290.** And all the mothers of the cowherd boys,
all of them send eatables to this forest.

**1291.** This is the village of Bhandagora — look, Srinivasa;
here are Sri Krishna's most wonderful pastimes.

**1292.** Now people call this village Bhadali;
bathing and so on in this kunda grants all perfections.

**1293.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Next there is My secret [place] known as Bhandagora;*
*people, O Earth, attain perfection there — of this there is no doubt.*

**1294.** *There, O fortunate one, is a kunda surrounded by trees, shrubs and creepers;*
*the man who, having fasted day and night, bathes there,*

**1295.** *having gone to the world of the Vidyadharas, rejoices — this is firmly established.*
*There, O Earth, I shall tell of a wonder — My supreme secret:*

**1296.** *on the twenty-four dvadashis devotion to Me is established [there];*
*at midnight [people] hear singing that delights the ear."*[^345]

**1297.** Having said this and shown many other places,
he, full of joy, came back to Nandishvara.

**1298.** Having told Srinivasa a little about the deeds of Nanda and others,
he stood beside Sri Pavana-sarovara.

**1299.** Seeing the hut of Sanatana Gosvami,
he lost his composure: tears pour from his eyes.

**1300.** Raghava Pandita says to Srinivasa:
"I shall tell a little of how the Gosvami lived here.

**1301.** Coming from Vrindavana to this secluded forest,
he, always overwhelmed by prema, worshipped Krishna.

**1302.** He lived in secret, with no concern for food;
no one knew who was living in this place.

**1303.** Krishna, in the guise of a cowherd boy, taking milk,
stood before the Gosvami, full of joy.

**1304.** In the dress of a herder of cows, a turban gracing His head,
holding a pot of milk in His hand, He says to the Gosvami:

**1305.** 'You live in seclusion, no one knows you;
I saw you when I came to herd the cows.

**1306.** Drink this milk, as I tell you;
I will take the pot — leave it here.

**1307.** If you live in a hut, all of us will be happy;
but you live like this — because of it the Vrajavasis will grieve.'

**1308.** Having said this, Gopala went away;
Sanatana, enchanted, drank the milk.

**1309.** As soon as he drank the milk, he lost his composure in prema;
drenched in tears, he lamented greatly.

**1310.** The Lord, remaining unseen, consoled Sanatana
and through the Vrajavasis had a hut built.

**1311.** Thus Sanatana came to have a dwelling;
from time to time Sri Rupa too stayed here.

**1312.** One day Sri Rupa Gosvami
resolved to feed Sanatana milk-rice and other things.

**1313.** Having resolved this, he again felt hesitant;
Radhika came to know what was in Sri Rupa's heart.

**1314.** Taking ghee, milk, rice, sugar and other things,
She came in the guise of a cowherd girl, full of joy.

**1315.** She says to Rupa: 'Svami, take all this;
quickly cook it, offer it to Krishna and eat.

**1316.** My mother told me to say these words:
let there never be any hesitation in your heart.'

**1317.** Having said this, Sri Radhika playfully went away;
Sri Rupa Gosvami happily and quickly cooked [the meal].

**1318.** Having offered it to Krishna, he serves [the meal]
to Gosvami Sanatana with a heart full of supreme bliss.

**1319.** Sanatana Gosvami, from the fragrance of the dishes,
does not know how much happiness arises in his heart.

**1320.** Putting one or two mouthfuls into his mouth, Sanatana
lost his composure: his tears cannot be stopped.

**1321.** Sanatana asked where the dishes had come from;
Sri Rupa told him everything in order.

**1322.** Hearing it, the Gosvami forbids him again and again:
'Do not trouble like this over eatables any more.'

**1323.** Having said this, he honored the Sri maha-prasada;
Sri Rupa Gosvami became deeply grieved.

**1324.** Sri Radhika, giving [him] Her darshan in a dream,
consoled Sri Rupa; Sanatana came to know [of it].

**1325.** O Srinivasa, what Sri Rupa's composure was like —
[this] wonder became manifest in the assembly of the Vaishnavas.

**1326.** One day, during talk of the separation of Radha and Krishna,
the Vaishnavas weep, falling senseless to the ground.

**1327.** Rupa's heart blazes like a tongue of flame,
and yet outwardly nothing shows.

**1328.** Sri Rupa's breath touched someone's body —
and blisters rose on his body, as if burnt by fire.

**1329.** Seeing this, everyone was astonished;
such are Sri Rupa's deeds — what more is there to say?

**1330.** What can I say — how much happiness there is in this Nandishvara!"
Having said this, he goes to the Gosvami's holy hut.

**1331.** There is the brahmana Sri Gopala Mishra, of noble conduct,
the son of Sanatana Gosvami's priest,

**1332.** a disciple of Sri Sanatana, fine in every respect;
seeing them all, his heart rejoiced.

**1333.** Sri Uddhava Dasa, Madhava and all who were there,
meeting one another, all became greatly joyful.[^346]

**1334.** The Vrajavasis, with jubilant hearts,
at once had eatables brought.

**1335.** That day there was a great festival there;
all spent the night in nama-sankirtana.

**1336.** Whoever hears such a wonderful account
will soon obtain the treasure of prema for Krishna.

**1337.** Sri Gopala Dasa and all the other wise ones —
the hearts of all were filled with great joy.

**1338.** Sri Raghava, taking the two, at daybreak,
having taken leave, goes along the path of the parikrama.

**1339.** Sri Pandita says to Srinivasa and Narottama:
ahead, look — this is the village of Baithan.[^347]

**1340.** Whenever the cowherds hold counsel,
they all come here and sit down.

**1341.** Here the cowherds sit — therefore this is Baithan;
now people call [it] by two names: Little and Big.

**1342.** Bound by the love of the Vrajavasis, with a joyful heart
Sanatana Gosvami lived here.

**1343.** How he lived here — of those beautiful deeds
I shall tell a little: by them the world is purified.

**1344.** These Vrajavasis constantly
hold Sanatana Gosvami dearer than life.

**1345.** When the Gosani makes the parikrama of Vraja,
he stays in village after village — there is no limit to that happiness.

**1346.** When, having stayed in one village, he goes to another,
the people of the village run after the Gosvami.

**1347.** Whether children or old people — no one can keep composure;
in separation from the Gosvami everyone weeps.

**1348.** Sanatana Gosvami, weeping too,
consoles them and sends them back to their homes.

**1349.** When all, restraining their weeping, have gone to their homes,
then Sanatana quickly goes on to another village.

**1350.** Whatever village he was heading for, the people of that village
see from afar Sanatana coming.

**1351.** Children, old people, young men, women and men —
all say, "Look, here is Rupa-Sanatana!"[^348]

**1352.** Wondrous is the love of the Vrajavasis:
even on seeing Rupa they say, "Rupa-Sanatana."

**1353.** None of the village folk can stay still:
they go out ahead to bring Sanatana in.

**1354.** As a poor man is happy on obtaining many jewels,
so is everyone happy on seeing Sanatana.

**1355.** All the very old and the old, women and men,
cherish Sanatana as a son.

**1356.** Someone says, "Ah, son, having forgotten us,
how have you lived? Where [were you]? We were dying with worry over it."

**1357.** Speaking thus, all gaze at Sanatana's face,
perform nirmanchhana with their own selves and feel great happiness at heart.

**1358.** Women, men and young people who were born in that village —
all of them, in the feeling of brothers, are overwhelmed with love.

**1359.** Someone says, "Brother, how have you lived?
It seems you never once remembered us.

**1360.** Why, brother, have you become so heartless towards us?"
So they say many things, and streams of tears flow from their eyes.

**1361.** Girls and boys come up to touch his feet;
he holds them all back, but cannot hold them back.

**1362.** The young wives of the village, staying a little way off,
all look at him, shyly.

**1363.** O Srinivasa, at the sight of Sanatana
no one even remembers to offer obeisances and the like.

**1364.** As he enters the village, all who come running to him
embrace him tightly and lead him along, holding his hand.

**1365.** Beneath a beautiful tree all, with joy in their hearts,
seat Sanatana and sit around him on all four sides.

**1366.** Curd, milk, fresh butter and other things from their homes
all bring with care to feed Sanatana.

**1367.** Having finished the merry meal after a while,
everyone, settled and calm, sits happily.

**1368.** Sanatana Gosvami, in deep affection,
asks everyone about their welfare in every respect:

**1369.** how many daughters and sons each has, where they are married,
what each one's name is, how the elders are, whether they live free from fear;

**1370.** how many cows, bulls and so on each has, how each one's farming goes,
how much grain is in whose house, how their affairs stand;

**1371.** how each one's health is, what each one's state of mind.
When he asks thus, everyone becomes very glad.

**1372.** One by one all tell the Gosvami everything;
hearing of anyone's sorrow, he himself becomes deeply sorrowful.

**1373.** Sanatana's consolation destroys their sorrow;
in such talks the night turns to dawn.

**1374.** In the morning Sanatana quickly performs his morning duties;
while he is bathing and so on, everyone comes.

**1375.** All quickly bring curd, milk and so on
and ask Sanatana Gosvami to eat.

**1376.** Sri Gosvami eats, having fed everyone,
and joyfully looks at how beautiful they all are.

**1377.** As before, when he goes from the village,
the Vrajavasis weep in distress.

**1378.** How they show their love — there is no end to telling it;
the Gosani consoles them all in many ways.

**1379.** When all go along with him some distance,
he adjures them by himself to make them turn back.

**1380.** Thus, going round from village to village,
Sanatana comes to the village of Baithan.

**1381.** Seeing Sanatana, all the people of the village —
in what bliss they were immersed, who can tell, and how much?

**1382.** Sanatana asks everyone about their welfare
and spends days and nights with joy in his heart.

**1383.** To stay [in a village] one night — that is his rule, everyone knows it;
therefore, in distress, they say to Sanatana:

**1384.** "If you stay a few days, it will be good for everyone.
Heed our words, do not be heartless.

**1385.** In the morning you will leave — that is your rule.
Give up this rule, save everyone's life."

**1386.** Thus the villagers say many things, weeping,
and for this reason he stayed here, giving happiness to all.

**1387.** The people of Baithan village and all [the villages] nearby —
all are constantly immersed in Sanatana's virtues.

**1388.** O Srinivasa, here there is great bliss.
Look at Nipavana: it enchants the mind with its beauty.[^349]

**1389.** Here is Krishna-kunda — here the pastimes are endless;
at this Kuntala-kunda Krishna dressed His hair.[^350]

**1390.** Here is the Bedokhor kunja: inside its little house
the Two delight, having shut the door of the kunja.[^351]

**1391.** Charana-pahari is the name of this hill;
here are the incomparable pastimes of Krishnachandra.[^352]

**1392.** Krishna, surrounded by His friends, climbed the hill;
far off the cows graze — He looks in all four directions.

**1393.** In a form that enchants the world, taking the flute in His hand,
He stood beneath a tree, bent in three places.

**1394.** The moment He began to play the flute, the world went mad;
all, wherever they were, came running [here].

**1395.** Hearing the song of the flute, all became motionless;
what can compare with that song? — this hill melted.

**1396.** Those who came here on hearing the sound of the flute —
look at their footprints on the stone.

**1397.** Here the marks of Sri Krishna's lotus feet remained;
for this reason the name Charana-pahari arose.

**1398.** Look: here is Krishna-kunda, the village of Haroyal;
here Rai and Ghanashyama joyfully delight.[^353]

**1399.** Playing at dice, Rai defeated Krishna;
having lost the game, Krishna was greatly ashamed.

**1400.** Lalita says, "Rai, in the game of dice
you easily defeated the Lord of your life.

**1401.** Your victory is in many ways.
We shall see who wins and who loses in Kandarpa's battle."

**1402.** Having said this, the sakhis left the Two in the nikunja temple
and, themselves unseen, watch Their play.

**1403.** Supreme bliss arose — what more is there to say?
In this Haroyal there are wondrous pastimes.

**1404.** Look: the village named Satoha shines in its beauty;
here the sage Sri Shantanu worshipped Krishna.[^354]

**1405.** Surya-kunda, Nandana-kupa, Vadya-shila, and also
a wondrous hill — here are Krishna's pastimes.[^355]

**1406.** Look at the village of Pai: Rai with Her friends,
searching for Krishna, found [Him] here.[^356]

**1407.** Look at this Chalana-shila: here Shyama-raya
cannot walk for prema and sits down on the stone.[^357]

**1408.** Look at the village of Kamari: here Krishna,
tormented by desire, gazes at the path by which Rai [is to come].[^358]

**1409.** Look at this village of Bichhor: here the Moon-faced One
meets with Krishna, with Her dear friends beside Her.[^359]

**1410.** When the play is over, the Two go to Their homes;
because of the separation [this place] is called Bichhor.

**1411.** Look at Kadamba-khandi and the village of Tiloyar:
here, absorbed in play, [They] know not a moment's rest.[^360]

**1412.** And here is Shringaravata: here Krishna
adorned Radhika in many different ways.[^361]

**1413.** Look: here is a wondrous place of Krishna's pastimes;
now it has become the village named Lalapura.

**1414.** And here is the village of Vasoni: from the fragrance of Krishna's body
will only the bees go mad? — it destroys the composure of the world.[^362]

**1415.** Here Radha and Krishna with Their dear friends
are ever immersed in the games of Holi and other play.

**1416.** O, look at the village of Paya: here Sri Krishna
drank milk together with all His friends.[^363]

**1417.** This is Kotaravana; everyone calls it Kotavana;
here Krishna happily sports with His friends.[^364]

**1418.** In this Dadhigrama Krishna plundered the curd;
with the gopis great merriment arose.

**1419.** Here is Sheshashayi — here is Kshira-samudra;
playfully Krishna lay down on the bed of Ananta.[^365]

**1420.** Sri Radhika serves His lotus feet;
what bliss arose — it cannot be described.

**1421.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the ninety-first verse:*
*"He whose beautiful, tender lotus feet, though She Herself is tender,*
*Sri Radha for Her own happiness raises high to the tips of Her breasts,*
*quite close, but then, afraid of their hardness, does not place [them there] —*
*may that Sheshashayi ever establish our dwelling in Sri Goshtha."*[^366]

**1422.** To see this Deity of Sheshashayi,
Sri Krishna Chaitanya-chandra came here.

**1423.** When He saw [Him], great delight arose;
in that absorption in prema the Lord lost His composure.

**1424.** Seeing the Lord's radiance, the fortunate people
went mad with bliss — streams [of tears] flow ceaselessly from their eyes.

**1425.** They said to one another: "This is not a human being at all;
He wears the dress of a sannyasi, but truly this is the Lord."

**1426.** Someone said: "O brother, it cannot be otherwise:
this sannyasi is the Lord Sheshashayi."

**1427.** So many spoke, and no one could keep calm;
again and again they gaze at the Lord's moon-like face.

**1428.** O Srinivasa, the Lord's deeds are boundless;
one can know them only if the Lord [Himself] makes them known.

**1429.** Here, look at the charming kadamba grove;
here the crown of rasikas sports in merriment.

**1430.** This is the border of Vraja — Khamba, Hare, the village of Khami;[^367]
here Krishna and Balarama merrily herd the cows.

**1431.** In the villages of Banachari and others — wondrous pastimes;
all these are the border of Vraja, O Srinivasa.[^368]

**1432.** The village near the Yamuna — here is Khararo;
here Balarama asks His friends about their welfare.[^369]

**1433.** Look at the place Ujani: here the Yamuna
flows upstream at the song of Sri Krishna's flute.[^370]

**1434.** Look at Khelanavana: here the two brothers
play with Their friends — and have no thought of eating.

**1435.** [Only] by their mother's efforts do Krishna and Balarama eat;
the name of this Khelana-vata is Sri Khela-tirtha.[^371]

**1436.** O Srinivasa, this is Rama-ghat;
here the son of Rohini performs the rasa-lila.[^372]

**1437.** Where Krishna with His beloveds enjoyed the rasa sport —
far from there is this rasa place of Rama.

**1438.** What is there to say — He is deep as millions of oceans,
Krishna's second body, supremely steadfast.

**1439.** Full of longing, He came from Dvaraka to Vraja
and stayed [here] two months — Chaitra and Vaishakha.[^373]

**1440.** He consoled everyone — Sri Nanda, Yashoda and the others —
and gladdened His friends in various ways.

**1441.** The son of Rohini, skilled in many kinds of entreaty,
calmed Krishna's beloveds in various ways.

**1442.** He pleased the hearts of His own beloved gopis,
with whom He had sported before in the spring.

**1443.** Who can describe that boundless merriment?
At that time Krishna slew Shankhachuda.

**1444.** With Baladeva's beloveds together with Krishna's beloveds
the play of Holi went on, and the merriment grew as was fitting.

**1445.** How Rama and Krishna, both, with Their own beloveds
enjoy Their pastimes — of that the knowing ones tell.

**1446.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Krishna-chaitanya-charita, [composed by] Sri Murari Gupta, in the fourth prakrama:*
*"And then look: here Sri Rama and Krishna in spring attire, the two knowers of rasa, adorned with gold,*
*played with the beauties of Vraja — each with His own group leaders.*[^374]

**1447.** *Dancing and singing with the gopis, filled with rasa,*
*They shone [amid] the lovely women who sang and danced."*

**1448.** Supremely wondrous are Baladeva's pastimes;
Baladeva's beloveds are beyond count.

**1449.** The girls not yet born at the time of Krishna's pastimes —
all of them are counted among Baladeva's beloveds.

**1450.** How Balai, who increases the love of all these gopis,
was absorbed in play — there is no end to telling it.

**1451.** Boundless is the good fortune of the months Chaitra and Vaishakha,
in which the son of Rohini sports in Vraja.

**1452.** *Thus [it is said] in the Tenth [Canto], in the sixty-fifth chapter, the eleventh verse:*
*"For two months — Madhu and Madhava — Lord Rama*
*stayed there, bringing delight to the gopis at night."*[^375]

**1453.** O Srinivasa, "Baladeva with His beloveds
will perform the rasa play" — with this joy in His heart

**1454.** (who can understand the deeds of Balarama?)
in great merriment He came here.

**1455.** Here is the lovely grove on the sandy bank of the Yamuna;
here a fragrant breeze always blows gently, gently.

**1456.** The night is bright with the rays of the full moon;
masses of blossoming flowers — a wondrous beauty.

**1457.** Bees with their mates hum charmingly;
various birds ceaselessly make various sounds.

**1458.** Hundreds of thousands of peacocks and peahens dance;
deer and does playfully roam in all four directions.

**1459.** Staying beneath a tree, the son of Rohini watches [this];
what an unprecedented pose [He has], enchanting the worlds!

**1460.** Seeing Sri Rama's beauty, with joy in their hearts,
the gods in heaven cry out: "Glory, glory!"

**1461.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (raga Velavali):*
Glory to the son of Rohini, the mighty Bala!
His luster steals the pride of the conch, the kunda jasmine, camphor and the Silver Mountain; lovely is [His] body. (Refrain.)[^376]

**1462.** Lovely hair, curling locks swaying, tilaka glittering — the thief of young women's hearts;
lotus eyes wide, bee-like brows trembling, earrings brightening the ears.

**1463.** The nose — [like] the beak of the king of birds, the face surpassing the moon ceaselessly rains nectar;
the arms rounded and gracefully curved, bracelets on the wrists, on the broad chest a jewel necklace shines.[^377]

**1464.** The waist, crushing the weight of the lion's pride, is clad in blue garments — incomparable;
the shapely knees delight people; to the cluster of nails on [His] feet Ghanashyama offers himself in sacrifice.[^378]

**1465.** O Srinivasa, on seeing Baladeva,
who in the three worlds could keep his composure?

**1466.** Here Rama shines on a throne of jewels;
extraordinary is the beauty of His attire for the rasa festival.

**1467.** Baladeva's beauty surpasses millions of Kandarpas;
the graceful curves of every limb enchant the best of sages.

**1468.** The radiance of His body illuminates the three worlds;
it steals the pride of the rays of millions of moons.

**1469.** On His head a charming mass of curly, glossy hair,
a crown of jewels encircled by a flower garland.

**1470.** The forehead shines, the brows are a row of bees;
the eyes, reaching to the ears, have the splendor of red lotuses.

**1471.** The nose, surpassing the beak of the king of birds, is beautiful;
incomparable and charming is [His] beautiful face.

**1472.** The luster of the lips surpasses the ripe bimba fruit;
the beauty of the lovely teeth destroys the pride of pearls.

**1473.** The pair of cheeks surpasses a silver mirror;
in the ear a single earring glitters.[^379]

**1474.** How sweet the chin — no comparison can be found;
with the bend of His neck He steals the lion's pride.

**1475.** The neck with three folds, the shapely sides;
young women cannot hold back their hearts, seeing the broad chest.[^380]

**1476.** What is an elephant's trunk before [His] beautiful arms!
Who, with great longing, does not beg for the rasa of their touch?

**1477.** He is adorned with armlets, bracelets and various ornaments;
in His left hand is a horn studded with various jewels.

**1478.** The vaijayanti garland on His neck sways ceaselessly;
bees roam over it and hum.

**1479.** The belly is charming, [charming] the navel, the waist very slender;
the bluish garment He wears clings to His body.

**1480.** The thighs — [like] inverted plantain trunks, the abode of rasa;
the soles of the feet defeat the pride of the dawn.

**1481.** The sweet charm of the feet increases everyone's joy;
on them the restless ankle-bells ceaselessly ring.

**1482.** With the radiance of His nails He dispels darkness;
what comparison to give — there is none in the universe.

**1483.** Such a dhyana (meditation) on Baladeva is proclaimed in the Puranas;
fortunate people see [Him] ceaselessly.

**1484.** The Lord who enchants the worlds, the son of Rohini,
whose horn-playing steals the minds of Brahma and the others.

**1485.** Here Baladeva, standing in a threefold bend,
with a joyful heart plays the enchanting horn.

**1486.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (raga Malakosha):*
Today is the sweet spring night, the full moon shines.
Gazing at the forest and the bank of the Yamuna, His heart maddened again and again, Baladeva sounds the horn — the sound enchants the world. (Refrain.)[^381]

**1487.** The sound draws the beloveds; touching [their] ears, leaving their homes,
[they] come — their hearts brimming with joy, their beauty delighting the mind.
The belts of little bells ring "rini-jhinin-jhanan", the sound of the ankle-bells steals composure;
the lovely tread of their lotus feet puts to shame the gait of the khanjana bird.

**1488.** Many beloveds on all sides — women in spotless attire,
flashing brighter than lightning, display their merriment.
The Lord, absorbed in supreme play, speaks so many [words] softly, softly, softly;
[His] clever, charming words destroy the pride of nectar.[^382]

**1489.** The host of lotus-faced women, with restless eyes like pairs of bees,
drinks the honey of [His] sweetness in rapture and flocks [to Him] with a dense humming.
Abundant thrills rise on the body, incomparable, ever-charming love;
how could Narahari understand this? — [all] are maddened by the supreme rasa of touch.[^383]

**1490.** Here are Sri Balai's most wondrous pastimes —
what can I say with one mouth, O Srinivasa!

**1491.** [Here] a breeze with the fragrance of night lotuses blows ceaselessly,
and how bright, how charming is the moonlight!

**1492.** Balarama, absorbed in play in the grove by the Yamuna,
incomparable, is surrounded by hundreds of thousands of beloveds.

**1493.** *Thus [it is said] in the Tenth [Canto], in the sixty-fifth chapter, the twelfth verse:*
*"In the grove by the Yamuna, bathed in the rays of the full moon and fanned by a breeze with the fragrance of night lotuses,*
*He enjoyed, surrounded by hosts of women."*[^384]

**1494.** With His beloveds He drank varuni — great merriment;
this story of Varuni is known everywhere.

**1495.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place, in the thirteenth verse:*
*"Sent by Varuna, the goddess Varuni, flowing from the hollow of a tree,*
*filled that whole forest with fragrance.*

**1496.** *Bala, smelling the fragrance of that stream of honey carried by the wind,*
*came there and drank together with the women."*[^385]

**1497.** The goddess presiding over intoxicating drink, born together with the nectar,
told Rama: "I am the daughter of Varuna."[^386]

**1498.** *Thus [it is said] in the Harivamsha:*
*"My father Varuna has sent [me] to You, O sinless one."*

**1499.** Here the son of Rohini with His beloveds
was ceaselessly enraptured at the beginning of the rasa.

**1500.** On mridanga, pinaka, vina and other instruments
many play in various styles.[^387]

**1501.** The skilled beloveds perform the alapa of various ragas,
displaying shrutis, svaras, murchhanas, gramas and the rest.

**1502.** The gopis sing of the deeds of the Lord of their life;
Brahma and the others, hearing that song, are enchanted.

**1503.** In the Sri rasa-mandala there is no limit to that happiness;
with song, music and dance Balai is in great rapture.

**1504.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (raga Shankarabharana):*
Baladeva dances, every limb [of His body] thrilling with abundant ecstasy.
Da drimi drimi drimi kata, dha drinda drindadha vidhuddhata,
taka takadhikitaka thori, kuku — softly sounds the mridanga. (Refrain.)[^388]

**1505.** He sways His head very sweetly; His chest is mighty, exceedingly broad;
the lovely, incomparable forest garland swings, [and with it] the bees.
His cheeks outshine silver mirrors, the fine earrings in His ears sway;
His sidelong glance is a khanjana bird, His brows [are curved]; merriment with the lovely women.

**1506.** The gestures of His hands are gracefully made; on His head is a crown of jewels and peacock [feathers];
His curling locks glitter so — [He] shatters the pride of Manmatha.
His soles are lovely land-lotuses; stepping here and there, [He] keeps various talas,
calling out: "Taka thai thai thai, titaka dhilanga."

**1507.** Jhunu jhunu jhunu jhunu — the sound of the ankle bells; hearing it, no one keeps composure;
the bells of the girdle jingle, rani rani they sound, and rapture arises in the heart.
The moon-faced beloveds [He] kisses, smiling ever so gently —
so sings Ghanashyama this heart-delighting wave of rasa.[^389]

**1508.** *Again, raga Kedara:*
It sounds: "Jhig jhig jhig jhendrang, drinda drinda drimi dig-drang";
the triputa tala is manifest, soft strokes, a swift tempo.
Taka thai thai tathai ta, thodi thunna thong kana
kana jhini na na na — [so it sounds]; the mind of Ratipati is enraptured.[^390]

**1509.** The beautiful, composed hero Bala dances on the bank of the Daughter of the Sun;
the beloveds, in the joyous ardor of the rasa, sport all around [Him].
The restless steps go jhinini; the jeweled bells of the girdle jingle;
jhunu jhunu jhunu — the sound of the ankle bells steals the minds of the sages.

**1510.** The jeweled earrings flash on His cheeks, the lovely forest garland sways;
drawn by the wealth of fragrance, the bees hum in swarms.[^391]
With a touch full of rasa [He] smiles gently; the splendid moon of His face shines;
the nectar-rasa [of it] the eyes of Ghanashyama drink — chakora birds.

**1511.** All the beloveds, with great bliss in their hearts,
surrounding Baladeva, dance wondrously.

**1512.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (raga Kedara):*
Today it is the full moon, the moon is full, the spring night is pure.
Dha dha dhigi tagadhilanga, drimi drimi drimi — the mridanga sounds;
Baladeva dances, and, surrounding [Him], all the lovely women sport. (Refrain.)

**1513.** The bells of the girdle, the soft sound of the ankle bells, the incomparable movement, the sweet singing,
the many gestures of the hands, the restless eyes, the curving poses — charming [women].
He sways His head ever so gently, He smiles — His rows of teeth shine.
What will Ghanashyama say? The fair-bodied [women] flash like lightning.

**1514.** *Again, raga Bhupali:*
How sweet is the spring night today!
The moon has lit up all the directions.

**1515.** On the expanse of the Yamuna's bank
Balai sports with His beloveds.

**1516.** What beauty the rasa-mandala has!
On all four sides are the charming gopis.

**1517.** Eagerly they play various instruments,
they sing songs in waves of rasa.

**1518.** Surrounding the Lord of their life they dance;
with their beauty they steal the mind of the world.

**1519.** Balarama, the crown of rasikas,
dances, surpassing millions of Kamas.

**1520.** Again and again He blows the lovely horn;
with that very sound He intoxicates the world.

**1521.** How many clever tricks He displays!
On His beloved's arm He places His arm.

**1522.** Bringing the moon of His face to [her] face,
in delight He cannot restrain His heart.

**1523.** He fulfils the desires of all;
this Narahari Dasa offers himself [for Him].[^392]

**1524.** O Srinivasa! The rasa-lila of Sri Rama
the Lord's devotees have described in many ways.

**1525.** Playfully drawing the Yamuna and bringing her here,
Baladeva held water sports with His beloveds.[^393]

**1526.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (raga Bhupali):*
The hero Bala, who delights in the Sri rasa,
is in rapture at every moment and cannot be steady.

**1527.** Who can understand this lila of Balai?
Without effort He drew the Yamuna with His plough.

**1528.** Settling with the host of lovely women,
He sports in the waters of the Yamuna in many ways.[^394]

**1529.** Having fought in the water, He comes up onto the bank;
then with the beauty of His garments and ornaments He steals [all] hearts.

**1530.** Balarama is the embodiment of rasa;
He drinks madhu and the rest, intoxicated with the intoxication of Madana.

**1531.** With His beloveds in the bower-house
He lies down on a bed of flowers; how much rises in His heart!

**1532.** Seeing that the night has ended, the beloveds
cannot leave the Lord of their life and go home.

**1533.** Balai, caressing [them] so much,
bids [them] farewell, and His heart breaks.

**1534.** All went each to her own home.
Narahari offers himself for this pastime of Balai.

**1535.** Here [there were] various sports with the beloveds;
at the end of the night all returned home.

**1536.** Here the Yamuna, seized with great fear,
fell at Baladeva's lotus feet and bowed down.

**1537.** Considering herself lowly, with an anguished heart,
joining both palms, she offered many prayers.

**1538.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (raga Deshapala):*
*O Rama, son of Rohini, lotus-eyed,*
*ornament of the Yadu dynasty, Baladeva, friend of the surrendered!*
*Affectionate to Your devotees, Halayudha, abode of joy,*
*home of virtues, remover of fear, sole ocean of mercy!*[^395]

**1539.** *O You who are honoured by the world, moon-faced, beautiful,*
*unsurpassed in playing the horn — dhiki dhikata dheya!*
*Sa ri ga, sa ri ga ma, pa ma ga ri ma pa dha ni ti —*
*ah, show mercy to me, O Lord of Nrihari — tenna!*[^396]

**1540.** With jubilation in her heart the Yamuna bows down [to Him] again.
What to say — she is skilled in caring for the welfare of others.

**1541.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (raga Shri):*
*Glory, glory to the beloved of Revati, the abode of rasa,*
*who delights the people of all the worlds — re!*
*His eyes are petals of a spotless lotus; He frees from the burden of composure,*
*He puts to shame the gait of the elephant — re!*[^397]

**1542.** *Moon-faced, master of new tandava dances,*
*Haladhara, ornament of the Yadu dynasty — re!*
*Like a conch and the kunda flower, clad in blue,*
*He shatters the pride of Makaradhvaja — re!*

**1543.** *Protector of those who take refuge, [Lord of] Narahari,*
*jha jha jha jha, trigadatiya!*
*Ei ai ai ai, ai ati ainava,*
*tenna tenna ti ati ai iya!*[^398]

**1544.** What shall I say, O Srinivasa, of that very story:
here Balai became gracious to the Yamuna.

**1545.** Various amusements [took place] in this rasa pastime;
this Rasa-sthali of Rama is famous in the world.

**1546.** What shall I say — beautiful is the region of Rama-ghat;
the communities of devotees constantly worship it.

**1547.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the ninety-fourth verse:*
*"That Krishna who, composed, was flowing to the salt ocean, devoid of connection with Krishna,*
*was drawn by the tip of the plough of the angry Plough-bearer;*
*even today all people see her so in this place.*
*Oh! With devotion I worship this wondrous region of Rama-ghatta."*[^399]

**1548.** Whoever has the wish to hear about Rama-ghat,
from him these worldly fetters fall away without effort.

**1549.** Rama, who delights in the Sri rasa — Nityananda-raya —
stayed here during His pilgrimage to the tirthas.[^400]

**1550.** With the cowherd boys He was always absorbed in play;
when hungry, He ate yogurt, milk, roots and fruits.

**1551.** In the mood of Baladeva He could not remain steady;
He hides Himself — [but] cannot stay hidden.

**1552.** All say: "This is that very son of Rohini
who wanders through Vraja in the guise of an avadhuta."

**1553.** O Srinivasa, seeing Nitai's ways,
all — whether children, old men or youths — were enchanted.

**1554.** Here are Nitaichand's wondrous pastimes.
This shakata tree here is His tooth-stick.[^401]

**1555.** Here at Rama-ghat a fortunate brahmana
could not sustain his life without Baladeva.

**1556.** Nityananda-Rama, to protect His devotee,
appeared to the brahmana in the form of Baladeva.

**1557.** Seeing here Nityananda-Balarama, who delights in the Sri rasa,
the Kalindi offered [Him] prayers.

**1558.** Here, seeing Nityananda's play, the gods
were overwhelmed — their tears could not be checked.

**1559.** Beneath this tree, on a raised platform of dust,
Nityananda-Haladhara lay in rapture.

**1560.** Lying there, Prabhu says again and again:
"In how many days will the deliverance of the faithless come?

**1561.** In how many days will the Lord of Navadvipa in Navadvipa
manifest Himself? [When] shall I go and see [Him] with my own eyes?"[^402]

**1562.** So much He speaks thus — no one can understand;
Nitai's wondrous lila is known throughout the world.

**1563.** Look: near Rama-ghat is Kachchhavana;
here the boys played [imitating] turtles.[^403]

**1564.** Look at Bhushanavana — this is a very secluded [place];
[here] the friends adorned Krishna with flower ornaments.[^404]

**1565.** And here as well — look at the place of Krishna's pastimes;
whose heart is not soothed on seeing all this?

**1566.** Having said this, Pandita walks slowly, slowly;
seeing the beauty of the forest, he cannot keep his composure.

**1567.** With jubilation in his heart he goes along the path to Bhandira;
now people call this [place] Akshayavata.

**1568.** Approaching Bhandira, with very sweet words,
with great affection Pandita says to Srinivasa:

**1569.** Look at the incomparable place of Bhandiravata;
here Krishna and Balarama sport delightfully.[^405]

**1570.** When [They] were playing games with Their friends in the guise of wrestlers,
the asura Pralamba came and mingled among them.

**1571.** Balarama playfully slew Pralamba.
At Bhandira are Krishna's various pastimes with His friends.

**1572.** One day Krishna, alone at the foot of Bhandira,
played the flute — by which the world is intoxicated.

**1573.** Hearing the sound of the flute, Radha lost Her patience;
coming quickly with Her sakhis, She met Krishna.

**1574.** Supreme bliss arose in the hearts of the Two;
with Their friends They sport in many playful ways.

**1575.** Sri Radhika says to Krishna in gentle words,
"What games do You play with Your friends in this region?"

**1576.** Sri Krishna says, "Here, dressing as wrestlers,
I happily wrestle with My friends.

**1577.** No one knows wrestling as I do;
without effort I defeat other wrestlers."

**1578.** Laughing, Lalita says to Krishna again and again,
"Today we shall see You fight in a wrestler's dress."

**1579.** Having said this, they all dressed as wrestlers;
Krishna, in a wrestler's dress, boasts without end.

**1580.** Looking at Krishna, Rai smiles softly, softly;
for the wrestling She enters the place of the contest.

**1581.** In the great wrestling there was neither victory nor defeat;
Kandarpa's joy became boundless.

**1582.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the ninety-third verse:*
*"Where my Mistress, full of rasa, the most beloved Radha, filled with pride,*
*having made wrestlers of Her own friends and eagerly becoming a wrestler Herself,*
*joyfully wrestled with the Slayer of Baka, who had fully taken on the role of a wrestler,*
*and gave delight to Madana — that Bhandiraka I worship."*[^406]

**1583.** Thus at Bhandira [They] were absorbed in all kinds of amusements;
who can describe the pastimes that took place at Bhandira?

**1584.** Near Bhandira, look at this village of Ara;
this is Munjatavi, also named Ishikatavi.[^407]

**1585.** Here Krishnachandra, having drunk the forest fire,
saved the cows, the cowherds and all the rest — there was great bliss.

**1586.** This here is the village of Bhandari on the bank of the Yamuna;
that is Munjatavi, as all people proclaim.[^408]

**1587.** O Srinivasa, look at this Tapovana:
here the daughters of the cowherds performed austerities.[^409]

**1588.** Look at Gopi-ghat: here the gopis came;
bathing in the Yamuna, they were filled with great delight.

**1589.** This is Chir-ghat: here the daughters of the cowherds
worshipped Katyayani, and the hearts of all rejoiced.[^410]

**1590.** Leaving their garments on the bank of the Yamuna,
all entered the water to bathe.

**1591.** Unnoticed, stealing everyone's clothes,
Hari watches the fun from up in a nipa tree.

**1592.** The daughters of the cowherds, greatly ashamed,
remaining in the water, beg Krishna for their clothes.

**1593.** Krishna, revealing what was in His own heart,
joyfully gave everyone their clothes.

**1594.** The daughters of the cowherds joyfully put on their clothes,
having surrendered their very selves to Krishna.

**1595.** Look at this Nanda-ghat: here Nanda and the others
bathed in the Yamuna — there is much to tell about this.

**1596.** Having fasted on ekadashi, on dvadashi
he enters the waters of the Kalindi to bathe.

**1597.** A messenger of Varuna seized Nanda and carried him off;
Krishna playfully brought Nanda back from there.[^411]

**1598.** O Srinivasa, here Nanda became afraid;
therefore Vajra founded a village named Bhaya.[^412]

**1599.** Having said this, they set out from the village of Bhaya
to see all the places that [lie] along the parikrama.

**1600.** He says to Srinivasa: look at this Vatsavana;
here the Four-faced One stole the calves.[^413]

**1601.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place, in the Vraja-vilasa, the ninety-sixth verse:*
*"When the Creator, eager to see with his own eyes the boundless greatness of his Lord,*
*swiftly stole the herd of calves and the crowd of boys who tended the calves,*
*Hari, becoming each of them, there gave joy to their mothers*
*and even ate [their] food — that place of the stealing of the calves I worship."*

**1602.** This here is the village of Unai: here with His friends
Krishna eats all sorts of foods in many playful ways.

**1603.** This is the village named Balihara: here
the Four-faced One, with a glad heart, steals the boys and the rest.[^414]

**1604.** Look here at the place named Parikham:
the Four-faced One stayed [here] to test Krishna.[^415]

**1605.** All people know this place by the name of Sei;
here Brahma was bewildered by Krishna's maya.[^416]

**1606.** Having stolen the boys and calves and hidden [them] in secret, Brahma
sees those same boys and calves beside Krishna.

**1607.** "Those are these, these are those," he says again and again;
for this reason [this place] received the name Sei.

**1608.** In this village of Chomuha Brahma, coming to Krishna,
offered praise to Krishna in countless ways.[^417]

**1609.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place, in the Vraja-vilasa, the ninety-seventh verse:*
*"Where Brahma, in great fear because of the offence committed by stealing the calves and the calf-herds,*
*fell to the ground in tears and with unprecedented verses praised*
*Mukunda, the son of the lord of Vraja, the wondrous herder of calves, faintly smiling —*
*that place named Bhiru-chaturmukha, together with [its] Lord, we ceaselessly praise."*[^418]

**1610.** [Here] Krishna killed Aghasura — this is Sarpa-sthali;
[its] name is Aghavana, and people call [it] Sapoli.[^419]

**1611.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place, in the Vraja-vilasa, the ninety-fifth verse:*
*"Where the Conqueror of Mura, seeing that His friends, dear to Him as life, had entered before Him*
*the belly of the most sinful Aghasura, foul with poisons burning like the fire at the edge of the aranis,*
*and were in distress, in anger at once entered [it], and, mighty, killed that villain*
*and saved His own [friends] — may that Sarpa-sthali protect [us]."*[^420]

**1612.** Here the gods shower flowers and cry out, "Victory!" —
for this reason this [place] is called the village of Jayet.[^421]

**1613.** All say, "In killing Aghasura He [showed Himself] clever (*siyān*)";
therefore this is the village of Seyano, [also] called Sehona.[^422]

**1614.** Look at these two villages, Taroli and Baroli;
all say [that they are] villages founded by the cowherds in ancient times.[^423]

**1615.** O Srinivasa, look also at a delightful place:
here the son of Nanda, Bhagavan, sports.

**1616.** Having said this, he climbed the hill by Krishna-kunda
and looks in all four directions, filled with joy.[^424]

**1617.** He says to Srinivasa: look, this is the village of Maghera;
formerly it was known by the name Maghahera.[^425]

**1618.** O, look here at the tamala grove:
at the meeting of Radha and Krishna great merriment grows [here].

**1619.** Having said this, he joyfully came down from the hill
and speaks to Srinivasa with the greatest affection:

**1620.** In this village of Atas a great wonder took place:
the sage Ashtavakra performed austerities here.[^426]

**1621.** This is Shakra-sthana, now [people] call [it] Shakaroya;
having poured rain upon Vraja, Shakra became afraid here.[^427]

**1622.** In this village of Varahar, in the form of a boar,
Krishna played with His dear friends.[^428]

**1623.** Look at the village of Harasali, O Srinivasa:
this is Rasa-sthali — here Krishna performed the rasa dance.[^429]

**1624.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place, in the Vraja-vilasa, the sixty-third verse:*
*"Where the Conqueror of Mura, dancing with the throng of lovely young cowherd women, radiant with skill,*
*leaves them and in seclusion, with love, adorns Sri Radhika*
*with a wealth of flower ornaments and delights [with Her] in an abundance of joys —*
*may that Rasa-sthali, filled with a sweetness wondrous to the three worlds, protect [us]."*[^430]

**1625.** Having said this, he, together with Srinivasa and Narottama,
came again to Nanda-ghat, filled with great joy.

**1626.** He says to Srinivasa: here, in this solitary place,
Sri Jiva lived, entirely unrecognized.

**1627.** I shall tell that story. One day in Vrindavana
Sri Rupa, sitting in solitude, was writing a book.

**1628.** It was the hot season, sweat covered [his] body;
Sri Jiva, standing to one side, was fanning [him].

**1629.** As boundless as the beauty of Rupa Gosvami was,
so [beautiful] was the appearance of Sri Jiva — [it was] the time of [his] youth.

**1630.** Who would not long to see Sri Rupa?
Sri Vallabha Bhatta came and met [him] in private.

**1631.** Seeing the mangalacharana of the book *Bhakti-rasamrita*,
the Bhatta says, "I will correct this."[^431]

**1632.** Having said this, he went to bathe on the bank of the Yamuna;
Sri Jiva went [there too], on the pretext of fetching water.

**1633.** [He] had no acquaintance with Sri Vallabha Bhatta;
he asks, "What doubt [do you have] about the mangalacharana?"

**1634.** Having listened, whatever Sri Vallabha Bhatta said,
Sri Jiva quickly refuted all of it.

**1635.** In the course of the talk a debate on many shastras arose;
the Bhatta could not refute Sri Jiva's words.

**1636.** Having discussed for some time and brought the discussion to a close,
the Bhatta went again [to Rupa] and says to Sri Rupa,

**1637.** "That young man who was beside you —
I have joyfully come to learn who he is."

**1638.** Sri Rupa says, "What shall I tell [you about him]?
[His] name is Jiva; he is my disciple, my brother's son.

**1639.** It is only a few days since he came from [our] homeland."
Hearing [this], the Bhatta praised [him] in every way.

**1640.** Honoured by Rupa, the Bhatta departed;
at that very moment Sri Jiva came back from the Yamuna.

**1641.** Sri Rupa says to Sri Jiva in gentle words,
"Out of kindness to me the Bhatta came to me.

**1642.** For my own good he said he would correct the book —
and you could not bear even such small words.

**1643.** Therefore go quickly to the eastern country;
when your mind becomes calm, come [back] to Vrindavana."[^432]

**1644.** At the Gosvami's order he set out eastward;
having gone some distance, he carefully calmed his mind.

**1645.** There was no order of the Gosvami to come near [him];
for this reason he came here, to this solitary forest.

**1646.** Living in a hut of leaves, in the greatest grief,
he sometimes eats a little, sometimes fasts.

**1647.** "Quickly separating my life from my body,
I shall attain the lotus feet of [my] master" — such was the thought in his heart.

**1648.** Unexpectedly Sanatana Gosvami came;
the villagers went out to meet [him] and took [him] to the village.

**1649.** Seating the Gosvami in great joy
and asking about his welfare, they then say slowly:

**1650.** "A handsome young ascetic
has been living in this forest for some days now.

**1651.** We try in many ways to feed him;
sometimes he eats fruits and roots, sometimes he goes without food.

**1652.** Only after much effort does he take a little wheat flour
and eat it, mixing it with water."

**1653.** Hearing this, he understood that Jiva was there;
melting with parental affection, he went to that place.

**1654.** Sri Jiva was sitting in his hut of leaves;
at the sight of the Gosvami he could not hold back his heart.

**1655.** Rolling on the ground, he falls at the Gosvami's feet;
seeing Sri Jiva's behaviour, everyone is amazed.

**1656.** Whatever Sanatana, overcome with affection, asked,
Sri Jiva related it all to him briefly and in order.

**1657.** Having heard, Sri Gosvami left Jiva right there
and, reassuring the villagers, went to Vrindavana.

**1658.** Hearing of the Gosvami's arrival, Sri Rupa at once
went to see the Gosvami.

**1659.** The Gosvami asks Sri Rupa for news:
"What still remains [to be done] in the *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu*?"

**1660.** Sri Rupa says, "The writing is nearly finished;
if Jiva were here, the revision would be done quickly."

**1661.** The Gosvami says, "Jiva is barely alive.
I saw his body swaying in the wind."

**1662.** Saying this, he told what had happened to Jiva;
Sri Rupa at once had Sri Jiva brought.

**1663.** Seeing Sri Jiva's condition, Sri Rupa Gosani
nursed him — there was no limit to his mercy.

**1664.** When Sri Jiva recovered, everyone's heart was glad;
Rupa-Sanatana entrusted [him] with all the work.

**1665.** By the grace of Sri Rupa and Sri Sanatana
the power of Sri Jiva's learning spread through the world.

**1666.** A digvijayi came to Vrindavana
with many people, expert in all the shastras.

**1667.** He said, "If you are unable to debate,
then quickly send me a certificate of my victory."[^433]

**1668.** Hearing this, Sri Jiva at once sent a letter;
on reading the letter, the digvijayi was defeated.

**1669.** All the digvijayis who come like this, full of pride,
are defeated and flee to their own lands.

**1670.** The power of Sri Jiva cannot be described.
O Srinivasa, this is his hut.

**1671.** Telling many such things, they crossed the Yamuna
and, coming to the village of Surukhuru, stayed [there] that day.[^434]

**1672.** How Sri Krishna showed favour to the gods there —
that he made known to Srinivasa and Narottama.

**1673.** From there he showed them distant villages too;
which pastimes took place where — of that he spoke briefly.

**1674.** Setting out from Surukhuru in the morning,
he says to Srinivasa: look, here is Bhadravana.

**1675.** By going to Bhadravana one becomes dear to Krishna;
by the power of this forest one attains the world of heaven.

**1676.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"There is a forest named Bhadravana, the sixth forest, excellent.*
*Going there, O Vasudha, My devotee, for whom I am the supreme refuge,*[^435]
*by the power of that forest goes to the world of heaven."*

**1677.** Look: utterly secluded is this Bhandiravana;
here Rama and Krishna play various games with Their friends.

**1678.** This Bhandiravana is dear to the yogis;
merely seeing it removes the torment of [dwelling in] the womb.

**1679.** This Bhandira is the best of all forests, the shastras say;
whoever sees Vasudeva here is not born again.[^436]

**1680.** Whoever regularly bathes and so on in Bhandira
is freed from all sins and goes to the world of Indra.

**1681.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The eleventh is Bhandira, excellent, dear to the yogis;*
*by merely seeing it a man does not enter a womb.*

**1682.** *Having reached Bhandira, the best of forests,*
*and having then seen Vasudeva, [one] is not born again.*

**1683.** *Whoever, self-controlled and restrained in eating, bathes in this Bhandira*
*is freed from all sins and goes to the world of Indra."*

**1684.** Having played with His friends in Bhandira, Sri Krishna
eats various foods, sitting in this shade.

**1685.** Therefore this village is called Chhaheri.
Look at this beautiful place near the Yamuna.[^437]

**1686.** This is Mathgrama — here there is great bliss;
here Rama and Krishna engage in various games with Their friends.[^438]

**1687.** A large vessel made of clay is called a math;
[this place] is famed for making maths — therefore [it is] Mathgrama.

**1688.** For churning yogurt and so on, the residents of Vraja
take countless maths [from here] — so everyone says.

**1689.** In this Bilvavana Rama and Krishna with Their friends
eat ripe bilva fruits with great merriment.

**1690.** The beautiful Bilvavana is worshipped by the gods;
whoever goes to this forest is honoured in Brahma-loka.

**1691.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The forest named Bilvavana is the tenth, worshipped by the gods;*
*going there, a man is glorified in Brahma-loka."*

**1692.** Whoever bathes in Sri Krishna-kunda in Bilvavana
is freed from all sins; he is supremely fortunate.

**1693.** Look: in very ancient times the course of the Yamuna was here,
and Mana-sarovara was on the far side of the Yamuna.[^439]

**1694.** Now the Yamuna has become two streams,
and in the middle is the most beautiful Mana-sarovara.

**1695.** Look also at the many villages in this region —
all of them are incomparable places of Krishna's pastimes.

**1696.** O Srinivasa, look: here is Lohavana;
in Lohavana Krishna wondrously herded the cows.

**1697.** This charming place is pervaded by the fragrance of various flowers;
here Bhagavan slew the demon Lohajangha.

**1698.** Its name is Lohajanghavana;
it delivers from all sins.

**1699.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The forest named Lohajanghavana, guarded by Lohajangha —*
*the ninth forest, O Devi — destroys all sins."*[^440]

**1700.** Look at the many enchanting places in this region:
everywhere the son of Nanda always sports.

**1701.** Saying this, [they] had darshan everywhere
of the Deities of Krishna, Balarama, Nrisimha and others.

**1702.** Going near the Yamuna, he says to Srinivasa:
at this ghat Krishna begins the boat pastime.

**1703.** It is a great frolic: Rai with Her friends,
taking milk and other things, comes to cross over.

**1704.** Seeing that unprecedented beauty, Krishna, enchanted,
stays to one side with a very old boat.

**1705.** Sri Radhika with Her friends says again and again,
"Take [us] across, boatman — we must quickly get to the other side!"

**1706.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padyavali, in [the section on] the boat pastime, verse 269:*
*"Glory to Hari, whom crowds of gopis call again and again, 'Take [us] across the Yamuna!' —*
*while He, pretending to lie down on the bank by the boat, becomes twice as lazy."*

**1707.** At last Krishna, seating [them] in that boat,
moves off a little way with great joy in His heart.

**1708.** What frolic arose I am unable to tell;
the poets have described this sport at length.

**1709.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padyavali, in the same place, verses 272 and 274–276:*
*"The boat is old, the river's waters are very deep,*
*we are young girls: thus everything [portends] disaster.*
*But this alone is the seed of rescue for us slender-waisted ones:*
*that You, O Madhava, are now the helmsman.*

**1710.** *At Your word alone, O Yadunandana, I at once threw into the water*
*the load of milk products and even my necklace;*
*I have also removed the silk cloth from these breasts —*
*and still the bank of the daughter of Kalinda is not near.*

**1711.** *Filled with floods of water and spun about by the winds,*
*this boat is about to sink into the deep waters of the Kalindi.*
*Alas, my ill fate! And Hari, His heart seized with great amusement,*
*still claps His hands again and again.*

**1712.** *My hands never rest from bailing out the water,*
*and still You go on with Your mocking words.*
*If I live, then never again,*
*O Krishna, will I set foot in Your boat."*

**1713.** Coming to Mahavana, Sri Pandita, absorbed in prema,
gently says to Srinivasa and Narottama:

**1714.** Look at the home of Nanda and Yashoda in Mahavana;
what sports took place here — who knows how to describe them?

**1715.** Look: here is the birthplace of Sri Krishnachandra;
seeing the face of the son here, Nanda and the others were overwhelmed.[^441]

**1716.** The cowherd men and women of Vraja come running into this courtyard;
here the festival of the son's birth took place.

**1717.** For the son's welfare Nanda gave many gifts;
supreme, wondrous happiness pervaded the world.

**1718.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the eighty-ninth verse:*
*"Where, at the great festival of the appearance of the Enemy of Mura, the lord of Vraja joyfully*
*gave the brahmanas twice nine lakhs of cows adorned with the splendour*
*of strings of large pearls set in gold, and also, with respect, divine ornaments,*
*mountains of jewels, prasthas of sesame and more — to that Brihat-kanana I bow."*[^442]

**1719.** *In the Gitavali of the Stavamala, the first [song] on Nanda's festival (raga Bhairava):*
*Yashoda gave birth to a wondrous son —*
*the throng of cowherds became exceedingly joyful. (Refrain.)*

**1720.** *Someone brings various gifts,*
*someone dances again and again.*

**1721.** *Someone sweetly sings a song,*
*someone scatters fresh butter with yogurt.*

**1722.** *Someone fulfils [others'] desires,*
*someone beholds the eternal form.*[^443]

**1723.** *Again in the same [work] (raga Ashavari):*
*The host of brahmanas is amply endowed with ornaments and cows;*
*now quickly satisfy singers like me as well, O lord of Vraja!*

**1724.** *O King Nanda, this wondrously beautiful son has been born to you!*
*Give the people of the goshtha what they desire — gifts befitting the festival. (Refrain.)*

**1725.** *The heart of people like me, rejoicing in the festival of beholding your son,*
*desires that wealth which none of the petitioners has received.*

**1726.** *In the blue swan sporting in the Manasa lake of Sri Sanatana's heart —*
*in this child of yours may the love of people like us always abide.*[^444]

**1727.** O Srinivasa, here is the limit of happiness:
here all the customary rites for Krishna's birth were performed.

**1728.** Look, here is the place of Nanda's cowshed;
here Nanda told Gargacharya what was in his heart.

**1729.** Out of fear of Kamsa, Garga secretly,
right here, with a joyful heart, performed the name-giving ceremony of Rama and Krishna.[^445]

**1730.** Here the prince of Vraja slew Putana;
right here the cremation of Putana['s body] took place.

**1731.** O Srinivasa, Krishna, while lying on His bed,
broke the cart right here.

**1732.** Lying on His back, Krishna is exceedingly beautiful;
seeing the wondrous pastime of [His] infancy, one is amazed.

**1733.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Padyavali, [in the description of] Sri Krishna's infancy, the hundred and thirtieth verse:*
