*"Whose hands and feet are deep red, whose tilaka of lovely gorochana shines,*
*who forcibly overturned the cart — the enemy of Mura, lying on His back, I worship."*[^446]

**1734.** Here Krishnachandra, climbing into His mother's lap,
drinks her breast milk in a most wondrous manner.

**1735.** Yashoda, gazing at Krishna's face,
overwhelmed with joy, nurses Him at her breast.

**1736.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padyavali, in the same place, the hundred and thirty-first verse:*
*"When with half-opened eyes He drinks His fill at one breast,*
*and with His hand strokes the other, wet with milk that has just flowed,*
*and His mother caresses Him with her fingers, and His face again and again breaks into a smile —*
*may the luster of Vishnu's teeth, white with the bright gleam of drops of milk, protect you."*

**1737.** Here Yashoda in great happiness coaxes Krishna to her;
He crawls on all fours — what a sweet smile on His face!

**1738.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Padyavali, in the same place, the hundred and thirty-second verse:*
*"Him whose eyes dart about at the sounds the mistress of the cowherd settlement makes with her lips,*
*who crawls over the ground on His two knees,*
*whose lips shine sweet with the nectar of a slight, fresh smile —*
*that child, dark as a tamala leaf, I worship."*

**1739.** Here, whatever the gopis ask Krishna about,
Krishna shows by pointing with His finger.

**1740.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place, the hundred and thirty-third verse:*
*"'Where is the face? Where are the eyes? Where is the nose?*
*Where is the ear, and where the topknot?' — asked in this way,*
*the Lord, placing the petal of His finger here and there,*
*delighted the crowd of cowherd women."*

**1741.** Here Krishna, grey with dust, laughs;
seeing [this], the mother says so much to her son in a soft voice.

**1742.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place, the hundred and thirty-fourth verse:*
*"Only just now [Your] body was washed and anointed with ointment —*
*and already, Krishna, Your body is grey with dust."*

**1743.** Supremely beautiful Krishna, sitting right here,
looks at His mother, wanting to drink milk.

**1744.** Here the wicked Trinavarta, seizing Krishna,
rose up into the sky, greatly elated.

**1745.** In great playfulness Krishna, looking about in all four directions,
slays here Trinavarta from Kamsa's abode.[^447]

**1746.** Here Krishna happily ate earth;
the queen of Vraja saw the universe in Krishna's mouth.

**1747.** For this reason this place is called Brahmanda-ghat.[^448]
Look at the wonderful beauty of the bank of the Yamuna.

**1748.** Yashoda, joyfully sitting together with the gopis,
watches the charming beauty of her son in this courtyard.

**1749.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place, the hundred and thirty-fifth verse:*
*"Five years old, very playful, running about the courtyard,*
*His eyes covered by His locks,*
*adorned with little bells, bracelets, a necklace and anklets —*
*bow down to the son of Nanda."*

**1750.** How Krishna in infancy displays youth —
poets joyfully describe this wondrous matter.

**1751.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place, [on] youth even in infancy, the hundred and thirty-sixth verse:*
*"Having placed lips to lips, neck to neck, tenderly eyes to eyes,*
*forehead to forehead, a gopi in agitation*
*laid the crying Krishna on her bosom, [thinking,] 'He is a child'; and He, for a long time*
*hiding His thrill, smiling, His body languid with love — may He protect [you]."*

**1752.** *In the same place, the hundred and thirty-eighth to the hundred and fortieth verses:*
*"When the Wearer of the forest garland performs childlike antics on His father's lap,*
*a row of smiles of the ever-new young wives of the cowherds blossoms forth."*

**1753.** *"'How much fresh butter did You take?' — so Yashoda asked Krishna;*
*'This much,' [He showed] before the elders, taking hold of Dhanishtha's breast. May He protect [you]."*[^449]

**1754.** *"'Where are you going, thief? Your delight is plain to see.*
*You are carrying here in your bodice a second ball of mine —*
*drop it!' — [so saying,] forcibly pressing the breasts of a young gopi,*
*Keshava, all aglow with thrills, is victorious in Gokula."*

**1755.** Here Krishna ponders in His mind [His] fear of His mother;
in stealing fresh butter He is exceedingly skilled.

**1756.** *In the same place, the hundred and forty-first verse:*
*"[The childhood] in which the pot of fresh butter is spotted from afar, in which one hurries crawling on the knees,*
*in which the face is turned away for fear of the mother — that indescribable childhood of Keshava I worship."*

**1757.** Here Krishna in a dream addresses the gods;
hearing those words, the mother is anxious at heart.

**1758.** *In the same place, the hundred and forty-seventh verse:*
*"'Shambhu, welcome! Sit here, here. Lotus-born, on the left.*
*Enemy of Krauncha, is all well? Lord of the gods, are you happy? Lord of wealth, you are not to be seen' —*
*hearing such words of the sleeping enemy of Kaitabha, His mother [said],*
*'What, what improper things are You babbling, child?' — may her 'thoo-thoo' protect you."*[^450]

**1759.** Here Nanda and Yashoda, to put Krishna to sleep,
tell [Him] in various ways the story of Sri Rama and others.

**1760.** *In the same place, the hundred and fifty-first and hundred and fifty-second verses:*
*"'There was [a king] named Rama.' — 'Mm.' — 'His wife was Sita.' — 'Mm.' — 'While he, at his father's word,*
*was living in the Panchavati forest, Ravana carried her off' — hearing from His mother*
*this ancient story about Himself, Krishna [cried out], 'Saumitri, where is the bow, the bow, the bow?!' —*
*may those agitated words protect you."*[^451]

**1761.** *"'It is night, the moon is high — You are not asleep, child?' — 'Mother, sleep does not come to me.' —*
*'To fall asleep, listen, son, to an extraordinary story.' — 'Tell it.' —*
*'From a pillar Narahari appeared, to tear apart a Danava' —*
*when this was said, a smile arose on the son of Devaki."*[^452]

**1762.** Here Yashoda tied Krishna to the mortar;
Krishna playfully accepted being bound.

**1763.** This is the holy place, Yamalarjuna-bhanjana-tirtha;[^453]
wondrous is the beauty of the kunda, and [its] water is perfectly clear.

**1764.** Bathing and fasting [here] bring endless fruit;
by coming to Mahavana [one] becomes honoured in the world of Indra.

**1765.** Look at Gopishvara, the destroyer of great sins;[^454]
Mahavana, dear to Krishna, is full of Krishna's pastimes.

**1766.** Look, here is the Sapta-samudrika well;[^455]
the fruits of offering pinda and so on [here] are declared in that Purana.

**1767.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The eighth [forest] is Mahavana; it is always dear to Me.*
*Going there, a man becomes honoured in the world of Indra.*[^456]

**1768.** *There too is Yamalarjuna-tirtha and a kunda,*
*where the cart was overturned and its pots and jars broken.*

**1769.** *By bathing and fasting there one obtains endless fruit.*
*There too [abides] Gopishvara, the destroyer of great sins."*

**1770.** O Srinivasa, Krishna Chaitanya here,
seeing how [the songs of] the birth festival were sung, rejoiced at heart.[^457]

**1771.** In a rapture of feeling the Lord became absorbed in dancing and singing;
by His mercy He drew all hearts.

**1772.** Seeing the Lord, people come running from all four directions;
losing their composure, they cry out, "Hari! Hari!"

**1773.** From everyone's eyes tears flow ceaselessly;
all say, "This is no sannyasi — this is certainly Krishna."

**1774.** Maddened by prema for the Lord, all the people
say so much and fall rolling on the ground.

**1775.** Who has the power to understand the ways of Sri Gaurachandra?
In Mahavana a great ocean of bliss arose.

**1776.** Seeing Madana-gopala, [the Lord] lost His composure;[^458]
who could describe this unearthly pastime of the Lord?

**1777.** O Srinivasa, look at this place:
right here lived Sanatana Gosvami.

**1778.** All the fortunate residents of Mahavana,
just by seeing Sanatana, would come to life.

**1779.** Sanatana, receiving great happiness
from the darshan of Madana-gopala, lived in Mahavana.

**1780.** This is Ramanaka-valu, on the bank of the Yamuna;[^459]
here Madana-gopala plays merrily.

**1781.** One day, with the children of the residents of Mahavana,
He came to this divine sandbank in the form of a cowherd boy.

**1782.** They play many games; seeing this, Sanatana
thinks, "This is no ordinary child."

**1783.** When, having finished playing, the boy went off [home],
Sanatana followed behind Him.

**1784.** The boy enters the temple, and there too goes Sanatana;
not seeing the boy, he sees Madanamohana.

**1785.** Bowing down to Madana-gopala, Sanatana
returned to his lodging without saying anything.

**1786.** Madana-gopala is subject to the Gosvami's prema;
His pastimes, full of rasa, spread throughout the world.

**1787.** Look at this well — everyone calls it Gopa-kupa.[^460]
Sri Gokula and Mahavana — the two are one.

**1788.** This Sri Gokula-Mahavana is exceedingly beautiful;
one after another [here] are the dwellings of Upananda and the other cowherds.

**1789.** In Gokula are Krishna's surpassing childhood pastimes,
by which the hearts of the cowherd men and gopis rejoice.

**1790.** O Srinivasa, this tree is ancient;
look at the beauty of this tree: it cannot be described.

**1791.** The residents of Gokula find refreshment here;
Gauranga, coming to Gokula, sat right here.

**1792.** How the Lord came here —
who could describe it in detail?

**1793.** From Prayaga, gradually reaching Agravana,
He soon arrived at the ashrama of Jamadagni.[^461]

**1794.** His wife was Renuka; the village is named Renuka —
[the place] where Sri Parashurama took birth.[^462]

**1795.** From Renuka, quickly, by way of Rajagrama,
coming to Gokula, He stayed under this tree.[^463]

**1796.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Chaitanya-charita, in the fourth prakrama, in the second sarga:*[^464]
*"Then, coming to Prayaga and seeing the Lord Sri Madhava,*
*filled with the nectar of the bliss of prema, He danced together with His companions.*

**1797.** *Seeing the glorious Akshaya-vata and bathing at the Triveni,*
*plunging into the Yamuna with the playfulness of an intoxicated elephant,*[^465]

**1798.** *with deep, thunderous roars of hunkara, covered with tears of prema and thrills,*
*proceeding [onward], He gradually crossed it and saw Agravana.*

**1799.** *There too is the village named Renuka, where the lord of warriors*
*and the great-souled Jamadagni descended upon this holy land.*[^466]

**1800.** *There, seeing the Yamuna, which ever flows toward Vrindavana,*
*and then going to Rajagrama and beholding Gokula, He was beside Himself."*

**1801.** Here, in great rapture in nama-sankirtana,
[the Lord] went with many people to the birthplace of Krishna.

**1802.** O Srinivasa, here, at the height of happiness,
all the rites customary at Krishna's birth were performed.

**1803.** Here all the elderly gopis with great joy
merrily sing auspicious songs about Krishna.

**1804.** Here sit Nanda and the other cowherds,
skilled in all kinds of counsel among themselves.

**1805.** Here, seeing various calamities from time to time,
all resolved: "Let us go and live in Vrindavana."

**1806.** The cowherds from Gokula, Raval and other [villages] —
look, by this path they all went to Vrindavana.

**1807.** On the way, in great merriment, beside Bhandiravana
they crossed the Yamuna in supreme joy.

**1808.** The calves and the rest were all gathered in one place;
therefore there [it] is called the village of Sakarauri.[^467]

**1809.** O Srinivasa, look at this village of Raval:
here is the incomparable dwelling of Vrishabhanu.

**1810.** Here Sri Radhika appeared,
and with her appearance happiness spread through the worlds.

**1811.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the ninetieth verse:*
*"Where in the mine of Kirtida's womb, praised by gods, sages and men eager for bliss,*
*there appeared the jewel — the birth of Gandharva — here, in the chief city of Vrishabhanu,*
*called Raval, surrounded by gopis, gopas and herds of cows,*
*may my flood of love abide."*[^468]

**1812.** *Thus [it is said] in a song:*
What bliss today in the house of Vrishabhanu!
From the womb of Krittika Radhika-devi is born.[^469]

**1813.** With the radiance of her beauty she lights up the ten directions;
whoever sees [her] even once, his sorrows go far away.

**1814.** Her most tender body surpasses golden fresh butter;
ah, I die! How graceful the curve of every limb!

**1815.** Mother and father cannot keep their composure;
with what tender longing they gaze again and again at the moon-like face!

**1816.** The joyful cries of "Glory, glory!" have filled the world;
the wives of the cowherds sing auspicious songs.

**1817.** All kinds of instruments sound, full of sweetness;
all the people dance and say, "Wonderful, wonderful!"

**1818.** Scattering curd, milk and turmeric about the courtyard,
they laugh and make [others] laugh, displaying so many antics.

**1819.** To the brahmanas and the bards gifts of every kind are given;
seeing this merriment, Ghanashyama is drunk with happiness.

**1820.** *Again:*
What bliss today fills Vraja!
Putting on new garments and ornaments, the wives of the cowherds run —
they cannot hold on to their composure. (Refrain.)

**1821.** In what wondrous attire they enter the house,
the cowherds, carrying loads on their shoulders!
Vrishabhanu, the jewel among kings, considers himself rich,
gazing at the moon of the little girl's face.

**1822.** Subhanu and Suchandrabhanu cannot keep their bodies steady;[^470]
all the cowherds dance, surrounding them.
Instruments of many kinds sound, they sing songs, drunk with prema,
waving their cloths, whirling round and round.

**1823.** One [pours] ghee, curd and milk, another turmeric water
on someone's head, slyly catching the moment.
How great is Mukhara's longing, how many auspicious rites she performs!
Narahari watches this fun.

**1824.** Mother and father, seeing [their daughter's] beauty at the hour of her appearance,
are beside themselves with joy — they cannot turn their eyes away.

**1825.** For their daughter's welfare they give many gifts;
who can describe it? [Only] the fortunate see it.

**1826.** Here Sri Radhika, together with many little girls,
wanders about, and seeing this, her mother rejoices.

**1827.** Here Vrishabhanu sits with his people;
there is no end to telling of the merriment in Raval.

**1828.** O Srinivasa, Gaurachandra with His associates,
coming from Gokula, stayed here.

**1829.** How deep was [His] absorption in ecstasy on seeing the village of Raval —
what to speak of others, even Shesha cannot describe it.

**1830.** From all four sides people come running and raise the name of Hari;
all say, "Look, brother — the Crest-jewel of sannyasis!"

**1831.** Drunk with drinking the nectar of the moon of the Lord's face,
someone, rejoicing, says to someone else:

**1832.** "I have concluded in my mind: this is surely Krishna;
in this guise He wanders in Vraja by His own will."

**1833.** Someone says, "On seeing this golden form,
what is in [my] heart does not come to my lips."

**1834.** Saying so much, the people, by Chaitanya's mercy,
cannot keep the strength of composure — streams pour from their eyes.

**1835.** Having manifested a wondrous pastime here, the Lord
went to Mathura with that Sanodiya brahmana.

**1836.** O Srinivasa, this place is utterly secluded;
here are Radhika's charming childhood pastimes.

**1837.** Speaking so much, [they] stayed the night in Raval;
in the rasa of talks about Krishna the night turned to dawn.

**1838.** In what prema Sri Raghava was immersed
together with Srinivasa and Narottama — who will describe it?

**1839.** Whoever hears all these narrations with care
attains the feet of Radha-Krishna and Chaitanya.

**1840.** In the morning [they] set out from Raval
and, crossing the Yamuna, came to Mathura.

**1841.** [There are] the dwellings of Ugrasena, Vasudeva and Kamsa;
[the place] where Kamsa seized Yashoda's daughter;

**1842.** where Kamsa raised his hand to kill the goddess;
the place where Vasudeva was in prison;

**1843.** the stone on which Vasudeva passed urine;[^471]
the path by which Vasudeva went, carrying Krishna;

**1844.** [the place] where Vasudeva crossed the Yamuna;
the path by which, leaving his son in Gokula, he returned home.

**1845.** Showing Srinivasa all those places,
Raghava Pandita tells so much in detail.

**1846.** Having bathed at Vishrama-tirtha with a joyful heart,
[they] came to the bank of the Krishna-ganga, to Ambika-kanana.[^472]

**1847.** Seeing Sri Ambika-devi and the Shiva called Gokarna,
Srinivasa and Narottama became filled with great happiness.

**1848.** Raghava Pandita says to both, softly, softly:
look at the wondrous place on the bank of the Krishna-ganga.

**1849.** Here the cowherds headed by Nanda, finely equipped,
came to see the festival of the deva-yatra.[^473]

**1850.** Mahadeva called Gokarna and Ambika — both of them —
Nanda-raya worshipped in every manner.

**1851.** In this lovely place Nanda lay asleep,
[and] suddenly he was seized by a huge black serpent.

**1852.** Seeing His father seized by the serpent, Krishna at that very moment,
smiling gently, touched the serpent with His foot.

**1853.** By the touch of the Lord's lotus feet [the serpent] rejoiced at heart;
the serpent's body was gone, [he] gained a divine form.

**1854.** Formerly [he] had been a Vidyadhara named Sudarshana,
and the serpent's body was from a brahmana's curse; [this he] told the Lord.[^474]

**1855.** Having offered praise to the Lord's lovely feet,
Sudarshana went to his own abode.

**1856.** Nanda and the other cowherds, in their love, became greatly joyful;
taking Rama and Krishna with [Their] friends, they returned home.

**1857.** Look: Sri Akrura-tirtha is the best of tirthas;[^475]
it is known everywhere as exceedingly dear to Krishna.

**1858.** What shall I say of the fruit of bathing [here] at the full moon:
[one] is freed from samsara — especially in [the month of] Kartika.

**1859.** The fruit that is gained by bathing in all the tirthas
is gained by bathing at Akrura-tirtha.

**1860.** Whoever bathes at this tirtha during a solar eclipse
gains the fruit of the Rajasuya and the Ashvamedha.

**1861.** *Thus [it is said] in the Saura Purana:*
*"Next is the most excellent Akrura-tirtha,*
*destroying all sins; it is exceedingly dear to Hari.*

**1862.** *A man who bathes at that best of tirthas on the full-moon day*
*is truly freed from samsara — especially at the full moon of Kartika."*

**1863.** *And in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Akrura is indeed the king of tirthas, the supreme secret among secrets;*
*[here] one gains that fruit [which comes] from bathing in all the tirthas.*

**1864.** *And again, having bathed at Akrura when the sun is seized by Rahu,*
*a person gains the fruit of the Rajasuya and the Ashvamedha."*

**1865.** O Srinivasa, in this Akrura-grama
Sri Krishna Chaitanya Prabhu lived in seclusion.

**1866.** In Vrindavana [there were] crowds of people; for this reason He
would come here and, with a joyful heart, take His meal.[^476]

**1867.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya Prabhu, the purifier of the worlds —
who will understand the workings of His mind?

**1868.** Look, Srinivasa: in this most lovely place
the sages headed by Angira performed a sacrifice.[^477]

**1869.** For food Krishna sent His friends here;
at the words of the cowherd boys the brahmanas grew angry.

**1870.** The friends went and reported everything to Krishna;
then Krishna sent [them] to the sages' wives.

**1871.** The sages' wives, with great joy of heart,
brought food here and gave it to Krishnachandra.

**1872.** Together with His companions Krishna eats the food right here;
there is no end to the merriment at the meal.

**1873.** Everyone's heart was filled with great bliss;
all know [this place] by the name Bhojana-sthala.[^478]

**1874.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the eighty-sixth verse:*
*"Ah! Here the glorious company of the beautiful wives of the learned sacrificers themselves, with joy*
*and devotion, fed to His heart's content Achyuta, surrounded by loving friends, together with Rama,*
*with four kinds of food, rich in qualities, putting nectar to shame —*
*to this place and to that [company] we bow."*[^479]

**1875.** O Srinivasa, behold the beauty of Vrindavana:
what can compare with it? — it captivates the minds of the kings of yogis and the kings of sages.

**1876.** Vrindavana, served by Vrinda, dear to Krishna,
destroys all sins; this [forest] is hard to attain and beautiful.

**1877.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"The twelfth [forest] is Vrindavana, protected by Vrinda;*[^480]
*O Earth, it is dear to Me and destroys all sins.*

**1878.** *There I shall play together with the gopis and the cowherd boys;*
*[this place] is delightful, renowned, and hard to attain for the gods and the Danavas."*

**1879.** Brahma, Rudra and others are devoted to the service of Vrindavana;
the sages meditate on Vrindavana unceasingly.

**1880.** As Lakshmi, devoted to bhakti, is most beloved,
so Vrindavana is dear to Govinda.

**1881.** There, where Mount Govardhana shines in its splendour,
Rama and Krishna with Their friends are engaged in herding the cows.

**1882.** Containing all the tirthas, it gives liberation to every living being;
Vrindavana, the abode of bliss, destroys all sorrows.

**1883.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, in the Mathura-khanda:*
*"Then [comes] holy Vrindavana, which has taken shelter of Vrinda-devi;*
*Hari presides in it, and Brahma, Rudra and others serve it.*

**1884.** *Vrindavana is dense, vast, spreading far and wide,*
*full of the ashramas of sages, with forest thickets of tulasi.*

**1885.** *As Lakshmi, ever devoted to bhakti, is the most beloved,*
*so Vrindavana on earth is Govinda's most beloved [place].*

**1886.** *Together with the calves and heifers Madhava plays,*
*dwelling within Vrindavana, with Rama, surrounded by the boys.*

**1887.** *Oh, delightful is Vrindavana, where Mount Govardhana [stands];*
*there are many tirthas there, made by the god Vishnu."*

**1888.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, in the Nirvana-khanda:*
*"The forest called the root of bliss destroys great sins,*
*removes all sorrows and gives liberation to every living being."*

**1889.** Vrindavana is a forest ever new;
the gopis are enchanted by the beauty of Vrindavana.

**1890.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Tenth [Canto], in the eleventh chapter, the twenty-eighth verse:*[^481]
*"The forest named Vrindavana, good for the cattle, with fresh groves,*
*is fit for the cowherd men, the gopis and the cows; [there are] holy hills, grasses and creepers."*

**1891.** *Thus [it is said] there, in the twenty-first chapter, the tenth verse:*[^482]
*"O friend, Vrindavana spreads the glory of the earth,*
*for it has gained its beauty from the lotus feet of the son of Devaki.*
*At Govinda's flute the intoxicated peacocks dance;*
*seeing this, all the other creatures on the mountain slopes stand still."*

**1892.** O Srinivasa, it is established in all the shastras:
Vrindavana is Krishna's most beloved abode.

**1893.** Animals, birds, trees, insects, humans and others —
whoever lives here attains Krishna's abode in the end.

**1894.** This forest of five yojanas is the form of Krishna's body;
in subtle form the gods and others always remain here.

**1895.** Krishna, who contains all the gods, never leaves it;
appearance and disappearance take place [here] age after age.

**1896.** Radiant Vrindavana is exceedingly beautiful;
without eyes of prema it is beyond the reach of physical eyes.

**1897.** *Thus [it is said] in the Gautamiya[-tantra] — the words of Sri Krishna to Narada:*
*"This delightful Vrindavana is wholly My abode.*

**1898.** *The animals, birds, trees, insects, humans and gods*
*who live here, in My dwelling place, go to My abode when they die.*

**1899.** *The daughters of the cowherds who live here, in My dwelling place,*
*are yoginis, eternally [abiding] with Me, devoted to My service.*

**1900.** *This forest of five yojanas is indeed My body;*
*this Kalindi, called the Sushumna, carries the supreme nectar.*[^483]

**1901.** *Here the gods and [all] beings remain in subtle form;*
*and I, who contain all the gods, never leave this forest.*

**1902.** *Here, age after age, [My] appearance and disappearance take place.*
*This radiant, delightful [abode] is invisible to the physical eye."*

**1903.** O Srinivasa, the greatness of Vrindavana —
each describes it in his own way, but no one finds its limit.

**1904.** Vrindavana is sixteen kroshas: so people say;
but it is renowned in the shastras that it extends over five yojanas.[^484]

**1905.** What people say is not false:
the inconceivable potency of the abode reconciles everything.

**1906.** The fortunate one who sees Govinda in Vrindavana
does not go to the city of Yama — [for this] there is evidence everywhere.

**1907.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"O Vasundhara, those who see Govinda in Vrindavana*
*do not go to the city of Yama; they go to the destination of the doers of good."*

**1908.** In Vrindavana is the abode of Sri Govinda-deva,
always surrounded by servants, exceedingly beautiful.

**1909.** O Srinivasa, what more can be said of it?
Whoever sees it even once has attained the goal [of life] on earth.

**1910.** *Thus [it is said] in the Skanda Purana, in the Mathura-khanda, in the words of Narada:*
*"In that Vrindavana is the holy abode of Govinda,*
*filled with His servants; there indeed I dwell.*

**1911.** *On earth, in that Vrindavana, O king, is Govinda's Vaikuntha;*
*there are Vrinda and the other servants, longing for Govinda.*

**1912.** *Those best of men, O lord of the earth, who have seen in Vrindavana*
*the great house of Govinda, have attained the goal [of life] on earth."*

**1913.** Sri Govinda-deva is the son of the king of Vraja Himself;
by His own will He plays as though He were a Deity.

**1914.** Worldly people see the form of a pratima;[^485]
His own people see Sri Govinda face to face.

**1915.** Taking upon Himself the mauna-mudra and the rest,[^486]
He gives His associates waves of the rasa of happiness.

**1916.** In Vrindavana, in the whorl of an eight-petalled lotus,
He delights with His Beloved — in what wondrous splendour!

**1917.** *Thus [it is said] in the Atharva-veda:*[^487]
*"In [the place] called Gokula, in Mathura-mandala, in the midst of Vrindavana, in a thousand-petalled lotus, in the middle of the sixteen petals, on the filaments of the eight petals, Govinda shines: dark, in yellow garments, two-armed, with a peacock feather on His head, with flute and staff in His hands, without qualities and with qualities, formless and with form, without activity and active.*[^488] *On His two sides are Chandravali and Radha," and so on.*

**1918.** *Thus [it is said] in a statement of the Sammohana-tantra:*
*"To Radha, the mistress of the Yogapitha, [abiding] together with Govinda,*
*full of abundant havas and bhavas, I bow unceasingly."*[^489]

**1919.** The Yogapitha in Vrindavana is the greatest wonder;[^490]
on the Yogapitha is Govinda's wondrous beauty.

**1920.** *Thus [it is said] in the Padma Purana, in the Vrindavana-mahatmya. Parvati said:*
*"What is the wonderful, amazing nectar of Govinda's beauty?*
*I wish to hear of it — tell me, O ocean of mercy."*

**1921.** *Ishvara said:*
*"In the middle of delightful Vrindavana, adorned with lovely mandara trees,*
*adorned with the branches and shoots of those trees rising a yojana high,*

**1922.** *in [that] shelter of the rasa of great bliss, is a great place, a great abode,*
*served by swarms of bees intoxicated by the fragrance of shoots and flowers.*

**1923.** *There, below, on the Siddha-pitha, is Govinda's imperishable place,*
*a place with seven enclosures, ever sought by the Shrutis.*

**1924.** *There, on a pure golden seat adorned with a jewelled pavilion —*
*in its midst is the brilliant Yogapitha of beautiful workmanship.*

**1925.** *It is built with eight corners, charming with manifold radiance,*
*and above it shines a throne of rubies and gold.*

**1926.** *On it is an eight-petalled lotus; in its whorl, the abode of happiness,*
*is Govinda's beloved place: what can be said of its glory?*

**1927.** *Here abides beautiful Govinda, served by multitudes of gopis —*
*Krishna, the lord of Vrindavana, with the divine form and age of Vraja,*[^491]

**1928.** *the lord of Vraja, ever full of majesty, the sole beloved of the beauties of Vraja,*
*bearing a wondrous form in the age of budding youth."*

**1929.** The lord of Vrindavana, Sri Govinda, the abode of prema,
always delights on the throne together with Radha.

**1930.** At the eight corners of the Yogapitha [He] is surrounded by the prakritis;[^492]
the throne, the jewelled pavilion and the rest are incomparable.

**1931.** *Thus [it is said] in the Varaha-tantra, in the fifth patala. Sri Varaha said:*
*"The whorl [of the lotus] is that great abode, Govinda's imperishable place;*
*above it is a golden seat adorned with a jewelled pavilion."*

**1932.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*"In the whorl is the great lila; on the mountain full of the rasa of that lila,*
*where Krishna is the eternal lord of the forest of Vrinda,*

**1933.** *Krishna became Govinda — what need of many other words?*
*The third petal is delightful — the best of all, the highest of the highest."*

**1934.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*"Govinda's beloved place — what can be said of its glory?*
*There abides Govinda, the beloved of the multitudes of gopis,*

**1935.** *with the divine form and age of Vraja, increasing the love of the gopis,*
*the lord of Vraja, holding back His majesty, the sole beloved of the children of Vraja."*

**1936.** *Thus [it is said]. The Earth said:*
*"Krishna is the supreme cause, called Govinda, higher than the highest,*
*the eternal lord of Vrindavana, the sole cause of the attributeless."*

**1937.** *Varaha said:*
*"Govinda together with Radha is seated on a golden throne —*
*with the beauty of form described before, with divine ornaments, most beautiful,*

**1938.** *bending in three places, lovely, most tender, the pupil of the gopis' eyes.*
*There, on the Yogapitha adorned with the golden throne, —*

**1939.** *absorbed in ardour in every limb, the principal beloveds of Krishna —*
*are the prakritis headed by Lalita, and the original prakriti, Radhika.*

**1940.** *In front is the goddess Lalita, and in the north-west Shyamala;*
*in the north Sri Madhumati, in the north-east Dhanya; Hari's beloved*[^493]

**1941.** *Vishakha is likewise in the east; after her, Shaibya in the south-east;*
*Padma in the south, Bhadra in the south-west: so they are placed in order.*

**1942.** *At the tips of the corners of the Yogapitha is the charming beloved Chandravali;*
*and the eight prakritis other than her are principal beloveds of Krishna.*

**1943.** *The principal and original prakriti is Radhika, surpassing all;*[^494]
*Chitravesha, Vrinda, Chandra, Madana-sundari,*

**1944.** *Supriya, Madhumati, Shashirekha, Haripriya —*
*in order, beginning from the front, are likewise placed in the cardinal and intermediate directions.*

**1945.** *Sixteen years old, the best of the prakritis, the principal beloved of Krishna —*[^495]
*is Radha, the Queen of Vrindavana; likewise Lalita too is dear [to Him]."*

**1947.** *Thus [it is said] in the Gautamiya-tantra:*[^496]
*"[Him who sits] on a jewelled throne joined to a jewel mountain,*
*in a golden pavilion amid the wish-fulfilling trees."*

**1948.** Govinda's sweetness intoxicates the world:
whoever sees Him even once finds nothing [else] pleasing.

**1949.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu, in the Purva-vibhaga, in the second lahari, the hundred and eleventh verse:*[^497]
*"Smiling, bending in three places, casting wide sidelong glances,*
*with lips soft as shoots placed on the flute, resplendent with a peacock feather —*
*this form of Hari called Govinda, here near Keshi-tirtha,*
*do not look at, O friend, if you [still] delight in the company of your kin."*

**1950.** Govinda, whose form is eternity, knowledge and bliss, is beautiful;
with the mauna-mudra, two-armed, exceedingly enchanting.[^498]

**1951.** *Thus [it is said] in the Gopala-tapani, in the Purva-vibhaga, the eleventh verse:*[^499]
*"With beautiful lotus eyes, like a cloud, clad in a garment the colour of lightning,*
*two-armed, with the mauna-mudra, the Lord wearing the forest garland,*

**1952.** *surrounded by cowherds, gopis and cows, sheltered beneath the tree of the gods,*
*adorned with divine ornaments, abiding in the middle of a jewelled lotus,*

**1953.** *fanned by the breeze that touches the waves of the Kalindi's waters —*
*meditating in the heart on such a Krishna, a person is freed from the cycle of births."*

**1954.** *In the same place, the thirty-fifth verse:*
*"That one Govinda, whose form is eternity, knowledge and bliss," and so on.*

**1955.** O Srinivasa! In sweet Sri Vrindavana,
who does not bow down at the feet of these three?

**1956.** Sri Govinda, Gopinatha, Madanamohana —
the feet of these three are everyone's all in all.

**1957.** Madana-gopala is called Madanamohana;
this name is renowned — everyone knows it.

**1958.** *In the Gopala-tapani, in the Purva-vibhaga, the thirty-seventh, forty-first and forty-third verses:*
*"To Gopala, to Govardhana, to Gopinatha, to Govinda — obeisance, obeisance!"*[^500]

**1960.** O Srinivasa! There is no end to telling of this;[^501]
all this is proclaimed in the Urdhvamnaya-tantra.[^502]

**1961.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Sri Parvati said:*
*"Who is this Govinda-deva whom you mentioned before?*
*What is His glory like, and what is His nature, O Shankara?"*

**1962.** *Sri Mahadeva said:*
*"Gopala is Govinda Himself; He is ever both manifest and unmanifest.*
*In Vrindavana, on the Yogapitha, He abides perpetually.*

**1963.** *In all four yugas He is the lord of Sri Vrindavana;*
*worshipped by Nanda-gopa and others, He is deeply worshipped even by Krishna.*[^503]

**1964.** *The stealer of the garments of the maidens of Vraja, the fulfiller of their vow,*
*whose form is a stone full of consciousness and bliss, He pervades the Vraja-mandala.*[^504]

**1965.** *Having passed beyond kaishora, He abides [so] day after day;*
*His mouth is honoured with tambula; He is the lord of Radhika's life.*

**1966.** *With four banks inlaid with jewels, full of swans, lotuses and so on,*
*[there is] a pond named Brahma-kunda; to its south*

**1967.** *shines a jewelled pavilion surrounded by mandara trees;*
*in its midst is the supreme seat of sovereignty called the Yogapitha.*

**1968.** *Here Krishna, coloured by the abundant rasa of sovereignty of the Queen of Vrindavana,*
*was conquered by Radha with Her bold laughter.*

**1969.** *Ever [here] are Vrinda — His bodily splendour — and Vira, who accomplishes everything;*
*to the east of the Yogapitha stands [she] who is called Lilavati.*[^505]

**1970.** *In the south stands Shyama, who delights in Krishna's play;*
*in the west there always abides the goddess named Bhagini.*[^506]

**1971.** *In the north there ever stands the goddess named Siddheshi.*
*In the east stands the Five-faced, and in the south the Ten-faced;*[^507]

**1972.** *in the west, the Four-faced; in the north, the Thousand-faced.*
*And she who holds a golden cane in her hand, appointed to command everywhere,*

**1973.** *named Madanonmadini, the dear friend of Radhika,*
*leads to the tree Govinda, who is distraught with mana.*[^508]

**1974.** *Even she who heightens the pride of the husband of Rati himself, here, in the abode of the form of the Pair, in the stronghold of Kama's pride,*
*with the sweetnesses of the fresh blue of the sapphire Hari builds up at every step the palace of Manmatha.*[^509]

**1975.** *First the twofold [syllable] of Manmatha, then the word 'shri-krishnaya',*
*after it 'govindaya', and then 'svaha': this twelve-syllable*
*great mantra of Govinda in time brings one to share in purva-anuraga.*[^510]

**1976.** *Next I shall tell [the mantra] of Govinda as the Pair:*
*after [the syllables] of Lakshmi and Manmatha, the words 'radha-govindabhyam namah'.*

**1977.** *Merely by knowing it, Radha and Krishna become gracious.*
*The rishi of these two [mantras] is Kama; the metre is declared to be virat;*

**1978.** *the devata is the eternal Govinda, and also Radha and Govinda;*
*the shakti is the Mistress of the Yogapitha; the six limbs [of nyasa] are with the kama-bijas.*[^511]

**1979.** *Let [one] meditate on Govinda-deva, sweet as a fresh cloud, dancing divine pastimes,*
*with a gleaming wrestler's tuck, holding in both hands the flute and a jewelled staff,*
*charming with the tassels of the broad ends of the spotless yellow cloth laid over His shoulders,*
*complete, Sri Mohanendra, whose right shin is crossed by His other foot.*

**1980.** *Having meditated thus, let him chant the mantra up to four lakhs [of times];*
*at the end of a homa with sesame and ghee, let him worship the Lord and the Lady of the Yogapitha*[^512]

**1981.** *with flowers of champaka, ashoka, tulasi, kahlara, and also with lotuses —*
*[and] he sees directly with his own eyes the Pair, Radha and Govinda.*

**1982.** *Here too the beautiful Madana-gopala is firmly established.*
*Gopala is in the form of kaishora, Govinda in a mature form.*

**1983.** *Compared with these two, Gopinatha is the most beautiful.*
*Gopala, then, is dhiroddhata; as dhirodatta is described*[^513]

**1984.** *Govinda; Gopikanatha is he whose form is dhira-lalita.*
*Gopala has the waist of a lion; graceful in three bends is*

**1985.** *Govinda; Gopikanatha is broad-chested, a connoisseur of love.*
*Three times a day there is now one sweetness, now another, in the Lord of the cowherds*[^514]

**1986.** *in the ravine of Govardhana, variegated with young shoots and so on.*
*Having passed beyond childhood, having gone beyond kaishora,*

**1987.** *immersed in Kandarpa, Sri Govinda shines*
*on this Yogapitha, captivating with many jewels.*

**1988.** *Such is its innate power: [Govinda] is soon pleased.*
*The perfection [attained] at other siddha-pithas over many years*

**1989.** *arises in a single day at the Yogapitha in Vrindavana.*
*In the early morning [it] is like the young sun, at sangava of auspicious radiance,*[^515]

**1990.** *at midday like the full-grown sun, in the afternoon like a lotus petal,*
*in the evening like a heap of vermilion, and at night pure as the moon;*

**1991.** *on dark nights of a dusky radiance, like the rays of a sapphire,*
*and in the rains it always shines with the green light of grass and emerald;*

**1992.** *in autumn like the disc of the moon, in late autumn like a ruby,*
*in winter like a diamond, in spring rosy as young shoots;*

**1993.** *in summer like a flood of nectar: [thus] the Yogapitha shines.*
*Ever covered with sweetnesses, surrounded by ashoka creepers,*

**1994.** *enveloped on all sides, below and above, by the rays of great jewels —*
*"Unassailable for Chandravali," "Temple of Radha's Good Fortune" —*

**1995.** *[it] is named "Sri Ratna-mandapa," and also "Shringara-mandapa,"*
*is named "Saubhagya-mandapa," "Maha-madhurya-mandapa,"*[^516]

**1996.** *is named "Samrajya-mandapa," and also "Surata-mandapa,"*
*is named "Ananda-mandapa," and also "Surata-mandapa" —*
*these are the eight names of the Yogapitha; listen, O Parvati.*[^517]

**1997.** *Whoever recites at dawn the eightfold names*
*of the most great Sri Yogapitha*
*thereby brings Govinda-deva under his sway*
*and attains prema for the Supreme Person."*
*Thus [ends] in the Urdhvamnaya the nineteenth patala, called "The Revelation of the Yogapitha."*[^518]

**1998.** Having said this, Sri Pandita, with jubilation in his heart,
slowly walks on from Bhojana-tila.[^519]

**1999.** Going a little way, he speaks sweet words:
here the sage Saubhari performed austerities.[^520]

**2000.** Look: on the bank of the Yamuna is a secluded place;
everyone knows the village called Gonarakha.[^521]

**2001.** This here is Kaliya-hrada — look, Srinivasa;[^522]
here took place Sri Krishna's most wondrous pastime.

**2002.** Climbing the kadamba of play on the bank of the Kalindi,
He leapt with a plunge into the waters of the Kalindi.

**2003.** He subdues Kaliya in the waters of the Kalindi;
He dances on the hoods of the serpent Kaliya — everyone sees [it].

**2004.** Krishna bestowed His mercy on the serpent Kaliya;
from here He sent him away to Ramanaka-dvipa.[^523]

**2005.** Whoever bathes and so on in this Kaliya-hrada
is freed from all sins without effort.

**2006.** Leaving the body here, [one] goes to Vishnu-loka;
the Puranas say that many other fruits too are obtained [here].

**2007.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Coming to the pool of Kaliya and engaging in play [there], O Vasundhara,*
*merely by bathing there [one] is freed from all sins.*

**2008.** *And whoever gives up his life here goes to My world."*

**2009.** *In the Tenth Canto [of the Bhagavatam], in the sixteenth chapter, the sixty-second verse:*[^524]
*"Whoever, bathing in this place of My play, satisfies the gods and others with its waters,*
*and, having fasted, remembering Me, performs worship, is freed from all sins."*

**2010.** That kadamba, climbing which Krishna leapt into the pool —
the beauty of that huge tree the shastras have revealed.

**2011.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Even now learned men see a great wonder:*
*to the east of Kaliya-hrada [stands] a revered kadamba tree —*

**2012.** *hundred-branched, O wide-eyed one, holy and fragrant;*
*in all twelve months it, charming, auspicious and cool,*
*blossoms, O wide-eyed one, [and] the ten directions shine."*

**2013.** This Kaliya-tirtha destroys sins committed at holy places;[^525]
bathing at Kali-tirtha brings the fulfilment of many aims.

**2014.** *Thus [it is said] in the Saura Purana:*
*"Then comes the tirtha called Kaliya-tirtha, the destroyer of sin,*
*where the Lord, as a child, danced on Kaliya's head.*

**2015.** *Whoever bathes there and worships Vasudeva*
*attains sayujya with Krishna, unattainable for the unfortunate."*[^526]

**2016.** Look: here is the Dvadashaditya-tirtha;[^527]
[here] one obtains the desired fruit — this is known from the Puranas.

**2017.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"A man who has bathed in Surya-tirtha and seen the Adityas, O Vasundhara,*
*reaches the world of Aditya and, having done what was to be done, rejoices.*

**2018.** *On the day of the Sun, at sankranti, at this tirtha, O Vasundhara,*
*[people] obtain what the heart desires — of this there is no doubt."*[^528]

**2019.** *In the Saura Purana:*
*"Then comes the purifying tirtha called the Dvadashaditya-tirtha;*
*merely by the sight of it, people's sin perishes."*

**2020.** O Srinivasa, Krishna, having subdued Kali[ya],
came from Kali-hrada onto this hill.

**2021.** The Suns, knowing that Krishna was badly chilled,
drove away the cold by displaying fierce heat.

**2022.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the eighty-second verse:*
*"Where the enemy of Mura, chilled, by the twelve suns with their fierce heat,*
*full of the weight of bhakti and prema — He of generous deeds, the beautiful one — was joyfully and especially served,*
*where He shines, lovingly surrounded by women and men and by herds of lowing cattle —*
*in this tirtha named Dvadasha-surya I always take refuge."*

**2023.** O Srinivasa, by Mahaprabhu's command
Sanatana, having come to Vraja, settled here.

**2024.** Sanatana had been told that the Lord would come;
for Him he made a place — look — in this solitude.[^529]

**2025.** Seeing Sanatana anxious, Gaurahari
mercifully showed Himself to him here in the guise of a dream.

**2026.** Gaurachandra is seated on a divine throne;
Sanatana, prostrating himself, fell at [His] feet.

**2027.** The Lord, firmly embracing Sanatana
and consoling him in every way, vanished from sight.

**2028.** Wondrous are the Lord's pastimes — who can understand them?
He always sports in Vrindavana as He wishes.

**2029.** Look at the holy place Praskandana: by bathing [here] sin departs;[^530]
and one who gives up life [here] attains Vishnu-loka.

**2030.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"I shall tell of yet another [place] — hear it, O Vasundhara:*
*[there is] an auspicious holy place named Praskandana, which takes away all sins.*

**2031.** *A man who bathes there is freed from all sins;*
*and if he gives up life here, he goes to My world."*

**2032.** O Srinivasa, from the heat of the suns
the cold went away, and sweat appeared on [His] body.

**2033.** That sweat-water mingled with the daughter of the Sun [the Yamuna];
for this reason the tirtha received the name "Praskandana".

**2034.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the eighty-third verse:*
*"From the abundant sweat that arose all over from long exposure to the burning sun*
*and flowed from Govinda's body, an exalted tirtha came into being.*
*Captivating with fragrances flowing from those tender, dense, most beautiful and splendid limbs [of His body],*
*with pure water and brilliant radiance — that Praskandana I honour with praises."*[^531]

**2035.** Having shown Srinivasa the Praskandana-ghat,
[Raghava], absorbed in prema, speaks in most sweet words:

**2036.** Besides Sri Krishna Chaitanya, Lord Advaita
[too] lived for some days in this forest.[^532]

**2037.** Under this banyan He worshipped Krishna;
who can understand His unfathomable intent?

**2038.** How this Lord was born and so on, and how He came here —
listen, Srinivasa, I shall tell of that briefly.

**2039.** Together with the master Madhavendra Puri,[^533] Shachi and Jagannatha,
Lord Advaita appeared [in the world].

**2040.** Advaita's mercy towards living beings is boundless:
under the guise of birth He blessed Bengal.

**2041.** In Bengal, near Shrihatta, is Navagrama;
there [lived] Kuvera Pandita, a descendant of Nrisimha.[^534]

**2042.** Kuvera Pandita was greatly blessed on the path of bhakti;
apart from Krishna's lotus feet, he knew nothing else.

**2043.** Such too was his wife Nabha Devi, devoted to her husband,
worshipped by the world — she is Advaita's mother.

**2044.** The two of them, coming to Shantipura, to the Ganga,[^535]
were ceaselessly immersed in conversation about Krishna.

**2045.** One day Sri Kuvera, together with Nabha,
hearing the blasphemy of Vaishnavas, wished to die.

**2046.** A certain fortunate person, seeing them both all but dead,
by Krishna's will calmed them.

**2047.** Even so, sorrowful, they lay down;
when a little sleep came, he sees a wondrous dream.

**2048.** A beautiful Purusha, full of great effulgence,
whose body surpasses a mountain of molten gold;[^536]

**2049.** this Purusha, to another beautiful Purusha,
holding [His] two hands, speaks most sweet words:

**2050.** "To dispel this suffering of beings stricken by Kali,
quickly descend You upon the earth.

**2051.** When You draw [Me], I shall not be able to stay [away];
with [My] elder brother I shall soon appear [in the world]."

**2052.** Hearing these words, with a heart full of joy,
at an auspicious moment He entered Nabha's womb.

**2053.** Seeing this, the brahmana's joy was boundless;
when his sleep broke, his heart was stirred.

**2054.** The brahmana, a great knower of the scriptures, reflected in his mind:
in Kali the Lord appears hidden.[^537]

**2055.** Thinking much in this way, he was overwhelmed;
together with his wife he could not stop his tears.

**2056.** From that day Nabha was with child;
[they] went again to Navagrama and settled [there].

**2057.** There Lord Advaita appeared;
great jubilation arose in the hearts of [all] the world.

**2058.** Suddenly this voice arose: "Through Him
Sri Krishna Chaitanya will appear on earth.

**2059.** He will soon bring Nityananda-Rama;
with hosts of associates [the Lord] will joyfully sport.

**2060.** He will destroy the sufferings of living beings — there is no more cause for worry;
in every home prema-bhakti will be preached.

**2061.** The ocean of the bliss of sankirtana will surge;
blessed is this [age of] Kali! — no one will be left deprived."

**2062.** Hearing such various voices, all rejoiced;
Kuvera's house became an abode of all that is auspicious.

**2063.** Day by day Lord Advaita grows;
the fortunate see [Him] with jubilant hearts.

**2064.** Advaita always keeps Himself hidden;
at times, by Sri Chaitanya's will, He becomes manifest.

**2065.** Having obtained Advaita, the people of Navagrama
float in bliss, forgetting sorrow and grief.

**2066.** "Kamalaksha" and "Advaita" are the Lord's two names;[^538]
"Advaita" — so all call [Him] ceaselessly.

**2067.** Advaita's childhood pastimes are exceedingly wondrous;
the fortunate see them — who has the power to describe them?

**2068.** Sri Advaita is to everyone like the pupil of the eye;
lying down and in their dreams [people] sing of Advaita's virtues.

**2069.** Blessed are all these people, I say again and again;
blessed is Bengal, where the Lord appeared.

**2070.** Sri Kuvera, full of prema-bhakti, most steadfast,
said to all: "I shall go to the bank of the Ganga."

**2071.** Together with his fellow villagers and beloved friends
he came from Navagrama to Shantipura.

**2072.** He settled in Shantipura with a contented heart;
at times he met his friends in Navadvipa.

**2073.** He carefully had Advaita study the shastras;
the Lord, the purifier of the fallen, became a pandita.

**2074.** Although the father and mother knew the true nature of their son,
out of parental love they kept none of it in memory.

**2075.** All the great panditas living in Shantipura
were amazed to see Advaita's acts.

**2076.** Some said: "Advaita is by no means a human being:
can a human so attract everyone's heart?

**2077.** Blessed is this brahmana Kuvera, who has such a son!
Through Him, it seems, good fortune will come to all."

**2078.** Thus everyone said many things;
Advaitachandra became the life of all.

**2079.** Who can understand the will of Advaita Prabhu?
He gives joy to His mother and father in many ways.

**2080.** After some time His father and mother passed from sight;
the Lord set out to perform [the rites at] Gaya.

**2081.** On the pretext of Gaya He travelled to all the tirthas;
from Madhavendra Puri He received the mantra of initiation.

**2082.** *Thus it was said by the ancients:*[^539]
*"I worship Sri Advaita Prabhu, the giver of prema-bhakti,*
*dear to Sriman Madhavendra Puri, belonging to the Sri Madhva-sampradaya."*

**2083.** Who has the power to understand Advaita's acts?
Intoxicated with prema, He wanders ceaselessly.

**2084.** Wandering on and on, He came to Mathura-mandala;
seeing the beauty of Vraja, bliss welled up [in Him].

**2085.** Having seen everything, He came to Vrindavana;
here the Vrajavasis lovingly sheltered [Him].

**2086.** He eats a little — fruits, roots, milk;
seeing Advaita's radiance, people marvel.

**2087.** Intoxicated with prema, He roars thunderous hunkaras;
"Shall I see Krishna?" — saying this, He weeps.

**2088.** Thus moment by moment various bhavas arise [in Him];
He worships Krishna here, near the Yamuna.

**2089.** Knowing [that] the time of Krishna Chaitanya's appearance [had come],
He departed from here for the land of Gauda.

**2090.** Advaitachandra's pastimes are like nectar;
O Srinivasa, the fortunate relish them.

**2091.** The banyan under which Advaita stayed
became famed everywhere as Advaita-vata.[^540]

**2092.** By the sight of this Advaita-vata all sins are destroyed,
and most rare prema-bhakti becomes attainable.

**2093.** Look at the trees and creepers on the bank of the Kalindi:
ever fresh, exceedingly beautiful.

**2094.** This tamarind tree is very old;
here Radha and Krishna sport together with the sakhis.[^541]

**2095.** Remembering the past, Krishna Chaitanya Gosani
came here and sat down — there was no limit to His happiness.

**2096.** Saying this, the Pandita was overwhelmed with prema;
he tells Srinivasa of the deeds of Gorachand.

**2097.** Sri Gaurasundara is the complete Brahman, the eternal,
the Lord of Navadvipa, Krishna, the son of the king of Vraja.

**2098.** In Navadvipa, in the house of Shachi and Jagannatha Mishra,
the Lord descended at Advaita's hunkaras.[^542]

**2099.** The wondrous pastimes of Gauranga in Navadvipa
cannot be described even with a thousand mouths.

**2100.** Some time after His father's departure,
He came to Gaya, as if following worldly custom, to perform [the rites].

**2101.** There Sri Ishvara Puri, most fortunate,
on seeing Gaurachandra felt as if he had found his life.

**2102.** The life of the devotees, the Lord Sri Gaurasundara,
showed Ishvara Puri the highest respect.

**2103.** When he spoke his own initiation mantra into His ear,
He received the mantra, falling to the ground and bowing down.

**2104.** Having made Ishvara Puri His guru, Gaura-raya
constantly floats in the streams from both His eyes.

**2105.** Having made Vishvambhara, the purifier of the worlds, his disciple,
Sri Ishvara Puri became intoxicated with the bliss of prema.

**2106.** If you say, "Gaurachandra is the guru of the world;
to hear that He has another guru is puzzling,"

**2107.** to this I reply: for the instruction of the people
He Himself, by practising dharma, establishes it.

**2108.** Who can know this unearthly pastime of the Lord?
He Himself glorified the Madhva sampradaya.

**2109.** When one enters a sampradaya, one's purpose is accomplished;
a mantra received in initiation elsewhere is certainly fruitless.

**2110.** The four sampradayas — Sri, Madhva, Rudra and Sanaka —
are known in [the age of] Kali: the Puranas tell [of this] in detail.[^543]

**2111.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Padma Purana:*
*Mantras that are without a sampradaya are considered fruitless.*
*Therefore in Kali four [founders of] sampradayas will appear.*[^544]

**2112.** *Sri, Madhva, Rudra and Sanaka — Vaishnavas who purify the earth —*
*these four will be the founders of sampradayas in Kali.*

**2113.** These four sampradayas hold the right to bhakti;
I shall tell briefly how the names of the sampradayas [arose].

**2114.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya Prabhu, the tree of desires,
in the form of Narayana is the guru of them all.

**2115.** Sri is the beloved of Narayana and also His disciple;
her wondrous deeds are described at length in all the scriptures.

**2116.** The word "Sri" means Lakshmi; her branches and sub-branches
became many — who could write them down?

**2117.** In that circle Ramanuja became the acharya;
from him the Ramanuja sampradaya went forth.

**2118.** Formerly his name was Sri Lakshmanacharya;
with great reverence everyone calls [him] Ramanujacharya.[^545]

**2119.** He composed the *Ramanuja-bhashya*, [named] after himself;
his branches and sub-branches covered the world.

**2120.** O Srinivasa! About the Madhva sampradaya
I shall say a little now, and further on I shall tell what [is due].

**2121.** The disciple of Sri Narayana is the merciful Brahma;
his disciples, their disciples and others filled the world.

**2122.** In that circle Sri Madhva became a disciple;
first of all he composed a commentary on the *Brahma-sutra*.

**2123.** For this reason he received the name Madhvacharya;
from then on the Madhvacharya sampradaya spread.[^546]

**2123a.** Sri Anandatirtha is another name of his;
he is known everywhere and is peerless in all virtues.[^547]

**2123b.** There is no end to his disciples and their disciples;
spreading bhakti, they filled every place.

**2124.** The disciple of Sri Narayana is the compassionate Rudra;
there is no end to his disciples and their disciples.

**2125.** In that circle Vishnusvami became a disciple;
he was intoxicated with the rasa of bhakti together with his disciples.[^548]

**2126.** Supreme power, learning in all the scriptures —
from him came the name "Vishnusvami sampradaya".

**2127.** Hear, Srinivasa, how the Sanaka sampradaya is:
from Narayana — the manifestation of the form of Hamsa.[^549]

**2128.** His disciples are the four great souls, Sanaka and the others;
their disciples and their disciples' disciples cannot be written down.

**2129.** In that circle Nimbaditya became a disciple;
from him the Nimbaditya sampradaya went forth.[^550]

**2130.** Nimbaditya's power is supremely astonishing;
his disciples and their disciples filled the world.

**2131.** In the sampradayas of Sri, Madhva, Rudra and Sanaka
many sampradayas arose by virtue of [their] power.

**2132.** Thus, among the followers of Ramanujacharya,
Ramanandacharya became venerable in every respect.[^551]

**2133.** His disciples, their disciples and others are many;
that sampradaya became known as "Ramanandi".

**2134.** In the Vishnusvami sampradaya Sri Vallabhacharya,
noble in every respect, composed the *Anubhashya*.

**2135.** He became known as [the founder of] the "Vallabhi";
what shall I say — such is the way of the other sampradayas as well.[^552]

**2136.** The Lord glorified the Madhva sampradaya in Kali;
I shall name the Lord's guru and the others, from the very first.

**2137.** The origin of all is the Lord of Paravyoma, Narayana;
His disciple is Brahma, the ornament of Brahma-loka.[^553]

**2138.** His disciple is Sri Narada Muni, full of prema;
Vyasa, the guru of Sri Shuka, is his disciple.

**2139.** The magnanimous Sri Madhva became Vyasa's disciple;
he composed a commentary under his own name — [his] glory is boundless.

**2140.** From then on the Madhvacharya sampradaya went forth;
Srimat Padmanabhacharya became his disciple.

**2141.** His disciple is Narahari, his [disciple] is Sri Madhava,
Sri Akshobhya is his disciple, renowned everywhere.[^554]

**2142.** Jayatirtha is his disciple, his [disciple] is Jnanasindhu;
his disciple is Mahanidhi, the friend of the poor and lowly.

**2143.** His [disciple] is Vidyanidhi, his is the renowned Rajendra,
his is Jayadharma Muni, wondrous in his deeds.

**2144.** In his circle Vishnu Puri became a disciple;
he brought forth the book *Bhakti-ratnavali*.[^555]

**2145.** Jayadharma Muni's disciple, pure in conduct,
is known by the name Sri Purushottama Brahmanya.

**2146.** His disciple is Vyasatirtha, a man of great learning;
it was he who wrote the book *Sri Vishnu-samhita*.[^556]

**2147.** His disciple is Lakshmipati, the abode of virtues;
his disciple is Madhavendra, the rising moon of bhakti.

**2148.** His disciple is Ishvara Puri, the treasury of mercy;
his disciple is the Lord Gaurachandra, Bhagavan.

**2149.** *Thus [it is said] in the Srimad Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika, [composed] by Sri Kavi Karnapura:*
*In the Kali-yuga there appeared four [founders of] sampradayas,*
*called Sri, Madhva, Rudra and Sanaka, as is stated in the Padma [Purana]:*[^557]

**2150.** *"Therefore in Kali four [founders of] sampradayas will appear —*
*Sri, Madhva, Rudra and Sanaka, Vaishnavas who purify the earth."*

**2151.** *Of these, the Madhva sampradaya is described here as the occasion requires.*
*Brahma, the lord of the universe, became the disciple of the Lord of Paravyoma.*

**2152.** *His disciple was Narada, and Vyasa became his disciple;*
*Shuka became Vyasa's disciple by restraining knowledge.*[^558]

**2153.** *His disciples and their disciples abide on earth in great numbers.*
*From Vyasa the renowned Madhvacharya received initiation into [the mantra of] Krishna.*

**2154.** *Having analysed the Vedas, he composed the samhita Shata-dushani,*
*in which Brahman with qualities is established over Brahman without qualities.*[^559]

**2155.** *His disciple was the noble Padmanabhacharya;*
*his disciple was Narahari, and his disciple the brahmana Madhava.*

**2156.** *His disciple was Akshobhya, and his disciple Jayatirtha;*
*his disciple was Jnanasindhu, his disciple Mahanidhi.*

**2157.** *His disciple was Vidyanidhi, his servant Rajendra,*
*his disciple Jayadharma Muni, in whose circle [was]*

**2158.** *Srimat Vishnu Puri, whose work is the Bhakti-ratnavali.*
*The disciple of Jayadharma was Brahmanya Purushottama.*

**2159.** *His disciple was Vyasatirtha, who composed the Vishnu-samhita;*
*his disciple was Srimat Lakshmipati, the shelter of the rasa of bhakti.*

**2160.** *His disciple was Madhavendra, by whom this dharma was set going —*
*the descent of the wish-fulfilling tree that stands in Vraja-dhama*

**2161.** *and bears the fruits called prita, preyas, vatsala and ujjvala.*[^560]
*His disciple was the illustrious renunciant, the Puri named Ishvara.*

**2162.** *Gaura, respectfully accepting Ishvara Puri [as His guru],*
*flooded the world — both the material and the non-material.*

**2163.** The disciple of Ishvara Puri is the Lord Gaura-raya;
the Lord sings Puri's glory with His own mouth.

**2164.** Who can understand the Lord's wondrous bhakti?
From the Lord the Nimananda sampradaya went forth.

**2165.** Among the Lord's names the chief is Nimai Pandita;
Nityananda Prabhu is very fond of this name.

**2166.** Seeing the Lord's Vaishnavas in Nadia,
people to this day extol them as "the Nimai sampradaya".

**2167.** Nimai bestowed bliss upon the world —
for this reason [He] is famed on earth as Nimananda.[^561]

**2168.** As I told earlier how the other sampradayas are,
so, by the Lord's power, is the Madhva sampradaya.

**2169.** *Thus [it is said] in a verse composed by Sri Gopala-guru Gosvami, disciple of Srimat Vakreshvara Pandita:*
*Sriman Narayana, Brahma, Narada and Vyasa,*
*Srila Madhva, Padmanabha, Nrihari and Madhava,*[^562]

**2170.** *Akshobhya, Jayatirtha, Jnanasindhu, Mahanidhi,*
*Vidyanidhi, Rajendra and Jayadharma Muni,*

**2171.** *Purushottama Brahmanya and Vyasatirtha Muni,*
*Srimat Lakshmipati, Sriman Madhavendra Puri, Ishvara [Puri].*

**2172.** *Then Sri Krishna Chaitanya, the tree of prema on earth,*
*He who is famed throughout the circle of the earth by the name Nimananda.*

**2173.** O Srinivasa, Gaurahari, having bestowed His mercy on Ishvara Puri,
set out from Gaya.

**2174.** Formerly in Navadvipa He kept Himself hidden from the devotees;
again He wishes to hide — but He cannot hide.

**2175.** Shortly, Gaurachandra, having come to Nadia,
revealed Himself by the wish of His dear devotees.

**2176.** All the devotees of the Lord — Advaita and the others —
were filled with great joy at heart.

**2177.** The happiness that grew at the meeting with Nityananda —
who could describe it, even with a hundred thousand mouths?

**2178.** With Nityananda, Advaita and the others, Gaura-raya
is ceaselessly intoxicated with sankirtana in Nadia.

**2179.** Performing supremely wondrous deeds day after day,
He resolved to give up the life of a householder.

**2180.** The Lord of the world, Gora, the enchanter of the universe,
out of mercy for living beings accepted sannyasa.

**2181.** Having accepted sannyasa, the Lord was overwhelmed [with love];
Nityananda took [Him] to Advaita's house.

**2182.** To see the Lord Gorachand, the crest-jewel of sannyasis,
people came running, unable to restrain themselves.

**2183.** Gods and men gathered together as one:
hundreds of thousands of people surrounded Advaita's house.

**2184.** All ceaselessly called out, "Hari, Hari!" —
heaven, earth and Patala were struck with wonder.

**2185.** The crest-jewel of sannyasis, Sri Krishna Chaitanya,
granting His darshan, blessed all people.

**2186.** Gaurahari dances in the sankirtana;
His body is adorned with sandal — a wondrous sweetness.

**2187.** On all sides all the devotees of the Lord
together sing the sweetest kirtana.

**2188.** Nityananda, Advaita, Srivasa and Gadadhara
do not leave the Lord's side, their hearts jubilant.

**2189.** Raising His arms, the Lord calls, "Hari, Hari!" —
and in the bliss of sankirtana floats in His tears.

**2190.** At the sight of such a Lord, Chaitanyachand,
the people were overwhelmed [with love], forgetting themselves.

**2191.** Some, stepping aside, said to one another,
"The Vedas describe this Ishvara in the form of a brahmana."

**2192.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sama-veda:*[^563]
*Om. When the seer sees the Golden One, the Creator, the Lord, the Purusha, the source of Brahman,*
*then the wise one, shaking off virtue and sin, stainless, attains the supreme equality.*

**2193.** Someone said, "Mishra Vishvambhara is in the form of a devotee,
endowed with all the marks; He is above all."

**2194.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Hence I, having accepted sannyasa, shall descend — endowed with qualities, detached, without desires, a god on earth, dwelling on the bank of the Alakananda, in Kali, after four thousand years have passed, within five thousand — golden in complexion, long-limbed, endowed with all the marks, entreated by Ishvara, tasting My own rasa, in the form of a devotee, by the name of Mishra, versed in yoga — upon her.*
*So in the third kanda of the Atharvana, after the Brahma-vibhaga.*[^564]

**2195.** Someone said, "In this Kali, in its first sandhya,
the Vedas sing of the oneness with His own shakti in this Gaurachandra."

**2196.** *Thus [it is said] in the Atharva-veda, in the Purusha-bodhini:*
*In the seventh — "of the golden Vishnu": by this — having attained oneness with His own shakti, at the end, at dawn, having descended together with His own, He teaches His own mantra.*[^565]

**2196a.** *Its explanation:*
*In the seventh — in the seventh manvantara, [the manvantara] of Vaivasvata Manu, the golden Bhagavan, with His own shakti — the hladini shakti — having attained oneness, at the end — in the Kali-yuga, at dawn — in the first sandhya, having descended, together with His own — with His associates, His own mantra — "Hare Krishna" and the rest — teaches, instructs the people.*[^566]

**2197.** Someone said, "Look: a golden body, smooth and glossy!
Ah, how wondrous are the ornaments of sandal!"

**2198.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sahasra-nama-stotra:*[^567]
*Of golden colour, with a golden body, with beautiful limbs, [adorned] with sandal and armlets.*

**2199.** Someone said, "Gora is the thief of everyone's life;
the three worlds are intoxicated with His deeds.

**2200.** The yellow colour He assumes in this blessed Kali;
white, red and black — in Satya, Treta and Dvapara."

**2201.** *Thus [it is said] in the Tenth Canto, in the eighth chapter, the thirteenth verse:*[^568]
*Indeed, He had three colours, assuming bodies in each yuga:*
*white, red and likewise yellow; now He has become black.*

**2202.** Someone said, "Within He is black in colour;
without, a captivating golden lustre is manifest.

**2203.** With Nityananda, Advaita and the others He sports;
by the worship of sankirtana He is attained."

**2204.** *Thus [it is said] there too, in the Eleventh Canto, in the fifth chapter, the thirty-second verse:*[^569]
*Black in colour, yet not black in lustre, with [His] angas, upangas, weapons and associates —*
*Him the intelligent worship with sacrifices consisting chiefly of sankirtana.*

**2205.** Someone said, "This Lord is the saviour of all;
there will never again be one so merciful.

**2206.** The dharma of the Kali-yuga is nama-sankirtana;
having descended, He joyfully established dharma."

**2207.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Gita, in the fourth chapter, the eighth verse:*
*For the protection of the righteous, for the destruction of the wicked*
*and for the establishment of dharma I appear from yuga to yuga.*

**2208.** Someone said, "Who can understand the pastimes of the Lord?[^570]
By accepting sannyasa He has made the Kali-yuga blessed."

**2209.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sahasra-nama-stotra:*
*He who accepts sannyasa, self-restrained, serene, devoted to steadfastness and peace.*

**2210.** Someone said, "In Kali the fortune of living beings is great:
having accepted sannyasa, the Lord destroys [their] evil-mindedness."

**2211.** *Thus [it is said] in the Upapurana — the words of Sri Krishna [addressed] to Vyasa:*
*I Myself, O brahmana, at times, having taken the ashrama of sannyasa,*
*make the people of Kali, struck down by sin, accept hari-bhakti.*

**2212.** Someone said, "By the gift of the great mantra of harinama
He Himself destroys the grievous sufferings of living beings."

**2213.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Hare Krishna Hare Krishna Krishna Krishna Hare Hare*
*Hare Rama Hare Rama Rama Rama Hare Hare.*

**2214.** Someone said, "The syllables of the names Hari, Krishna and Rama
give birth to a wondrous meaning — the best of the wise relish it."

**2215.** *Thus [it is said] in the verses composed by Sri Gopala-guru Gosvami:*
*Having made known the truth of Bhagavan — the form full of consciousness and bliss —*
*He removes ignorance and its effects; therefore He is called Hari.*

**2216.** *She, the embodiment of Krishna's bliss, steals the mind of Sri Krishna;*
*therefore by this very [word] "Hara" Sri Radha is glorified.*

**2217.** *The master of the one happiness — bliss, dark, lotus-eyed,*
*the delight of Gokula, the son of Nanda — He is called Krishna.*

**2218.** *The essence and whole wealth of artfulness, the presiding deity of embodied pastimes,*
*ever giving delight to Radhika — He is named Rama.*

**2219.** Thus, speaking of many things, all the people
surrendered themselves at the feet of Sri Chaitanya.

**2220.** The crest-jewel of sannyasis, the Lord Gaura-raya,
thus passes [His days] in bliss in Advaita's house.

**2221.** All who had come from Navadvipa to Shantipura
He comforted at heart in various ways.

**2222.** Having consoled mother Sri Shachi in every way,
He placed the dust of her lotus feet on His head.

**2223.** Shachi Thakurani, overwhelmed with affection,
gave this order: "Stay in Nilachala."

**2224.** By His mother's order the Lord set out;
who can describe how the devotees became?

**2225.** In the feigned guise of a sannyasi, wandering through all lands,
He entered the Mathura-mandala.

**2226.** Taking with Him the Sanodiya brahmana of Mathura,
absorbed in bhakti, He roams Vraja in great joy.

**2227.** Wherever He Himself had formerly performed whatever pastimes —
as though not knowing, He asks everyone about them.

**2228.** To hear [of them] from the mouths of others is boundless joy for Him;
with such sport the son of Shachi is intoxicated.

**2229.** Gradually wandering through the groves and forests,
He came to Vrindavana by way of Mathura.

**2230.** What transformations of feeling [He had] on the bank of the Yamuna —
even with a hundred thousand mouths I could not describe.

**2231.** Countless, countless people come running from all sides,
and, greatly intoxicated with prema, sing of Gaura's qualities.

**2232.** For fear of the crowds the Lord, going to Akrura,
takes His alms there, finding solitude.[^571]

**2233.** From time to time, sitting beneath the tamarind tree,
the Lord in His own bliss floats in His tears.

**2234.** Beneath this tamarind (amli) a great wonder took place:
He showed great mercy to Krishnadasa Rajput.[^572]

**2235.** O Srinivasa, from this Amli-tala
the Lord went to Nilachala, by the wish of the devotees.

**2236.** Whoever beholds this tamarind tree
will surely have his desire fulfilled.

**2237.** Look at this wondrous banyan on the bank of the Yamuna:
everyone calls it Shringaravata.

**2238.** Here Sri Krishna, in various pastimes of dressing and the like,
increased the joy of Subala and His other friends.

**2239.** Some also call it "Nityananda-vata";
whatever anyone says, all of it is true.

**2240.** How Nityananda came here
I shall tell in brief; listen.

**2241.** Nityananda-Rama is one body with Chaitanya;
His birthplace is in Rarh, the village of Ekachakra.[^573]

**2242.** His father was Hadai Pandita, His mother Padmavati;
their life was in their son — what power [have I] to describe their love?

**2243.** The son of Padmavati, in supreme bliss,
manifested various pastimes in the village of Ekachakra.

**2244.** The pastimes [He] had performed in various avataras —
in that mood He showed them all to the people.

**2245.** The people living in the land of Ekachakra are fortunate:
Nityananda-chandra is their wealth and life.

**2246.** Nityananda, increasing everyone's love,
stayed at home for twelve years.

**2247.** Who can understand Nityananda's heart?
Without Sri Krishna Chaitanya He finds no rest.

**2248.** One day Prabhu reflected within Himself:
"If I go there now, it is not fitting.

**2249.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya, having appeared in Navadvipa,
abides in the mood of childhood, keeping Himself hidden.

**2250.** When He reveals Himself and sports with the devotees,
then, going to Navadvipa, I will meet Him.

**2251.** But now I will quickly set out on a pilgrimage to the tirthas."
Reflecting thus, the Lord smiles within Himself.

**2252.** At that time a worthy sannyasi came to the village;
he asks the people where the house of Hado Pandita is.[^574]

**2253.** Learning from the people, he went to the house of Hado Ojha;
seeing the sannyasi, Ojha was filled with great joy.

**2254.** At once Ojha, preparing various dishes,
offered them to the sannyasi for him to eat.

**2255.** The sannyasi says, "Brahmana, I will ask for something;
if you can promise it, then I will eat."

**2256.** Having given his promise, he fed the sannyasi;
on departing, the sannyasi asked for Nityananda and took [Him] away.

**2257.** Nityanandachand, steadying His heart,
goes with the sannyasi, having consoled His father and mother.

**2258.** In this way He left home;
what wise man can understand this most wondrous pastime?

**2259.** Fresh youth, beauty that enchants the worlds —
whoever sees [Him] even once, his eyes are refreshed.

**2260.** In whatever direction Nityananda, full of prema, walks,
in that direction people run with impatient hearts.

**2261.** The Lord, showing mercy to all people,
walks alone with the gait of a great king of elephants.

**2262.** As He made a pilgrimage to the tirthas in Dvapara,
in the same way He [now] wanders through all the tirthas.[^575]

**2263.** Wandering, He went south to Pandurpura;
there the Lord saw Sri Vitthalanatha.[^576]

**2264.** In that village lives a gentle brahmana;
he is a fellow disciple of Sri Madhava Puri.[^577]

**2265.** The brahmana, bringing Nityananda to his own home,
carefully feeds [Him] fruits, roots, milk and so on.

**2266.** The people of Pandurpura are most fortunate:
seeing Nityananda, all of them find solace for their souls.

**2267.** What the Lord's state of mind is — who knows?
Having seen Sri Vitthalanatha, He stays in solitude.

**2268.** Suddenly, in answer to that brahmana's longing,
his guru Lakshmipati came to the village from afar.

**2269.** [He came] with many disciples, learned in all the shastras;
who can describe his affection for his disciples?

**2270.** He is very old, [his] deeds are indescribable;
he is known everywhere — a great noble one on the path of bhakti.

**2271.** Who can tell the glory of Lakshmipati,
whose disciple is Madhavendra Puri? That says it all.

**2272.** Madhavendra Puri is full of the rasa of prema-bhakti;
by remembering his name every perfection is attained.

**2273.** Sri Ishvara Puri, Ranga Puri and the others —
all are disciples of Madhava, intoxicated with the rasa of bhakti.[^578]

**2274.** In Gauda, Utkala and other lands are Madhava's followers;
all are devotees of Krishna, wholly devoted to prema-bhakti.[^579]

**2275.** In the Madhva sampradaya his fame is supreme;
an ocean of virtues, he is very dear to Lakshmipati.

**2276.** Lakshmipati, in the house of that brahmana, his disciple,
took his meal while conversing about Krishna.

**2277.** Lakshmipati says to him again and again,
"What auspiciousness I see in your home today!

**2278.** How many times I have come to your house,
yet such happiness never arose in my heart.

**2279.** From this I understand that some devotee is present here."
The brahmana says, "Your mercy is powerful."

**2280.** By the Lord's will [this] did not come to the brahmana's mind;
thus the day passed in many conversations.[^580]

**2281.** At night, sitting in solitude, the best of sannyasis
sings of the charming deeds of Baladeva.

**2282.** He has unalloyed devotion to Lord Baladeva;
weeping, he speaks, addressing Baladeva:

**2283.** "O Baladeva, show mercy to me, lowly and wicked —
let [Your] fame resound throughout the world!"

**2284.** Saying many such things, he cannot keep his composure:
he rolls on the ground, tears stream from his eyes.

**2285.** He is very old as it is, and on top of that his grief is boundless —
he grew so faint that it cannot be told.

**2286.** In extreme distress the sannyasi cannot calm himself;
suddenly, by the Lord's will, sleep overcomes him.

**2287.** In the form of Balarama, Nityananda, playfully,
appeared to Sri Lakshmipati in a dream.

**2288.** What beauty! It dispels the pride of Kandarpa;
[His] sweet body puts a silver mountain to shame.

**2289.** Arms reaching to the knees, a broad chest;
the enchanting sweep of the eyes reaches to the ears.

**2290.** In [His] ear is a single earring that charms the mind of the world;
tucked under His left arm is a sweet-sounding horn — it looks splendid.[^581]

**2291.** [His] body is adorned with various ornaments;
there is nothing in all the world to compare [Him] with.

**2292.** [His] face surpasses the full moon;
in the guise of speech He pours out an ocean of nectar.

**2293.** To dear Lakshmipati He speaks softly:
"Hearing of your grief, I feel My heart break.

**2294.** O Lakshmipati! Krishna is the Lord of My life;
birth after birth be His servant."

**2295.** Lakshmipati, clasping the Lord's feet, says,
"May such a sense of difference never be in me!"[^582]

**2296.** Hearing these words of Sri Lakshmipati,
Lord Baladeva, smiling, says something:

**2297.** "To this village has come the son of a brahmana;
in the guise of an avadhuta he will become your disciple.

**2298.** With this mantra you will make him your disciple."
Saying this, He speaks the mantra into his ear.

**2299.** Receiving that mantra, Lakshmipati rejoiced;
the Lord, having shown His mercy, vanished.

**2300.** Waking at dawn, the sannyasi ponders within himself;
at that time Nityananda came to that very place.

**2301.** Seeing Nityananda's radiance, the sannyasi reflects:
"What wondrous radiance! This is by no means a human being."

**2302.** Reflecting thus in many ways, the wisest of sannyasis
gazes with unblinking eyes at the beautiful holy face.

**2303.** The Lord bows down, prostrating Himself on the ground;
in haste the sannyasi lifted [Him] up and took [Him] in his arms.

**2304.** Nityananda says to the sannyasi again and again,
"Give Me initiation into the mantra and deliver Me."

**2305.** At these sweet words of Lord Nityananda
the sannyasi is awash in tears and cannot calm himself.

**2306.** Not daring to transgress the command of Sri Baladeva,
that very day he gave Nityananda the mantra of initiation.[^583]

**2307.** Having given the mantra of initiation, embracing Nityananda,
he was overwhelmed; his heart overflows with bliss.

**2308.** Nityananda, dear to Lakshmipati, the merciful —
what can He not do, He who acts by His own will?

**2309.** He increased the great joy of the Madhva sampradaya —
the Lord, affectionate to His devotees, the source of the bliss of prema.

**2310.** *Thus it was said by the ancients:*[^584]
*"I worship Lord Nityananda, dear to Sriman Lakshmipati,*
