*who increases the bliss of the Sri Madhva sampradaya, who is affectionate to His devotees."*

**2311.** Having become Lakshmipati's disciple, Nityananda
increased his most wondrous bliss.

**2312.** Very soon He went away from there to another place;
others cannot understand this pastime of the Lord.

**2313.** The sannyasi, left without Nityananda, became distraught;
he says nothing to anyone, pondering within himself.

**2314.** At the end of the night a light sleep overcame him;
Lord Nityananda appeared to him in a dream.

**2315.** Seeing Nityananda, Lakshmipati, for all his great steadiness,
cannot hold back the tears of both his eyes.

**2316.** At that very moment the Lord took the form of Baladeva;
seeing this, Lakshmipati falls at [His] holy feet.

**2317.** Bathed in tears, he says again and again,
"This incarnation of Yours is to deceive me!

**2318.** Brahma and the others do not know [You], others cannot know;
only one whom You Yourself grant knowledge can know.

**2319.** Why did You make such a mockery of me, a wretched fool?
Show mercy, Lord — I have taken shelter."

**2320.** Hearing such words of Sri Lakshmipati,
He at that very moment took the form of Nityananda.

**2321.** The sweetness of [His] form surpasses a mass of lightning;
beholding that beauty, Lakshmipati lost his composure.

**2322.** Nityananda-Rama shows mercy:
He fulfilled Sri Lakshmipati's desire.

**2323.** Forbidding [him] to tell others of all this,
the Lord, consoling [him] once more, vanished.

**2324.** No longer seeing the Lord, Lakshmipati grew sorrowful;
sleep went far away — he sees that the night has passed.

**2325.** He says nothing to anyone and cannot keep his composure;
from that day his state became wondrous.

**2326.** Seeing this, the disciples grew anxious;
suddenly Lakshmipati disappeared.

**2327.** What do I know to tell of the life of Lakshmipati,
who is known in the world as dear to Nityananda?

**2328.** The devotion of the people of Pandura cannot be described;
even today, by Nitai's mercy, their devotion is strong.[^585]

**2329.** Meanwhile Nityananda Prabhu, by His own will,
travels to the holy places with joy in His heart.

**2330.** After some days He met
with Madhavendra near Pratichi-tirtha.[^586]

**2331.** The prema that was revealed when the two met —
who could describe it? Only one who saw it knows.

**2332.** Madhavendra regards Nityananda as a friend;
Nityananda regards Madhavendra as his guru.

**2333.** *Thus [it is said] in the Chaitanya-bhagavata, in the Adi-khanda — the words of Madhavendra:*
"I have understood that Krishna loves me:
now I have found such a friend as Nityananda."[^587]

**2334.** *In the same place — the words of the poet:*
"Toward Madhavendra, Nityananda Mahashaya
holds no attitude other than that toward a guru."

**2335.** Sri Ishvara Puri and the others were amazed to see this;
in all of them deep affection for Nityananda arose.

**2336.** For some days the two were immersed in Krishna-rasa;
in the joy of their hearts they passed days and nights.

**2337.** Nityananda, taking leave of Puri,
went to Setubandha for the darshan of Rameshvara.[^588]

**2338.** Sri Madhava Puri, taking his disciples, Ishvara and the others,
took leave and set out for Sarayu-tirtha.[^589]

**2339.** Both were as if lifeless in separation from each other;
only their absorption in Krishna-prema preserved them both.

**2340.** Although Sri Nityananda is supremely steady,
He wandered everywhere, unable to be at rest.

**2341.** After some days, coming to the city of Mathura, the Lord,
absorbed in a childlike mood, plays with the boys.

**2342.** Whoever sees Nityananda-chand even once
cannot leave His company even for half a moment.

**2343.** Supremely sweet is the form of Nityananda-raya;
countless people run to see Nityananda.

**2344.** Nityananda does not stay in one place:
He wanders through Vraja, finding great bliss.

**2345.** From time to time He goes to Sri Gokula-Mahavana
and, seeing Madana-gopala, stays there.

**2346.** Seeing Nanda's home, how much rises in His heart!
Weeping, He goes on with His pilgrimage.

**2347.** *Thus [it is said] in the Chaitanya-bhagavata, in the Adi-khanda:*
"Seeing in Gokula the house and dwelling of Nanda,
the Lord sat down and wept profusely."[^590]

**2348.** Then the Lord, bowing to Madana-gopala,
went to Hastinapura, the city of the Pandavas.[^591]

**2349.** Having seen all the forests and come to Vrindavana,
He plays wonderful games on the bank of the Yamuna.

**2350.** Beneath this wondrous banyan
He now sits, now rises, now rolls in the dust.[^592]

**2351.** Now He adorns Himself with various flowers,
now He says, "Where is My life, My Kanai?"[^593]

**2352.** Nityananda, in ecstatic mood, staggers;
His long eyes are full of tears.

**2353.** Thus Nityananda sports in Vrindavana;
who has the power to understand Nityananda's ways?

**2354.** He came to know that Sri Gaurasundara in Navadvipa,
who had sported in a hidden form, now sports in a manifest form.

**2355.** Smiling to Himself, Nityananda-Haladhara,
His body constantly thrilled with horripilation,

**2356.** lost His composure, drawn by the Lord,
and went from here to Navadvipa.

**2357.** For twenty years He travelled to the holy places;
who could describe the pastimes He had in each place?

**2358.** This is the place of the play of Prabhu Sri Nityananda;
whoever sees it is supremely fortunate.

**2359.** O Srinivasa, this is Chir-ghat;
some call it Chayana-ghat.[^594]

**2360.** One day Radha and Krishna with the sakhis,
after the rasa and other pastimes, came here to bathe.

**2361.** Leaving their garments and other things beneath this kadamba tree,
they entered the water wearing fine, short garments.

**2362.** They had grown weary from various pastimes;
their weariness was relieved by the cool touch of the Yamuna.

**2363.** In the water-play great merriment arose;
all of them went and entered the lotus thicket.

**2364.** Krishna, on some pretext, came to the tree,
hid the garments, and entered the water again.

**2365.** After sporting in the water for some time, they come up onto the bank;
not seeing their garments, all grow anxious at heart.

**2366.** Krishna, beholding at that time a wondrous beauty,
gave everyone their garments, joking.

**2367.** The relief of weariness, the theft of the garments and so on took place here;
and in this place, too, Krishna performed various pastimes.

**2368.** O Srinivasa, Radha and Krishna with the sakhis
are absorbed in the play of nidhuvana here in Nidhuvana.[^595]

**2369.** See, O Srinivasa, this Keshi-tirtha;
its glory is proclaimed in many Puranas.

**2370.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Where Keshi was felled, the merit is a hundred times that of the Ganga;*
*and even there, O Earth, there is a special [greatness] at Keshi-tirtha.*

**2371.** *By offering pinda there one obtains the fruit of offering pinda at Gaya."*

**2372.** Krishna slew Keshi with great sport
and in great joy washed His hands in the Yamuna.

**2373.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the eighty-fifth verse:*
*"He who with his neighing, in great intoxication of pride, shook the three worlds,*
*and with the rolling of his wide-open eyes burned the whole world on every side —*
*that hostile Keshi the Destroyer of Baka then tore apart like a blade of grass;*
*where He washed His blood-stained hands, that Keshi-tirtha I worship."*[^596]

**2374.** O Srinivasa, here at Sri Dhira-samira
are Krishna's kunja-lilas in countless ways.[^597]

**2375.** Here is the wondrous meeting of Sri Radha and Krishna;
in great happiness Their dear ones relish it.

**2376.** *Thus [it is said] in the Gita-govinda, in the fifth canto, in the second song — the words of the messenger to Sri Radhika:*
*"There where before, together with you, He attained the perfections of the husband of Rati —*
*in that very kunja, the great tirtha of Manmatha, Madhava now again,*
*meditating on you incessantly and even repeating the mantra-syllables of your words,*
*longs once more for the nectar of the close embrace of your pitcher-like breasts."*[^598]

**2377.** *In the same place — the song:*
*"He has gone to the tryst, the essence of the bliss of love, in attire as enchanting as Madana's.*
*O you of lovely hips, do not delay your going — follow that lord of your heart.*
*At Dhira-samira, on the bank of the Yamuna, in the forest the Wearer of the forest garland waits."*[^599]

**2378.** See: here is the appeasing of Sri Radhika's mana;
this is Manikarnika: Krishna sports in this forest.[^600]

**2379.** O Srinivasa, here near the Yamuna
is Vamshivata, full of supremely wondrous beauty.[^601]

**2380.** The shade of Vamshivata removes the sorrows of the world;
here Gopinatha always sports in bliss.

**2381.** In an attire that enchants the worlds, in charming poses,
He draws the gopis with the sound of His flute.

**2382.** *Thus [it is said] in the Chaitanya-charitamrita, in the Adi-lila, in the first chapter, the seventeenth verse:*
*"The glorious Gopinatha, who begins the rapture of the rasa dance, standing on the bank by Vamshivata,*
*drawing the gopis with the sounds of His flute — may He bestow blessing upon us."*

**2383.** This place of Vamshivata has been flooded by the Yamuna:
Vamshivata disappeared into the Yamuna.

**2384.** Bringing one of its branches, the Gosvami himself
planted it near the former [place].[^602]

**2385.** See, Srinivasa, this supremely charming place:
here a cool breeze always blows softly, softly.

**2386.** At the sound of the flute, leaving everything, with impatient hearts,
the gopis come and meet Krishna here.

**2387.** The gopis swim in the ocean of Krishna's beauty;
Krishna, seeing the gopis, cannot remain still.

**2388.** Taking hold of His composure, with joy in His heart —
who understands the inner meaning? — how He asks after their welfare.[^603]

**2389.** Here Krishna tested the prema of the gopis
and gave them much instruction to go back home.

**2390.** Seeing, at the beginning of the rasa, inequality (asamata) among the gopis,
He resolved to vanish together with Radha.[^604]

**2391.** Here Krishnachandra, having vanished,
listened with delight to the lament of the gopis.

**2392.** Without Krishna the gopis, here, of the trees and creepers
ask about Krishna with anxious hearts.

**2393.** The gopis, imitating Krishna's lilas,
here described Radhika's good fortune.

**2394.** Here Krishna did what pleased Radhika's heart,
and at this very place, leaving Her, He vanished.

**2395.** Here the other gopis, seeing Radhika,
said many things with hearts in the utmost turmoil.

**2396.** All together, longing for Krishna's darshan,
they sang of Krishna's qualities in countless particulars.

**2397.** Here Sri Krishna gave them His darshan;
the gopis were immersed in supreme bliss.

**2398.** The gopis carefully seated Krishna here;
here there was much conversation between them.

**2399.** See, Srinivasa, the bank of the Sri Yamuna:
here Krishna began the great rasa dance.[^605]

**2400.** Surrounded by hundreds of millions of lovely women, joyfully
Krishnachandra sports in the Sri rasa-mandala.

**2401.** In the Sri rasa play the night became like a kalpa;
Vyasa and other poets have described it in many ways.[^606]

**2402.** Surrounded by the jewels among women, Krishna, the crown of rasikas,
is intent on the rasa sport that draws all hearts.

**2403.** *Thus [it is said] in the Srimad-Bhagavatam, in the Tenth Canto, in the thirty-third chapter:*[^607]
*"There Govinda began the rasa sport together with the jewels among women,*
*devoted [to Him], pleased, their arms linked with one another.*

**2404.** *The festival of the rasa began, adorned with the circle of the gopis —*
*[begun] by Krishna, the master of yoga, who between each two of them*

**2405.** *took His place and held them by the neck, so that each woman thought He was beside her. Then the sky*
*was crowded with hundreds of aerial chariots*
*of the dwellers of heaven with their wives, their hearts stolen by eagerness.*

**2406.** *Then kettledrums resounded, showers of flowers fell;*
*the lords of the Gandharvas with their wives sang His spotless glory.*

**2407.** *The sound of the bangles, anklets and little bells of the women*
*[who were] with their Beloved rose in the rasa-mandala as a loud tumult.*

**2408.** *There, together with them, the Lord, the son of Devaki, shone exceedingly —*
*like a great emerald amid golden jewels.*

**2409.** *With their steps, the movements of their arms, the play of their brows with smiles,*
*their bending waists, the swaying cloths over their breasts, the earrings swinging at their cheeks,*
*their faces perspiring, the knots of their braids and girdles loosened, Krishna's beloveds,*
*singing of Him, shone like flashes of lightning in a ring of clouds.*

**2410.** *Dancing, they sang aloud — with melodious voices, devoted to love,*
*delighted by Krishna's touch; with their singing this [universe] was filled.*

**2411.** *One [of them], together with Mukunda, drew out modes that did not merge [with His voice];*
*pleased, He honored her: 'Excellent, excellent!'*
*[Another] drew out the same [melody] in the dhruva measure — and to her He gave great honor."*

**2412.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Gopala-champu, in the Purva-prabandha, in the twenty-sixth purana, up to the thirty-third verse — [a song] in a suitable raga:*[^608]
*"Glory, glory, O essence of good qualities!*
*O You who have descended, shining, in Gokula to fulfil in the world what is especially cherished! (Refrain.)*

**2413.** *O You whose service is longed for by the Lotus-born, by Ishvara and by the consort of the Lord of Vaikuntha!*
*You shine, O Lord, in the rasa, where the play grows ever richer, with Your own beloveds.*

**2414.** *O You whose companions are like dancers, who hold all the arts, who create mutual joy!*
*O You whose pain is soothed by the cowherd wives, whose great bliss is loudly proclaimed by embraces!*

**2415.** *O You who take many forms throughout the circle encircled by sattvika [bhavas] born of exchanged glances!*
*O You whose fulfilment of desire, aided by a price — the glances of the young women of Vraja — has come under [their] control!*[^609]

**2416.** *Plays interwoven with the steps of lotus feet, the movements of sprout-like hands and [the play of] brows,*
*a series of bendings of the waist, the swinging of jeweled earrings, thrilling hair and sweat —*

**2417.** *[all this] with You, who resemble a cloud, each lovely woman displays like lightning.*
*But does this comparison point to any measure or degree [of difference] between you?*

**2418.** *Among the sweet-voiced, eager to dance, rejoicing only in Your joy,*
*their hearts enveloped by the intoxication of the nectar of Your touch, bought by bhava,*

**2419.** *young women who have flooded the Source of the universe with the bliss born of song —*
*You who shine [among them] partake of that bliss. Obeisance to this Lord!*

**2420.** *She who, together with You, astonished, sings the modes with utmost purity —*
*she makes all the others sing [a melody] held in check by her own skill.*

**2421.** *She who in singing displays an excellence greater still, increasing joy —*
*she is Sri Radha, perfect in worship, honored by You with marks of esteem.*[^610]

**2422.** *She whose jasmine and bracelets slip off in the rasa, in the play born of weariness —*
*she with exquisite art places her hand on Your shoulder, where an ornament shines.*

**2423.** *And she who rapturously covers with kisses Your bolt-like arm upon her shoulder —*
*she rejoices, unmindful of her body, and her hair joyfully stands on end.*

**2424.** *When on a pretext You touch the fine mirror of a cheek where an earring swings,*
*in the kiss, exchanging the juice of tambula, You melt.*

**2425.** *This girl, for whom the jingling [of ornaments] in dance, song and movement creates a fine rhythm —*
*O younger brother of Rama — has placed Your hand at her heart, as if it were an incomparable lotus.*[^611]

**2426.** *And now, O You whose body is surrounded by women wearied by the course of the rasa,*
*O You in whom the sight of the drops of weariness on those circling about has awakened the greatest tenderness!*

**2427.** *O You adorned with a multitude of garlands of bright glories that the poets have established!*
*Glory, glory, glory, glory, glory, glory, glory, glory, glory, glory, O You who sport in the rasa!"*

**2428.** O Srinivasa, [here] the rasa play spread wide:
on the bank of the Yamuna there is no end to that beauty.

**2429.** The night is bright with the rays of the full moon;
the beauty of the Yamuna's waters — how shall I describe it to others?[^612]

**2430.** Here Krishnachandra with His beloveds
sported in the waters of the Yamuna in many delightful ways.

**2431.** Krishna, fond of fun, absorbed in sport in the kunjas —
how He rested, who could describe, however much [he tried]?

**2432.** At daybreak Krishna with His beloveds —
how they went home, the wise describe.

**2433.** *Thus [it is said] in the same [work], in the twenty-ninth purana, up to the ninety-third verse, raga Lalita:*
*"[In Him] who then, awaking in the finest of kunjas,*
*saw the flood of the sun's rays,*

**2434.** *who breaks the sleep of His beloved*[^613]
*and gathers His companions —*

**2435.** *in the Destroyer of Kamsa my mind is immersed,*
*in Him who wears a peacock feather on the crown of His head. (Refrain.)*

**2436.** *[In Him] who again and again delights the throng of young women,*
*who together with her goes outside unwarily,*[^614]

**2437.** *who sets off along the path through the dense thicket*
*and there bestows much bliss,*

**2438.** *who by hope has checked the poison of separation —*
*in [that] Radiance which brings eternal bliss [my mind is immersed]."*[^615]

**2439.** In the play of the great rasa, of all the gopis
the heart's desires were fulfilled by the prince of Vraja.

**2440.** The Sri rasa play is the abode of great bliss;
whoever hears of all this has his desires fulfilled.

**2441.** O Srinivasa, Krishna, the enchanter of the worlds,
who enjoys the Sri rasa play, is the treasure of Radhika's life.

**2442.** Radha, the enchantress of the worlds, who plays in the rasa,
dearer to Krishna than life, is the crown jewel of women.

**2443.** Whatever gives Krishna joy, that She always does;
apart from Sri Radhika, nothing else pleases Krishna.

**2444.** With Sri Radhika and Radhika's sakhis
[He] is always enraptured in the rasa play in Vrindavana.

**2445.** Here one day there was great merriment;
as I tell of those events, the longing [to tell] grows.

**2446.** Vrinda thought, "Today, in many ways,
I shall see the play of Rai and Kanu with the sakhis."

**2447.** For this reason Vrinda, taking her attendants,
makes ready for the beginning of the rasa-lila.

**2448.** The ways in which she arranges the dancing ground —
all those fine distinctions even the *Natya-shastra* does not know.[^616]

**2449.** As moonlight is pure and bright,
so is the dancing ground — its white beauty is wondrous.

**2450.** In the spacious courtyard of this kunja dwelling
what wondrous beauty the moonlight creates!

**2451.** On all sides are white seats of flowers, and above them all,
in the middle, she carefully places a white throne.

**2452.** She puts rolls of tambula in jeweled caskets,
whose fragrance draws all hearts.

**2453.** Flower ornaments and other things of many kinds,
fragrant sandal and the rest — there is no counting them.

**2454.** Hundreds of thousands of chamara whisks steal the heart with their beauty;
mridangas and many other instruments she sets out in rows.

**2455.** She commands the parrots, cuckoos and other birds:
"Sing the endless deeds of Krishna and Radhika."

**2456.** She tells the peacocks to dance,
and bids the bees to hum.

**2457.** At that very moment an attendant of Vrinda
speaks very softly to Sri Vrinda-devi:

**2458.** "Worried that the Two were late in coming, you
ordered me [to go], and I went there.

**2459.** On Paurnamasi's advice Krishna, joyful,
was in the flower garden, watching the road for Rai.

**2460.** Sri Radhika, coming from home with the sakhis,
met Krishna in this flower garden.

**2461.** At the meeting of the Two Paurnamasi rejoiced
and told the Two of what you are doing."

**2462.** Even as she was saying this, the Two arrived;
how charmingly [Their] lotus feet stepped!

**2463.** The Two, placing Their lovely arms on each other's shoulders,
absorbed in rasa, stand gazing at each other.

**2464.** In the telling, there is no limit to that beauty;
in the gazing, the eyes forget to blink.

**2465.** The radiance of the beauty of the Two lights up the three worlds,
putting to shame the mass of rain clouds and the lightning.

**2466.** Surrounded by the sakhis headed by Lalita, [They] are exceedingly beautiful;
the radiance of the bodies of all [the sakhis] sparkles.

**2467.** With wondrous grace they walk into the depths of the kunja;
softly, softly, ceaselessly the anklets ring.

**2468.** Rai and Kanu, entering the kunja with the sakhis,
joyfully behold the splendor Vrinda has arranged.

**2469.** Smiling, the Two sit on that wonderful throne;
all around, the sakhis in their bliss forget themselves.

**2470.** Hundreds of thousands of maidservants fan [Them] with chamara whisks;
the parrots, cuckoos and other [birds] sing the qualities of the Two.

**2471.** The bees hum like the sweetest music;
all around the peacocks and peahens dance.

**2472.** At Vrinda's bidding all displayed their own skills;
in this way Vrinda made her heart's desire known.

**2473.** Most clever Krishna, rasa embodied,
smiling, what He said to Vrinda with the corner of His eye!

**2474.** As Vrinda offered sandal and the rest, and the flower ornaments,
the merriment that grew — who can describe it?

**2475.** Lalita, opening that casket of tambula,
was delighted, placing tambula in Rai's hand.

**2476.** Sri Radhika, joyfully taking a roll of tambula,
with a graceful gesture placed [it] in Krishna's moon-like mouth.

**2477.** Softly, softly Krishna smiles, His heart impatient;
chewing the tambula, He displays many playful moods.

**2478.** Intending to enjoy the Sri rasa pastimes,
He gazes at Rai's face with a wondrous grace.

**2479.** Krishna is the embodiment of bliss, the treasury of rasa;
His grace surpasses millions upon millions of Kandarpas.

**2480.** The peacock feather on His head shines without end;
the flute is placed at His lips — how sweet [His] attire!

**2481.** To fulfil Vrinda's desire,
Krishna sports here together with Sri Radhika.

**2482.** His beloveds are countless; among them the best is Radha,
who fulfils all of Sri Krishna's longings.

**2483.** Who can describe what Radhika's attire is like?
For the attire of Lalita and the others no comparison can be found.

**2484.** Radhika's companions are beyond counting;
the hosts of sakhis headed by Lalita have no end.

**2485.** Surrounded by hundreds of thousands of young beauties,
Krishna sports with His arm upon Rai's shoulder.

**2486.** In the Sri rasa pastimes beauty filled the world;
everyone became immersed in sangita.[^617]

**2487.** What can be said? Wondrous are the ways of sangita:
captivating every heart, it is spread everywhere.

**2488.** O Srinivasa! Long ago Brahma, from the Vedas,
revealed the Sangita-veda — [this] is known to the world.

**2489.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Long ago the Lotus-born, drawing out the essence of the four Vedas,*
*fashioned this fifth Veda, called sangita.*

**2490.** Sama, Rig, Atharva and the rest — the four Vedas;
from them are born song, recitation, rasa and acting.

**2491.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*From the Riks arose recitation, from the Samans song was born,*
*from the Yajuses acting was born, and the rasas are held to come from the Atharva.*

**2492.** Brahma, Shiva and others are the propagators of this sangita;
it, the sweetest of all, pervades all the worlds.

**2493.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Brahma, Isha, Nandi, Bharata, Durga, Narada, Kohala,*
*Dashasya, Vayu, Rambha and others are the propagators of sangita.*[^618]

**2494.** Sangita in its essence is three: song, instrumental music and dance;
some call two — song and instrumental music — sangita.

**2495.** The power of song, dance and instrumental music is immense:
it captivates the hearts of all — gods, humans and others.

**2496.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sangita-parijata:*[^619]
*Song, instrumental music and dance — this triad is called sangita;*
*because song is the chief among them, it is named sangita.*

**2497.** *In the Sangita-shiromani:*
*Song, instrumental music and dance — this triad is called sangita;*
*some [say] that sangita is only song and instrumental music together.*
*It is celebrated as captivating the minds of animals, humans, gods and others.*[^620]

**2498.** Sangita is of two kinds — by the distinction of marga and deshi.
In heaven is [the sangita] based on marga; its teacher is Brahma.

**2499.** Deshi, [varying] by the various lands, abides on earth.
Thus these two — marga and deshi — are well known in the shastras.

**2500.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sangita-sara:*
*By the distinction of marga and deshi, sangita is twofold:*
*that based on marga is in heaven; that based on deshi delights the earth.*

**2501.** *In the Sangita-parijata:*
*By the distinction of marga and deshi, sangita is said to be twofold.*
*The sangita called marga the Creator himself told to Bharata.*

**2502.** *Bharata, having learned from Brahma the sangita called marga,*
*performed it before Shambhu together with the apsaras and gandharvas.*

**2503.** *And sangita [that varies] by the difference of lands they call deshi.*

**2504.** The cause of the origin of song and the rest is nada;
nada is Hari Himself; who can know the truth of nada?

**2505.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Without nada there is no song, without nada there is no svara,*
*without nada there is no raga — therefore the world consists of nada.*[^621]

**2506.** *In the Sangita-damodara:*
*Without nada there is no knowledge, without nada there is no Shiva.*
*The supreme light has the form of nada; Hari Himself has the form of nada.*

**2507.** *Anjaneya [says]:*[^622]
*Sarasvati does not know the farther shore of the ocean of nada:*
*even today, fearing to drown, she carries a gourd upon her breast.*

**2508.** Nada arises from fire and air;
from ether and the other [elements], and from air too, that nada is born.

**2509.** The place where nada is born is below the navel;
rising above the navel, it is finally manifested in the mouth.

**2510.** The ways [of describing] how nada arises are many;
some define the arising of nada briefly.

**2511.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sangita-sara:*
*The syllable "na" is the life-breath (prana-vayu), the syllable "da" is fire;*
*because it is born from these two, therefore it is called nada.*[^623]

**2512.** *That is, nada is born from these two — prana and fire.*

**2513.** *In the Sangita-muktavali:*
*That which, born of ether, fire and air and rising above the navel,*
*comes to manifestation in the mouth is called nada.*

**2514.** Nada is threefold: it occurs in the living, it occurs also in the non-living,
and it is also possible from the union of the living and the non-living — these are the three.

**2515.** [The nada] of the living is born of the body; that which is without a living body is determined as the non-living;
the flute and the like are known as [sources of nada] from the living and the non-living.

**2516.** The sound arises through air touching the mouth and nose;
therefore [it] is said to be born of the living and the non-living.

**2517.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*And it is born of the living, born of the non-living, and arises from both:*
*the first is born of the body, the second arises from the vina,*
*and the third arises from the flute and the like — thus it is held to be threefold.*

**2518.** In practice nada is threefold — mandra, madhya and tara:
by their places — in the heart, in the throat, in the head — in order; so it is known.[^624]

**2519.** Madhya is twice as high as mandra,
tara is twice as high as madhya — these are the three.

**2520.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*In practice the wise call this nada threefold:*
*mandra abides in the heart, madhya in the throat, and tara in the head;*
*each following one, it is said, is twice the preceding in measure.*

**2521.** Such is the origin of nada and the way of knowing nada.
In the rasa dance the gopis pour forth song.

**2522.** The power of sangita — [revealed] in the song that pours from the gopis' mouths for Krishna's delight —
is declared in all the shastras.

**2523.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Even those versed in shruti, smriti and other literature and in the various shastras,*
*if they do not know sangita, are considered two-footed beasts.*

**2524.** *Knowledge, sacrifices, hymns and all the rest give the fruits of the trivarga;*
*the knowledge of sangita alone bestows the fruits of the chaturvarga.*[^625]

**2525.** *It is said in particular in the Sangita-damodara:*
*He in whose heart there is no joy from charming sangita*
*is an ox among men in [this] world: he has been cheated by the Creator himself.*

**2526.** *By song deer and even birds are taken captive;*
*snakes come helplessly, and children do not cry.*

**2527.** *Increasing supreme bliss, granting the desired fruit, subduing,*
*stealing the hearts of all people, the seed of liberation — supreme is song.*

**2528.** O Srinivasa! Krishna, the abode of rasa,
the crest-jewel of those who know song, sports in the rasa dance.

**2529.** Supreme, wondrous beauty is in the Sri rasa-mandala;
with rapture they display song to one another.

**2530.** The characteristics of song are of many kinds;
widely known is song with dhatu and matu.[^626]

**2531.** These dhatu and matu awaken attraction;
dhatu is the component of song, matu is raga and the rest.

**2532.** *In the Sangita-sara:*
*Song is delightful, being joined with dhatu and matu.*

**2533.** *The component of song is dhatu; raga and the rest is called matu.*

**2534.** Dhatu is "nadatmaka" — on this there is much deliberation.
"Nadatmaka" means that whose "atma," [that is,] own nature, is nada.

**2535.** *In the Narada-samhita:*
*Song is said to be [that which is] joined with dhatu and matu;*
*of these, that which is sung and consists of nada is called dhatu.*

**2536.** Here the word "nada" [covers] the shrutis and svaras born of nada,
the murchhanas, the so-called talas, the gramas — a great many kinds.[^627]

**2537.** From nada many shrutis are born;
from the shrutis are born the svaras — shadja and the rest.

**2538.** From the svaras murchhanas are born; from the murchhanas
the so-called talas, arising from the gramas; [this] is known to the world.[^628]

**2539.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*From nada the shrutis were born, from them the svaras, shadja and the rest,*
*from them arise the murchhanas, from them the so-called talas, born of the gramas.*

**2540.** O Srinivasa! In keeping with this subject,
the order of which I shall speak I set forth in few words.

**2541.** Nada, shruti, svara, grama, murchhana — [so] it is established;
tala, varna, graha-svara and amsha-svara,

**2542.** nyasa-svara, jati — all these in this order
I shall set forth briefly; others expound them in detail.

**2543.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Nada, shruti, svara, grama, murchhana, tala, varna,*
*the svaras called graha, amsha and nyasa, and jati — here in this order.*[^629]

**2544.** I have told of nada; now learn of shruti and the rest,
which Krishna with His beloveds reveals in song in the rasa dance.

**2545.** O Srinivasa! In this Sri rasa-mandala —
what shall I say! — all these took embodied form.

**2546.** How the shrutis become manifest from nada —
in revealing this, Krishna's playful delight is boundless.

**2547.** That nada, struck by air, [becomes] twenty-two shrutis;
twenty-two nadis, [running] crosswise and upward, are situated in the heart.[^630]

**2548.** As many nadis, so many shrutis — this is known everywhere;
ascending in order, higher and higher, they are discerned on the vina and [other instruments].

**2549.** In a throat spoiled by phlegm and the like the shrutis are not manifest.
Thus everyone explains [this] in many ways.

**2550.** *Thus [it is said] on the shrutis:*
*That nada, struck by air, becomes twenty-two shrutis;*
*twenty-two nadis, [running] crosswise and upward, are situated in the heart.*

**2551.** *As many as they are, so many shrutis are declared;*
*ascending in order, higher and higher, they are discerned only on the vina and [other instruments],*
*since in a throat spoiled by phlegm and the like they do not become manifest.*

**2552.** The twenty-two shrutis among the seven svaras beginning with shadja —
the order of their division the best of the learned state thus:

**2553.** madhyama, panchama and shadja have four shrutis [each],
the svara rishabha and the svara dhaivata have three shrutis [each],[^631]

**2554.** gandhara and nishada have two — these are the twenty-two.
From the shrutis the svaras are born — this is very well known.

**2555.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Four shrutis are held to belong to panchama, shadja and madhyama,*
*three to rishabha and dhaivata, two to gandhara and nishada.*

**2556.** The names of the shrutis differ in different regions;
I shall name those accepted by many, from which shadja and the rest are born.

**2557.** Nandi, Vishala, Sumukhi and Vichitra — these four;
from them is born the svara shadja, captivating all hearts.

**2558.** Chitra, Ghana and Kandalika — these three in rishabha;
in gandhara, Saragha and Mala — two shruti names.[^632]

**2559.** In the svara madhyama, Magadhi, Shiva, Matangika
and Maitreyi: these four excel in every respect.

**2560.** Bala, Kala, Kalarava and Sharngaravi by name —
in panchama these four incomparable shrutis.

**2561.** Mata, Rasa and Amrita — these three in dhaivata;
in nishada, Vijaya and Madhukari — two shrutis.[^633]

**2562.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Nandi, Vishala, Sumukhi and Vichitra are known as shadjas*
*("shadjas," that is, those that generate shadja);*
*Chitra, Ghana and Kandalika — three — are named in rishabha.*

**2563.** *In gandhara, Saragha and Mala; in madhyama, Magadhi, Shiva,*
*Matangika and Maitreyi — four are declared.*

**2564.** *Bala, Kala, Kalarava and also Sharngaravi in panchama;*
*Yata, Rasa and Amrita — three — in the one named dhaivata.*

**2565.** *And two in the one named nishada: Vijaya and also Madhukari.*
*Thus the twenty-two shrutis of the svaras are stated.*
*(In the words "shrutis of the svaras" the genitive expresses the relation of the generated and the generator, as in "the father of the sons"; the meaning is "generating the svaras.")*

**2566.** Other names of the shrutis are Siddhi, Prabhavati and the rest;
in this there are many more varieties as well.

**2567.** *Thus [it is said] in Kohala:*
*Siddhi, Prabhavati, Kanta and Subhadra, the charming ones,*
*issued from the mouth of Prajapati, produce the svara shadja.*
*And so on.*

**2568.** How the svaras [lie] in the places of the shrutis the universe does not know;
the knowers of sangita merely describe [their] characteristics.[^634]

**2569.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Even Brahma cannot truly name the svaras in the places of the shrutis:*
*the path of fish moving in the waters is not to be traced.*

**2570.** O Srinivasa! Who knows the true nature of the shrutis?
Only in the rasa dance did it become manifest — in charming song.

**2571.** As Krishnachandra makes the shrutis known,
so Sri Radhika wondrously manifests [them].

**2572.** The joy of Lalita and the other sakhis is boundless;
the gods, filled with wonder, shower flowers.

**2573.** The shrutis praise each its own good fortune;
the shrutis together with the svaras attract every heart.

**2574.** *Now, the svaras:*
That which in the place of a shruti delights the heart is svara;
or: all the svaras captivate the listener.

**2575.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Svara is that which, manifesting in the place of a shruti, delights the heart;*
*by this the word "svara" is said to be yoga-rudha.*
*Or else: because they delight the listener's mind, therefore [they] are svaras.*[^635]

**2576.** The names of the seven svaras are shadja, rishabha, gandhara,
madhyama, panchama, dhaivata and also nishada.

**2577.** Sa, ri, ga, ma, pa, dha, ni are their other names;
the seven svaras have three states — mandra, madhya and tara.

**2578.** The places of these three, in order, are the heart, the throat and the head;
from mandra the song is [each time] twice, twice as high.

**2579.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Shadja and rishabha, gandhara, madhyama and also panchama,*
*dhaivata and nishada — here seven svaras are named.*

**2580.** *Sa, ri, ga, ma, pa, dha, ni — these are their other names.*
*They are threefold, abiding in the states of mandra, madhya and tara.*

**2581.** *For they have three places: mandra arises in the heart,*
*madhya in the throat, tara in the head; each following one is twice [as high].*

**2582.** How the seven svaras beginning with shadja arise,
the knowers of sangita have proclaimed with great delight.

**2583.** *Among them, the svara shadja:*
Chest, nose, throat, palate, tongue and teeth —
in these six places the svara shadja is born.

**2584.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Because it is born from six, touching the nose, throat, chest, palate,*
*tongue and teeth, therefore it is known as shadja.*[^636]

**2585.** *But Damodara says otherwise:*
*Air, intensified in the navel, in the nadi and in the heart,*
*in the two sides and in the head — from [these] six shadja is born.*[^637]

**2586.** Thus in the shastras it is well established how the svara shadja arises;
the origin of rishabha and the other svaras is simply set forth.

**2587.** *Now, the svara rishabha:*
*When air, rising from the root of the navel, produces a sound*
*and issues forth easily, like [the bellow] of a bull, [it] is known as rishabha.*[^638]

**2588.** *Now, the svara gandhara:*
*Air, rising from the navel and setting [sound] in motion in the throat and in the ear,*
*issues forth with sound — therefore [the svara] is called gandhara.*[^639]

**2589.** *Now, the svara madhyama:*
*Madhyama is born from the middle place of the body*
*and from the root of the navel; by nature it is deep, [yet] somewhat high.*

**2590.** *Now, the svara panchama:*
*Prana, apana, samana, and likewise udana and vyana —*
*from their union the svara panchama is born.*[^640]

**2591.** *He states the fixed places of these:*
*prana is in the heart, apana in the anus, samana is in the middle of the navel,*
*udana abides in the region of the throat, vyana pervades the whole body.*

**2592.** *Now, the svara dhaivata:*
*That which, going to the lower part of the navel and reaching the bladder, rises upward again*
*and goes as if running to the region of the throat — that is dhaivata.*

**2593.** *Now, the svara nishada:*
*Since here these six svaras, shadja and the rest, all charming, sit down,*
*therefore it is called nishada in the world.*[^641]

**2594.** Know the forms of the seven svaras according to the doctrine of similar sounds:
the peacock utters the svara shadja — [this] is known to the world.

**2595.** The chataka — rishabha, the goat — gandhara,
the krauncha — the one called madhyama, the cuckoo — panchama: so it is held.[^642]

**2596.** The frog — dhaivata; the elephant utters the svara nishada.
Such are the forms of the svaras; some state another view.

**2597.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*The peacock utters shadja, the chataka speaks rishabha,*
*the goat proclaims gandhara, the krauncha voices madhyama,*

**2598.** *the cuckoo sings panchama, the frog voices dhaivata,*
*the elephant utters nishada — this is accepted by Brahma and others.*

**2599.** *But Damodara [says]:*
*The peacock, the bull, the goat, the krauncha, the cuckoo, the horse*
*and the elephant, in order, utter these svaras, so hard to attain.*

**2600.** Further, these seven svaras have four designations:
vadi, samvadi, vivadi and anuvadi.[^643]

**2601.** Among the seven svaras, vadi is the name of the svara
which, through frequent use, determines the raga and the rest.

**2602.** [The svara] with shrutis equal to panchama's is samvadi;
sometimes the svara madhyama is not samvadi.

**2603.** Gandhara, nishada, and also rishabha, dhaivata —
these four are vivadi, enemies: [so] it is firmly accepted in the shastras.

**2604.** In another view, the svaras rishabha and dhaivata, and also
gandhara and nishada, are vivadi: so it is held.

**2605.** The svara that remains of all these svaras
the best of the learned call the anuvadi svara.

**2606.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Further, the svaras, called vadi, samvadi, vivadi and anuvadi,*
*are declared to be of four kinds; of these, vadi is said to be the one*

**2607.** *which, abundant in use, indicates the determination of the raga and the rest;*
*[the svara] with shrutis equal to panchama's is samvadi; sometimes it is not [so].*

**2608.** *Ga and ni are vivadi to ri and dha, or else those two to these two;*
*the remaining one is anuvadi — such is Dattila's view.*[^644]

**2609.** The vadi svara is the king, the samvadi is determined to be the minister;
the vivadi svara is the enemy: this is held everywhere.

**2610.** The anuvadi is the retinue of the king and the minister;
all these natures are beyond the grasp of others.

**2611.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*The vadi is the king, the samvadi the minister, and the vivadi the enemy;*
*the anuvadi is declared to be the servant of the king and the minister.*

**2612.** O Srinivasa! All these charming svaras
Krishna, the crown of rasikas, reveals in song.

**2613.** Before Krishna Lalita sings, taking up the vina,
skilled in manifesting the forms of the svaras and the rest.

**2614.** Hearing [this], the gandharvas are ashamed at heart:
who can understand the longing that they feel?

**2615.** The svaras deem themselves blessed;
the wondrous course of the svaras is manifested in the gramas.

**2616.** *Now, the gramas:*
The combining of the subtle states of the svaras is called grama;
shadja, madhyama and gandhara are the names of the three gramas.

**2617.** The two — shadja and madhyama — are known on earth;
in the world of the gods gandhara is esteemed — by every view.

**2618.** Among the three gramas the shadja-grama is the best;
the grama is the basis of the murchhanas: so the shastras determine.

**2619.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Grama is the subtlest state of the svaras,*
*[their] combination, the assemblage of places; it is threefold:*
*shadja and likewise madhyama are on earth,*
*while the one named gandhara is indeed in the world of the gods.*

**2620.** *And further:*
*A group of well-arranged svaras is considered a grama.*

**2621.** *In the Sangita-parijata:*
*Further, the gramas are declared to be three, having the form of a collection of svaras;*
*they are endowed with the names shadja, madhyama and gandhara;*
*they are the basis of the murchhanas; of the three, the shadja-grama is the best.*

**2622.** In the three gramas the seven svaras are manifested as murchhanas;
in the shadja-grama it is established: sa-ri-ga-ma-pa-dha-ni.

**2623.** Ma-pa-dha-ni-sa-ri-ga is in the madhyama-grama;
ga-ma-pa-dha-ni-sa-ri in gandhara — this is certain.

**2624.** *In the Parijata:*[^645]
*Sa-ri-ga-ma-pa-dha-ni, and ma-pa-dha-ni-sa-ri-ga,*
*and ga-ma-pa-dha-ni-sa-ri are the murchhanas of the three gramas.*

**2625.** *Others too [say]:*
*Sa-ri-ga-ma-pa-dha-ni — these are the murchhanas of the shadja-grama;*
*ma-pa-dha-ni-sa-ri-ga — the murchhanas of the madhyama-grama;*
*ga-ma-pa-dha-ni-sa-ri — the murchhanas of the gandhara-grama.*

**2626.** Thus in each grama the seven svaras are amply unfolded;
by the order of all the distinctions there are twenty-one kinds.

**2627.** All this is known; [so] Bharata and others define it:
jati, shruti, svara and the rest attain [the nature of] grama.

**2628.** *Kohala too [says]:*
*Through the jatis and shrutis the svaras attain the nature of grama.*

**2629.** O Srinivasa! In this sweet Vrindavana,
in supreme bliss, in the rasa dance, Krishna together with His beloved

**2630.** reveals the three gramas in various ways,
at which wonder arises in Shiva, Brahma and the others.

**2631.** Radhika, praising the Lord of her life again and again,
conducts the grama so that Krishna is struck with wonder.

**2632.** Losing His composure, Krishna embraces Rai;
the jubilation of the sakhis headed by Lalita is boundless.

**2633.** What playful delight is in that song — what power have I to tell it?
In the three gramas the murchhanas appear in various ways.

**2634.** *Now the murchhanas.*
The murchhana is born of the grama, Bharata says.
The svara, unfolding in the grama, becomes raga.

**2635.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Where the svara, being unfolded, attains the nature of raga,*
*that the [followers of] Bharata call murchhana, born of the grama.*[^646]

**2636.** *And further:*
*Where the svara, merely by being unfolded, has attained the nature of raga —*
*that the sage calls murchhana.*
*Born of the grama, joined with the seven svaras,*
*in the three sthanas they again [number] twenty-one.*[^647]

**2637.** In the three gramas there are thrice seven murchhanas of the svaras;
the murchhanas are named Lalita, Madhyama, Chitra and the rest.[^648]

**2638.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Lalita, Madhyama, Chitra, Rohini and Matangaja,*
*Sauvira, Varnamadhya, Shadjamadhya and Panchami,*

**2639.** *Matsari, Mridumadhya, Shuddhanta and Kalavati,*
*Tivra, Raudri, and also Brahmi, Vaishnavi, Khechari, Vara,*

**2640.** *Nadavati and Vishala — renowned in the three gramas:*
*thus the Moon-crested One named the twenty-one murchhanas.*[^649]

**2641.** By the knowledge of the murchhanas happiness grows at every moment;
Bharata and others tell the purpose of murchhana.

**2642.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Performing a murchhana at the crown of the head, even the slayer of a brahmana is freed.*[^650]

**2643.** O Srinivasa! The murchhana is born of the grama;
what kinds there are in it — that other people do not know.

**2644.** Together with His beloveds Krishna, in the joy of His heart,
reveals [them] in a wondrous manner in the rasa pastimes.

**2645.** What shall I say! Krishna, the crown of great rasikas,
unfolds many talas and charming songs.

**2646.** *Now the talas.*[^651]
The murchhanas are indeed the talas, the pure ones and the others;
the talas are born of the seven svaras — so too it is defined.

**2647.** The talas are forty-nine: [so] it is proclaimed in the shastras;
separately there are the kuta-talas, very numerous.

**2648.** They are five thousand and thirty-three;
the names of the talas are many, their power immense.

**2649.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*The murchhanas themselves, based on ascent, are the pure talas.*

**2650.** *But Damodara [says]:*[^652]
*Those by which the applications [of the svaras], resting on the remainder of the murchhana, are unfolded —*
*those are the talas, forty-nine in number, born of the seven svaras.*

**2651.** *From them alone arise others — the kuta-talas, each separately;*
*their varieties are very many — who will tell them in full?*

**2652.** *The many varieties of the gramas, murchhanas and talas*
*are not stated, since they are of no use for the present subject and are unknowable.*

**2653.** *This is said in the section on talas:*
*These talas are five thousand and thirty-three.*

**2654.** *Having praised Shiva with the agnishtomika tala, one becomes auspicious.*
*Here the pure talas have names — Agnishtoma and the rest —*[^653]

**2655.** *but, owing to their unfitness for practice, I have not listed them.*[^654]

**2656.** The good fortune of these talas is immense:
taking embodied form, they sport before Krishna.

**2657.** Lalita and the other sakhis of Radhika, the group leaders (yutheshvaris),
each separately set the talas in motion.

**2658.** Rai and Kanu, in supreme bliss, together with the sakhis
reveal varna and song in wondrous arrangements.[^655]

**2659.** *Now varna is described.*
The svara employed at the beginning of the act of singing is varna;
it is of four kinds, by which the singer is gladdened.

**2660.** Sthayi varna, the arohi and avarohi varnas, and also
sanchari — the characteristics of these four are proclaimed.

**2661.** When one and the same svara is used with pauses again and again,
it is sthayi varna — so it is acknowledged in all views.

**2662.** The arohi and avarohi svaras [after] sthayi are according to the sense [of their names];
the varna in which these three are mixed is accepted as sanchari.[^656]

**2663.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*The svara employed at the beginning of the act of singing is called varna;*
*it is of four kinds: sthayi, arohi, avarohi and sanchari.*

**2664.** *The definition of each:*
*If one and the same svara is used with pauses again and again,*
*that is to be known as sthayi varna; the other two have names according to their sense.*
*The other two are the arohi svara and the avarohi svara; they "have names according to their sense," that is, their names follow their meaning: the sense is — "arohi" in the meaning "it ascends," "avarohi" — "it descends."*

**2665.** *In the Sangita-parijata:*
*If the use is with pauses again and again on each single svara,*
*that is to be known as sthayi varna; the other two have names according to their sense;*
*the varna [arising] from their mixture is called sanchari.*
*"Their" — [that is,] of the sthayi, arohi and avarohi svaras.*

**2666.** Sa-ri-ga-ma-pa-dha-ni — these varnas are the seven svaras;
by particular arrangements [of them there arise] a great many alankaras.[^657]

**2667.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*The varnas become alankaras through the particularities of arrangement.*

**2668.** The sthayi are twenty-six, the arohi certainly twelve,
the avarohi twelve, the sanchari twelve.

**2669.** All together there are sixty-two kinds of alankaras;
among them are many varieties — [this] is proclaimed in the shastras.

**2670.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*The sthayi are twenty-six, the arohi twelve,*
*the sanchari twelve and the avarohi twelve.*
*Thus the sixty-two well-known alankaras are proclaimed.*

**2671.** The purposes of alankara are many:
the shastras name the strengthening of practice in the knowledge of the svaras and so on.

**2672.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Firm practice in the knowledge of the svaras and the attainment of delight arise;*
*variety in the knowledge of the varnas — [such is] the purpose of alankara.*

**2673.** *In the Sangita-parijata:*
*Without alankaras the ragas do not attain expansion.*

**2674.** *On sthayi varna.*
The guidance on the alankaras of sthayi varna is given thus:
the varna on which [the figure] begins comes again at the end.

**2675.** Here I explain the alankara named Bhadra:
its arrangement is an order in which one svara at a time is dropped.

**2676.** *Thus [it is said] in the Parijata:*
*Where, beginning [from a svara] and going on to the next, one again utters the previous svara —*
*that alankara the wise Anjaneya called Bhadra.*
*Here the order [is formed] by dropping one svara at a time.*
*Example: sa-ri-sa, ri-ga-ri, ga-ma-ga, ma-pa-ma, pa-dha-pa, dha-ni-dha, ni-sa-ni, sa-ri-sa.*

**2677.** *On arohi varna.*
Thus I point the way in the arohi alankaras:
[the alankara] named Vistirna has long varnas on the seven svaras.

**2678.** *In the Parijata:*
*Where from the initial svara of the murchhana there is ascent in order*
*with long svaras, pausing on each, that [alankara] is called Vistirna.*
*Example: sā rī gā mā pā dhā nī.*

**2679.** The first two [svaras] short, the third syllable long —
[this] is the charming alankara named Prachchhadana.

**2680.** *In the Parijata:*
*Making the first two short and making the third long —*
*[this] the all-knowing Hanuman calls the excellent Sandhi-prachchhadana.*[^658]
*Example: sa-ri-gā, ri-ga-mā, ga-ma-pā, ma-pa-dhā, pa-dha-nī, dha-ni-sā.*

**2681.** [The alankara] named Udvahita: the first [svara] is uttered four times,
the second twice, the third and fourth once each.

**2682.** *In the Parijata:*
*The first svara four times, the second twice;*
*uttering the third once and likewise the fourth once —*
*[this] is the alankara Udvahita, proclaimed by Hanuman.*[^659]
*Example: sa-sa-sa-sa ri-ri ga ma, ri-ri-ri-ri ga-ga ma pa, ga-ga-ga-ga ma-ma pa dha, ma-ma-ma-ma pa-pa dha ni, pa-pa-pa-pa dha-dha ni sa.*

**2683.** *On avarohi varna.*
The avarohi alankaras are of the same kind;
to speak [of them] is superfluous — others too do not speak of them.

**2684.** *In the Parijata:*
*These [same], in descending order, are the twelve avarohi;*
*because of the prolixity I have not written out the descent.*

**2685.** *On sanchari varna.*
It moves everywhere — therefore it [is called] sanchari;
I point the way, by which the singer has joy.

**2686.** The first two varnas are repeated three times; after that
the third varna, and after it the second syllable.

**2687.** Thus is described the alankara named Prasada;
in knowing all this there is joy — [so] it is proclaimed in the shastras.

**2688.** *In the Parijata:*
*And since they move about everywhere, therefore [they are] sanchari.*

**2689.** *Repeating the first pair three times and uttering the third and [then] the second,*
*the wise have called that alankara Prasada.*
*Example: sa-ri sa-ri sa-ri ga-ri, ri-ga ri-ga ri-ga ma-ga, ga-ma ga-ma ga-ma pa-ma, ma-pa ma-pa ma-pa dha-pa, pa-dha pa-dha pa-dha ni-dha, dha-ni dha-ni dha-ni sa-ni.*

**2690.** Here there is one alankara named Akshepa:
three svaras are uttered in order, beginning from the first.

**2691.** *In the Parijata:*
*Where three svaras [go] in order, that the wise have called Akshepaka.*
*Example: sa-ri-ga, ri-ga-ma, ga-ma-pa, ma-pa-dha, pa-dha-ni, dha-ni-sa.*

**2692.** The varna named Kokila is like the lion's backward glance:
sa-ri-ga, sa-ri-ga-ma — such is its manner.[^660]

**2693.** *In the Parijata:*
*Sa-ri-ga and sa-ri-ga-ma — from these Kokila arises.*
*Example: sa-ri-ga sa-ri-ga-ma, ri-ga-ma ri-ga-ma-pa, ga-ma-pa ga-ma-pa-dha, ma-pa-dha ma-pa-dha-ni, pa-dha-ni pa-dha-ni-sa.*

**2694.** All these varna-alankaras on the svaras are sweet;
[He] utters them so that sorrow goes far away.

**2695.** From the lovely moon-like face of Sri Krishnachandra
nectar seems to flow in the form of varna-alankaras.

**2696.** Sri Radhika, together with the sakhis headed by Lalita,
sings the varna-alankaras with supremely wondrous delight.

**2697.** The pride of the gandharvas and the others was shattered;
there is no comparison in the world — so sweet [it is].

**2698.** Praising everyone, Krishna with a joyful heart
gazes with unblinking eyes at Rai's face.

**2699.** Graha-svara, amsha-svara and nyasa-svara — these three —
Krishna, the abode of rasa, playfully reveals.

**2700.** *Now [it is told] of the graha-svara:*
The svara among the seven that is placed at the beginning of a song and the like —
that the sages Bharata and others call the graha-svara.

**2701.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*That is called the graha-svara which is placed at the beginning of a song and the like.*

**2702.** *And in the Sangita-parijata:*
*That which is set at the beginning of a song and the like is called the graha-svara.*

**2703.** *Now [it is told] of the amsha-svara:*
The amsha-svara reveals anuraga in song;
Bharata and others thus describe its great power.

**2704.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*That which in song reveals charm, which all [the others] follow,*
*which itself becomes graha in the use of nyasa and the rest,*
*which abounds everywhere — that amsha, the vadi, is like a king.*[^661]

**2704a.** *"The vadi is that which determines the raga and the rest," [says] the author of the Gita-prakasha. By the words "which itself becomes graha" it is meant that the amsha-svara is the cause of the graha-svara.*[^662]

**2705.** *And further:*
*Those that are called the life of the ragas the wise call amsha-svaras.*

**2706.** *In the Sangita-parijata:*
*That which abounds in use is called the amsha-svara.*[^663]

**2707.** *Now [it is told] of the nyasa-svara:*
The nyasa-svara brings a song and the like to completion;
he who knows this attains bliss.

**2708.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*The nyasa-svara is declared to be that which completes a song and the like.*

**2709.** *Likewise in the Sangita-parijata:*
*The nyasa-svara should be known as that which concludes a song.*

**2710.** O Srinivasa! Krishna, absorbed in the rapture of rasa,
reveals the arrangement of the graha-, amsha- and nyasa-svaras.

**2711.** So that Shiva, Brahma and others are astonished,
He spreads forth the svaras, the jatis and the rest.

**2712.** *Now [it is told] of jati:*
That from which a raga is born is called jati;
it is the mother of the raga; further, the kinds of jati are three.

**2713.** That called shuddha, that called vikrita, and from the union of these two
that called sankirna — these three the wise speak of.[^664]

**2714.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*That from which the raga is born is here jati; she is also the mother of the raga.*
*[She is] threefold: called shuddha, vikrita, and from the union of the two, sankirnaka.*

**2715.** The seven shuddha jatis are named after the svaras, shadja and the rest;
those born of the shuddha are vikrita, say the learned.

**2716.** Those called vikrita are eleven, the shastras determine;
the remaining, called sankirna, are born of the vikrita.

**2717.** Shuddha and vikrita — these are eighteen kinds;
these two [kinds] the acharyas accepted.

**2718.** The shuddha jatis bear the names Shadji, Arshabhi and so on;
the vikrita bear the names Shadja-kaishiki and so on.

**2719.** Shadja-kaishiki is born from the union of Shadji and Gandhari;
so [arise] those called vikrita — [this] is known everywhere.

**2720.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*The shuddha jatis are seven; they are named after the svaras, shadja and the rest.*
*They themselves [become] vikrita; the remaining are born of the mixture of the vikrita.*

**2721.** *Others [say]: "Thus [the jatis] are twofold." This was stated by Harinayaka:*
*"Taken together, the shuddha and the vikrita jatis are declared to be eighteen;*
*they are the mothers of the ragas."*
*This very view seems to be the principal one, since it was accepted by the ancient acharyas.*[^665]

**2722.** *This is stated in another treatise:*
*Shadji, Arshabhi, Gandhari, Madhyami and likewise Panchami,*
*Dhaivati and then Naishadi — these seven are the shuddha jatis.*

**2723.** *There are Shadja-kaishiki, and after it Shadja-madhyama,*
*Gandhara-panchami, Andhri, and likewise Shadjodichyavati,*[^666]

**2724.** *Karmavari, Nandayanti, and also Gandharodichchara,*
*Madhyamodichchara, Rakta-gandhari and also Kaishiki.*[^667]

**2725.** *Thus eleven vikrita are declared by Bharata and others.*
*Now we shall state the causes [of the origin] of the vikrita formed from the shuddha.*[^668]

**2726.** *From the union of Shadji and Gandhari is born Shadja-kaishiki;*
*from Shadji and Madhyami is born Shadja-madhyama;*
*from Gandhari and Panchami is born Gandhara-panchami — and so on.*

**2727.** The kinds of connection of these eighteen with the gramas
Bharata and other authors of books have described in detail.

**2728.** From shruti at the beginning to jati at the end — [all] I have told briefly;
know that all this is in part made manifest on the vina.

**2729.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Whatever I have set forth, beginning with shruti and ending with jati,*
*all that the wise may know in part only on the vinas, and not otherwise.*

**2730.** The jatis, mothers of the ragas, took embodied form in the rasa dance;
they deem [this] their own good fortune — there is no end to telling.

**2731.** O Srinivasa! The rasa pastime is above all;
who knows how to tell what the sweetness of [that] song is like?

**2732.** The sound of the voices of Rai and Kanu, surpassing the sound of the vina,
reveals the jatis, at which the sakhis rejoice.

**2733.** When [They] reveal the ragas, each one separately,
what delight increases — who can describe it?

**2734.** *Now [it is told] of raga:*
Bharata and others give this definition of raga:
the ragas tinge [with delight] the hearts of [beings] in the three worlds.

**2735.** Sixteen thousand ragas the shastras determine;
all of them shine on the four sides of Meru.[^669]

**2736.** Among all these ragas thirty-six ragas
are renowned in the world — so say all the knowers.

**2737.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Those by which the hearts of those dwelling in the three worlds are tinged [with delight]*
*are called ragas — [so say] the sages Bharata and others.*

**2738.** *In the Narada-panchama-sara-samhita:*[^670]
*Krishna began sangita, enchanting [all] with the sound of the flute;*
*in Krishna's presence the gopis began to sing, each one [her own song];*
*from that sixteen thousand ragas were born.*

**2739.** *And further:*
*Of these ragas, thirty-six ragas are renowned in the world;*
*and all of them, too, abide on the four sides of Meru, [say] some.*

**2740.** Of the thirty-six: six ragas and thirty raginis;
each raga has five wives — this too is well accepted.[^671]

**2741.** The six ragas are Bhairava and the rest; in order, for these six,
the raginis are Bhairavi and the rest, known in the shastras.

**2742.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sangita-damodara:*
*Bhairava, then Vasanta, the raga Malava-kaishika,*
*the raga Shri, the raga Megha and likewise Nata-narayana.*[^672]

**2743.** *These are the six male ragas; in order I shall name their raginis:*
*Bhairavi, Kaishiki, and also Bhasha, Velavali*[^673]

**2744.** *and Vangali — these raginis are here the beloveds of Bhairava.*
*Andolita, Deshakhya, Lola, Prathama-manjari*
*and Mallari — these raginis always follow Vasanta.*

**2745.** *Gauri, Gunakiri, and also Varadi and Kshamavati*
*and Karnati — these raginis are dear to Malava-kaishika.*

**2746.** *Gandhari, Deva-gandhari, Malavashri, Savari*
*and also Ramakiri — these raginis are dear to the raga Shri.*

**2747.** *Lalita, Malasi, Gauri, Nati and likewise Devakiri —*
*these raginis are the raga Megha's own beloveds.*

**2748.** *Taramani, Svadhabhiri, Kamodi and likewise Gurjari*
*and Kakubha — these raginis are dear to Nata-narayana.*

**2749.** Some say: six ragas and thirty-six raginis,
each raga has six wives — and this too is quite consistent [with the shastras].

**2750.** *Thus [it is said] in the Narada-panchama-sara-samhita:*
*The ragas are six, and the raginis thirty-six, of lovely form.*
*The raga consists of Shiva and Shakti; [it is] an ocean of the rasa of supreme prema:*
*by merely hearing it Vishnu melted.*

**2751.** *Among them, the ragas:*
*Malava, Mallara, the raga Shri and Vasantaka,*
*then Hindola and Karnata — [thus] the six male ragas are celebrated.*[^674]

**2752.** *Dhanasi, Malasi, Ramakeri and likewise Sindhura,*
*Ashavari and Bhairavi — these are dear to Malava.*

**2753.** *Velavali, Pravara, Kanadi and likewise Madhavi,*
*Kodi and Kedarika — these are dear to Mallara.*

**2754.** *Velapari, Gauri, Gandhari and likewise Subhaga,*
*Kaumari and Vairati — these are dear to the raga Shri.*

**2755.** *Todi, Panchami, Lalita, Patha-manjari,*
*Gurjari and Vibhasha — these are dear to Vasanta.*

**2756.** *Mayuri, Dipika, Deshakari and Pahada,*
*Varadi and Marahatta — these are the wives of Hindola.*

**2757.** *Natika, then Bhupali, Ramakeri and likewise Gada,*
*Kamodi, then Kalyani — these are dear to Karnata.*

**2758.** Thus the learned speak in various ways;
this is reconciled by the fact that different kalpas are meant.

**2759.** In different lands the ragas bear different names;
no one can give an [exact] determination of the ragas.

**2760.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*The truth of the ragas, which bear different names in different lands,*
*no one can determine — neither with the vina, nor I.*[^675]

**2761.** The knowers of music determine three kinds of ragas:
sampurna, shadava and audava — these three.[^676]

**2762.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Among them, sampurna, shadava and audava — thus those ragas are threefold.*

**2763.** *Among them, the sampurna:*
The ragas that are sung with seven svaras
the knowers of song call sampurna.

**2764.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Among them, the sampurna are those that are born from seven svaras.*

**2765.** Those with seven svaras are "sampurna": these ragas are called full;
there are many — the raga Shri, Nata, Karnata and others.

**2766.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*These — the raga Shri, Nata, Karnata, Gupta-vasantaka,*
*Shuddha-bhairava, Vangala, the raga Soma, Amra-panchama,*

**2767.** *Kamoda, the raga Megha and likewise Dravida-gaudaka,*
*Varati, Gurjari, Todi, Malavashri and Saindhavi*

**2768.** *(Malavashri means Malasi, Saindhavi means Sindhuda),*
*Devakri, Ramakri and likewise Prathama-manjari,*
*Nata, Velavali, Gauri and others are held to be sampurna.*
*(By the words "and others" other [ragas] too, Nata and the rest, are included.)*

**2769.** *This is stated in the Sangita-sara:*
*Nata, Ghantarava, Natta-narayanaka and Bhupati,*
*and also Shankarabharana — these are held to be full ragas.*[^677]

**2770.** The fruit of singing these sampurna ragas is immense;
this is known everywhere; [so] the knowers of music determine.

**2771.** *Thus [it is said] in the Kohaliya:*[^678]
*Long life, dharma, fame, renown, intelligence, happiness and wealth,*
*increase of the kingdom, offspring — arise from the full ragas.*

**2772.** The forms of the ragas sampurna and the rest, the kinds of rasa and the rest —
what can be said? All this is [set forth] at length in the shastras.

**2773.** Among the sampurna and the rest, certain ragas some sing
the other way round; by the difference of kalpas that too is true.

**2774.** *Now [it is told] of the shadava:*
All the ragas that arise from six svaras
the knowers of music call shadava.

**2775.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*The ragas that arise from six svaras are called shadava.*

**2776.** Gauda, Karnata-gauda and other ragas are among the shadava;
the knowers of sangita say that in singing them there is much fruit.

**2777.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Gauda, Karnata-gauda, Deshi, and likewise Dhannasika,*
*Kolahala, Ballali, Desha, Ashavari, and likewise*[^679]

**2778.** *Khambavati, Harshapuri, Mallari, then Hunchika —*
*these and others are declared shadava, in the opinion of Harinayaka.*[^680]

**2779.** *By the words "and others," others too are included — Shrikantha and the rest. As it is said in the Sangita-sara:*
*Shrikantha, and also Bhauli, Tara, Shalaga, Gaudaka,*
*Shuddha-abhiri, Madhukari, Chhaya and Nilotpala —*
*so in the enumeration of the shadava.*[^681]

**2780.** *Kohala tells the fruit:*
*Valour in battle, beauty, loveliness, the praise of one's virtues*
*[come from] singing the shadava ragas — so the ancient sages have declared.*

**2781.** *Now the audava.*
The ragas that arise from five svaras are audava;
the wise name many ragas among the audava.[^682]

**2782.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Those which are born of five svaras are known as audava.*

**2783.** Madhyamadi, Mallara and other ragas are among the audava;
much fruit is gained in this audava singing.

**2784.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Madhyamadi, Mallara, Deshapala, Malava,*
*Hindola, Bhairava, Nagadhvani, and likewise Gondakriti,*

**2785.** *then Lalita, Chhaya, Todi, and likewise Velavali;*
*Saindhavi with "Pratapa" before it is named, and likewise the second;*
*these and others are declared audava — ragas that captivate people's hearts.*[^683]

**2786.** *By the words "and others," Turushka, Gauda and the rest are also included. As it is said in the Sangita-sara in the enumeration of the audava:*
*Turushka, Gauda, Gandhara, Pulinda, Megharanjaka — so.*[^684]

**2787.** *Kohala tells the fruit:*
*To destroy disease, to destroy enemies, to dispel fear and grief,*
*and in rites for pacifying the planets, the audava should be sung.*

**2788.** *Now the sankirna.*
The ragas that have been described, through mutual mixing,
the wise call sankirna; in them is beauty for the ear.[^685]

**2789.** *On this Harinayaka [says]:*
*From the mutual mixing of these ragas [arise] a great many names;*
*some of them, beautiful to the ear, are called sankirna.*

**2790.** Pauravi, Kalyani and others are called sankirna;
the meaning of "sankirna" is: made of the union of two, three or more ragas.

**2791.** *Here, Pauravi.*
From parts of Deshi and Mallari [comes the raga] that bears the name Pauravi;
thus the wise reveal these easily grasped ragas.

**2792.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*From parts of [the raga] called Desha and of Mallarika*
*arises this Pauravi — so it is taught.*

**2793.** *Kalyani:*
*From [the ragas] called Varati, Nata and Karnataka*
*arose this charming [raga] called Kalyanika.*

**2794.** *Saranga:*
*Saranga arises from Todi and Dhannasika.*

**2795.** *Gauri:*
*From the raga Shri and the raga Gauda arises Gauri.*

**2796.** *Nata-mallarika:*
*Born of parts of Nata and Mallaraka*
*is Nata-mallarika.*

**2797.** *Vallavi:*
*From the union of Desha and Ashavari, it is declared, [arises] Vallavi.*

**2798.** *Karnatika:*
*From parts of Karnata and Bhairava*
*[arises the raga] called Karnatika, [sung] with a quiver of the voice.*

**2799.** *Mukhavari:*
*From the union of Saindhavi and Todika arose Mukhavari.*[^686]

**2800.** *Ashavari:*
*From the union of Mallara, Saindhavi and Todi arises Ashavari.*

**2801.** *Ramakeli:*
*From the union of Gurjari and Deshika was born Ramakeli.*

**2802.** *There are many other ragas too with the marks of sankirna;*
*as each is heard in [a given] land, so the wise should know it.*

**2803.** The times at which each of these ragas is fit to be sung —
all those times are stated in the shastras of sangita.

**2804.** Singing out of [its] time is a fault in the singer;
singing the raga Gurjari and the like destroys that fault.

**2805.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Transgressing the [proper] time in singing surely brings ruin to all;*
*[but] in group singing, at the king's command and on the stage it brings no fault.*[^687]

**2806.** *And those who, out of greed and delusion, or in separation, sing [out of time] —*
*their fault, it is said, the sweet Gurjari destroys.*

**2807.** Vasanta, Ramakeli, Gurjari — these three:
singing [them] at any time gives rise to no fault.

**2808.** *Thus [it is said] in the Ratna-mala:*
*Vasanta, Ramakeli, and also the sweet Gurjari —*
*[when] they are sung at any time, no fault whatever arises.*

**2809.** *Narada, however, states a particular rule:*
*At night, after ten dandas have passed, the singing of all [ragas] is enjoined.*[^688]

**2810.** All these ragas, taking on form, attentively
consider themselves blessed in the rasa-mandala.

**2811.** What shall I say, O Srinivasa! In the Sri rasa-mandala,
from the singing of various ragas an ocean of joy surges up.

**2812.** This singing has no equal in all the world;
supreme, wondrous nectar [they] shower upon one another.

**2813.** Krishna, gazing at the lotus face of Rai,
reveals in song so much wondrous skill!

**2814.** The characteristics of song were partly stated before;
now learn how the kinds of song were revealed.

**2815.** Song is of two kinds — anibaddha and nibaddha;
anibaddha, as it is defined, has the form of the alapa of a raga.[^689]

**2816.** Song devoid of bandha is anibaddha;
the alapa of a raga is said to be the manifesting of the raga.

**2817.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*Song, it is declared, is twofold: anibaddha and nibaddha;*
*alapti is without bandha; it has the form of the alapa of a raga.*

**2818.** *On this it is said:*
*Because alapti is devoid of bandha, it is called anibaddha.*

**2819.** *"The alapa of a raga" means its manifesting.*

**2820.** Varnalankara in alapa is of two kinds:
one is "a-ta-na-ri," the other "sa-ri-ga-ma" and the rest.[^690]

**2821.** These four [syllables] "a-ta-na-ri" are merely hunkara;
in order they denote Hari, Gauri, Hara and Brahma.[^691]

**2822.** *Thus [it is said] in the Narada-samhita and other [books]:*
*As from hunkara the Veda is born — in the form of "Om,"*
*[so here]: by the word "ta" Gauri is meant, by the word "na" Hara is meant;*
*the sounds "ta-na" rise gradually from the hunkara.*

**2823.** *And there too:*
*By the sound "a" Hari is designated, by the sound "ri" the Grandsire;*
*in the word "a-ta-na-ri" is the manifestation of them all.*

**2824.** Sa, ri, ga, ma, pa, dha, ni — the seven varnalankaras;
the svaras beginning with shadja — this is varnalapa; so it is known.

**2825.** In alapa the places of the gamakas are exceedingly varied;
in it are many charming nuances — this is well known.[^692]

**2826.** All that [is sung] without tala enters into alapa;
the knowers of song name countless kinds of alapa.

**2827.** *Harinayaka, however, [says]:*
*Joined with varnalankaras, adorned with the places of the gamakas,*
*charming with many nuances — [such] is called alapti by the knowers.*

**2828.** *By this [it is meant] that all that [is sung] without tala enters into alapa. Varnalankara, moreover, is meaningless sounds such as hunkara, and the varnalankara "sa-ri-ga-ma" and so on, named in [the teaching of] sangita.*

**2829.** *The kinds of alapti are many; for fear of prolixity they are not stated.*

**2830.** O Srinivasa! In the midst of the Sri rasa-mandali
all perform alapa in countless ways.

**2831.** Whom would that alapa not astonish?
What is the paltry kokila before the sound of those voices!

**2832.** At the time of alapa there is wondrous play:
each displays their own skill.

**2833.** Krishna, the crest-jewel of rasikas, performs alapa on the flute;
He intoxicates the world — what can it be compared to?

**2834.** On the vina the Queen of Vrindavana performs alapa;
who can describe the sweetness of Her alapa?[^693]

**2835.** Lalita and the other sakhis perform alapa on various instruments;
what to speak of others — hearing it, stone melts.

**2836.** Who could tell of the alapa with one mouth?
It surges up like the waves of an ocean of nectar.

**2837.** Immersed in anibaddha singing, they with one another
perform nibaddha song in various ways.

**2838.** *Now nibaddha is described.*
When [song] is bound by dhatus and angas, it is called nibaddha;
nibaddha is these three: shuddha, chhayalaga, kshudra.[^694]

**2839.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*That which is bound by dhatus and angas is called nibaddha;*
*and it is held to be threefold: shuddha, chhayalaga, kshudra.*

**2840.** *Here shuddha is described.*
That which is joined with alapa, dhatus and angas is shuddha;
alapa here is defined as meaningful words.

**2841.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*That which is joined with alapas, dhatus and angas is called shuddha.*
*The knowers of the tradition [say] that by "alapas" here meaningful words alone [are meant].*

**2842.** *Harinayaka, however, says that alapa is the alapti of gamakas devoid of syllables.*

**2843.** The three kinds of nibaddha song have been defined;
some name [them] thus: shuddha, salaga, sankirna.[^695]

**2844.** *In the Sangita-sara:*
*By the distinction of shuddha, salaga and sankirna, song is held to be threefold.*
*Here by the word "sankirna" kshudra alone is meant.*

**2845.** *For it is said by the same [author]: "And it is threefold: shuddhaka, chhayalaga, kshudraka."*

**2846.** Some say: nibaddha song has three names —
prabandha, vastu, rupaka; this is well known.

**2847.** If in it there are four dhatus and six angas,
then [this] excellent construction is called prabandha.[^696]

**2848.** The wise call shuddha song prabandha;
now learn the characteristics of vastu and rupaka.

**2849.** With three dhatus and the rest and with five angas, vastu is defined;
with two dhatus and two angas, it is called rupaka.[^697]

**2850.** *[The words] of Harinayaka:*[^698]
*Nibaddha has three names: prabandha, vastu, rupaka.*

**2851.** *That composition which is bound with four dhatus, formed with six angas*
*and excellent is called prabandha.*

**2852.** *(Thereby it is said that prabandha is shuddha song itself.)*
*With three and more dhatus and with five angas it is called vastu;*
*with two dhatus and likewise with two angas it is celebrated as rupaka.*

**2853.** *Now on dhatu:*
The component part (avayava) of a prabandha is defined as dhatu;
know that a particular part is called avayava.[^699]

**2854.** Some say: the dhatus are four — udgrahaka, and
melapaka, dhruva, abhoga — in this order it is established.

**2855.** Udgraha is first, melapaka comes after it,
after that dhruva, at the end abhoga — these are the four.[^700]

**2856.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*The component part of a prabandha is dhatu; it is declared to be fourfold:*
*udgrahaka, melapaka, dhruva and abhoga — in order.*

**2857.** *Udgraha is the first part; then, it is taught, melapaka;*
*after [it] dhruva, [so called] because of its constancy; and abhoga is held to be the last.*

**2858.** In defining prabandha some assert thus:
udgraha, dhruva, abhoga — these three are the dhatus.

**2859.** The first line of a song is called udgraha;
dhruva is in the middle, and at the end abhoga is defined.

**2860.** *Thus [it is said] in the [Sangita-]shiromani:*
*Udgraha is the first line, as the ancient teachers have said;*
*dhruva, [so called] because of its constancy, is in the middle, and abhoga, it is taught, is the last.*
*"Because of its constancy" means because of its fixedness, [since it] is taken up again and again.*[^701]

**2861.** The line that is between dhruva and abhoga
the knowers call the dhatu named antara.

**2862.** *Thus it is said by Harinayaka:*
*Arising between dhruva and abhoga is another dhatu, called antara.*

**2863.** In the abhoga come the names of the poet and the hero;
for this reason the knowers of song give [it] the name "abhoga."

**2864.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*In the abhoga is the name of the poet, and likewise the name of the hero.*

**2865.** Such as the dhatus in a prabandha are, such is their definition;
the knowers of song display [them] in various songs.

**2866.** *An example in a song — [the raga] Patamanjari:*[^702]
The full moon has risen in the night,
its rays drive the darkness far away.
The expanse of the bank of the Daughter of the Sun
shines with abundant whiteness. (Udgraha.)

**2867.** Gently, gently blows the fragrant,
cool breeze from Malaya.
Swarms of bees hum densely; how many
cuckoos and parrots call! (Melapaka.)

**2868.** The Youth of Vraja sports:
seeing the sweet charm of the Vrinda forest,
[He] is utterly enraptured. (Dhruva.)

**2869.** Today in the beautiful rasa-mandala, unattainable [even] for the gods, there is great merriment.
Taking the flute in [His] hand, [He] touches [it] to [His] lips; joy fills [His] heart.

**2870.** Excellent songs of many kinds, full of Radhika's qualities and deeds, [He] composes;
absorbed in singing, [He shows] an incomparable manner that steals all the pride of the Lord of Rati. (Antara.)

**2871.** In the graceful play of [His] lotus eyes [He] pours forth rasa like a cloud [pouring rain].
What shall Ghanashyama tell? Incomparable love has become manifest in the world. (Abhoga.)[^703]

**2872.** *Now on the angas:*
The dhatus of a prabandha are five — so the shastras determine.
Prabandha song with six angas is current everywhere.[^704]

**2873.** Svara, biruda, pada, tenaka, patha, tala —
with these six angas a song is supremely sweet.[^705]

**2874.** Svara is defined as sa, ri, ga, ma, pa, dha and the rest;
[words] joined with the names of qualities are, in [this] teaching, called biruda.
