having gone to Yashomati's house, cooks together with the sakhis.
Kanu milks the cows, bathes happily,
and, having eaten with His companions, [enjoys] the many delights of rest.

**3402.** In the forenoon [He] goes to the forest with the cows;
having played, [He] nimbly hastens to the bank of the kunda.
Enduring separation from the beloved, the lovely girl again
restlessly watches the path of the messenger She Herself has sent.

**3403.** At midday the beautiful girl with the sakhis near Her own kunda
rejoices, having met the beloved:
stealing the flute, drinking honey, bathing, worship of the Sun,
and so many other varied pastimes.

**3404.** Gori goes home and in the afternoon, having dressed,
with the sakhis keeps gazing at the beloved's path.
Shyama goes home with the cows and His friends;
seeing that face, the people of Vraja are filled with great happiness.

**3405.** In the evening the mother fondles [Him];
milking the cows and so on — much merriment.
The various foods sent by Rai
[He] happily eats together with dear Subala and the others.[^823]

**3406.** At the time of pradosha the Nagara of Vraja, having dressed
and listened to the singing of the skilled, goes to the kunja.
Rai, the jewel of women, adorned, with unseen steps
[goes] with the sakhis, full of joy at meeting Shyama.

**3407.** In the sweet night, engaged in new dance and song —
the pastimes of the rasa dance, unequalled in the world;
in the kunja house, the love-play and quarrels of the Two;
the sakhis [and] Ghanashyama happily serve [Them] at rest.

**3408.** O Srinivasa, here, on this bank of the Yamuna,
Krishna swings with His beloved on a wondrous swing.

**3409.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (raga Mallara):*
Today the King of nagaras swings
in the midst of a most lovely nikunja.

**3410.** Ropes freshly made of jewels;
on them [He] shines, lost in delight.

**3411.** On the left side the beautiful girl shines,
charming the mind of Shyamasundara.

**3412.** The beauty of the Two is an incomparable radiance:
lightning and the massed clouds are left far [behind].

**3413.** On the bodies are ornaments of gold and jewels,
and on them most wondrous garments.

**3414.** On the neck a lovely necklace sways;
the play of the eyes — what can compare with it?

**3415.** On the moons of the faces, a most sweet smile;
ceaselessly a flood of nectar pours.

**3416.** The Two, placing lips to lips,
remain with body blended with body.

**3417.** Lalita and the other sakhis all around,
seeing this play, float in such bliss!

**3418.** Laughing, they swing [the swing] gently, gently;
together they sing songs in fine measure.

**3419.** Some play the mridanga and other [instruments];
some wave a lovely chamara whisk.

**3420.** Endless are the signs of the rainy season;
a gentle breeze blows over the lovely land.

**3421.** All around, trees and creepers, delightful [to see];
many flowers in abundant bloom.

**3422.** Bees wander, their humming everywhere;
peacocks, cuckoos, many birds.

**3423.** Loud and thick is the voice of the frogs;
the rain-clouds of rasa hang low, pouring down.

**3424.** Whatever comparison one tells is too little;
Ghanashyama is lost in this merriment.

**3425.** Look, Srinivasa, [here is] the place of the Holi play;[^824]
here — what wondrous pastimes of Rai and Kanu!

**3426.** *In a song (raga Vasanta):*
Today in supreme delight and joy Shyama, the King of connoisseurs of rasa,
having dressed, sports in the midst of a new kunja house.

**3427.** Radha, moon-faced, adorned — no comparison is enough;
beside Her Beloved, in graceful poses, She shines, overwhelmed with rasa.[^825]

**3428.** The Two throw phagu on each other's bodies — [the bodies] became crimson;
musk, sandal, fragrant pollen, kunkuma They apply again.

**3429.** The companions, seeing the manifold beauty of the Two,
play so many instruments and sing of [Their] deeds, mad with joy.

**3430.** The restless Enchanter of the mind again and again shoots [a jet] from the pichkari;
body and garment are soaked — struck [by the jet], the tender [girl] is startled.[^826]

**3431.** Lalita pours water with ground collyrium on the Nagara's head;
shouting "Ho, ho, ho, hori!" she claps her hands.

**3432.** The best of dancers, skilled in playful quarrels, seizes one and draws her near —
He kisses [her] face; on another's lotus breast He places His hand.

**3433.** Another He embraces again and again, speaking most sweet words;
He rains the arrows of His glances — at the touch the body thrills.

**3434.** Who could reach the end of the joy of such phagu play?
The king of seasons, the spring, deems [it] the fruit of its great merit.

**3435.** Auspiciously — "Jaya, jaya!" — the cuckoos call ceaselessly.
What can Ghanashyama say? I offer myself up for this boundless merriment!

**3436.** O Srinivasa, here [there was] a great merriment:
by the wish of the sakhis Rai became the Kunja-devi.[^827]

**3437.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (in any raga):*
The beautiful girl, having taken counsel with the sakhis,
merrily sets out to meet Shyama.
Entering the nikunja, She sits alone, happily,
covering Her body with a lovely cloth.

**3438.** The best of nagaras, under a tree, restless,
gazes at Rai's path under the weight of prema.
Looking at that lovely girl in the kunja, He rushes [there],
goes up and quietly asks Vrinda-devi:

**3439.** "Tell me, tell me: who, alone in the new nikunja,
sits in such unprecedented attire?
Such beauty I have never seen on earth —
how could [even] Uma's form compare [with her]?"

**3440.** Hearing this, Vrinda says to the son of the king of Vraja:
"This is the goddess of this nikunja.
All my power — know this, You —
comes from serving her feet."

**3441.** Hearing these words, the one versed in rasa, intent on going,
with the greatest reverence, in hope of seeing [Her],
restless of heart, entering the lovely kunja,
stands beside that new goddess.

**3442.** Joining both hands, He says: "Today all
[my] desires will be fulfilled through serving you.
Do not deceive [me], be merciful — [I] shall be happy;
I humbly make my plea — listen, O goddess!

**3443.** "My beloved, dear as life, the darling of my heart,
the daughter of Vrishabhanu, the jewel of women —
not seeing her, my life cannot bear;
a moment I count as so many hundreds of yugas.

**3444.** "She is of a noble family, always very gentle;
her heart trembles in fear of the elders.
Therefore, O bestower of good, do this,
so that no one can say anything to her."

**3445.** Having said this, Kanu bows down,
touching [Her] feet, offering a handful of flowers.
Seeing that, Lalita and the others, staying hidden,
laugh, their hearts thrilling exceedingly.

**3446.** Vrinda-devi says: "What are You doing, Dark One?
What fruit will You gain from such worship?
With each limb [of Yours] worship each limb [of hers] —
only then will this goddess be pleased."

**3447.** Hearing this, the Moon-faced One, keeping Her face under the veil,
smiles softly, floating in bliss.
She who with the corner of Her eye restrains Vrinda
[Herself] again shows a slight smile.

**3448.** Maddened by the intoxication of Madana, the Nagara,
seeing [this], laughs, floating in the waters of bliss:
"Come, come, my goddess, dear as life!" —
saying this, He lifts [Her] and takes [Her] onto His lap.

**3449.** Lalita, leaving [her hiding place] among the creepers, [comes] near
and says, "How much shall I admonish [You]?
Calling [her] Kunja-devi, You are not afraid at all —
[yet] You are a rake, eager for viparita-rati.[^828]

**3450.** Do You see no sin in this?" Hearing, [He] answers,
"The sin will go, receiving your touch."
Hearing this, the sakhis together with Narahari
laugh, covering their mouths with the ends of their cloth.

**3451.** O Srinivasa! One day here
Sri Krishna became greatly agitated without Rai.

**3452.** Hearing from the mouth of a messenger that Radhika was coming,
the son of the king of Vraja became mad with great joy.

**3453.** He fixed His eyes and mind on the path by which Radhika was coming,
and became so bewildered that He did not know Himself.

**3454.** And here Radha, by the wish of [Her] dear sakhis,
goes to Krishna as if a messenger of Chandravali.

**3455.** *Thus [it is said] in a song (in any raga):*
Nectar-faced Radha, keeping the happy sakhis
at some distance, in great merriment,
[His] beloved, dear as life, goes alone to [Him],
unrecognized — a messenger of Chandravali.[^829]

**3456.** In the nikunja the nagara, all atremble, in the hope
of seeing Rai, is beside Himself.
How many desires He cherishes in His mind,
and again and again He looks toward the path of His beloved.

**3457.** And there the bees in multitudes hover and play,
humming as if on some pretext.
"Chandravali's messenger — why does she wander in the forest?" —
not understanding, the Dark One starts on hearing [of her].

**3458.** At that very moment that messenger quickly
came up to [Him], looking at Him.
[His] face became gloomy and darkened;
knowing [He was] in a fix, [He was] troubled in mind.

**3459.** Steadying Himself again, displaying cunning,
[He] says affectionately to the messenger,
"Go, [I am] right behind you, I will follow quickly."
The messenger says, "I cannot go [without You].

**3460.** Without You Chandravali does not live —
why do You delay? Go and see her state!
Get up, get up, there can be no more delay!" —
so saying, she rushes [to Him] and seizes [His] feet.

**3461.** At the touch — and the messenger's touch is the supreme [bliss] —
in how many ways He seems to hold on to His composure!
Receiving the sweet touch of the messenger, the moon Shyama
became helpless, as if bitten by a snake.

**3462.** With restless eyes He looks at Vrinda
and says, "Tell Me, tell Me, what has happened to Me?"
Vrinda says, "Why worry? It will be well —
embrace the messenger just once."

**3463.** Hearing, the jewel of the clever unrestrainedly
embraces the messenger and floats in bliss.
All the sakhis, watching this from afar,
look at Vrinda and smile slightly.

**3464.** Lalita, [coming out] from among the lovely mallika jasmine creepers,
in anger, frowning, says,
"Go, go there — there is no point in [Your] staying here;
Your incomparable ways I cannot bear.

**3465.** However much You [pretend], O rake —
let all those words stay aside:
what there is between You and Chandravali,
that I have learned through the messenger.

**3466.** Ah, how wonderful is Your love of this kind:
the thrill of ecstasy is never seen on [Your] body [with us].
Why be shy before all of us?
Drink the nectar of Chandravali to Your heart's content!"

**3467.** Hearing, Kanu says, "This moon-like face,
surpassing Chandravali, is a heap of nectar.
I have received permission — now I shall drink," —
so saying, smiling, He kisses [Her] face.[^830]

**3468.** Taking [Her] by the chin with His sprout-like hand,
He jests in the rapture of rasa;
seating [Her] on His thighs, adorning [Her] beautifully,
He sports [with Her] on a new couch.

**3469.** Knowing the time is right, two dear sakhis
carefully relieve [Their] fatigue.
Receiving a sign, Narahari joyfully
fans [Them] with a chamara, beholding Their beauty.

**3470.** O Srinivasa, and how [They] sport in this rasa-kunja —
who can tell?

**3471.** The sakhi unfolds the supremely wonderful lila,
and with joy of heart the sakhi relishes it.

**3472.** Without a sakhi joy never arises;
the greatness of the sakhi is known everywhere.

**3473.** *Thus [it is said] in the Ujjvala-nilamani, in [the section on] the kinds of sakhis, the first verse:*
*The sakhi is she who fully unfolds the pastimes of prema,*
*and [she is also] a casket for the jewel of trust; therefore [she] is examined here in detail.*

**3474.** O Srinivasa, Krishna is the embodiment of rasa;
what power have I to tell which lilas [He performed] in which places?

**3475.** In all the varieties of nayaka He sports;
the crown of nayakas is the son of the king of Vraja.

**3476.** *Thus [it is said] in the Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu:*
*The crest-jewel of nayakas is Krishna, the Supreme Lord Himself.*

**3477.** By the distinction of abodes the kinds of nayaka are ninety-six, 96;
in Vraja Krishna is purnatama, [His] bhava is upapati.[^831]

**3478.** With thousands upon thousands of yutheshvaris
He playfully displays the acts of all [kinds of] nayaka.[^832]

**3479.** Among the yuthas the most excellent are Chandravali and Sri Radhika;
everywhere it is known that of them Radhika is the higher.

**3480.** *Thus [it is said] in the Ujjvala-nilamani:*
*But even among them, in every way the most excellent are the two — Radha and Chandravali,*
*whose yuthas [number] millions of doe-eyed ones.*

**3481.** *The rasa was crowded with hundreds of millions of young maidens*
*on that bank of the Yamuna — such is the tradition of the agamas.*

**3482.** *But even of these two Radhika is higher in every way:*
*She is the embodiment of mahabhava; She far surpasses [all] in qualities.*

**3483.** What Krishna's pastimes with Sri Radhika are like —
who has the power to describe that in detail?

**3484.** Here Krishna sports in the greatest merriment,
displaying the acts of a dhirodatta nayaka.

**3485.** The dhirodatta is highly skilled in every respect,
supremely grave, [his] ways pure — humility and the rest.

**3486.** *Thus [it is said] in the Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu:*
*Grave, humble, forbearing, compassionate, firm in his vows,*
*not boastful, with hidden pride, magnanimous — [such is] the dhirodatta.*
*This one is like Raghunatha.*[^833]

**3487.** Krishna, the charming dhiralalita nayaka,
sports unceasingly in this kunja temple.

**3488.** Clever, carefree, very fond of jesting,
subject to His beloved, [His] ways are supreme bliss.

**3489.** *There:*
*Clever, in fresh youth, a master of jests,*
*carefree — [such is] the dhiralalita; he is mostly subject to his beloved.*
*This one is like Kandarpa.*

**3490.** The dhirashanta nayaka is Sri, the son of the king of Vraja,
a knower of the shastras, master of His senses, supremely righteous.

**3491.** He displays humility and other virtues at His beloved's side;
in this kunja house there are wonderfully marvellous pastimes.

**3492.** *There:*
*Even-tempered, enduring hardship, discerning,*
*endowed with humility and other virtues — [such a one] is called dhirashanta.*
*This one is like Yudhishthira.*

**3493.** Whatever the virtues and acts of a dhiroddhata nayaka are,
Krishna displays [them] here so that His beloved rejoices.

**3494.** Self-praise and the rest in Him are the greatest wonder;
the merriment that [was] in this kunja cannot be described in full.

**3495.** *There:*
*Jealous, proud, wily, wrathful, fickle*
*and boastful — [such a one] the wise have called dhiroddhata.*
*This one is like Bhimasena.*

**3496.** O Srinivasa, Krishna, the embodiment of rasa,
revealed the ways of an anukula nayaka.

**3497.** There is no equal to the anukula nayaka:
anukula is attachment to one nayika.

**3498.** The anukula nayaka is Sri, the prince of Vraja;
here are [His] wonderful pastimes with Rai alone.

**3499.** *Thus [it is said] in the Ujjvala-nilamani:*
*He who, through excessive attachment to [one] woman, has given up longing for other beauties,*
*like Rama toward Sita — he is called anukula.*

**3500.** *Toward Radha alone is Krishna's famed devotion (anukulata):*
*at the sight of Her He never recalls association with others.*

**3501.** How Krishna is subject to Radha's prema, what the love of the two of Them is —
that way the poets describe in various manners.

**3502.** *Thus [it is said] in the Gaura-charitra-chintamani — Sri Yamuna speaks to Ganga — in a song (raga Pauravi):*[^834]
O you, dear [to me] as life, friend full of joy!
I am sold to your virtues.
Now I shall tell — listen: [of her]
with whom Shyamasundara's love is greater.

**3503.** Chandravali in Vraja is a renowned beauty;
before [her] wondrous beauty Rama is ashamed;
a young beauty full of rasa —
in virtues and character she has no equal.

**3504.** In a fine new nikunja temple
she sports merrily with Shyama every day;
Shyama, full of rasa, is likewise enraptured —
who can grasp the way of [His] subjection to her prema?

**3505.** When [He] floats in the ocean of supreme bliss,
touching that jewel of a beauty,
immersed in drinking the nectar of [her] moon-face —
then nothing [else] comes to the nagara's mind.

**3506.** If at that time the fragrance of Radha's body,
reaching [Him], touches [His] nostrils ever so slightly —
at once, leaving her, the restless
Dark One runs as if gone mad.

**3507.** What more shall I say? Know in your heart
with whom Kanu's love is greater.
Narahari offers himself for such prema —
who can find the bottom in counting [Her] virtues?

**3508.** *Again there (raga Kamoda):*
What shall I say, O [friend]: incomparable in the world
is the love of Radha and Madhava.
The life is one, but the bodies separate: who
fashioned [Them so], with what joy?

**3509.** If you say, "Since the two are one, why then
did the colour of the two become different?" —
of that I shall tell you a little;
listen to it with care.

**3510.** There are many colours; among them is shyama,
[but] in the gaura colour there is greater beauty.
Showing its utmost to the world,
[it] shines, alluring the eyes of the people of the world.[^835]

**3511.** And I say more: O [friend], the restless Dark One,
when He sees playful Radha,
eagerly longing, spreads out both arms
and yearns to take [Her] in His embrace.

**3512.** If at that time people of the rivals' party
suddenly see this nearby,
some, deceived by the semblance of a cloud with lightning,
cannot make out this great play.[^836]

**3513.** And I say more: that skilful poet, the Creator,
to give great joy to many people,
gazing on the loveliness of shyama and gaura,
[resolved,] "I shall compose comparisons in many ways."

**3514.** That is why [They are] so [different] — [yet] not different at all:
Shyama's body is fashioned of Rai's prema.
The bodies of Radha and Kanu are full of prema —
the love of these bodies is known in the world.

**3515.** The ways of these two — what will others know?
Only the rasikas know.
Whoever is deprived of this rasa,
Narahari counts him equal to a beast.

**3516.** O Srinivasa, one day here
a meeting with Chandravali was arranged.

**3517.** Krishna became eager to meet her;
she set out for the tryst with her own sakhis.

**3518.** At that time, hearing that Radhika was going to the nikunja,
the son of the king of Vraja sets out from here.

**3519.** Coming near Radhika, [He] lost His composure
and forgot everything — the meeting with Chandravali and the rest.

**3520.** What pastimes there were in this kunja with Rai —
that I do not know how to tell, O Srinivasa.

**3521.** Krishna is the dakshina-nayaka, His actions full of rasa;
one who is the same toward all nayikas is called dakshina.[^837]

**3522.** How Krishnachandra sports here with His beloveds —
who could tell it?

**3523.** *There:*
*One who, even with his heart given to another, does not abandon respect, fear, love and courtesy toward his former beloved —*
*he should be known as dakshina.*[^838]

**3524.** *Or:*
*Also one who is the same toward many nayikas is called dakshina.*

**3525.** The way that belongs to the nayaka who is dakshina and anukula
Krishna, the embodiment of rasa, displayed in the rasa [dance].

**3526.** *Thus [it is said] in the Chaitanya-charitamrita, in the Madhya-khanda, in the eighth chapter:*
"The sacred rasa pastimes with a hundred crores of gopis;
among them, in one form He remains at Radha's side.[^839]

**3527.** Seeing ordinary love — equality everywhere —
Radha's crooked love turned into contrariness.

**3528.** Angered, She left the rasa [dance] in pique and went away;
not seeing Her, Hari was distraught.

**3529.** Krishna's whole desire, His wish, is the rasa-lila;
in the desire for the rasa-lila Radhika is the [binding] chain.

**3530.** Without Her the rasa-lila does not please [His] heart;
leaving the circle, He went to search for Radha.

**3531.** Wandering here and there and finding Radha nowhere,
He grieves, wearied by the arrows of Kama.

**3532.** With a hundred crores of gopis [His] desire is not quenched —
from this I infer the virtues of Sri Radhika."

**3533.** Here Krishna displays [the nature] of the shatha-nayaka:
to the face — pleasing; behind the back He acts unpleasingly.

**3534.** *Thus [it is said] in the Ujjvala-nilamani:*
*One who speaks pleasingly to her face, but elsewhere does much that is unpleasing*
*and [commits] a hidden offense — him the wise call shatha.*

**3535.** Here Krishna displays the actions of the dhrishta-nayaka
before the nayika, rejoicing.

**3536.** Even with the marks of enjoyment with another nayika He is fearless,
exceedingly skilled in the use of false words.

**3537.** *There:*
*One who is fearless though the marks of enjoyment with another young [woman] are plain on him,*
*and skilled in false speech — he indeed is called dhrishta.*

**3538.** Here Krishna, with Radha, the beloved dear [to Him] as life,
[enjoys] pastimes in which He is beside Himself — who can describe them?

**3539.** Radha, who is in the middle stage [of youth], is a mine of jewel-like virtues;
She is known as the crown of all nayikas.

**3540.** As in Krishna the states of all nayakas are possible,
so in Sri Radhika are the states of all nayikas.

**3541.** *There:*
*As all the states of the nayaka without exception [belong] to Madhava,*
*so the states of the nayika, it is held, [belong] for the most part to Radha.*

**3542.** By distinction of place svakiya and parakiya are determined;
the varieties of nayika are three hundred and sixty.[^840]

**3543.** In Vraja Radha is the parakiya, the highest nayika;
in the varieties — mugdha and the rest — She sports without limit.[^841]

**3544.** O Srinivasa, in this nikunja house
Krishna sports with the mugdha-nayika.

**3545.** The mugdha is under the sway of the sakhi, her youth fresh;
in the skill of the new arts of love she is little practised.

**3546.** In mana she is gentle, incapable [of it];
having united her with Krishna, the sakhi attains great happiness.

**3547.** *There:*
*The mugdha has new youth and [new] desire, is contrary in union, under the sway of the sakhi,*
*exceedingly bashful in love's acts, [yet making] charming hidden efforts;*

**3548.** *when the beloved has offended, her gaze is choked with tears;*
*she is unable to speak either pleasing or unpleasing words, and in mana she always turns away.*

**3549.** *She who turns away in mana is thus:*
*gentle, and also incapable [of mana] — so she who turns away in mana is of two kinds.*[^842]

**3550.** Look at this nikunja, O Srinivasa:
here are Krishna's pastimes with the madhya beloved.

**3551.** The madhya has manifest youth and is experienced in every way;
in mana she has three varieties: dhira and the rest.[^843]

**3552.** *There:*
*[The madhya] has bashfulness and love's desire in equal measure, possesses blossoming youth,*
*is somewhat bold in speech, capable of union up to swooning;*

**3553.** *the madhya is in mana now soft, now harsh;*
*in her conduct in mana she is threefold: dhira, adhira, and [combining] both.*

**3554.** The dhira-madhya, [being] in mana, here, in the precincts of this kunja,
addresses to Krishna barbed words [and] pure reproach.[^844]

**3555.** *There:*
*The dhira, however, speaks to the offending beloved with barbed words, mockingly.*

**3556.** In this kunja the adhira-madhya, angry with the Lord of her life,
[speaks] fearless, cruel words that please the sakhis.

**3557.** *There:*
*The adhira in anger repulses the beloved with cruel words.*

**3558.** The dhira-adhira-madhya here, with tears,
speaks barbed words to Krishna, glancing at the sakhis.

**3559.** *There:*
*The dhira-adhira, however, speaks to the beloved with barbed words, with tears.*

**3560.** The madhya-nayika is the summit of all rasas;
the madhya Radha here bestows bliss on Krishna.

**3561.** *There:*
*All the excellence of rasa befits the madhya alone,*
*for in her innocence and boldness are plainly joined.*

**3562.** In this kunja the pragalbha, a beauty in the full bloom of youth,
displays so many artful ways to give Krishna happiness!

**3563.** How eager she is for union cannot be told;
in her conduct in mana the pragalbha has three varieties: dhira and the rest.

**3564.** *There:*
*The pragalbha is in the full bloom of youth, blinded by intoxication, eager for abundant union,*
*versed in manifesting many bhavas, having overpowered her beloved with rasa;*
*her words and acts are very bold, and in mana she is extremely harsh.*

**3565.** In this kunja the dhira-pragalbha, skilled in mana,
concealing her anger, is indifferent to union.

**3566.** *There:*
*The dhira is indifferent in union, conceals [her feelings] and [remains] respectful.*

**3567.** The adhira-pragalbha in this nikunja house
strikes Krishna with the lotus [adorning her] ear, with cruel threats.

**3568.** *There:*
*The adhira, having threatened cruelly in anger, strikes the beloved.*

**3569.** The anger of the dhira-adhira-pragalbha is not noticeable;
in this kunja she threatens Krishna a little, with hints.[^845]

**3570.** *There:*
*She who has the qualities of the dhira and the adhira is called dhira-adhira.*

**3571.** See, Srinivasa: in this kunja Sri Radhika,
filled with prema, moves Krishna to abhisara.

**3572.** Sri Radhika, making abhisara in secret,
in attire suited to the time, meets Krishna.

**3573.** The abhisarika-nayika Radhika, the beauty,
comes and meets [Him], now with the sakhis, now alone.[^846]

**3574.** *There:*
*She who calls her beloved to a tryst and also goes [to him] herself —*
*she is the abhisarika; in attire fit for the way she [is] jyotsni or tamasi.*[^847]

**3575.** *As if hiding within her own body out of bashfulness, with all her ornaments [hushed] to silence,*
*veiled, with one loving sakhi, let her go to her beloved.*

**3576.** The vasaka-sajja-nayika in this kunja house
joyfully, with the sakhi, prepares the bed and the rest.

**3577.** On the path by which Krishna will come she fixes her eyes,
again and again she looks out for the messenger.

**3578.** The vasaka-sajja-nayika Radhika, the beauty —
what actions She displays I cannot tell.

**3579.** *There:*
*She who, when her beloved is to come at her turn (vasaka), adorns her body*
*and [makes ready] the chamber — she is the vasaka-sajjika.*[^848]

**3580.** *Her actions are planning love's play, watching the path,*
*merry talk with the sakhi, looking again and again for the messenger, and the like.*

**3581.** In this kunja a meeting had been agreed upon;
because of Krishna's delay that eagerness faded.

**3582.** Separation grew, the longing had no limit —
Radhika here is in the state of virahotkanthita.

**3583.** She wonders why Krishna has not come;
[Her] actions — burning of the heart, trembling and the rest — cannot be told.

**3584.** *There:*
*She who longs when her blameless dearest one delays —*
*[her] the knowers of feelings call virahotkanthita.*

**3585.** *Her actions are burning of the heart, trembling, guessing at the cause,*
*joylessness, shedding of tears, telling of her own state, and the like.*

**3586.** Bearing on Himself the marks of enjoyment with another beloved,
Krishna came into this kunja.

**3587.** Glancing in great anger at the brazen nayaka,
Radha here is in the state of the khandita-nayika.

**3588.** *There:*
*She to whom her lover, having let the agreed time pass, having enjoyed another,*
*marked with the signs of enjoyment, comes [only] in the morning — she indeed is khandita.*
*She is given to anger, sighs, silence and the like.*

**3589.** Rai in the tamala kunja is in the state of vipralabdha:
Krishna was to come — and has not come, She ponders in Her heart.

**3590.** This is that very tamala kunja — see, Srinivasa,
how the actions of the vipralabdha are displayed everywhere.

**3591.** *There:*
*When, a tryst having been appointed, by fate the beloved [dear to her as] life does not come,*
*she whose heart is tormented is called by the wise vipralabdha;*
*[she] is given to despondency, anxious thoughts, sorrow, tears, swooning, sighs and the like.*

**3592.** Rai in this kunja is in the state of kalahantarita:
when the mana has passed, She is tormented here by remorse.

**3593.** Her actions — incoherent speech and the rest — cannot be told;
seeing [this], the sakhis consider various means.

**3594.** *There:*
*She who before the sakhis in anger repulses her beloved who has come and fallen [at her feet],*
*and afterwards is tormented — she indeed is kalahantarita.*
*Her [actions] are incoherent speech, burning anguish, exhaustion, sighs and the like.*[^849]

**3595.** Radhika here is in the state of proshita-bhartrika:
when Krishna goes to a distant land, She cannot find rest.

**3596.** *There:*
*When Krishna goes to a distant land, [the heroine] becomes proshita-bhartrika.*[^850]
*Her [actions] are glorifying the beloved, dejection, thinness and sleeplessness,*
*untidiness, restlessness, numbness, anxious thoughts and the like — so it is held.*

**3597.** When Akrura, taking Krishna, was setting out for Mathura,
what Rai became here cannot be told.

**3598.** *Thus [it is said] in the poem Hamsa-duta, the second verse:*
*When Mukunda, the Madana of the gopis' hearts, from the house of Nanda*
*set out for Madhupuri, following the son of Gandini,*
*then Radha, suffering in separation, whirling in dense eddies, sank*
*into the fathomless river of anxiety whose waters are torment.*[^851]

**3599.** What shall I say? Akrura has no fame in Vraja:
even today, in pain, they call Akrura "krura."[^852]

**3600.** [All] censure Akrura to one another again and again:
he does not understand what the inmost heart of Vraja is like.

**3601.** To Gandini, his own mother, at the time of his birth
he gave great suffering — he, her son.

**3602.** No one can bear to hear Akrura's name;
merely remembering [him], they swim in an ocean of grief.

**3603.** Look: on the bank of the Yamuna is a kunja full of beauty;
here Rai and Kanu — with what bliss They sport!

**3604.** Whatever Rai says to Krishna at the end of love's play,
that very thing Krishna does, wholly subject to prema.

**3605.** Radha manifests the state of svadhina-bhartrika;
the wondrous delights [here] at every moment cannot be told.[^853]

**3606.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Ujjvala-nilamani, in [the section on] the kinds of heroines, the forty-ninth definition:*
*She whose beloved is near and under her sway is svadhina-bhartrika;*
*she engages in play in the water and in the forest, gathers flowers and so on.*

**3607.** O Srinivasa! In this flower grove
Radha and Madhava wander, surrounded by the sakhis.

**3608.** In anuraga Radhika's heart overflows;
[one of] the actions of anuraga — the state of prema-vaichittya is attained.

**3609.** *In the same place, in [the section on] sthayi-bhava, the hundred and twelfth definition:*
*The raga that makes the beloved, though constantly experienced, ever new and new,*
*and itself becomes ever new and new, is called anuraga.*[^854]

**3610.** *Mutual subjugation, and likewise prema-vaichittya,*
*the greatest longing to be born even among insentient things,*
*in separation His appearing [before the eyes]: such are the actions here.*

**3611.** And how [it is]: in the state of prema-vaichittya, from excess of prema,
the thought of separation arises — and Radhika is distraught.

**3612.** "Where is Krishna?" [She] says, and tears pour from Her eyes;
Krishna is right near — this She does not remember.

**3613.** *In the same place:*
*When even in the nearness of the beloved, by the very nature of the excellence of prema,*
*pain [arises] from the thought of separation, that is called prema-vaichittya.*

**3614.** Prema-vaichittya is not separate from sambhoga:
the [kinds] sampanna and samriddhiman are fully in accord with it.[^855]

**3615.** The play of prema-vaichittya is supremely sweet;
poets describe it, and by this suffering goes far away.

**3616.** *As [it is said] in a song (raga Kamoda):*
Rai and Kanu, in the rapture of rasa,
sit on one seat, and the sakhis on all four sides.

**3617.** What waves of anuraga!
The fair one cannot keep Her composure; Her body has grown weak.

**3618.** Again and again [She] asks a sakhi:
"Where has the lord of my life gone, leaving me?

**3619.** Shall I ever find the lord of my life again?" —
saying this, She strikes Her own head with Her hand.

**3620.** [She] is drowned in the tears of both eyes;
heaving long sighs, She rolls on the ground.

**3621.** The crown of rasikas, Shyama-raya,
seeing [Her] grave condition, consoles Radha.

**3622.** The consolation refreshed [Her] life;
the thought of separation vanished, sorrow went far away.

**3623.** What did the sakhi say with the corner of her eye?
Thrilled with rapture, [They] sport in secret.

**3624.** Kala embraces [Her], opening His arms;
in shyness Rai, Her face lowered, smiles softly, softly.

**3625.** Madhava cannot hold back His composure:
intoxicated with the wine of Madana, He presses His face to [Her] face.

**3626.** As soon as [He] touches the high breasts with His hand,
[She] knows not where She is: how much rises in the heart!

**3627.** Smiling, [He] loosens the knot of [Her] waistband;
[They] remain on the bed of flowers, stretched out.[^856]

**3628.** Body mingled with body, They shine thus,
like a blue sapphire and gold, like lightning and a cloud.

**3629.** The merriment grows beyond measure:
having set each other's attire in order, the two gaze [at each other].

**3630.** Knowing the [right] time, a companion,
to ease [Their] weariness, speaks of many things softly, softly.

**3631.** Narahari, at the sakhi's sign,
makes a fresh breeze to dispel the sweat.

**3632.** O Srinivasa! In this grove by the Kalindi
Krishna sports with friends of five kinds.

**3633.** Cheta, vita, vidushaka, pithamarda,
and also priya-narma — these five are His helpers.[^857]

**3634.** In various ways they help Krishna;
who does not sing the virtues of all these friends?

**3635.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Ujjvala-nilamani, in [the section on] the kinds of helpers, the first definition:*
*Now, His helpers are of five kinds: the chetaka, the vita,*
*the vidushaka, the pithamarda, and likewise the priya-narma-sakha.*
*Skill in jesting, ever-deep attachment,*

**3636.** *knowledge of place and time, adroitness, the power to appease angry gopis,*
*the keeping of secret counsel and the like — these are held to be the virtues of the helpers.*

**3637.** Here Krishna with the chetas — Bhringa, Bhangura and others —
sports; all of them are skilled in every kind of contrivance.

**3638.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place:*
*The cheta is clever at arranging [meetings], secret in his deeds, bold of mind;*
*here in Gokula he is said to be Bhangura, Bhringara and others.*[^858]

**3639.** Here too are the vita friends — Kadara, Bharati and others,
skilled in adorning Krishna: wondrous is [their] way.[^859]

**3640.** *In the same place:*
*Skilled in attire and adornment, cunning, expert in gatherings,*
*knowing the arts of the science of love — he is called a vita;*
*Kadara, Bharatibandha and the rest are declared vitas.*

**3641.** Here the vidushaka friends — Vasanta and others —
increase the merriment while Krishna eats.

**3642.** *In the same place:*
*Named Vasanta and so on, greedy for food, fond of quarrels,*
*causing laughter by grotesque gestures, speech and dress — [such is] the vidushaka;*
*as this Madhumangala, famed in the Vidagdha-madhava.*

**3643.** The pithamarda Shridama — there is no end to his virtues;
how much he helps Krishna here!

**3644.** *In the same place:*
*He who in virtues is like the hero (nayaka) and follows him with love*
*is called a pithamarda; such is Shridama to Hari.*

**3645.** Here are the priya-narma-sakhas — Subala and others:
whatever brings Krishna happiness, that they do in every way.

**3646.** *In the same place:*
*Knowing the most intimate secrets, having taken up the mood of a sakhi,*
*he, the best of all loving friends, is the priya-narma-sakha;*
*in Gokula he is Subala, and likewise Arjuna and others.*[^860]

**3647.** O Srinivasa! In this charming grove Krishna
Himself meets [the gopis]: with the sound of the flute He draws the gopis.

**3648.** Just as Radhika has a svayam-duti and apta-dutis,
so too does Sri Krishna: in this is boundless bliss.[^861]

**3649.** *In the same place:*
*The messengers who will be described in the section on Hari's beloveds —*
*here too they should be understood by the knowers of rasa as is fitting.*

**3650.** *There — svayam (Himself) and vamshi (the flute). ("Svayam" means svayam-duti.)*

**3651.** Vira, Vrinda and others are Sri Krishna's apta-dutis;
in this kunja they bring the two [of Them] together — what a wondrous way!

**3652.** *In the same place:*
*Vira, Vrinda and others are also celebrated as Krishna's trusted messengers:*
*Vira is bold of speech, Vrinda skilled in flattering words.*

**3653.** *Vira and the rest are said to be Hari's own special messengers;*
*but those who will be described, ending with Lingini, are common to both.*[^862]

**3654.** What shall I say of how here Radhika's sakhis and the others
help [Her]? This cannot be told in detail.

**3655.** Radhika's sakhis are of five kinds: sakhis, and further
nitya-sakhis, prana-sakhis and the rest — so [it is] known.

**3656.** Taking all these sakhis, the beautiful Radhika
stays in this kunja, watching the path of Krishna.

**3657.** *In the same place:*
*The sakhis of the Queen of Vrindavana are held to be of five kinds:*
*sakhis, and nitya-sakhis, and certain prana-sakhis,*
*and priya-sakhis, and the renowned parama-preshtha-sakhis.*

**3658.** The sakhis Kusumika, Vindhya, Dhanishtha and others here
diligently fulfill what is in Radhika's heart.

**3659.** *In the same place:*
*The sakhis are celebrated as Kusumika, Vindhya, Dhanishtha and others.*

**3660.** The nitya-sakhis — Kasturi, Manimanjarika and others —
here ceaselessly fulfill Radha's wishes.

**3661.** *In the same place:*
*And the nitya-sakhis are Kasturi, Manimanjarika and others.*

**3662.** The prana-sakhis — Vasanti and others — are almost equal to Radha;
in this kunja they increase the merriment of Radha and Krishna.

**3663.** *In the same place:*
*The prana-sakhis are Shashimukhi, Vasanti, Lasika and others;*
*they have for the most part attained likeness (svarupata) to the Queen of Vrindavana.*
*("Svarupata" means equality.)*[^863]

**3664.** The priya-sakhis — Kurangakshi and others, incomparable —
in this kunja, seeing the beauty of the two [of Them], are overwhelmed.

**3665.** *In the same place:*
*The priya-sakhis are Kurangakshi, Sumadhya, Madanalasa,*
*Kamala, Madhuri, Manjukeshi, Kandarpa-sundari,*
*Madhavi, Malati, Kamalata, Shashikala and others.*

**3666.** The parama-preshtha-sakhis — Lalita and others — here,
having brought the two [of Them] together, are full of great joy at heart.

**3667.** *In the same place:*
*And the parama-preshtha-sakhis are Lalita together with Vishakhika,*
*Suchitra, Champakalata, Tungavidya, Indulekhika,*

**3668.** *Rangadevi and Sudevi — these eight are foremost of all the groups;*[^864]
*among them, in two alone, by the supreme height of prema,*
*here and there at times, here and there at times, a pre-eminence is, as it were, seen.*

**3669.** O Srinivasa! In this nikunja abode
the svayam-duti and the apta-dutis display [their] cleverness.

**3670.** *Thus [it is said] in the same place:*
*Now, of these who have recourse to helpers out of thirst for union with Krishna,*
*the way [they] employ messengers in purva-raga and the rest is described;*
*the messenger here is declared to be of two kinds: herself (svayam) and trusted (apta).*

**3671.** The svayam-duti here, seeing Krishna,
achieves her aim — by word, by body and by glance.

**3672.** The svayam-duti Sri Radhika is skilled in every respect;
[She] sports in this kunja — there is no limit to the happiness.

**3673.** *In the same place:*
*She whose shyness breaks down from excessive eagerness and who, bewitched by raga and the like,*
*herself declares [her love] — she is therefore known as svayam-duti.*
*[Her] own declarations are said to be of three kinds: verbal, bodily and visual.*

**3674.** O Srinivasa! In this kadamba grove
the apta-dutis always desire Radha's happiness.

**3675.** The doings of the trusted messengers cannot be told;
they are three: amitartha, nisrishtartha, patrahari.[^865]

**3676.** *In the same [work]:*
*She who would not break trust even at the cost of life,*
*affectionate and eloquent — she is the messenger of the fair-browed gopis;*
*she is of three kinds: amitartha, nisrishtartha and patrahari.*
*(Vishrambha means trust.)*

**3677.** The amitartha messenger, highly skilled in [reading] hints,
devising a means, unites the two here.

**3678.** *In the same [work]:*
*She who, knowing from signs the feeling of both or of one of the two,*
*unites the two by [various] means — she is the amitartha.*

**3679.** To the nisrishtartha messenger the burden of the task is entrusted;
in this kunja she arranges the meeting of the two by stratagem.

**3680.** *In the same [work]:*
*She to whom one of the two has entrusted the burden of the task,*
*and who brings both together by stratagem — she is called the nisrishtartha.*

**3681.** The patrahari messenger merely takes a letter
and gives [it] to the two, and the two meet, coming to the nikunja.

**3682.** *In the same [work]:*
*She who only carries messages for the young pair is the patraharika.*

**3683.** The messengers — the craftswoman, the astrologer, the ascetic, and also
the maidservant, the foster-sister — are known everywhere;[^866]

**3684.** the forest goddesses, the sakhi and others — all of them in these kunjas
with joyful hearts reveal each her own qualities.

**3685.** *Thus [it is said] in the same [work]:*
*They are the craftswoman, the astrologer, the ascetic, the maidservant,*
*the foster-sister, the forest goddess, the sakhi and others — in Vraja.*

**3686.** The craftswoman, versed here in many crafts,
showing [her] craft, delights the two.

**3687.** The trusted messenger who is an astrologer, expert in calculation,
foretells the meeting of the two in this kunja today.[^867]

**3688.** The ascetic is one in the dress of a female ascetic, like Paurnamasi;
Paurnamasi, coming here, unites the two.

**3689.** *In the same [work]:*
*The ascetic, in the dress of a female ascetic, is described as like Paurnamasi.*

**3690.** The maidservants, Lavanga Manjari and others, joyfully
unite Radhika with Krishna in this kunja.[^868]

**3691.** *In the same [work]:*
*Lavanga Manjari, Bhanumati and others are the maidservants.*

**3692.** The foster-sister, bringing Radha from Javata,
playfully unites Her with Krishna in this kunja.

**3693.** The forest goddesses stay in the forest at all times;
in this kunja they see the meeting of Rai and Kanu.

**3694.** The sakhi playfully unites the two in this kunja;
the way of the sakhi is well known — who does not sing [her] glory?

**3695.** *In the same [work]:*
*She who guilelessly bears a mutual prema greater even than for herself,*
*trusting, alike in age, dress and the like, is considered a sakhi.*

**3696.** *Her service as messenger to both is twofold: explicit and suggested.*
*(Tat means of this sakhi; ubhayoh means of the hero and the heroine.)*

**3697.** In various ways, in this nikunja abode,
the sakhi expands the happiness of the two in sambhoga.[^869]

**3698.** As primary and secondary, sambhoga is of eight kinds:
after purva-raga and the rest, sankshipta and the rest — so it is set forth.

**3699.** *Thus [it is said] in the same [work]:*
*The feeling that, through the favourable enjoyment of seeing, embracing and the like,*
*rises to the exultation of the young pair is called sambhoga.*

**3700.** *By the wise it is declared twofold: primary and secondary.*
*Primary sambhoga is in the waking state; it is of four kinds.*

**3701.** *Those arising in order after purva-raga, after mana and after the two [kinds of] pravasa*
*they know as sankshipta, sankirna, sampanna and samriddhiman.*[^870]

**3702.** In purva-raga there is sankshipta sambhoga: briefly
the sakhi unites the two here in her own way.

**3703.** *In the same [work]:*
*Where the young pair, out of fear, shyness and the like,*
*enjoy restrained caresses — that [sambhoga] is called sankshipta.*

**3704.** When the mana has been overcome in various ways,
here, in sankirna sambhoga, the sakhi finds happiness.

**3705.** *In the same [work]:*
*Where the caresses are mixed with the remembrance of offences and the like,*
*that [sambhoga] is sankirna, pleasing like slightly heated sugarcane.*

**3706.** After a pravasa not far away there is sampanna; it has two kinds;
here the sakhi tastes the happiness of sambhoga.

**3707.** *In the same [work]:*
*The enjoyment when the beloved has come together [with her] after pravasa is called sampanna;*
*that union is twofold: agati and pradurbhava.*

**3708.** *Agati:*
*Arrival in accordance with worldly conduct is agati.*

**3709.** *Pradurbhava:*
*When before His dearest ones, overwhelmed by the agitation of prema, Hari*
*suddenly appears — that is called pradurbhava.*

**3710.** Samriddhiman sambhoga comes after distant pravasa;
by the distinction of concealed and manifest it unfolds in this kunja.

**3711.** *In the same [work]:*
*The overflowing enjoyment of a young pair separated through dependence [on others],*
*for whom the sight of each other is hard to obtain, is called samriddhiman.*

**3712.** *In the same [work]:*
*Some accept a twofold form of these, by the distinction of concealed and manifest;*
*though it is accepted, it is not stated here, since it does not produce great exultation.*

**3713.** O Srinivasa, by this path Rai joyfully
enters this kunja house together with Her companions.

**3714.** However many companions Radhika has, there is no end to them;
among the sakhis headed by Lalita there is a wondrous beauty.

**3715.** The sakhi Sri Lalita, perfect in all qualities,
is surrounded by her eight [companions], Ratnaprabha and the others.[^871]

**3716.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Brihat-krishna-ganoddesha-dipika:*
*Ratnaprabha, Ratikala, Subhadra, Bhadrarekhika,*
*Sumukhi, and Dhanishtha, and Kalahamsi, Kalapini.*[^872]

**3717.** Vishakha's beauty has no comparison;
surrounded by her eight [companions], Madhavi and the others, she shines.

**3718.** *Thus [it is said] in the same [work]:*
*Malati, Madhavi, Chandrarekhika, and also Kunjari,*
*Harini, [the one] named Chapala, Surabhi and Shubhanana.*[^873]

**3719.** Skilled in everything, of excellent conduct, Suchitra[^874]
is accompanied by her own eight, Kurangakshi and the others.

**3720.** *In the same [work]:*
*Kurangakshi, Sucharita, Mandali, Manikundala,*
*Chandrika, Chandralatika, Kundakakshi, Sumandira.*

**3721.** Champakalata's sweetness is wondrous beyond measure;
with her eight [companions], Rasalika and the others, the beauty is a marvel.

**3722.** *In the same [work]:*
*Rasalika, Tilakini, Shauraseni, Sugandhika,*
*Kamini, Kamanagari, Nagari, Nagavenika.*[^875]

**3723.** Who can keep composure at [the sight of] Sri Rangadevi's beauty?
The beauty of her eight, Manjumedha and the others, steals the heart.

**3724.** *In the same [work]:*
*Manjumedha, Sumadhura, Sumadhya, Madhurekshana,*
*Tanumadhya, Madhusandra, Gunachuda, Varangada.*

**3725.** Sudevi is always blossoming with love for Radhika;
her eight, Tungabhadra and the others, are well known.

**3726.** *In the same [work]:*
*Tungabhadra, Rasottunga, Rangavati, Susangata,*
*Chitralekha, Vichitrangi, Medini, Madanalasa.*[^876]

**3727.** Tungavidya is a supreme beauty, her loveliness is great;
her eight, Kalakanthi and the others, are of wondrous ways.

**3728.** *In the same [work]:*
*Kalakanthi, Shashikala, Kamala, Madhura, Indira,*
*Kandarpasundari, Kamalatika, Premamanjari.*[^877]

**3729.** Indulekha is of excellent conduct and attracts all hearts;
to what can her eight, Kaveri and the others, be compared?

**3730.** *In the same [work]:*
*Kaveri, Charukavara, Sukeshi, Manjukeshika,*
*Harahira, Mahahira, Harakanthi, Manohara.*

**3731.** O Srinivasa, together with the companions of Lalita and the others,
in this kunja, joyfully seeing the meeting of the two,

**3732.** moment by moment [they] cannot contain their hearts for exultation —
[such] are the supremely wondrous deeds of the sakhis, Lalita and the others.

**3733.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Ujjvala-nilamani:*
*Mutual praise of the love (1) and the qualities (2) [of both], awakening attachment in them (3),*
*[arranging] the abhisara (4) for the two of them, giving a sakhi to Krishna (5),*
*jests (6), consolation (7), dressing (8), skill in revealing [the feelings of] the heart (9),*
*hiding her faults (10), deceiving the husband and others (11),*[^878]

**3734.** *instruction (12), bringing [them] together at the right time (13), service with the fan and the like (14),*
*reproaches to both of them (15), and also sending messages (16),*
*efforts and the like to preserve the heroine's life (17) — [these] are the activities of a sakhi.*

**3735.** O Srinivasa, there are no words to describe it:
this place is a flower garden that charms Krishna's heart.

**3736.** What wondrous beauty within this forest!
[Here is] the one beyond the gunas, in the form of a linga, named Gopishvara.[^879]

**3737.** This Sadashiva protects the forest of Vrinda;
if one worships him, all undertakings succeed.

**3738.** The gopis, always [longing] for union with Krishna,
ceaselessly and with care worship [him], offering various gifts.

**3739.** Can I tell of his glory, so very great?
Worshipped by the gopis — therefore his name is Gopishvara.

**3740.** Indra and the other gods always offer [him] praise;
by his mercy love for Vrindavana [grows].

**3741.** *Thus [it is said]:*
*I bow to the glorious Gopishvara, Shankara, full of mercy —*
*the god who removes all afflictions and bestows love for the forest of Vrinda.*

**3742.** *And thus [it is said] in the Stavamrita-lahari:*
*O lord of the land of Vrindavana, glory to you, [who dwell] with Uma, crowned with the moon,*
*worshipped by Sanandana, Sanatana and Narada!*
*O Gopishvara, for the lotus feet of the Couple who sport in Vraja*
*grant prema free of all motive; obeisances, obeisances to you!*[^880]

**3743.** Look: here is Brahma-kunda — a place most secluded,
covered with many shrubs and creepers, exceedingly beautiful.[^881]

**3744.** Whoever bathes here and spends one night fasting
sports joyfully with the gandharvas and others.

**3745.** Whoever gives up life here attains Vishnu-loka;
the glory of Brahma-kunda is revealed in the Puranas.

**3746.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*There, at the most blessed [kunda] of Brahma, surrounded by many shrubs and creepers —*
*there a man should bathe, staying [there] one night;*

**3747.** *[then] he rejoices, sporting with the gandharvas and apsaras;*
*and whoever gives up life there goes to My abode.*[^882]

**3748.** What other wonders [there are] beside Brahma-kunda —
how shall I tell of them? The Puranas have proclaimed them.

**3749.** *Thus [it is said] in the Varaha Purana:*
*"There, on its northern side, is an ashoka tree shining with whiteness;*[^883]
*in the month of Vaishakha, on the dvadashi of the bright fortnight,*

**3750.** *it blossoms at midday, bringing joy to My devotees;*
*no one knows [of this] except a pure bhagavata."*

**3751.** Here Vrinda-devi revealed [her] innermost thoughts
and fulfilled the desire of the sage Narada.

**3752.** O Srinivasa, this is Venu-kupa;[^884]
here is Krishnachandra's wondrous sport.

**3753.** Knowing that [His] beloveds were thirsty, Krishna,
taking the flute in His hand, fixed His gaze on the ground.

**3754.** As soon as He blew the flute, the sound entered the underworld,
and suddenly the well was filled with water.

**3755.** All drank the water and praised Krishna;
therefore the name "Venu-kupa" is known in the world.

**3756.** O Srinivasa, on the day of the subduing of Kaliya
Krishna swallowed the forest fire right here.[^885]

**3757.** Whoever sees this place of the forest fire
is freed from the forest fire of samsara.

**3758.** This is Sri Govinda-svami-tirtha, the most excellent;[^886]
behold [its] unprecedented beauty, which has no equal.

**3759.** Whoever bathes here has [his] desires fulfilled;
here are Govinda's most wondrous pastimes.

**3760.** *Thus [it is said] in the Saura Purana:*
*"There is a most excellent tirtha called Govinda-svami-tirtha —*
*[the tirtha] of Vishnu, the son of Vasudeva, exceedingly hard to attain.*

**3761.** *Here dwells Achyuta in the form of the Deity, named Govinda-svami;*
*bathing there and worshipping Him, the saintly desire liberation."*

**3762.** As I hear, the many pastimes in Vraja, all [their] sweetness and the rest,
are beyond the reach even of Brahma and others — how much then can others know?

**3763.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the hundred and fourth verse:*
*"Neither Brahma, nor Narada, nor Hara, nor the best of the devotees full of prema*
*are able here to know easily and fully its overflowing sweetness;*
*only Baladeva, together with his mother, [knows it] clearly and completely,*
*and this Uddhava, through prema, knows [it] thoroughly — how then can that Vraja be described?"*

**3764.** These twelve forests attract all hearts;
devotees, prostrate on the ground, worship them constantly.

**3765.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the ninety-eighth verse:*
*"Clearly resplendent with multitudes of shining wish-fulfilling creepers and trees,*
*whose masses of flowers are touched by armies of swarms of bees maddened by fragrance,*
*and shining — oh, wonder! — with a host of broad ponds, hills and rivers —*
*to those twelve forests, the dearest to Krishna, I bow deeply again and again."*

**3766.** O Srinivasa, the devotee always prays
that, even with other company, he may dwell in Vraja.

**3767.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the hundred and fifth verse:*
*"Nowhere else, not even for a moment, not even in the city of Achyuta,*
*not even bathed in the ocean of the nectar of prema, not even together with Achyuta's saintly [servants], will I dwell;*
*but here, together with the residents of Vraja, whoever they may be —*
*in conversation with them may my dwelling be ever full, at every moment."*

**3768.** Those who live in the land of Vraja are dear to Krishna;
the fortunate constantly bow to them all.

**3769.** *Thus [it is said] in the Stavavali, in the Vraja-vilasa, the hundredth verse:*
*"Where Brahma, though charged with many duties, always, day after day,*
*joyfully longs for a supreme birth among the grasses, bushes and the like,*[^887]
*— those dearest ones who in their turn live right there, in the land of Vraja,*
*adorned with piety — to each and every one of them I bow with the utmost humility."*

**3770.** All the grasses, bushes, insects and the like that are in Vraja —
the fortunate bow to them all incessantly.

**3771.** *Thus [it is said] in the same, the hundred and second verse:*
*"Whatever there is in the cowherd settlement — grass, bushes, insects and the rest —*
*all of it is full of every bliss, dear to Mukunda, supremely favourable to [His] pastimes:*
*this has been clearly established again and again by the scriptures and by the longing entreaty even of Brahma and others;*
*therefore all of this I worship."*

**3772.** Someone, uttering the names of Radha and Krishna, with the tears of [his] eyes
longs to sprinkle the places of Krishna's pastimes.

**3773.** *Thus [it is said] in the same, the hundred and third verse:*
*"Wandering from hollow to hollow along the winding paths of the King of mountains [Govardhana],*
*incessantly crying out like a madman, 'Radhe! Krishna!',*
*falling here and there — when will I, beside myself,*
*sprinkle all the places of pastimes with the waters overflowing from my two eyes?"*

**3774.** O Srinivasa, the sweetness of Vrindavana —
in [my] heart is the desire always to keep [it], filling [my] eyes [with it].

**3775.** Taking you two along, I wandered in great bliss;
I thought in my heart: it will not be so again.

**3776.** Birth after birth you two are servants of the Lord.
Saying this, the Pandita lost all composure in his heart.

**3777.** Narottama and Srinivasa Acharya Thakura
float in tears; both lost their composure.

**3778.** They fall at the Pandita's feet and roll [on the ground];
the Pandita, taking [them] on his lap, sprinkles [them] with the tears of his eyes.

**3779.** Singing of the deeds of Radha and Krishna and of Chaitanya,
they became intoxicated; no memory of the body remained in their minds.

**3780.** Bowing again and again to the land of Vrindavana,
they offer a prayer that cannot be described.

**3781.** Thus, sitting in seclusion, the three
after some time regained their composure.

**3782.** They set out for the darshan of Sri Govinda-deva,
whose beauty, sweetness and the rest the learned describe.

**3783.** *Thus [it is said] in the Sri Chaitanya-charitamrita:*
"In Vrindavana is a wish-fulfilling tree, a golden house,
the great Yogapitha; there is a throne of jewels.[^888]

**3784.** On it sits the son of the king of Vraja Himself,
named Sri Govinda — the Crusher of Manmatha Himself.[^889]

**3785.** On Him Padmasana always meditates in [his] world,
worshipping [Him] with the eighteen-syllable mantra."[^890]

**3786.** [He] is the son of the king of Vraja Himself; there is nothing else in this.
The ignorant person who regards [Him] as a pratima[^891]

**3787.** has no deliverance from that offence;
he falls into a terrible hell — what more can I say?

**3788.** *Thus [it is said] in the Brahma-vaivarta Purana:*
*"Even having obtained human birth, most rare and desired by the gods,*
*those who have not taken shelter of Govinda have long cheated themselves.*

**3789.** *They are not fit to be seen or spoken with; they are the lowest in the three worlds —*
*those who are averse to the pair of Sri Govinda's feet."*

**3790.** *And likewise:*
*"Whoever sees Govinda on the swing, Madhusudana on the platform*
*and Vamana on the chariot has no rebirth."*[^892]

**3791.** Having seen Sri Govinda, the three
were in great bliss; [their] eyes and hearts were soothed.

**3792.** Sri Krishna Pandita, joyfully seeing the three,[^893]
gave [them] prasada — a garland — and asked after their welfare.

**3793.** Raghava Pandita told everything in due order;
joyful, they had darshan everywhere.

**3794.** They quickly went to Sri Jiva Gosvami's residence;
great was Sri Jiva's bliss on seeing them all!

**3795.** Sri Raghava Pandita told Sri Jiva Gosvami
everything; hearing it, [he] rejoiced at heart.

**3796.** Staying one or two days in Vrindavana,
Raghava Pandita quickly went to Govardhana.

**3797.** O listeners, the parikrama of Mathura-mandala
I have told in brief; in it is wondrous glory.

**3798.** Those who diligently read or hear of this glory
attain the highest destination and deliver both sides [of the family].[^894]

**3799.** *Thus [it is said] in the Adi-varaha Purana:*
*"Those, O greatly fortunate one, who read and attentively hear*
*of the glory of Mathura attain the supreme destination;*
*they deliver two hundred generations on both sides [of the family]."*

**3800.** How much happiness there is in wandering through Sri Vraja-mandala —
only he knows this who follows Vraja.

**3801.** Sing the names of the places of the pastimes in Vraja —
without effort all desires will be fulfilled.

**3802.** Relish the pastimes together with the devotees —
by the mercy of the devotees you will obtain pure bhakti.

**3803.** With devotees be careful in every way,
so that no displeasure arises in their hearts.

**3804.** If displeasure arises, everything meets with obstacles;
since the occasion has come, I will tell something here.

**3805.** One day Sri Rupa Gosvami in Vrindavana
was contemplating in his mind, with a joyful heart:

**3806.** the sakhis are adorning Radhika;
Krishna, standing behind, is watching.

**3807.** Radhika does not know that Krishna sees;
in the sakhis' hearts grows the playful wish to let [Her] know.

**3808.** Having arranged [Her] hair in a wondrous style,
a sakhi held a mirror before Radhika.

**3809.** Sri Radhika, wishing to behold the beauty of Her own face,
sees Krishna's moonlike face in that mirror.

**3810.** Rai was thrown into confusion, exceedingly bashful;
taking a veil, She quickly covers all Her limbs.

**3811.** The sakhis laugh — great merriment arose;
and Sri Rupa Gosvami laughed along with them.

**3812.** At that time a Vaishnava came,
his heart very eager to see Sri Rupa.

**3813.** Sri Rupa, seeing [him], laughs and said nothing;
dejected, [the Vaishnava] went to Sanatana.

**3814.** The Vaishnava says, "I went to see Sri Rupa;
seeing me, he began to laugh.

**3815.** Grieved at heart, I said nothing to him;
not understanding the reason, I have come to ask a little."

**3816.** Sanatana told why he had laughed;
hearing it, the Vaishnava's mind was filled with remorse.

**3817.** The Vaishnava says, "Why did I go at such a time!
Not understanding his heart, I have committed an offence."

**3818.** Thus that Vaishnava became greatly agitated;
Sanatana Gosvami calmed him.

**3819.** Meanwhile Rupa had been absorbed in beholding the lila;
that bliss vanished at that very moment.

**3820.** Sri Rupa, agitated, looks in all four directions;
he concluded in his mind, "Someone came here.

**3821.** I have committed an offence by failing to honour him."
Reflecting thus, he goes to the Gosvami.

**3822.** That Vaishnava, seeing Sri Rupa coming,
going out some distance [to meet him], falls on the ground and bows.

**3823.** Very humbly he says to Sri Rupa,
"I have committed an offence — forgive [me], revered sir.

**3824.** Just a little while ago I went there;
not understanding your behaviour, I thought something to myself.

**3825.** Coming to the Gosvami, I told [him of it];
he, showing mercy, dispelled the misconception.

**3826.** If you too show me mercy,
then my mind will be at peace — [that is why] I have told you."

**3827.** Hearing this, Sri Rupa, distressed at heart,
falling to the ground, bows and speaks with joined palms:

**3828.** "I have committed so many offences that I cannot say;
forgive my offence, showing mercy."

**3829.** Absorbed in bhakti-rasa, both showed great humility;
having asked forgiveness for the offence, both became calm.

**3830.** Both came to Sanatana Gosvami;
for some time they were absorbed in the rasa of talks about Krishna.

**3831.** Everyone heard of this episode of Sri Rupa;
hearing it, all were greatly amazed.

**3832.** O brothers, be careful with Vaishnavas;
even at the cost of your life, beg forgiveness for offences.

**3833.** Beware of seeing faults in Vaishnavas;
constantly sing the virtues of Vaishnavas.

**3834.** All the bhagavatas of former times say this:
the actions and gestures of Vaishnavas the wise do not fathom.

**3835.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya Prabhu, through His dear devotees,
gave instruction to others in just this way.

**3836.** Holding the lotus feet of the devotees upon your head,
constantly drown in the ocean of bhakti-rasa.

**3837.** Meditating on the feet of Srinivasa Acharya,
Narahari Dasa tells the *Bhakti-ratnakara*.

*Thus in the Sri Bhakti-ratnakara ends the fifth wave, called "The Description of the Parikrama of Vraja and Other Matters".*

[^1]: Govardhana is the sacred hill in Vraja that Krishna lifted on His little finger; Raghava Gosvami lived at its foot (cf. 23–24).
[^2]: Mathura is the city on the Yamuna where Krishna appeared; Mathura-mandala (30) is the whole region of Vraja around it.
[^3]: Keshava-deva is the Deity of Krishna at the place of His appearance in Mathura. Sri Subuddhi is Subuddhi Raya (19), a former ruler of Gauda who became a Vaishnava by Mahaprabhu's mercy and lived in Mathura (CC Madhya 25); not Subuddhi Mishra of Tarangas 1–2.
[^4]: The verse number is given in the text of the editions. Champakalata is one of Radha's eight principal friends. The *Bhakti-ratna-prakasha* (cf. 22) is a work of Raghava Gosvami.
[^5]: Dasa Gosvami is Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami, who lived at Radha-kunda.
[^6]: Vrindavana is the principal settlement of Vraja, where the Gosvamis lived. "The Lords" (prabhugaṇa) are probably the Deities of Vrindavana (Govinda, Gopinatha, Madanamohana) and possibly the Gosvamis themselves.
[^7]: Raghava's account begins at 31. Vajranabha (Vajra), Krishna's great-grandson, was installed as king of Mathura after the Lord's departure (cf. *Bhag.* 1.15).
[^8]: On Mahaprabhu's journey to Vraja see CC Madhya 17–18; on His charge to compile the scriptures on Mathura and recover the forgotten holy places, CC Madhya 23 (His instruction to Sanatana).
[^9]: A yojana is an ancient Indian measure of distance, four kroshas. From here to 73 Raghava cites the scriptures on the glory of Mathura.
[^10]: Hara and Gauri are Shiva and Parvati; in 58 Shiva addresses Parvati as "O Mahadevi." The words of the Adi-varaha Purana (41, 50, 60, 66, 72) are spoken by Lord Varaha to the Earth (Vasundhara, Vasudha).
[^11]: "Equal": A, B and C read "samasta," the 1960 edition (OCR) "samandha"; translated by the probable reading "samaṁ"; reading unclear (OCR).
[^12]: "Sets out to see it" follows A and C (yāy); the 1960 edition reads "cāy," "wishes to see it," which is closer to the verse in 64.
[^13]: The city of Madhu (Madhupuri) is Mathura, named after the asura Madhu.
[^14]: Madhupuri ("the city of Madhu") is Mathura.
[^15]: In the Adi-varaha Purana the Lord Varaha converses with the goddess Earth; "Devi" here and below is an address to her.
[^16]: Narada's words to Dhruva. Madhuvana is one of the twelve forests of Vraja, near Mathura; here Dhruva performed austerities.
[^17]: The quotation continues the account of Madhuvana. Lavana was a rakshasa, son of the demon Madhu; he was slain by Shatrughna, Ramachandra's brother, who founded the city of Mathura in Madhuvana.
[^18]: Harimedhas is a name of Vishnu; "he" is apparently Dhruva, who performed austerities in Madhuvana. The 1960 edition (anvaya) reads otherwise: "the God of gods" is Mahadeva, and it is he who performed austerities.
[^19]: Literally "of forty yojanas; then Mathura is situated": whether this is an extent or a distance is unclear (cf. twenty yojanas in 123–124). A yojana is a measure of length of about 13 km.
[^20]: Maunji is the rite of initiation in which a girdle of munja grass is put on; i.e., birth, initiation, dying and cremation of the body in Mathura. The 1960 edition (anvaya) has lifelong brahmacharya, dying and cremation; the four kinds of liberation are salokya and the others.
[^21]: Chandalas and pukkasas are outcaste groups. Pinda is the memorial offering to the ancestors.
[^22]: A half-verse; the text does not name the source.
[^23]: Vishranti-tirtha is the Vishrama ghat on the Yamuna at Mathura. The guardian of the holy land (kshetrapala) of Mathura is Shiva; the kathasara of the 1960 edition calls him Bhuteshvara.
[^24]: "The Lord" (ishvara) — so in the text; a hyperbole: the virtues of Mathura are inexhaustible.
[^25]: A half-verse; the speaker is the Lord Varaha.
[^26]: Shaukari is Soron (Shukara-kshetra) by the Ganga, the place of Varaha's appearance; Vateshvara is Bateshwar on the Yamuna below Agra, with its Shiva temple; Yayavara has not been identified.
[^27]: Adi-Shukara ("the Primeval Boar") is Varaha, who lifted the Earth from the waters (cf. 131).
[^28]: Shurasena was an ancient ruler of Mathura who gave his name to the country (Shurasena); according to the Puranas, Krishna's grandfather, the father of Vasudeva.
[^29]: Reading unclear (OCR): all three copies have barāhadaśana hrada (in the 1960 edition in quotation marks, as one name). Possibly distorted present-day local names of Yayavara and Shaukari.
[^30]: The quotation is abridged ("and so on"). Who frees Yayavara is not named in the passage (apparently the Yamuna, the passage being from the Yamuna-mahatmya); in 131 "he" is Yayavara.
[^31]: The twelve forests of Vraja: west of the Yamuna — Madhuvana, Talavana, Kumudavana, Bahulavana, Kamyavana, Khadiravana and Vrindavana; east of it — Bhadravana, Bhandiravana, Bilvavana, Lohavana and Mahavana.
[^32]: A gavyuti is two kroshas; twelve gavyutis are twenty-four kroshas (cf. 136).
[^33]: Keshava-deva is the Deity of Krishna in Mathura (Adi-Keshava).
[^34]: Harideva is the Deity of Krishna at Govardhana, on the bank of Manasi-ganga.
[^35]: Govinda is the Deity of Sri Govinda in Vrindavana.
[^36]: Vishranti is the Deity at the Vishrama ghat (Vishranti-tirtha) in Mathura (cf. 106).
[^37]: Mathura is described as a lotus with Keshava-deva at its center; Sri Varaha-deva is the Deity of Lord Varaha (the Boar) in Mathura, on the southern "petal."
[^38]: The verse, like the preceding ones, is from the *Adi-varaha Purana*: Lord Varaha speaks to the goddess Earth (Bhumi, Vasundhara — 154).
[^39]: Chaturmasya is the four months of the rainy season, when, according to the Puranas, Lord Vishnu sleeps (cf. 154).
[^40]: Madhuvana is the first of the twelve forests of Vraja; here, the environs of Mathura (cf. 157–158).
[^41]: The Sanodiyas are a community of brahmanas. Mahaprabhu took His meal at the house of a Sanodiya brahmana, a disciple of Madhavendra Puri (CC Madhya 17).
[^42]: Ardhachandra ("the Half-moon") is a place in the middle of Mathura, shaped, according to verse 199, like a half-moon; probably the curve of the Yamuna bank with its ghats.
[^43]: Krishna's birthplace in Mathura (Janmasthana) — the prison of King Kamsa, where Vasudeva and Devaki were confined.
[^44]: The Six-faced One (Shadanana) is Karttikeya (Skanda), the son of Shiva.
[^45]: On Mahaprabhu's dance before Keshava-deva in Mathura, see CC Madhya 17.
[^46]: Deities of Vishnu in Mathura named in the *Adi-varaha Purana* (verse 220).
[^47]: The 1912/13 edition adds a note to the name Ekanamsha: "eka — the principal, anamsha — without parts, that is, complete"; she is "the one born in the house of the cowherd Nanda from the womb of Yashoda," that is, Sri Krishna's Yogamaya.
[^48]: Mahavidyeshvari ("the Mistress of the Great Knowledge") is apparently a fourth goddess (Mahavidya Devi is worshiped in Mathura); the anvaya of the 1960 edition takes the word as an epithet of the three goddesses named.
[^49]: Shiva Bhuteshvara is the kshetrapala, the guardian protector of Mathura; his temple is one of the city's chief shrines.
[^50]: In this verse the Lord Varaha addresses Shiva (Mahadeva), not the Earth, as He usually does in the *Adi-varaha Purana*.
[^51]: In the 1960 edition the verse is preceded by the line "And in the same place"; the 1888 and 1912/13 editions lack it.
[^52]: The *Nirvana-khanda* is a section of a Purana; the author does not say which. The Lord is speaking.
[^53]: Vishranti ("rest"), or Vishrama-tirtha, is Vishrama ghat on the Yamuna in Mathura, where, according to tradition, Krishna rested after slaying Kamsa; the chief of the ghats of Mathura.
[^54]: Sri Chaitanya's visit to Mathura and His bath at Vishrama ghat — CC Madhya 17.
[^55]: A play on words: *vishranti* means "rest, repose." The 1888 and 1912/13 editions read *śāśvatāḥ* (probably a corruption), the 1960 edition *śāśvatīm* ("eternal"); translated after the 1960 edition.
[^56]: Kalinda is the mountain from which the Yamuna descends (hence her name Kalindi). Shaukari, according to the 1960 commentary, is the Ganga at Shukara-tala; the confluence with the Bhagirathi is understood there as the Ganga's meeting with the ocean.
[^57]: So in the 1888 and 1912/13 editions (*uttara-kūloktam*); the 1960 edition reads *uttarottaroktam*: "the fruit [at these places] is declared ever greater and greater." The meaning of the verse is unclear.
[^58]: Gatashrama ("He whose weariness has passed") — the Deity of Krishna at Vishrama ghat.
[^59]: Ardhachandra ("half-moon") is a crescent-shaped area in the middle of Mathura (cf. 5.198–202). There follow the twelve tirthas south of Vishranti (249–285), then the tirthas to the north (from 286).
[^60]: Reading unclear (OCR): *guhā-/guhya-tīrtha* (the 1960 edition interprets it as "Guha"); in verses 252 and 256, *guhyam*, "secret." "Guhya-tirtha" is adopted.
[^61]: The agnishtoma is a Vedic soma sacrifice.
[^62]: Bali, king of the asuras, grandson of Prahlada; from him the Lord Vamana begged three steps of land.
[^63]: A sankranti is the day the Sun enters a new sign of the zodiac. The rajasuya is the Vedic royal sacrifice.
[^64]: Vatasvami ("Lord of the banyan") is the name of the Sun god (Surya) worshiped at this tirtha.
[^65]: Dhruva, the prince who, on Narada's instruction, performed austerities in Madhuvana (*Bhag.* 4.8). A play on words: *dhruva* means "certain," "unchanging"; Dhruva-loka is the Pole Star, Dhruva's abode.
[^66]: Shraddha is the memorial rite in which food — pinda — is offered to the ancestors (pitris).
[^67]: The pitri-paksha is the fortnight of the waning moon (in autumn) devoted to the remembrance of the ancestors.
[^68]: Gaya is a place of pilgrimage in Bihar where pinda is offered to the ancestors.
[^69]: Madhuvana ("the forest of Madhu") here apparently means Mathura itself (cf. 5.204: "Mathura within Madhuvana").
[^70]: After the twelve tirthas south of Vishrama (284), the tirthas to the north are listed. Asikunda is a tirtha in Mathura.
[^71]: It may also be understood as "greatly renowned in Brahma-loka" (*brahma-loke 'tiviśrutam*); translated after the interpretation of the 1960 edition. There too *niyatāsanaḥ* is understood as "steady in his seat."
[^72]: Soma-loka — the world of the Moon god (Soma).
[^73]: An avarna is a person outside the varnas, of low birth; a yati is an ascetic who has subdued his senses, a sannyasi (so the 1912/13 edition also explains).
[^74]: Sarasvati-patana — literally "the falling (inflow) of the Sarasvati." On avarna and yati, see 5.301.
[^75]: Chakra-tirtha ("the tirtha of the disc") is one of the twelve tirthas on the Yamuna north of Vishranti (see the note to 313).
[^76]: Dashashvamedha — "[the tirtha of] the ten horse sacrifices" (ashvamedha).
[^77]: Vighnaraja ("the king of obstacles") is Ganesha.
[^78]: A tirtha of the same name is also among the twelve tirthas south of Vishranti (5.280).
[^79]: The twelve tirthas south of Vishranti are listed up to 5.284; the twelve to the north in 5.286–312 (from Nava-tirtha to Koti-tirtha).
[^80]: Cf. CC Madhya 17: Mahaprabhu bathes at the twenty-four ghats of Mathura.
[^81]: Shesha (Ananta) is the thousand-headed serpent, the couch of Vishnu, who eternally glorifies the Lord with a thousand mouths (so the 1912/13 edition also explains: "Shesha is Ananta-deva").
[^82]: Vishvanatha ("the Lord of the universe") is Shiva; Gokarna is the name of his tirtha in Mathura.
[^83]: Krishna-ganga is a tirtha (ghat) on the Yamuna in Mathura.
[^84]: The four Deities at Asikunda: Varaha, Narayana, Vamana and Langali ("the holder of the plough") — Balarama. In the text the names are given in the feminine (Narayani, Langali; in the shloka all four), in agreement with the word murti (Deity).
[^85]: Chatuh-samudrika — "[the well] of the four oceans."
[^86]: Krishna ("the Dark One," feminine) is a name of the Yamuna.
[^87]: The Lord is Varaha, who in the Adi-varaha Purana speaks with the Earth (Vasundhara). The Mathuras are the native brahmanas of Mathura.
[^88]: "Who knows no hymns" (*anṛca*): the 1912/13 edition explains — "without mantras, or one who has not studied the Vedas."
[^89]: The pastimes indicated in 360–369 are described in the Bhagavatam (*Bhag.* 10.41–44, 10.48): Sudama the garland-maker, who wove garlands for Krishna and Balarama; Kamsa's washerman, who refused to give Them garments; Kamsa's bow; the elephant Kuvalayapida; the fight with the wrestlers in the arena; the slaying of Kamsa; Kubja.
[^90]: Kamsa-khali — according to a note in the 1912/13 edition, the place where Krishna dragged Kamsa and slew him.
[^91]: Kubja ("the hunchback") was a maidservant of Kamsa who prepared sandalwood paste for him; she anointed Krishna with it, and He straightened her (*Bhag.* 10.42).
[^92]: Gopala is the Deity revealed by Madhavendra Puri on Govardhana. When Gopala came to Mathura for a month, Rupa Gosvami and his companions came there to see Him (CC Madhya 18).
[^93]: The son of Rohini (385) is Balarama; Nityananda is Balarama Himself, and so in 387 Mathura is called the land of His former birth. On Nityananda's pilgrimage to the holy places before He met Mahaprabhu, see CB Adi 9.
[^94]: The source of the shloka is not given. Vasava is Indra; his city is Amaravati. The 1960 edition reads keśavasya, "the city of Keshava."
[^95]: "Shri" is Shrivana, that is, Bilvavana; "Maha" is Mahavana (so the 1960 edition also explains).
[^96]: The first half-line (mahāvanaṁ gokulākhyaṁ madhu vṛndāvanaṁ tathā) is found only in the second copy of the 1912/13 edition (B); it is absent in the 1960 edition, and line A in the working text is incomplete.
[^97]: An upavana is a lesser forest, a grove (as distinct from the twelve principal forests, vana).
[^98]: The asura is Dhenuka, who had taken the form of an ass; according to the *Bhagavata Purana* (*Bhag.* 10.15) Balarama killed him, hurling him onto a palm tree.
[^99]: Dantavakra, king of Karusha and cousin of Shishupala, was Krishna's enemy; Krishna killed him with His club (*Bhag.* 10.78). More on this in 409–420.
[^100]: Datiha is a village west of Mathura; here, by tradition, Krishna killed Dantavakra.
[^101]: The meeting of Krishna with the people of Vraja at Kurukshetra at the time of a solar eclipse is in *Bhag.* 10.82.
[^102]: Krishna's return to Vraja after slaying Dantavakra is known from the *Padma Purana* (Uttara-khanda); Jiva Gosvami also describes it in the *Gopala-champu* (cf. 429–431).
[^103]: "Ayore" (Braj āyo re) means "He has come!"
[^104]: A play on words: gaurava means "honor, respect."
[^105]: The *Gopala-champu* is a work of Jiva Gosvami. The daughter of the Sun (tapanajā) is the Yamuna. "Encampment" (vraja) is a cowherd settlement.
[^106]: The abode of Purushottama is Puri (Jagannatha Puri), called Purushottama-kshetra.
[^107]: The sense seems to be: the circuit will not at once follow the order of the forests given in the *Adi-varaha Purana*, but will gradually come to it. The reading "সেরূপ নহিব, ক্রমে হইব তেমন" (serūpa nahiba, krame haiba temana) is the same in A, B, C and G.
[^108]: Shashthikara (ṣaṣṭhīkarāṭavī, "the forest of Shashthikara"; A and C ṣaṣṭīkarā, B yaṣṭīkarā — reading unclear, OCR) is apparently present-day Chhatikara between Mathura and Vrindavana, where the temple of Garuda-Govinda stands. The present-day name in the text is ṣaṣṭhīgharā (B yaṣṭhīgharā).
[^109]: Shakatarohana means "mounting the cart." In 441 it is said that today this is the village of Shakata (śakaṭā grām — so in the scans of A and C).
[^110]: The Vidyadharas are semi-divine celestial beings.
[^111]: Garuda is the bird who carries Vishnu. Shridama is Krishna's cowherd friend, the brother of Srimati Radhika.
[^112]: The *Laghu-bhagavatamrita* is a work of Rupa Gosvami. Tarkshya is Garuda. So in the 1912/13 edition (B: laghu-bhāgavatāmṛte); the 1960 edition reads "Sri Bhagavatamrita."
[^113]: Gandheshvara — literally "the Lord of fragrances" (gandha, "fragrance").
[^114]: Satoha is a village south of Mathura; its kunda is known as Shantanu-kunda. The text reads sātoṅā (সাতোঙা, in the scans of A and C; the 1960 edition has sātoñā).
